     No displacement → could be either, but leans inducement
     Strong displacement → leans CHoCH (institutions drove through)

  3. HTF context:
     HTF strongly in the current trend direction → favors inducement
     HTF at a major reversal zone → favors CHoCH

  4. What happens immediately after?
     Quick reversal with displacement → inducement confirmed
     Continuation + LH/HL forms in new direction → CHoCH confirmed

The system should NOT make a definitive call immediately.
Label it as "minor break — monitoring for inducement or CHoCH"
and update as follow-through data comes in.

────────────────────────────────────────
INDUCEMENT AS AN ENTRY SIGNAL
────────────────────────────────────────
Inducement, once confirmed, is actually one of the best entry
opportunities:

  Uptrend + minor swing low breaks + reverses with displacement:
    → Liquidity has been taken (stops below the minor swing)
    → Institutions have filled orders (using that liquidity)
    → Price is ready to make a new HH
    → The displacement candle after the inducement often creates
      an OB and/or FVG that serves as the entry zone

  This IS the "sweep → displacement → OB/FVG → entry" pattern
  that Entry Model B captures. Inducement is the mechanism,
  Entry Model B is the execution.

================================================================================
4.7  STRUCTURE MAPPING ACROSS MULTIPLE TIMEFRAMES
================================================================================

The system must maintain a COMPOSITE structural view that
integrates all timeframes into a single assessment.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE COMPOSITE VIEW
────────────────────────────────────────
After processing each timeframe (W → D → 4H → 1H):

  COMPOSITE_STRUCTURE = {
    weekly_trend:     "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "RANGING"
    weekly_strength:  "STRONG" | "WEAK"
    daily_bias:       "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "UNCLEAR"
    daily_event:      last BOS/CHoCH type and when
    four_h_direction: "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "TRANSITION"
    four_h_alignment: "WITH_DAILY" | "PULLBACK" | "CONFLICT"
    one_h_status:     "IMPULSE" | "PULLBACK" | "TRANSITION"
    one_h_entry_ready: true | false
    overall_bias:      "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "NEUTRAL"
    overall_confidence: percentage
    conflicts:         list of any timeframe disagreements
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
CONFLICT RESOLUTION
────────────────────────────────────────
When timeframes disagree:

  Weekly Bullish + Daily Bullish + 4H Bullish + 1H Bullish:
  → No conflict. Maximum confidence. Full size entries.

  Weekly Bullish + Daily Bullish + 4H Bearish + 1H Bearish:
  → 4H and 1H are in pullback against W/D. This is normal.
  → Bias remains bullish (Daily rules for intraday)
  → Wait for 4H (then 1H) to realign bullish
  → Entry at the pullback bottom within a Daily demand zone

  Weekly Bullish + Daily Bearish:
  → Significant conflict. Daily has changed against Weekly.
  → Two possibilities:
    a) Daily is in a pullback within Weekly uptrend → will realign
    b) Daily is starting a reversal → Weekly may follow eventually
  → Action: REDUCED confidence. If trading, trade with DAILY bias
    (bearish) but with smaller size and tighter management.
  → Watch for Daily to either: resume bullish (scenario a, safe
    to buy again) or confirm bearish with BOS (scenario b, bearish
    setups now primary).

  Weekly Bearish + Daily Bullish:
  → Daily may be in a bear rally (counter-trend within Weekly)
  → Dangerous to buy — the Weekly is against you
  → If Daily bullish is confirmed with multiple BOS → could be
    a genuine Weekly reversal beginning
  → Action: very cautious. If taking longs, use HTF Reversal Trade
    rules (75% confidence, 4:1 R:R, 50% size) OR wait for Weekly
    to show CHoCH before fully committing to bullish.

  Any timeframe unclear/ranging:
  → Reduce confidence for that layer
  → If DAILY is ranging → overall bias = NEUTRAL → HOLD
  → If 4H is ranging while Daily is trending → trade with Daily,
    but expect 4H to be choppy (wider SL may be needed)

────────────────────────────────────────
STRUCTURE STATE TRACKING
────────────────────────────────────────
The system should maintain a running state for each timeframe:

  For each TF (W, D, 4H, 1H):
  {
    current_trend:      "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "RANGING"
    trend_strength:     "STRONG" | "MODERATE" | "WEAK"
    last_bos_index:     candle index of last BOS
    last_bos_quality:   score (5-25)
    last_choch_index:   candle index of last CHoCH (if any)
    last_choch_quality: score (5-25)
    key_swing_high:     {index, price, quality} of the important SH
    key_swing_low:      {index, price, quality} of the important SL
    in_pullback:        true/false
    pullback_depth:     percentage from last impulse extreme
  }

This state is updated every time new candle data is processed.
When a BOS or CHoCH occurs, the state transitions:
  BOS: trend continues, update last_bos, new key_swing
  CHoCH: trend may be changing, update last_choch,
         set trend_strength to "WEAK" or transition to opposite

================================================================================
4.8  COMMON STRUCTURAL ANALYSIS MISTAKES
================================================================================

These errors lead directly to wrong bias → wrong trades → losses.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: CALLING CHoCH ON MINOR SWING BREAKS
────────────────────────────────────────
Uptrend is making HH/HL. Price breaks below a small dip (N=2
swing low between two major HLs). Trader calls CHoCH and sells.
Price resumes the uptrend. Loss.

Fix: Only call CHoCH when a MODERATE+ quality swing is broken.
Minor swing breaks are inducement until proven otherwise.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: IGNORING HTF WHEN LTF STRUCTURE CHANGES
────────────────────────────────────────
1H turns bearish (CHoCH + BOS down on 1H). Trader immediately
shorts. But Daily is strongly bullish, and price is pulling back
to a Daily OB. 1H bearish is a pullback, not a trend change.
Price bounces from the Daily OB and the short gets stopped out.

Fix: Always check: is the LTF structure break WITHIN the HTF
structure (internal) or breaking the HTF structure (external)?
If internal → it is a pullback. Wait for realignment.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: TREATING EVERY BOS AS EQUAL
────────────────────────────────────────
Not all BOS events are equal. A BOS with displacement, high volume,
breaking a strong swing = highly reliable continuation signal.
A BOS with weak candle, low volume, breaking a minor swing =
could easily reverse.

Fix: Use the BOS Quality Score. Only trade based on BOS with
score 14+ (Standard or Strong).

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: NOT WAITING FOR THE PULLBACK AFTER BOS
────────────────────────────────────────
BOS occurs (new HH in uptrend). Trader buys immediately at the
new high. Price pulls back to the OB/FVG (normal behavior after
BOS). Trader's entry is underwater and possibly stopped out
before price continues.

Fix: After BOS, EXPECT a pullback. Wait for it. Enter on the
pullback at the OB/FVG with LTF confirmation. This gives better
entry, tighter SL, and higher R:R.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: CHANGING BIAS ON EVERY 1H CANDLE
────────────────────────────────────────
Trader sees a bearish 1H candle and thinks "structure is turning."
Then a bullish candle and thinks "still bullish." Then bearish again.
This creates analysis paralysis and random entries.

Fix: Structure changes at CONFIRMED SWING BREAKS (CHoCH/BOS),
not at individual candles. A bearish candle within a bullish
structure is normal. A confirmed break of the HL is not.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 6: COUNTING EVERY SWING FOR STRUCTURE
────────────────────────────────────────
On 15m, there might be 30 swing points in a day. Using all of them
for structural analysis creates a confusing mess where "structure"
changes every 30 minutes.

Fix: Use appropriate N values. On 15m, use N=3 for structure (not
N=2). This reduces the swing count to meaningful levels. For micro-
structure on the entry TF, N=2 is acceptable but only for the
specific purpose of entry SL placement, not for bias determination.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 7: FORGETTING THAT STRUCTURE IS FRACTAL
────────────────────────────────────────
Each timeframe has its own complete structure. A bearish structure
on 15m can exist within a bullish structure on 4H within a bearish
structure on Weekly. These are not contradictions — they are the
fractal nature of markets.

Fix: Always know which timeframe's structure you are referencing.
"Structure is bearish" is meaningless without specifying the TF.
"1H structure is bearish while 4H structure is bullish, indicating
a pullback" is a complete and actionable assessment.

4.11  RAILROAD TRACKS (ENGINEERED REVERSAL PATTERN)
================================================================================
Added from official ICT foundational source material: a specific
two-candle reversal signature, mechanically detectable and worth
naming explicitly when it appears at a significant level (HTF swing,
OB, or FVG).

DETECTION FROM OHLC:
  Two consecutive candles [i], [i+1] where:
    - candle[i] and candle[i+1] are OPPOSITE colors
      (one bullish, one bearish)
    - Their bodies are approximately EQUAL in size:
      abs(body[i] - body[i+1]) / max(body[i], body[i+1]) < 0.25
      (within ~25% of each other - "roughly mirrored")
    - Both candles have small wicks relative to their bodies
      (body_pct > 60% for both)
    - This pair occurs AT a significant level (recent swing high/low,
      OB zone, or FVG boundary) - the pattern alone in open space is
      not meaningful.

WHAT IT MEANS:
  Two candles of near-identical size moving in opposite directions at
  the same level is a strong visual signature of an "engineered"
  reversal - price was pushed decisively one way, then reversed with
  matching force, at a level that already carries structural
  significance. Treat as a confluence factor strengthening a reversal
  read at that level - not a standalone signal by itself.

================================================================================
================================================================================
END OF [MARKET_STRUCTURE]
================================================================================



================================================================================
[ORDER_BLOCKS]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Order Blocks are the most important zones in ICT methodology. They
represent price levels where institutions placed large orders that
were not fully filled during the initial move. When price returns to
these zones, the remaining unfilled orders activate, causing a
reaction — typically a continuation in the direction of the original
institutional move.

An Order Block is NOT simply "the last red candle before a green move"
or "the last green candle before a red move." That oversimplified
definition leads to identifying dozens of false OBs that have no
institutional significance. A genuine Order Block is identifiable by
what happens AFTER it: a displacement move that breaks structure. The
displacement is the proof that institutional orders were placed there.
Without displacement, there is no evidence of institutional activity,
and the zone is just another candle.

This section defines exactly how to detect, validate, grade, and trade
Order Blocks from raw OHLC data. Every step is grounded in numbers,
not visual impressions.

================================================================================
5.1  WHAT AN ORDER BLOCK ACTUALLY IS
================================================================================

To detect Order Blocks correctly, the system must understand what
they represent at the institutional level.

THE INSTITUTIONAL PROBLEM:
An institution needs to buy 500 million dollars worth of BTC.
If they place a single market order for $500M, the price will spike
dramatically (slippage). Instead, they accumulate over time:
  - Place limit buy orders at a specific price zone
  - Wait for sellers to come to that zone
  - As sellers hit their bids, the institution fills gradually
  - After sufficient filling, the institution allows price to move
    (they stop absorbing selling, and their buying creates demand
    imbalance → price rises with displacement)

THE FOOTPRINT IN OHLC DATA:
The candle(s) where the institution was accumulating appear as:
  - One or more candles moving AGAINST the eventual institutional
    direction (bearish candles before a bullish move, or vice versa)
  - These candles are the LAST opposing candles before the displacement
  - The displacement itself (strong move with large body, high volume)
    is the evidence that institutional orders were filled at that zone

THE ORDER BLOCK IS:
  The zone defined by the last opposing candle(s) before displacement.
  This zone contains unfilled institutional orders. When price returns,
  those orders activate → price reacts from this zone.

THE ORDER BLOCK IS NOT:
  - Any random candle before a move (must have displacement after)
  - A candle before a normal-sized move (displacement criteria must be met)
  - A zone that has been tested multiple times (orders get filled/exhausted)
  - A visual pattern (it is a logical construct based on institutional behavior)

================================================================================
5.2  BULLISH ORDER BLOCK — DETECTION ALGORITHM
================================================================================

A Bullish OB is a demand zone where institutions placed BUY orders.
Price moving down into this zone is expected to bounce (buyers activate).

────────────────────────────────────────
STEP-BY-STEP DETECTION FROM OHLC DATA
────────────────────────────────────────

STEP 1: FIND THE IMPULSE (the displacement move)

Scan the candle array for BULLISH displacement:
  For each candle[i]:
    Calculate range = high - low
    Calculate body = |close - open|
    Calculate body_pct = body / range
    Get vol_ratio = volume / SMA(volume, 20)

    An impulse is a sequence of 1-5 consecutive candles where:
      - The cumulative range of the sequence ≥ 2× ATR(14)
      - The majority of candles are bullish (close > open)
      - At least one candle in the sequence meets displacement criteria:
        range > 2× ATR, body_pct > 70%, vol_ratio > 1.5
      - OR: the cumulative move (sequence high - sequence low) ≥ 2× ATR
        even if no single candle individually meets displacement criteria
        (institutional moves sometimes spread across 2-3 candles)

    Record: impulse_start_index, impulse_end_index
            impulse_low = min(low[start..end])
            impulse_high = max(high[start..end])
            impulse_range = impulse_high - impulse_low

    A valid impulse must also show:
      - Net bullish direction: close[end] > open[start]
      - body_pct of the overall move > 60% when measured as:
        |close[end] - open[start]| / impulse_range

STEP 2: VERIFY STRUCTURE BREAK (BOS)

The impulse must break a prior swing high:
  Check: does impulse_high exceed the most recent swing high
  that existed BEFORE the impulse started?

  If yes → the impulse broke structure (BOS confirmed)
           → this is an institutionally significant move
  If no  → the impulse did not break structure
           → the move may be significant but the OB is weaker
           → demote the OB quality (see grading section)

  Note: the structure break confirms that the impulse was not just
  a bounce within a range, but a genuine directional move with
  enough force to change the structural landscape.

STEP 3: LOCATE THE ORDER BLOCK CANDLE(S)

Go backward from impulse_start_index.
Find the LAST bearish candle (close < open) before the impulse:

  ob_candle_index = impulse_start_index - 1
  While candle[ob_candle_index] is bullish (close > open):
    ob_candle_index -= 1
    If ob_candle_index < 0: no OB found, abort

  candle[ob_candle_index] is the Order Block candle.

  If there are CONSECUTIVE bearish candles immediately before the
  impulse, the OB zone may include multiple candles:
    Scan backward while candle is bearish AND within 3 candles
    of the impulse start.
    The OB zone = from the earliest bearish candle to the one
    immediately before the impulse.

  Typically, the OB is 1 candle. Sometimes 2. Rarely 3.
  More than 3 consecutive bearish candles before a bullish impulse
  suggests extended selling, which weakens the OB concept (the
  accumulation was not concentrated in a clean zone).

STEP 4: DEFINE THE OB ZONE

Two methods for defining the zone boundaries:

STANDARD METHOD (wider zone, conservative):
  OB_top = candle[ob_candle_index].open
    (the open of the bearish candle — where selling began)
  OB_bottom = candle[ob_candle_index].low
    (the lowest point — where buying absorbed all selling)
  Zone = OB_bottom to OB_top

REFINED METHOD (tighter zone, aggressive):
  OB_top = candle[ob_candle_index].open
  OB_bottom = candle[ob_candle_index].close
    (the close of the bearish candle — only the body, not the wick)
  Zone = OB_bottom to OB_top

  Why close instead of low? The body represents where actual
  transactions settled. The wick below was rejected — price
  didn't stay there. Institutional orders were filled in the
  body range, not in the wick.

Which method to use:
  - For HTF OBs (Daily, 4H): use STANDARD method
    (wider zone = more likely to catch the retest)
  - For LTF OBs (1H, 15m, 5m): use REFINED method
    (tighter zone = better R:R for entry)
  - When an OB overlaps with an FVG: use the overlap zone
    (the intersection of both = highest probability reaction)

STEP 5: RECORD THE OB

Store:
  {
    type: "BULLISH_OB"
    timeframe: "4H" (or whichever TF detected on)
    ob_candle_index: index
    ob_top: price (open of OB candle)
    ob_bottom: price (low or close of OB candle)
    ob_ce: (ob_top + ob_bottom) / 2 (Consequent Encroachment)
    impulse_range: distance of the displacement move
    impulse_ratio: impulse_range / ob_candle_range
    bos_confirmed: true/false
    bos_swing_index: which swing was broken
    volume_on_impulse: max vol_ratio in the impulse
    volume_on_ob: vol_ratio of the OB candle
    tests: 0 (fresh — never retested)
    status: "ACTIVE"
    quality_tier: calculated in Step 6
    created_fvg: true/false (did the impulse create an FVG?)
    fvg_overlap: true/false (does the OB overlap with the FVG?)
  }

STEP 6: ASSESS QUALITY (see section 5.4)

================================================================================
5.3  BEARISH ORDER BLOCK — DETECTION ALGORITHM
================================================================================

A Bearish OB is a supply zone where institutions placed SELL orders.
Price moving up into this zone is expected to reverse down.

The algorithm is the mirror of Bullish OB detection:

STEP 1: Find BEARISH impulse (displacement down)
  - Sequence of 1-5 candles moving down aggressively
  - Cumulative range ≥ 2× ATR
  - At least one displacement candle (or cumulative displacement)
  - Net bearish: close[end] < open[start]

STEP 2: Verify BOS — impulse breaks a prior swing LOW
  If impulse_low < most recent swing low → BOS confirmed

STEP 3: Locate the OB candle
  Go backward from impulse start
  Find the LAST BULLISH candle (close > open) before the impulse
  This is the Bearish OB candle

STEP 4: Define the zone
  STANDARD: OB_bottom = candle.open, OB_top = candle.high
  REFINED:  OB_bottom = candle.open, OB_top = candle.close
  (For a bearish OB, the open is lower than the close on a bullish
   candle, so: OB_bottom = open, OB_top = close or high)

  Correction for clarity:
  The OB candle is BULLISH (close > open):
    OB_bottom = candle.open (Standard) or candle.close (if using low end)
    OB_top = candle.high (Standard) or candle.close (Refined)

  Standard: open to high (full candle range where buying occurred)
  Refined: open to close (body only — where transactions settled)

STEP 5: Record (mirror of bullish, with type "BEARISH_OB")
STEP 6: Assess quality

================================================================================
5.4  ORDER BLOCK QUALITY GRADING
================================================================================

Not all Order Blocks are worth trading. The quality grading system
ensures the system only acts on institutionally significant zones
and ignores noise.

────────────────────────────────────────
6 QUALITY CRITERIA
────────────────────────────────────────

CRITERION 1: IMPULSE RATIO (most important)
  impulse_ratio = impulse_range / ob_candle_range

  What this measures: how powerful was the move that left this zone?
  A tiny OB candle followed by a massive displacement = strong evidence
  of concentrated institutional orders.

  ≥ 3.0 → Score: 5/5 (explosive — institutions were very aggressive)
  2.0-2.99 → Score: 4/5 (strong displacement)
  1.5-1.99 → Score: 3/5 (moderate — acceptable)
  1.0-1.49 → Score: 2/5 (weak — impulse barely larger than OB)
  < 1.0 → Score: 1/5 (no real impulse — NOT a valid OB)

  If impulse_ratio < 1.0, the zone should not be classified as an OB.
  There was no displacement. It is just a normal candle followed by
  a normal-sized move.

CRITERION 2: STRUCTURE BREAK (BOS AFTER OB)
  Did the impulse break a prior swing (creating a BOS)?

  Yes, broke a STRONG swing (quality 20+) → Score: 5/5
  Yes, broke a MODERATE swing (quality 15-19) → Score: 4/5
  Yes, broke a WEAK swing → Score: 3/5
  No swing broken, but moved significantly → Score: 2/5
  No swing broken, move was contained → Score: 1/5

  Why this matters: BOS proves the institutional move was strong
  enough to change market structure. Without BOS, the move from
  the OB may have been a normal bounce within a range — and the
  OB may not hold on retest.

CRITERION 3: VOLUME PROFILE
  Compare volume on the impulse vs volume on the OB candle.

  The institutional logic:
  - OB candle: institutions are quietly accumulating (absorbing)
    → volume may be moderate (they don't want to show their hand)
  - Impulse: institutions let price run (stop absorbing the other side)
    → volume increases as the move triggers stops and attracts
    momentum traders

  impulse_max_vol_ratio > 2.0 AND ob_vol_ratio < 1.5 → Score: 5/5
    (high impulse vol + moderate OB vol = textbook institutional pattern)
  impulse_max_vol_ratio > 1.5 AND ob_vol_ratio < 2.0 → Score: 4/5
  impulse_max_vol_ratio > 1.0 → Score: 3/5
  impulse_max_vol_ratio < 1.0 → Score: 2/5 (low volume impulse = suspect)
  Both OB and impulse have low volume → Score: 1/5 (thin market, unreliable)

  Special case: very high volume on the OB candle (vol_ratio > 2.5)
  combined with high volume on the impulse → could be genuine
  (heavy absorption then release). Score: 3/5 (ambiguous but possible)

CRITERION 4: FRESHNESS (number of retests)
  tests = 0 → Score: 5/5 (FRESH — never touched, all orders unfilled)
  tests = 1 → Score: 3/5 (Mitigation Block — some orders filled,
                           still has remaining orders)
  tests = 2 → Score: 2/5 (Weakened — most orders likely filled)
  tests ≥ 3 → Score: 0/5 (EXHAUSTED — do not trade this OB)

  How to count tests:
  A "test" occurs when price returns to the OB zone and the BODY
  of any candle enters the zone:
    For Bullish OB: candle.close < ob_top AND candle.low ≤ ob_top
      (price came down into the zone)
    For Bearish OB: candle.close > ob_bottom AND candle.high ≥ ob_bottom
      (price came up into the zone)

  A wick into the zone WITHOUT body entry is NOT a full test:
    It counts as a "wick test" — the zone rejected price, which
    actually STRENGTHENS the OB (it held). Do not increment
    the test count for wick-only touches.

CRITERION 5: PREMIUM/DISCOUNT CONTEXT
  Is the OB in the correct zone for its type?

  Bullish OB in Discount zone (below EQ) → Score: 5/5
    (buying where it's cheap — institutional logic)
  Bullish OB at EQ (around 50%) → Score: 3/5
    (neutral zone — could work but no edge from positioning)
  Bullish OB in Premium zone (above EQ) → Score: 1/5
    (buying where it's expensive — against institutional logic)
    This Bullish OB is unlikely to hold. Institutions don't
    accumulate longs in premium. This OB was likely a retail
    trap or a minor bounce, not genuine accumulation.

  Bearish OB in Premium → Score: 5/5 (selling where it's expensive ✓)
  Bearish OB at EQ → Score: 3/5
  Bearish OB in Discount → Score: 1/5 (selling cheap — unlikely to hold)

CRITERION 6: TIMEFRAME WEIGHT
  The timeframe on which the OB was identified:

  Weekly OB → Score: 5/5 (extremely rare, extremely strong)
  Daily OB → Score: 5/5 (rare, very strong)
  4H OB → Score: 4/5 (moderately common, strong)
  1H OB → Score: 3/5 (common, moderate — needs HTF support)
  15m OB → Score: 2/5 (very common, weak alone — for entry only)
  5m OB → Score: 1/5 (extremely common, noise alone — sniper entry only)

  A 15m or 5m OB has value ONLY when it sits inside a HTF OB zone.
  A 5m OB in open space (not at any HTF level) is noise.

────────────────────────────────────────
QUALITY TIERS
────────────────────────────────────────
Sum the 6 criteria scores (each 0-5): Total range 0-30

  S-TIER (26-30): Exceptional
    All criteria met at highest level.
    Fresh + displacement + BOS + correct zone + HTF + volume
    This is the best possible setup. Maximum confidence.
    Typically appears only on HTF (Daily/4H) and is rare.
    When you find one → this is a high-conviction trade.

  A-TIER (21-25): Strong
    Most criteria met at high level.
    Strong OB with good context. Trade with full confidence.

  B-TIER (16-20): Good
    Core criteria met but some weaknesses.
    Acceptable for trading WITH additional confluences.
    Needs: alignment with HTF bias + LTF confirmation.

  C-TIER (11-15): Weak
    Significant weaknesses. Multiple criteria below par.
    Trade ONLY with exceptional additional confluences:
    (FVG overlap + liquidity swept + Kill Zone + 5+ confluences)
    Consider reducing position size to 50%.

  D-TIER (6-10): Poor
    Major issues. OB is likely not institutionally significant.
    Do NOT trade this OB.
    If price reaches it, it will likely break through.

  F-TIER (0-5): Invalid
    Not a real Order Block. Discard from analysis.

────────────────────────────────────────
PRACTICAL GRADING EXAMPLE
────────────────────────────────────────
Scenario: 4H Bullish OB detected at $98,200-$98,450

  Criterion 1 (Impulse Ratio):
    OB candle range: $250, Impulse range: $1,200
    Ratio: 4.8 → Score: 5/5

  Criterion 2 (BOS):
    Impulse broke the previous 4H swing high at $99,600
    That swing was quality 18 (Moderate) → Score: 4/5

  Criterion 3 (Volume):
    OB candle vol_ratio: 1.1 (normal)
    Impulse max vol_ratio: 2.4 (high)
    High impulse + normal OB → Score: 5/5

  Criterion 4 (Freshness):
    Tests: 0 (fresh, never retested) → Score: 5/5

  Criterion 5 (Premium/Discount):
    Current range: SL=$97,000, SH=$101,500
    EQ = $99,250
    OB at $98,200-$98,450 → below EQ → Discount → Score: 5/5

  Criterion 6 (Timeframe):
    4H → Score: 4/5

  Total: 5+4+5+5+5+4 = 28/30 → S-TIER

  Assessment: exceptional OB. If price returns to this zone with
  LTF confirmation, this is a high-conviction long entry.

================================================================================
5.5  ORDER BLOCK INVALIDATION
================================================================================

An OB is invalidated when evidence shows that the institutional
orders in the zone have been overwhelmed or exhausted.

────────────────────────────────────────
INVALIDATION RULE 1: BODY CLOSE THROUGH
────────────────────────────────────────
For a Bullish OB:
  If any candle's CLOSE is below the OB_bottom → OB is DEAD.

  What this means: price entered the zone and SETTLED below it.
  The institutional buy orders in the zone were not sufficient
  to hold price. They were overwhelmed by selling. The zone has
  failed as demand.

For a Bearish OB:
  If any candle's CLOSE is above the OB_top → OB is DEAD.

CRITICAL DISTINCTION — WICK vs BODY:
  A WICK through the OB zone is NOT invalidation.
  In fact, a wick through that returns inside = the OB HELD.
  This is a liquidity sweep of the OB zone:
    - Stops placed below the OB are triggered (liquidity)
    - Institutional orders in the zone absorb the selling
    - Price reverses back into/above the zone
    - This actually CONFIRMS the OB's strength

  The system must distinguish:
    candle.close below OB_bottom → invalidation (zone failed)
    candle.low below OB_bottom but close above → sweep (zone held = stronger)

  This distinction is one of the most valuable edges in OB trading.
  Many traders see the wick below and panic. The professional sees
  the wick below with body closing back inside and recognizes:
  "the zone was tested with a sweep and held — this is now stronger."

────────────────────────────────────────
INVALIDATION RULE 2: EXCESSIVE RETESTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  tests ≥ 3 → OB is EXHAUSTED (functionally dead)

  Each retest fills some of the institutional orders in the zone.
  After 3 retests, most orders have been filled. The zone no longer
  has sufficient unfilled orders to cause a meaningful reaction.

  Test count tracking:
    test = 0: Fresh (strongest)
    test = 1: Mitigated once (Mitigation Block — still has orders)
              Strength reduced to ~70% of original
    test = 2: Mitigated twice (weakened significantly)
              Strength reduced to ~40% of original
    test ≥ 3: Exhausted (dead — remove from active OBs)

  Remember: wick touches that don't enter with body are NOT tests.
  Only body entry into the zone counts as a test.

────────────────────────────────────────
INVALIDATION RULE 3: TIME DECAY (IPDA CONTEXT)
────────────────────────────────────────
OBs have a time-based relevance:
  Within 20-day IPDA range → highest priority (most relevant)
  Within 40-day range → moderate priority
  Within 60-day range → lower priority
  Beyond 60-day → consider expired for intraday purposes

  An OB created 90 days ago that has never been tested is technically
  still "fresh" (test = 0), but its practical relevance for intraday
  trading is low. Institutional positioning from 3 months ago may
  no longer be relevant to current market dynamics.

  Exception: Weekly and Monthly OBs can remain relevant for much
  longer (months to years). Time decay applies primarily to 4H and
  lower timeframe OBs.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHAT HAPPENS WHEN AN OB IS INVALIDATED
────────────────────────────────────────
When a Bullish OB is invalidated (body close below):
  1. Mark it as DEAD in the OB tracking system
  2. Do NOT use it for any future analysis or entry
  3. BUT: this failed OB may become a BREAKER BLOCK (see section 5.6)
     The zone that was demand has now been proven to be too weak.
     When price returns to it from below, it may act as resistance
     (the trapped buyers from the failed OB will sell to exit at
     breakeven or small loss).

When a Bearish OB is invalidated (body close above):
  Same process. The failed supply zone may become a bullish Breaker.

================================================================================
5.6  OB LIFECYCLE AND ADVANCED TYPES
================================================================================

An Order Block is not a static zone. It evolves through a lifecycle,
and certain lifecycle events create distinct subtypes that the system
must recognize.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE OB LIFECYCLE
────────────────────────────────────────

Stage 1: FORMATION
  Impulse with displacement creates the OB.
  Status: FRESH (test = 0)
  Strength: 100%
  Action: Wait for price to return to this zone.

Stage 2: FIRST RETEST (MITIGATION)
  Price returns to the OB zone. Body enters.
  Two sub-outcomes:
    a) Price reacts (bounces from the zone):
       Status: MITIGATED (test = 1)
       Strength: ~70%
       This is now a Mitigation Block.
       Still tradeable but with reduced confidence.

    b) Price breaks through (body close beyond zone):
       Status: DEAD
       The OB has failed. See INVALIDATION section.
       May become a Breaker Block.

Stage 3: SECOND RETEST
  Price returns to the Mitigation Block again.
    a) Reacts: test = 2, Strength: ~40%. Barely tradeable.
       Needs exceptional additional confluence to consider.
    b) Breaks: DEAD. Breaker Block potential.

Stage 4: THIRD+ RETEST
  Status: EXHAUSTED. Remove from active zones.
  No institutional orders remain. The zone is noise now.

────────────────────────────────────────
BREAKER BLOCK
────────────────────────────────────────
A Breaker Block is a FAILED Order Block that converts to the
opposite type. This is one of ICT's most powerful concepts.

HOW IT FORMS:

Bullish Breaker (from failed Bearish OB):
  1. A Bearish OB exists at $101,000-$101,300
  2. Price rises into the zone (testing the supply)
  3. Price BREAKS THROUGH — candle closes above $101,300
  4. The Bearish OB is invalidated (it failed as supply)
  5. The zone $101,000-$101,300 is now a BULLISH BREAKER
  6. When price returns to this zone from above, it acts as
     DEMAND (support) instead of supply

  Why? Because:
  - Traders who sold at the OB are now trapped (they're short
    and price is above their entry)
  - When price returns to their entry level, they cover (buy back)
    to exit at breakeven
  - This buying creates demand at the zone
  - Additionally, the break of the OB signaled strength, and the
    return to the zone is a pullback within that strength

Bearish Breaker (from failed Bullish OB):
  Mirror image. A Bullish OB fails (price breaks below it).
  The zone converts to supply. Trapped longs sell on the return.

DETECTION FROM OHLC:
  1. System has a DEAD OB (invalidated by body close through)
  2. After invalidation, monitor for price to return to the zone
     FROM THE OTHER SIDE:
     - Failed Bearish OB: price pulls BACK DOWN to the zone
     - Failed Bullish OB: price pulls BACK UP to the zone
  3. If price returns AND shows a reaction (rejection candle,
     displacement, LTF structure shift) → Breaker Block confirmed

QUALITY OF BREAKER BLOCKS:
  The strength of a Breaker depends on:
  - How strong the ORIGINAL OB was (S-tier OB that failed → strong Breaker)
  - How aggressively the OB was broken (displacement through → strong)
  - Whether the break was a genuine structural event (BOS after break)
  - Freshness: a Breaker, like an OB, weakens with each retest

  Breaker Blocks are typically B-TIER to A-TIER in quality.
  They are rarely S-TIER (because the original zone already showed
  weakness by failing once).

────────────────────────────────────────
PROPULSION BLOCK
────────────────────────────────────────
A Propulsion Block is the FIRST Order Block that forms after a
Change of Character (CHoCH). It carries special significance because
it represents the first institutional positioning in the new trend
direction.

DETECTION:
  1. CHoCH has been detected on a timeframe
  2. After the CHoCH, look for the first impulse with displacement
     in the new direction
  3. The OB formed by this impulse = Propulsion Block

WHY IT MATTERS:
  - It is the first evidence of institutional commitment to the new trend
  - Institutions who initiated the CHoCH move need more orders to
    build their full position
  - They placed their first orders at the CHoCH zone, and the
    Propulsion Block is where they continue building
  - Price returning to the Propulsion Block is a high-probability
    entry because: institutions want more at this price, and they
    have already shown directional commitment (the CHoCH)

QUALITY:
  Propulsion Blocks are inherently strong (A to S tier) when:
  - The CHoCH had displacement
  - The Propulsion Block itself has displacement
  - The two together show institutional aggression in the new direction

  Weaker when:
  - The CHoCH was weak (no displacement, minor swing break)
  - The Propulsion Block displacement was moderate

────────────────────────────────────────
REJECTION BLOCK
────────────────────────────────────────
A Rejection Block is formed by a candle with a very long wick at a
significant level. It is not a traditional OB (which is based on
the last opposing candle before an impulse). Instead, it is based
on the wick of a single candle that shows extreme institutional
rejection.

DETECTION:
  1. Find a candle where:
     - wick (upper or lower) ≥ 70% of total range
     - The candle is AT a HTF level (Daily/Weekly OB, major swing,
       session high/low)
     - vol_ratio > 2.0 (very high volume on this candle)
     - range > 1× ATR (the candle must have meaningful size)

  2. Define the zone:
     For a Bullish Rejection Block (long lower wick at demand):
       Zone = body low (close or open, whichever is lower)
              to wick extreme (the low of the candle)
       This is the area where massive buying absorbed selling
       and pushed price back up aggressively within a single candle.

     For a Bearish Rejection Block (long upper wick at supply):
       Zone = body high (close or open, whichever is higher)
              to wick extreme (the high of the candle)

  3. The zone acts like an OB:
     When price returns to this zone, expect a reaction.
     The institutional orders that caused the rejection are
     partially unfilled in this area.

QUALITY:
  Rejection Blocks at Weekly/Daily levels with vol_ratio > 3.0
  can be S-TIER (extremely rare but extremely strong).

  Rejection Blocks at 4H/1H levels are B to A-TIER.

  Rejection Blocks on 15m/5m are rarely significant enough to trade
  independently (treat as entry confirmation within a HTF setup).

  Key requirement: the level must be institutionally significant.
  A long wick in the middle of nowhere is just a spike, not a
  Rejection Block.

────────────────────────────────────────
STACKED ORDER BLOCKS
────────────────────────────────────────
When two or more OBs from different timeframes overlap in the same
price zone, they create a "stacked" zone of exceptional strength.

DETECTION:
  1. Identify OBs on each timeframe
  2. Check for price overlap:
     If OB_A (4H) zone 98200-98450 AND OB_B (1H) zone 98250-98380:
     → Overlap exists: 98250-98380
     → This overlap is a Stacked OB

SIGNIFICANCE:
  - Two institutional timeframes agree on the same demand/supply zone
  - The probability of reaction is much higher than a single OB
  - Stacked OBs with FVG overlap (see FAIR_VALUE_GAPS section) are
    the single highest-probability zones in ICT methodology

QUALITY:
  Automatically upgrade by one tier:
  - B-TIER individual OBs → A-TIER when stacked
  - A-TIER individual OBs → S-TIER when stacked

────────────────────────────────────────
OB NARROWING (REFINEMENT)
────────────────────────────────────────
A technique for getting tighter entries (and better R:R) by using
a lower timeframe to identify the precise area within an HTF OB
where institutional orders are concentrated.

PROCESS:
  1. Identify a HTF OB (e.g., 4H Bullish OB at $98,200-$98,450)
  2. Drop to LTF (e.g., 15m or 5m)
  3. Within the HTF OB zone, look for the LTF OB:
     - Where did the LTF displacement originate within this zone?
     - The LTF OB within the HTF OB = the refined entry zone
  4. The refined zone is typically 30-50% of the HTF OB size

  Example:
    4H OB: $98,200-$98,450 (250 point zone)
    Within this zone on 15m: displacement originated from
    15m bearish candle at $98,300-$98,350
    Refined entry zone: $98,300-$98,350 (50 point zone)
    SL: below $98,200 (the HTF OB bottom)
    R:R improves dramatically with the tighter entry

  IMPORTANT: the SL stays beyond the HTF OB (not just the LTF OB).
  If the HTF OB fails, the LTF refinement doesn't matter.
  The refinement is for ENTRY precision, not for SL tightening
  beyond the HTF structural level.

================================================================================
5.7  TRADING FROM ORDER BLOCKS
================================================================================

This section connects OB analysis to actual trade execution.

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY METHODS
────────────────────────────────────────

METHOD 1: AGGRESSIVE (Limit Order at OB edge)
  Place a limit order at:
    Bullish OB: buy limit at OB_top (or just above OB_bottom for tighter entry)
    Bearish OB: sell limit at OB_bottom (or just below OB_top)

  Pros: best possible entry price, tightest SL, highest R:R
  Cons: price may not reach the OB (NO_FILL), or may wick through
        and stop you out before reversing (SL_TOO_TIGHT risk)

  Best for: S-TIER and A-TIER OBs with strong HTF support

METHOD 2: CE ENTRY (Limit Order at OB midpoint)
  Place a limit order at:
    OB_CE = (OB_top + OB_bottom) / 2

  Pros: balanced between fill probability and entry quality
  Cons: SL is slightly wider than aggressive, R:R slightly lower

  Best for: A-TIER and B-TIER OBs. This is the DEFAULT method.
  If unsure, use CE entry.

METHOD 3: CONSERVATIVE (Market Order after confirmation)
  Wait for price to enter the OB zone AND show a reaction:
    - Rejection candle (wick > 2× body) at the zone
    - LTF CHoCH/BOS confirming reversal from the zone
    - Displacement away from the zone (institutional response)

  Then enter with a market order (or limit on the first pullback
  after the rejection/displacement).

  Pros: highest confirmation, lowest risk of the OB failing
  Cons: worst entry price, widest SL, lowest R:R
        May miss trades where price touches OB and reverses instantly

  Best for: B-TIER and C-TIER OBs where additional confirmation
  is needed before committing.

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
SL for Bullish OB entry:
  Below OB_bottom + buffer
  Buffer = max(0.3 × ATR, 0.2% of price)

  Why below OB_bottom? Because if price closes below the entire OB
  zone, the zone has failed. The institutional orders were overwhelmed.
  The thesis is invalid.

  Why the buffer? To avoid being stopped by a wick that sweeps the
  OB bottom (which is actually bullish — the OB held a sweep).
  The buffer gives room for this sweep-and-hold pattern.

SL for Bearish OB entry:
  Above OB_top + buffer

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGET PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
TP1: The nearest valid unswept opposing liquidity level or PD Array; report actual R:R
  For Bullish OB entry:
    Look UP for the nearest: BSL (swing high), EQH, session high,
    Bearish OB (where sellers may step in), unfilled Bearish FVG
  Place TP1 slightly before (0.1-0.2% below) the target level
    (price often reverses just before reaching the exact level)

TP2: The Draw on Liquidity or HTF target
  The major liquidity pool that the current move is targeting
  Or: HTF OB on the opposite side
  Or: leave open with trailing stop (for runners)

R:R CHECK:
  After determining Entry, SL, TP1:
    R:R = |TP1 - Entry| / |Entry - SL|
    Report the actual R:R without changing the structural target.
    A user-defined risk policy may skip execution, but does not change analysis levels.

────────────────────────────────────────
COMBINING OB WITH FVG (THE STRONGEST SETUP)
────────────────────────────────────────
When an OB and FVG overlap in the same zone, this creates the
highest-probability setup in ICT methodology.

Why: Two independent institutional footprints confirm the same zone.
  - OB: institutions placed orders here
  - FVG: institutions moved price so fast they left an imbalance here
  - Both pointing to the same zone = double confirmation

Detection:
  1. Detect OB zone (e.g., $98,200-$98,450)
  2. Detect FVG zone (e.g., $98,100-$98,350)
  3. Calculate overlap: $98,200-$98,350
  4. The overlap zone is the highest-priority entry area
  5. CE of the overlap = best entry point

When OB + FVG overlap:
  - Upgrade quality by one tier automatically
  - Use the OVERLAP zone for entry (not the full OB or full FVG)
  - SL beyond the wider of the two zones (the full OB bottom)
  - Confidence boost: +5 to +10% on the confidence score

================================================================================
5.8  OB TRACKING AND STATE MANAGEMENT
================================================================================

The system must maintain a running list of active OBs for each
timeframe and update their status as new data comes in.

────────────────────────────────────────
OB LIST STRUCTURE (per timeframe)
────────────────────────────────────────
For each timeframe (W, D, 4H, 1H, 15m, 5m):
  active_obs = [
    {
      type: BULLISH_OB | BEARISH_OB | BREAKER | PROPULSION | REJECTION
      ob_top: price
      ob_bottom: price
      ob_ce: price
      quality_tier: S | A | B | C | D | F
      quality_score: 0-30
      tests: 0, 1, 2
      status: ACTIVE | MITIGATED | EXHAUSTED | DEAD
      created_at: candle index/timestamp
      impulse_ratio: number
      bos_confirmed: boolean
      fvg_overlap: boolean
      notes: string (any special conditions)
    },
    ...
  ]

────────────────────────────────────────
UPDATE RULES
────────────────────────────────────────
On each new candle:
  For each active OB:

    BULLISH OB:
      If candle.close < ob_bottom:
        → status = DEAD (invalidated)
        → Check if Breaker Block should be created

      If candle.low < ob_bottom but candle.close > ob_bottom:
        → Sweep of OB (wick penetration)
        → OB is STRONGER (not weaker)
        → Note: "swept and held"

      If candle.low ≤ ob_top and candle.close entered the zone:
        → tests += 1
        → If tests ≥ 3: status = EXHAUSTED

    BEARISH OB:
      If candle.close > ob_top:
        → status = DEAD
        → Check for Breaker Block

      If candle.high > ob_top but candle.close < ob_top:
        → Sweep, OB is stronger

      If candle.high ≥ ob_bottom and candle body entered zone:
        → tests += 1
        → If tests ≥ 3: status = EXHAUSTED

────────────────────────────────────────
PRIORITIZATION
────────────────────────────────────────
When multiple OBs exist on the same timeframe:
  Priority: S-tier > A-tier > B-tier > C-tier
  Within same tier: fresher (fewer tests) > more tested
  Between timeframes: higher TF > lower TF

  The system should highlight the TOP 3 most significant active OBs
  per direction (3 bullish, 3 bearish) across all timeframes.
  These are the zones that the next trade should revolve around.

================================================================================
5.9  COMMON OB MISTAKES
================================================================================

These mistakes lead to false OB identification and trading losses.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: CALLING EVERY CANDLE BEFORE A MOVE AN "OB"
────────────────────────────────────────
Not every move is preceded by an OB. The move must be a DISPLACEMENT
(meeting the criteria in OHLC_PROCESSING section 2.3). Without
displacement, the "OB" is just a normal candle before a normal move.

Fix: Always verify impulse_ratio ≥ 1.5 (preferably ≥ 2.0) before
classifying a zone as an OB.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: TRADING EXHAUSTED OBs
────────────────────────────────────────
An OB that has been tested 3+ times is dead. The institutional
orders have been filled. There is no reason for price to react here
again. Yet traders keep marking these zones and expecting bounces.

Fix: Strictly enforce the test count. ≥ 3 tests = EXHAUSTED = dead.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: IGNORING THE CONTEXT (PREMIUM/DISCOUNT)
────────────────────────────────────────
A bullish OB in the premium zone (above EQ) is unlikely to hold.
Institutions do not accumulate longs at expensive prices. Yet the
OB "looks good" on the chart. The trader buys and gets stopped out
as price continues down through the zone.

Fix: Always check Premium/Discount context. Bullish OB must be in
discount (or at worst, near EQ). Bearish OB must be in premium.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: USING LTF OBs WITHOUT HTF CONTEXT
────────────────────────────────────────
A 5m OB looks perfect. It has displacement, BOS, everything. But
it is in the middle of a 4H range with no HTF zone nearby. The 5m
OB is noise. It formed from a minor intraday move with no
institutional backing from higher timeframes.

Fix: LTF OBs (15m, 5m) are valid ONLY when they are located within
or at a HTF zone of interest (4H/Daily OB, FVG, or liquidity level).

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: CONFUSING WICK THROUGH WITH INVALIDATION
────────────────────────────────────────
Price wicks below the OB bottom and the trader panics: "OB is broken!"
But the CLOSE is back inside the zone. The OB held. In fact, it just
swept the stops below it (collecting liquidity) and reversed — this is
the STRONGEST confirmation an OB can give.

Fix: Only BODY CLOSE through the zone = invalidation. Wick through
with close back inside = the OB is STRONGER, not weaker.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 6: TREATING ALL OBs AS EQUAL
────────────────────────────────────────
A Daily OB with S-TIER quality is fundamentally different from a 15m
OB with C-TIER quality. Both are "Order Blocks" but they are not
equally tradeable.

Fix: Use the quality grading system. Only trade A-TIER and above with
full conviction. B-TIER needs extra confluences. C-TIER needs
exceptional circumstances. D-TIER and below = do not trade.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 7: NOT UPDATING OB STATUS
────────────────────────────────────────
An OB was identified 50 candles ago. Since then, it has been tested
twice, partially broken, and price has moved far away. But the system
still has it marked as "FRESH." The trader enters on the third test
thinking it's still strong. It breaks through.

Fix: Maintain real-time OB status tracking. Every new candle must
update every active OB's test count and status.

================================================================================
END OF [ORDER_BLOCKS]
================================================================================



================================================================================
[FAIR_VALUE_GAPS]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
A Fair Value Gap (FVG) is an imbalance in price delivery — a zone where
price moved so aggressively that one side (buyers or sellers) completely
overwhelmed the other, leaving a gap in the price action where efficient
two-sided trading did not occur.

Why do FVGs matter? Because institutions operate on the principle of
"fair value." They need to fill orders at prices where both sides of the
market participated. When price moves too fast and skips a zone, that
zone represents unfilled institutional business. The market's natural
tendency is to return to these zones to complete the price delivery —
to fill the gap.

An FVG is NOT just a visual gap on a chart. It is a specific three-candle
formation that is mathematically detectable from OHLC data. The gap
exists between the wick of candle 1 and the wick of candle 3, with
candle 2 being the aggressive candle that caused the imbalance. The
system must detect these precisely, assess their quality, track their
fill status, and use them for both entry zones and target levels.

FVGs are the second most important institutional footprint after Order
Blocks. When an FVG overlaps with an OB, this creates the single
highest-probability setup in ICT methodology.

================================================================================
6.1  BULLISH FVG — DETECTION FROM OHLC
================================================================================

A Bullish FVG is an upside imbalance — price moved up so aggressively
that a gap was left below. This gap acts as DEMAND (support). When
price returns to fill it, buyers are expected to step in.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE THREE-CANDLE FORMATION
────────────────────────────────────────

For any three consecutive candles indexed [i], [i+1], [i+2]:

  A Bullish FVG exists when:
    candle[i].high < candle[i+2].low

  This means: the HIGH of the first candle is LOWER than the LOW
  of the third candle. There is a price zone between them where
  no trading occurred — the second candle (i+1) moved through this
  zone so fast that it was never properly traded.

  FVG Zone:
    FVG_bottom = candle[i].high     (top of first candle)
    FVG_top    = candle[i+2].low    (bottom of third candle)
    FVG_size   = FVG_top - FVG_bottom

  Consequent Encroachment (CE):
    CE = (FVG_top + FVG_bottom) / 2
    CE is the MIDPOINT of the FVG. This is the single most important
    price level within the FVG. More on CE in section 6.3.

  The Creating Candle:
    candle[i+1] is the "creating candle" — the one that caused
    the imbalance. Its characteristics tell you about the quality
    of the FVG (see section 6.5).

────────────────────────────────────────
VISUAL UNDERSTANDING (from numbers)
────────────────────────────────────────

  candle[i]:   O:98000 H:98200 L:97900 C:98150
  candle[i+1]: O:98150 H:99300 L:98100 C:99200  ← creating candle
  candle[i+2]: O:99200 H:99500 L:98900 C:99400

  Check: candle[i].high = 98200
         candle[i+2].low = 98900

  98200 < 98900? YES → Bullish FVG exists

  FVG_bottom = 98200
  FVG_top    = 98900
  FVG_size   = 700 points
  CE         = 98550

  The zone between 98200 and 98900 was never properly traded.
  candle[i+1] moved through it in a single candle. If price
  returns to this zone, institutional orders are expected to
  activate (buying), causing a bounce.

────────────────────────────────────────
IMPORTANT: THE GAP IS BETWEEN CANDLE 1 AND CANDLE 3
────────────────────────────────────────

A common misunderstanding: the FVG is NOT the body of candle[i+1].
The FVG is the space between candle[i]'s high and candle[i+2]'s low.

candle[i+1] could have wicks that extend into the FVG zone — that
does not eliminate the FVG. The gap is defined by the relationship
between candles [i] and [i+2], not by candle [i+1]'s internal
structure.

However, if candle[i+1]'s low goes below candle[i]'s high (for a
bullish scenario), there is no gap — the candle retraced into the
previous candle's range. This would fail the mathematical test
(candle[i].high would NOT be less than candle[i+2].low if the
creating candle dipped back down).

The mathematical test is absolute: candle[i].high < candle[i+2].low.
If this condition is not met, there is no Bullish FVG. Period.

================================================================================
6.2  BEARISH FVG — DETECTION FROM OHLC
================================================================================

A Bearish FVG is a downside imbalance — price moved down so
aggressively that a gap was left above. This gap acts as SUPPLY
(resistance). When price returns to fill it, sellers are expected
to step in.

For any three consecutive candles [i], [i+1], [i+2]:

  A Bearish FVG exists when:
    candle[i].low > candle[i+2].high

  This means: the LOW of the first candle is HIGHER than the HIGH
  of the third candle. Price dropped through the zone between them
  without efficient trading.

  FVG Zone:
    FVG_top    = candle[i].low      (bottom of first candle)
    FVG_bottom = candle[i+2].high   (top of third candle)
    FVG_size   = FVG_top - FVG_bottom

  CE = (FVG_top + FVG_bottom) / 2

Example:
  candle[i]:   O:101000 H:101200 L:100800 C:100900
  candle[i+1]: O:100900 H:100950 L:99600  C:99700  ← creating candle
  candle[i+2]: O:99700  H:100100 L:99500  C:99600

  Check: candle[i].low = 100800
         candle[i+2].high = 100100

  100800 > 100100? YES → Bearish FVG exists

  FVG_top    = 100800
  FVG_bottom = 100100
  FVG_size   = 700 points
  CE         = 100450

  The zone 100100-100800 is the imbalance. If price returns up to
  this zone, expect selling (institutional supply activation).

================================================================================
6.3  CONSEQUENT ENCROACHMENT (CE)
================================================================================

CE is the midpoint of the FVG: (FVG_top + FVG_bottom) / 2

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY CE IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LEVEL IN AN FVG
────────────────────────────────────────

Institutional price delivery seeks BALANCE. When an FVG is created,
the market has delivered price inefficiently. When price returns to
fill the FVG, institutions consider the gap "fairly addressed" when
price has traded through at least the midpoint (CE).

Empirically (across thousands of ICT-documented examples):
  - Price reaching the CE and reversing = the most common outcome
  - Price filling to the edge and reversing = less common
  - Price filling the entire FVG = moderately common
  - Price filling past the FVG = FVG has failed

This means:
  - CE is the optimal ENTRY point when using FVG as an entry zone
    (you get a better price than the FVG edge, and the most common
    reaction point is right at CE)
  - CE is the first REACTION level to watch when price approaches
    the FVG (if price reaches CE and shows rejection → FVG is working)

────────────────────────────────────────
CE AS ENTRY LEVEL
────────────────────────────────────────
For a Bullish FVG entry:
  Entry = CE (midpoint of the gap)
  SL = below FVG_bottom (or below the OB if FVG overlaps with OB)
  Advantage: tighter SL than entering at FVG_top, better R:R

For a Bearish FVG entry:
  Entry = CE (midpoint of the gap)
  SL = above FVG_top

When CE overlaps with an OB zone → this is the highest-conviction
entry point available. Two independent institutional references
pointing to the exact same price.

────────────────────────────────────────
CE AS A DECISION LEVEL
────────────────────────────────────────
When monitoring FVG fill behavior:
  - Price enters the FVG and reaches CE → watch for reaction
  - Rejection at CE (wick to CE, body reverses) → FVG is valid, expect
    continuation in the FVG's direction
  - Clean break through CE with body → FVG may fail, prepare for full fill
  - Full fill (price passes through entire FVG) → FVG is either dead
    or becomes a potential OB zone itself (the FVG acted as a liquidity
    magnet and is now filled)

================================================================================
6.4  FVG FILL TYPES — DETECTION FROM OHLC
================================================================================

When price returns to an FVG, the manner in which it interacts with
the gap tells you about the strength of the institutional interest.
The system must classify each FVG interaction.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 1: TOUCH AND REVERSE (strongest reaction)
────────────────────────────────────────
For Bullish FVG:
  Price drops down and reaches FVG_top (the upper edge of the gap)
  but does NOT enter significantly into the gap.
  Reverses with displacement.

Detection:
  candle.low ≤ FVG_top + (FVG_size × 0.1)  (within 10% of edge)
  AND candle.close > FVG_top
  AND next candle is bullish with body_pct > 50%

Meaning:
  The institutional demand at this FVG is so strong that price
  cannot even penetrate into the gap. Buyers are stepping in
  at the very edge. This is the strongest type of FVG reaction.

Confidence: very high that the FVG is valid and the directional
move will continue.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 2: FILL TO CE (most common)
────────────────────────────────────────
For Bullish FVG:
  Price drops into the FVG and reaches approximately the CE level
  (within 10% of CE), then reverses.

Detection:
  candle.low ≤ CE + (FVG_size × 0.1) AND candle.low ≥ FVG_bottom
  AND subsequent candle(s) reverse bullish

Meaning:
  The market has "fairly valued" the gap by trading through the
  midpoint. Institutional orders at and around CE have been filled.
  The remaining unfilled orders below CE provide the foundation
  for the reversal.

This is the most common FVG behavior. The CE entry strategy is
designed around this type.

Confidence: high — the FVG is performing as expected.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 3: FULL FILL AND HOLD (valid but weakened)
────────────────────────────────────────
For Bullish FVG:
  Price drops through the ENTIRE FVG (below FVG_bottom) but the
  BODY does not close below FVG_bottom. A wick goes below but
  the candle closes back inside or above the FVG.

Detection:
  candle.low < FVG_bottom
  AND candle.close ≥ FVG_bottom
  AND the candle shows rejection (lower_wick > body)

Meaning:
  The FVG was completely traversed, but the level held on a closing
  basis. The wick below was a sweep of stops placed below the FVG.
  The close back inside suggests institutional buying absorbed the
  selling. Similar to how an OB wick sweep strengthens the OB.

Confidence: moderate — the FVG held but was significantly tested.
The FVG is now "tested" (increment its test count).

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 4: FULL FILL AND BREAK (FVG invalidated)
────────────────────────────────────────
For Bullish FVG:
  Price drops through the entire FVG AND a candle CLOSES below
  FVG_bottom.

Detection:
  candle.close < FVG_bottom

Meaning:
  The institutional demand that created this FVG has been overwhelmed.
  The FVG is DEAD. Do not use it for future analysis.
  Mark status = INVALIDATED.

Note: the area may later become relevant as a Breaker or as part
of a new structural zone, but it is no longer an FVG.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 5: PARTIAL FILL (weak interaction)
────────────────────────────────────────
For Bullish FVG:
  Price drops toward the FVG but only enters the top 20-30% of
  the gap before reversing. Does not reach CE.

Detection:
  candle.low ≤ FVG_top
  AND candle.low > CE
  AND reversal occurs

Meaning:
  Price acknowledged the FVG but did not commit to filling it
  properly. This is a weak interaction:
  - If the reversal has displacement → acceptable (FVG demand
    was so strong it didn't need to fill further)
  - If the reversal is weak (small candle, low volume) → the FVG
    may not hold on the next test

Confidence: moderate to low — depends on the reversal quality.

────────────────────────────────────────
FOR BEARISH FVGs:
────────────────────────────────────────
All five types apply in mirror:
  Touch and Reverse: price rises to FVG_bottom edge, reverses down
  Fill to CE: price rises to CE, reverses
  Full Fill Hold: wick above FVG_top, close below
  Full Fill Break: close above FVG_top → invalidated
  Partial Fill: enters bottom 20-30% only

================================================================================
6.5  FVG QUALITY ASSESSMENT
================================================================================

Not all FVGs are equal. A small FVG on a 5m chart during Asian session
is fundamentally different from a large FVG on a Daily chart during
NY AM Kill Zone. The quality assessment determines which FVGs are
worth trading and which should be noted but not acted upon.

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITERION 1: SIZE RELATIVE TO PRICE (1-5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  FVG_size_pct = FVG_size / current_price × 100

  > 0.8% → Score: 5/5 (large, significant imbalance)
  0.5-0.8% → Score: 4/5
  0.3-0.5% → Score: 3/5 (moderate — standard tradeable FVG)
  0.15-0.3% → Score: 2/5 (small — may be noise on HTF, acceptable on LTF)
  < 0.15% → Score: 1/5 (tiny — likely noise, ignore unless on 5m for sniper entry)

  Why size matters: a larger FVG represents a more significant
  imbalance. Institutions moved price further without allowing
  efficient trading. More unfilled business remains in a large gap
  than in a tiny one.

  Context adjustment:
  - On 5m, a 0.15% FVG is acceptable (small timeframe = smaller gaps)
  - On Daily, a 0.15% FVG is noise (Daily moves are much larger)
  - Scale your expectations to the timeframe

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITERION 2: CREATING CANDLE CHARACTERISTICS (1-5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  The candle[i+1] that caused the imbalance:

  Displacement candle (Grade A-B from OHLC_PROCESSING):
    range > 2× ATR, body_pct > 70%, vol_ratio > 1.5
    → Score: 5/5 (this FVG was created by genuine institutional force)

  Strong candle but not full displacement:
    range > 1.5× ATR, body_pct > 60%, vol_ratio > 1.2
    → Score: 4/5

  Normal candle:
    range ~1× ATR, body_pct > 50%
    → Score: 3/5

  Weak candle:
    range < 1× ATR OR body_pct < 50%
    → Score: 2/5 (the "gap" was created by a normal-sized candle —
    less evidence of institutional force)

  Very weak:
    Small candle, low volume
    → Score: 1/5 (this is likely a natural gap in thin market conditions,
    not an institutional imbalance)

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITERION 3: VOLUME AND TRADE COUNT ON CREATING CANDLE (1-5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  vol_ratio of candle[i+1]:
  > 2.0 → Score: 5/5 (high volume confirms institutional participation)
  1.5-2.0 → Score: 4/5
  1.0-1.5 → Score: 3/5 (average — acceptable)
  0.7-1.0 → Score: 2/5 (below average — thin market gap)
  < 0.7 → Score: 1/5 (very low volume — gap is likely noise)

  If num_trades available:
  trades_ratio > 2.0 → add +1 to score (cap at 5)
  (Many individual trades = many participants = genuine institutional event)

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITERION 4: TIMEFRAME (1-5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Weekly FVG → Score: 5/5 (extremely rare, extremely powerful
    — price will almost certainly return to fill this)
  Daily FVG → Score: 5/5 (rare, powerful — major magnet for price)
  4H FVG → Score: 4/5 (moderately common, strong)
  1H FVG → Score: 3/5 (common, moderate — needs context)
  15m FVG → Score: 2/5 (very common — for entry timing only)
  5m FVG → Score: 1/5 (extremely common — sniper entry only)

  A Daily FVG unfilled is one of the most reliable targets in ICT.
  Price will eventually return to it (could take days or weeks, but
  the probability approaches certainty).

  A 5m FVG that doesn't fill within 20-30 candles is often forgotten
  by the market entirely.

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITERION 5: FRESHNESS / AGE (1-5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  tests = 0 (never touched since creation) → Score: 5/5 (FRESH)
  tests = 1 (touched once, held) → Score: 3/5
  tests = 2 (touched twice, still holds) → Score: 2/5
  tests ≥ 3 → Score: 0/5 (EXHAUSTED — dead FVG)

  How to count FVG tests:
  A test occurs when price returns to the FVG zone and any part of
  a candle's BODY enters the zone:
    Bullish FVG: candle.close < FVG_top AND candle.low ≤ FVG_top
    Bearish FVG: candle.close > FVG_bottom AND candle.high ≥ FVG_bottom

  Wick-only entries into the zone are NOT full tests (same logic as OBs).
  A wick into the FVG that reverses = the gap held = confidence maintained.

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITERION 6: CONTEXT AND OVERLAP (1-5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  FVG overlaps with an OB zone → Score: 5/5
    (The strongest possible setup. Two institutional footprints
    at the same level.)

  FVG is within the OTE zone (62-79% retracement) → Score: 4/5
    (FVG at the optimal retracement level = institutional sweet spot)

  FVG is in the correct Premium/Discount zone:
    Bullish FVG in Discount (below EQ) → Score: 4/5
    Bullish FVG in Premium → Score: 1/5 (wrong zone for demand)
    Bearish FVG in Premium → Score: 4/5
    Bearish FVG in Discount → Score: 1/5

  FVG at a session level (PDH/PDL, Asian H/L) → Score: 3/5

  FVG in open space with no other confluence → Score: 1/5

────────────────────────────────────────
FVG QUALITY TIERS
────────────────────────────────────────
Sum the 6 criteria (each 0-5): Total range 0-30

  S-TIER (25-30): Exceptional FVG
    Large gap, displacement creating candle, high volume, HTF,
    fresh, at confluence zone (OB overlap or OTE)
    → Primary entry zone or target level

  A-TIER (20-24): Strong FVG
    Most criteria met at high level
    → Reliable for entry or targeting

  B-TIER (15-19): Good FVG
    Solid but some weaknesses
    → Tradeable with additional confluence

  C-TIER (10-14): Weak FVG
    Multiple weaknesses
    → Note but do not rely on alone

  D-TIER (5-9): Poor FVG
    Likely noise
    → Do not trade

  F-TIER (0-4): Invalid
    Not a meaningful FVG
    → Discard

================================================================================
6.6  FVG INVALIDATION
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
INVALIDATION RULE 1: BODY CLOSE THROUGH
────────────────────────────────────────
For Bullish FVG:
  If any candle's CLOSE is below FVG_bottom → FVG is DEAD.
  The institutional demand was not sufficient to hold the gap.
  The imbalance has been resolved by the opposing side.

For Bearish FVG:
  If any candle's CLOSE is above FVG_top → FVG is DEAD.

CRITICAL: same as OBs, WICK through ≠ invalidation.
  A wick below FVG_bottom with close back inside = FVG HELD.
  This is actually a sweep of stops below the FVG.
  The FVG is now tested (increment test count) but stronger.

────────────────────────────────────────
INVALIDATION RULE 2: EXCESSIVE TESTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  tests ≥ 3 → FVG is EXHAUSTED.
  The imbalance has been filled through repeated visits.
  No remaining unfilled institutional orders.
  Remove from active FVG list.

────────────────────────────────────────
INVALIDATION RULE 3: TIME RELEVANCE
────────────────────────────────────────
  FVGs on lower timeframes (15m, 5m) that remain unfilled for
  extended periods lose relevance:
    5m FVG unfilled after 100+ candles (~8 hours): relevance fading
    15m FVG unfilled after 80+ candles (~20 hours): relevance fading
    1H FVG unfilled after 100+ candles (~4 days): still relevant
    4H FVG unfilled after 60+ candles (~10 days): still relevant
    Daily FVG unfilled after 40+ candles (~2 months): still relevant
    Weekly FVG unfilled after 20+ candles (~5 months): still relevant

  The time windows above are guidelines, not hard rules. A 15m FVG
  that aligns with a 4H OB zone remains relevant regardless of age
  because the HTF significance preserves it.

  A standalone 5m FVG with no HTF backing that hasn't been filled
  in 150+ candles → effectively dead.

================================================================================
6.7  INVERSE FVG (IFVG)
================================================================================

An Inverse FVG is a concept where a filled FVG flips its role.
When a Bullish FVG is completely filled AND BROKEN (body close below
FVG_bottom), the zone that was previously demand can become supply
on a return visit from below.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE LOGIC
────────────────────────────────────────
Bullish FVG at 98200-98900:
  - Initially: demand zone (price should bounce here)
  - Price returns and fills through the entire gap
  - Body closes below 98200 → FVG invalidated
  - NOW: price rallied back up to 98200-98900
  - The zone may act as SUPPLY (resistance) because:
    a) Traders who bought in this zone are now trapped (they bought
       and price went below → they'll sell to exit at breakeven)
    b) The zone proved it's NOT demand → it may now be supply

────────────────────────────────────────
DETECTION
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Bullish FVG existed at zone X
  2. FVG was invalidated (body close below FVG_bottom)
  3. Price moved away (below the zone)
  4. Price returns UP to the zone
  5. → The zone is now an Inverse FVG (bearish supply)

  Same logic inverted for Bearish FVG → Bullish IFVG.

────────────────────────────────────────
QUALITY OF IFVGs
────────────────────────────────────────
  IFVGs are generally B-TIER or C-TIER:
  - They represent a zone that already failed once
  - The trapped-trader logic is valid but not as strong as a fresh OB
  - Use IFVGs as additional confluence, not as primary entry zones
  - Best when: IFVG aligns with a Breaker Block (both represent
    the same "failed zone flips" concept → mutual confirmation)

================================================================================
6.8  FVG AS ENTRY ZONE vs FVG AS TARGET
================================================================================

FVGs serve two distinct purposes in ICT analysis. The system must
know which role each FVG plays in the current context.

────────────────────────────────────────
FVG AS ENTRY ZONE
────────────────────────────────────────
When an FVG is between the current price and a HTF POI:

  Example:
    Price is at 100,000
    Bullish FVG at 99,200-99,600 (below current price)
    Daily OB at 98,800-99,100 (below the FVG)

  In this scenario:
    The FVG is a potential entry zone for a long trade.
    Price may pull back to the FVG during a bullish pullback.
    If the FVG aligns with OTE, is fresh, and has good quality
    → enter long at FVG CE with SL below the Daily OB.

  FVG as entry zone works best when:
    - It's in the correct P/D zone (discount for bullish)
    - It overlaps with or is near an OB
    - It's fresh (never tested)
    - The creating candle was a displacement candle
    - It's on a timeframe appropriate for entry (15m, 5m for intraday)
    - HTF bias supports the direction

────────────────────────────────────────
FVG AS TARGET (DRAW ON LIQUIDITY)
────────────────────────────────────────
When an UNFILLED FVG exists between the current price and a
liquidity pool in the trade direction:

  Example:
    You are long from 99,500
    Bearish FVG at 100,800-101,200 (above — unfilled)
    BSL at 101,500 (above the FVG)

  In this scenario:
    The Bearish FVG is a TARGET. Price is being drawn toward it
    because the market wants to fill the imbalance.

    If the FVG is between your entry and the major BSL:
      TP1 = FVG_bottom (100,800) — price may react here
      TP2 = BSL (101,500) — the ultimate liquidity target

  FVG as target works best when:
    - It's an HTF FVG (Daily, 4H) — these are powerful magnets
    - It's unfilled (never tested)
    - It's in the direction of the current trend
    - There is liquidity (BSL/SSL) beyond the FVG
      (price goes to fill the FVG, then continues to the liquidity)

────────────────────────────────────────
THE SYSTEM MUST DISTINGUISH THESE ROLES
────────────────────────────────────────
For each active FVG, determine:
  Is it ABOVE current price or BELOW?
  Is it in the TREND direction or against it?

  Bullish trend + Bullish FVG below price = ENTRY ZONE (pullback target)
  Bullish trend + Bearish FVG above price = TARGET (unfilled supply to fill)
  Bearish trend + Bearish FVG above price = ENTRY ZONE (rally target)
  Bearish trend + Bullish FVG below price = TARGET (unfilled demand to fill)

  FVGs WITH the trend direction AND below/above current price in the
  trade direction = ENTRY OPPORTUNITIES when price retraces.

  FVGs AGAINST the trend direction AND in the path of the trend
  = TARGETS that price is being drawn to.

================================================================================
6.9  FVG + OB OVERLAP — THE STRONGEST SETUP
================================================================================

When a Fair Value Gap and an Order Block overlap at the same price
zone, this creates the single highest-probability setup in all of
ICT methodology.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY THIS COMBINATION IS SO POWERFUL
────────────────────────────────────────
Two independent institutional footprints confirm the same zone:

  Order Block: "Institutions placed orders here. When price returns,
  those unfilled orders will activate."

  Fair Value Gap: "Price moved through here too fast, creating an
  imbalance. The market wants to return here to complete fair
  value delivery."

  Combined: "Institutions placed orders here AND price was delivered
  inefficiently through this zone. TWO reasons for price to return
  AND react."

These are not correlated signals. An OB is based on the candle
BEFORE the impulse. An FVG is based on the relationship between
the candles AROUND the impulse. They are detected through
completely different mathematical tests. When both point to the
same zone, this is genuine confluence — not double-counting.

────────────────────────────────────────
DETECTION
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Detect all OBs (per ORDER_BLOCKS section)
  2. Detect all FVGs (per this section)
  3. For each OB, check if any FVG overlaps:
     Overlap exists if:
       MAX(ob_bottom, fvg_bottom) < MIN(ob_top, fvg_top)

     If yes:
       overlap_bottom = MAX(ob_bottom, fvg_bottom)
       overlap_top = MIN(ob_top, fvg_top)
       overlap_ce = (overlap_top + overlap_bottom) / 2

  4. The OVERLAP zone is the highest-priority entry area.
     Enter at overlap_ce for the tightest possible entry at the
     highest-probability zone.

────────────────────────────────────────
QUALITY UPGRADE
────────────────────────────────────────
When OB + FVG overlap:
  - Upgrade OB quality by 1 tier (B → A, A → S)
  - Upgrade FVG quality by 1 tier
  - Confidence boost: +8% to +12% on the overall signal

  An overlap between a B-TIER OB and a B-TIER FVG:
    Individual: moderate setups
    Combined: A-TIER equivalent — strong trade

  An overlap between an A-TIER OB and an A-TIER FVG:
    Combined: S-TIER equivalent — the best possible trade

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY STRATEGY FOR OVERLAP ZONES
────────────────────────────────────────
  Entry: overlap_ce (midpoint of the overlap)
  SL: below/above the WIDER zone (the OB bottom or FVG bottom,
      whichever is further + buffer)
  TP1: nearest valid unswept opposing PD Array; report actual R:R
  TP2: Draw on Liquidity

  The entry is at the overlap CE because this is where both the
  OB's unfilled orders AND the FVG's fair value convergence meet.
  Maximum institutional significance at this exact point.

================================================================================
6.10  FVG TRACKING AND STATE MANAGEMENT
================================================================================

The system must maintain a running list of active FVGs for each
timeframe and update their status on every new candle.

────────────────────────────────────────
FVG DATA STRUCTURE
────────────────────────────────────────
For each detected FVG:
  {
    type: "BULLISH_FVG" | "BEARISH_FVG"
    timeframe: "4H" (or whichever TF)
    fvg_top: price
    fvg_bottom: price
    fvg_ce: price
    fvg_size: price distance
    fvg_size_pct: size as % of price
    creating_candle_index: index of candle[i+1]
    creating_candle_range: range of that candle
    creating_candle_body_pct: body_pct of that candle
    creating_candle_vol_ratio: vol_ratio
    quality_tier: S | A | B | C | D | F
    quality_score: 0-30
    tests: 0, 1, 2
    status: "ACTIVE" | "PARTIALLY_FILLED" | "FULLY_FILLED" | "INVALIDATED"
    fill_type: null | "TOUCH_REVERSE" | "FILL_TO_CE" | "FULL_FILL_HOLD" |
               "FULL_FILL_BREAK" | "PARTIAL_FILL"
    ob_overlap: true/false
