    overlap_zone: {top, bottom, ce} or null
    role: "ENTRY_ZONE" | "TARGET" | "UNDETERMINED"
    created_at: timestamp
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
UPDATE RULES (on each new candle)
────────────────────────────────────────
For each active FVG:

  BULLISH FVG:
    If candle.close < fvg_bottom:
      → status = INVALIDATED (body close below = dead)
      → fill_type = "FULL_FILL_BREAK"
      → Check for Inverse FVG potential

    If candle.low < fvg_bottom AND candle.close ≥ fvg_bottom:
      → fill_type = "FULL_FILL_HOLD"
      → tests += 1
      → If tests ≥ 3: status = "FULLY_FILLED" (exhausted)

    If candle.low ≤ fvg_ce AND candle.low ≥ fvg_bottom:
      → fill_type = "FILL_TO_CE"
      → tests += 1
      → Status remains ACTIVE (CE fill is normal behavior)

    If candle.low ≤ fvg_top AND candle.low > fvg_ce:
      → fill_type = "PARTIAL_FILL"
      → Note but do not increment test count
        (partial fill is not a full interaction)

    If candle.low ≤ fvg_top AND next candle reverses with displacement:
      → fill_type = "TOUCH_REVERSE"
      → Strongest confirmation

  BEARISH FVG:
    Mirror all conditions:
    close above fvg_top = invalidated
    high above fvg_top but close below = full fill hold
    etc.

────────────────────────────────────────
PRIORITIZATION
────────────────────────────────────────
When multiple FVGs exist:
  Priority: higher TF > lower TF
  Within same TF: fresher > tested
  Within same freshness: larger > smaller
  OB overlap > no overlap

The system should track the TOP 3 most significant active FVGs
per direction across all timeframes:
  Top 3 unfilled Bullish FVGs (demand zones / entry opportunities)
  Top 3 unfilled Bearish FVGs (supply zones / entry opportunities)
  Plus: any HTF FVGs that serve as targets (Draw on Liquidity)

================================================================================
6.11  COMMON FVG MISTAKES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: TREATING EVERY 3-CANDLE GAP AS A TRADEABLE FVG
────────────────────────────────────────
On a 5m chart, dozens of FVGs form every day. Most are noise — tiny
gaps from normal market fluctuation, not institutional imbalances.

Fix: Apply the quality assessment. Only trade A-TIER and above.
B-TIER needs extra confluence. C-TIER and below are for noting,
not trading.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: IGNORING THE CREATING CANDLE
────────────────────────────────────────
The FVG is detected mathematically from candles [i] and [i+2].
But the CREATING candle [i+1] tells you WHY the gap exists. A gap
created by a displacement candle (high volume, large body) is
fundamentally different from a gap created by a thin market spike.

Fix: Always check the creating candle. If it doesn't show
displacement characteristics (or at least above-average range
and volume), demote the FVG quality.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: ENTERING AT FVG EDGE INSTEAD OF CE
────────────────────────────────────────
Entering at FVG_top (for bullish) gives the worst possible entry
within the gap. If price fills to CE (the most common behavior),
your entry is already underwater before the reaction occurs.

Fix: Default entry = CE unless there is a specific reason to use
the edge (e.g., FVG + OB overlap CE is at a different level).

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: NOT DISTINGUISHING FVG ROLE (ENTRY vs TARGET)
────────────────────────────────────────
A Bearish FVG above your long entry is a TARGET (price is going
there to fill it), not a reason to sell. Confusing the role leads
to fighting the trend.

Fix: Always classify: is this FVG an entry zone or a target?
Use the rules in section 6.8 based on trend direction and
relative position to current price.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: USING LTF FVGs WITHOUT HTF CONTEXT
────────────────────────────────────────
A 5m FVG in the middle of nowhere is noise. It formed from a
minor intraday fluctuation with no institutional significance.

Fix: LTF FVGs (15m, 5m) are valid for entry ONLY when they
exist within or near a HTF zone of interest (4H/Daily OB, FVG,
or liquidity level). A 5m FVG inside a 4H OB overlap zone is
extremely precise and valuable. A standalone 5m FVG is nothing.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 6: CONFUSING WICK THROUGH WITH INVALIDATION
────────────────────────────────────────
Same as OBs: a wick through the FVG with close back inside is
NOT invalidation. It is a sweep. The FVG held. It may actually
be stronger now because stops below it were triggered and
absorbed.

Fix: Only BODY CLOSE through = invalidation. Train the system
to recognize wick sweeps as bullish for FVGs (not bearish).

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 7: EXPECTING EVERY FVG TO FILL
────────────────────────────────────────
In strong trends, some FVGs never fill. Price creates an FVG,
pulls back slightly, then continues without returning to fill it.
These FVGs remain "open" indefinitely. Waiting for a fill that
never comes means missing the entire trend.

Fix: FVGs are one tool, not the only tool. If price is trending
strongly and your HTF bias is confirmed, enter on LTF structure
shift even if the ideal FVG fill hasn't occurred. Use the FVG
as a zone of interest, not an absolute requirement.

HTF FVGs (Daily, Weekly) will ALMOST ALWAYS fill eventually.
LTF FVGs (15m, 5m) may never fill in strong trends — and that's
perfectly acceptable. The trend is more important than the gap.

6.6  BALANCED PRICE RANGE (BPR)
================================================================================
Added from official ICT foundational source material (2024 Mentorship
notes): a Balanced Price Range forms when price creates an Inversion
FVG (a failed FVG that flips to the opposite role) and then a fresh
FVG forms in the OPPOSITE direction inside/around the same zone - i.e.
price travels up-down-up (or down-up-down) through the same small
range, "balancing" it from both sides.

DETECTION FROM OHLC:
  1. Identify a Bearish FVG or Bullish FVG that gets fully closed
     (price trades back through it and reverses direction) - this
     invalidated/inverted FVG is the IFVG.
  2. Immediately after, a NEW FVG forms in the ORIGINAL direction
     again, overlapping or adjacent to the same price zone as the
     IFVG.
  3. The candles between these two opposing FVG events (the ones that
     traveled up, then down, then up again through the same zone) mark
     the BPR range: BPR_low = min(low) of those candles, BPR_high =
     max(high) of those candles.

WHY IT MATTERS - PRECISE STOP LOSS PLACEMENT:
  The BPR is one of the most precise SL reference zones in ICT
  methodology: "You do NOT want to see prices go below/above that BPR
  level." Because the range has been traded through twice already
  (both directions), a genuine break BELOW a bullish BPR (or ABOVE a
  bearish BPR) means the zone has truly failed - not just noise.
  Use BPR_low (bullish case) or BPR_high (bearish case) as a tighter,
  more defensible SL than a wider swing-based stop, when a BPR is
  present near the entry.

  The down-close (or up-close) candles that form the BPR can also be
  read as a valid Order Block in their own right - the two concepts
  (BPR and OB) commonly overlap at the same zone; when they do, treat
  it as added confluence, not a separate signal.

================================================================================
END OF [FAIR_VALUE_GAPS]
================================================================================




================================================================================
[LIQUIDITY_MAPPING]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Liquidity is the fuel that moves markets. Institutions cannot execute
large orders without liquidity — they need someone on the other side of
their trade. The most concentrated, predictable sources of liquidity are
the clusters of stop loss orders placed by retail traders at obvious
levels: above swing highs, below swing lows, above equal highs, below
equal lows, above/below trendlines, and at session extremes.

ICT methodology is built on one fundamental principle:
  Price moves from one liquidity pool to the next.

Every institutional move has a purpose: to reach a pool of stop orders
that provides the liquidity institutions need to fill their positions.
The entire market cycle — accumulation, manipulation, distribution —
exists to engineer price toward these pools.

Understanding liquidity is not optional. It is the answer to the most
actionable question in trading: "Where is price going next?"

This section defines how to identify, classify, and map all forms of
liquidity from OHLC data, how to distinguish between genuine sweeps
(reversals) and real breakouts (continuation), and how to build a
comprehensive liquidity map that guides every trading decision.

================================================================================
7.1  THE TWO SIDES OF LIQUIDITY
================================================================================

All liquidity falls into two categories based on WHERE the stop
orders are clustered:

────────────────────────────────────────
BUY-SIDE LIQUIDITY (BSL)
────────────────────────────────────────
LOCATION: Above swing highs, above equal highs, above resistance levels

WHO PUT IT THERE:
  - Short sellers placed their stop losses above swing highs
    ("If price goes above this high, my short thesis is wrong")
  - Breakout traders placed buy stop entries above highs
    ("If price breaks above, I want to buy the breakout")

WHAT THESE ORDERS ARE:
  Stop loss orders from shorts = BUY orders (to close the short)
  Breakout entries from longs = BUY orders (to open a long)
  Combined: a cluster of BUY orders sitting above the high

WHAT HAPPENS WHEN BSL IS TAKEN:
  Price rises above the swing high, triggering these buy orders.
  The buy orders execute as MARKET BUYS, which provides:
  - For institutions wanting to SELL: the buy orders are the
    counterparty. Institutions sell INTO the buy orders.
  - Result: price spikes above the high (triggering stops), then
    reverses down as institutions distribute their sell orders.

  OR: if the move is genuine (not a sweep):
  - The buy orders add fuel to an existing uptrend
  - Price continues higher after breaking the level
  - This is a real breakout, not a sweep

THE KEY QUESTION: After BSL is taken, does price REVERSE or CONTINUE?
  Reverse = the BSL was swept (institutions used it to sell)
  Continue = the BSL was a genuine breakout (trend continuation)
  This distinction is covered in section 7.6.

────────────────────────────────────────
SELL-SIDE LIQUIDITY (SSL)
────────────────────────────────────────
LOCATION: Below swing lows, below equal lows, below support levels

WHO PUT IT THERE:
  - Long traders placed stop losses below swing lows
  - Breakdown traders placed sell stop entries below lows

WHAT THESE ORDERS ARE:
  Stop losses from longs = SELL orders
  Breakdown entries from shorts = SELL orders
  Combined: a cluster of SELL orders below the low

WHAT HAPPENS WHEN SSL IS TAKEN:
  Price drops below the swing low, triggering sell orders.
  For institutions wanting to BUY: the sell orders provide liquidity.
  Institutions buy INTO the triggered sell orders at favorable prices.
  Then price reverses up.

  OR: genuine breakdown → price continues lower.

================================================================================
7.2  LIQUIDITY SOURCES — COMPLETE CATALOG
================================================================================

The system must identify ALL sources of liquidity in the current
chart context. Each source has different significance and probability.

────────────────────────────────────────
SOURCE 1: SWING POINT LIQUIDITY (PRIMARY)
────────────────────────────────────────
Every swing high has BSL above it.
Every swing low has SSL below it.

The AMOUNT of liquidity depends on the swing's quality:
  STRONG swing (quality 21-25) = heavy liquidity
    Many traders see it, many stops placed near it
  MODERATE swing (quality 15-20) = moderate liquidity
  WEAK swing (quality 10-14) = light liquidity
  NOISE swing (quality <10) = negligible liquidity

Detection: directly from SWING_DETECTION output.
For each swing, the liquidity level = the swing's price.
  BSL level = swing_high.price
  SSL level = swing_low.price

How far stops are placed beyond the swing:
  Retail traders typically place stops:
  - Just above/below the swing price (tight stops): 0.1-0.2%
  - With a buffer (standard): 0.2-0.5%
  - Wide: 0.5-1.0%

  The LIQUIDITY ZONE (where most stops cluster):
  For BSL: swing_high.price to swing_high.price + (0.3% × price)
  For SSL: swing_low.price - (0.3% × price) to swing_low.price

  Price does not need to reach the exact swing price to trigger
  some stops. But it needs to exceed the swing price to trigger
  the majority.

────────────────────────────────────────
SOURCE 2: EQUAL HIGHS / EQUAL LOWS (STRONGEST)
────────────────────────────────────────
EQH/EQL are the DENSEST liquidity pools because they are the most
visible patterns on any chart. Every retail trader learns "double
top = sell, double bottom = buy" and places stops accordingly.

Detection: from SWING_DETECTION section 3.4
  EQH: |SH_A.price - SH_B.price| / SH_A.price < 0.003
  EQL: |SL_A.price - SL_B.price| / SL_A.price < 0.003

Liquidity scaling:
  Double (2 touches): significant liquidity
  Triple (3 touches): very heavy liquidity
  Quadruple+ (4+ touches): massive liquidity — sweep almost certain

Why triple+ is so reliable:
  When three swing highs form at the same level, EVERY type of
  trader sees it:
  - Swing traders: "triple top resistance, I'll short here"
    → SL above the level
  - Day traders: "clear resistance, I'll short" → SL above
  - Breakout traders: "if it breaks, I'll buy" → buy stops above
  - Algorithmic systems: "resistance at X" → orders around X

  The result: a massive cluster of stops all within 0.3% above
  the triple top. This is irresistible liquidity for institutions.
  The probability of a sweep approaches 90%+ for triple tops/bottoms.

────────────────────────────────────────
SOURCE 3: SESSION LIQUIDITY
────────────────────────────────────────
Session highs and lows create well-defined, time-based liquidity:

PREVIOUS DAY HIGH (PDH) / PREVIOUS DAY LOW (PDL):
  Detection: max(high) and min(low) of all candles from the
  previous trading day (00:00-23:59 NY time)

  Significance: HIGH
  Every trader on every platform can see PDH/PDL.
  Stops cluster above PDH and below PDL.
  London and NY sessions frequently target PDH/PDL.
  Turtle Soup model (Entry Model B sub-variant) specifically
  trades the sweep of PDH/PDL.

PREVIOUS WEEK HIGH (PWH) / PREVIOUS WEEK LOW (PWL):
  Detection: max(high) and min(low) of all candles from the
  previous trading week (Monday 00:00 to Friday 23:59 NY time)

  Significance: VERY HIGH
  PWH/PWL are major institutional targets.
  The current week's narrative often involves targeting the
  previous week's extreme.
  A sweep of PWH/PWL is a significant event that can define
  the direction for the rest of the week.

ASIAN SESSION HIGH / LOW:
  Detection: max(high) and min(low) of all candles from
  18:00-00:00 NY time (or the precise Asian range candles)

  Significance: MODERATE-HIGH for London and NY sessions
  London session frequently sweeps one side of the Asian range
  as the Judas Swing (manipulation phase of AMD).
  If Asian High is swept → expect the real move DOWN
  If Asian Low is swept → expect the real move UP
  (This is the classic ICT London Kill Zone setup)

LONDON SESSION HIGH / LOW:
  Detection: max(high) and min(low) from 02:00-08:30 NY time

  Significance: MODERATE for NY session
  NY may continue London's direction OR reverse it.
  If NY reverses London → NY sweep of London H/L = entry signal

CURRENT DAY OPENING PRICE:
  Detection: open of the first candle at 00:00 NY time

  Not traditional "liquidity" but a reference level:
  Price above opening = intraday bullish bias
  Price below opening = intraday bearish bias
  See ICT_TIME_SESSIONS section for True Day usage.

────────────────────────────────────────
SOURCE 4: TRENDLINE LIQUIDITY
────────────────────────────────────────
Trendlines are visible to every retail trader. When 3+ swing lows
form an ascending line (uptrend), traders place stops below the line.
This creates a LINE of liquidity (not a point).

Detection from OHLC:
  Step 1: Identify 3+ swing lows that are progressively higher
  Step 2: Calculate the slope:
    slope = (SL_latest.price - SL_earliest.price) /
            (SL_latest.index - SL_earliest.index)
  Step 3: Project the line forward:
    trendline_at_candle[x] = SL_earliest.price +
      slope × (x - SL_earliest.index)
  Step 4: The liquidity sits BELOW this projected line
    (for ascending trendline — stops are below)

  For descending trendlines: 3+ lower swing highs, liquidity above

Significance: MODERATE
  Trendline liquidity is real but less precise than point-based
  liquidity (swing highs/lows). The exact level changes with each
  new candle (the line extends). Useful as a confluent factor but
  not as a primary liquidity target.

Trendline break:
  When price breaks below an ascending trendline:
  - Stops below the line trigger (SSL taken)
  - This may be a sweep (price returns above the line) or a genuine
    trend break (price continues below)
  - Check for displacement after the break to determine which

────────────────────────────────────────
SOURCE 5: ROUND NUMBER LIQUIDITY
────────────────────────────────────────
Round numbers (psychological levels) attract stop orders simply
because humans think in round numbers.

For BTC:
  Major: $100,000, $90,000, $80,000 (every $10K)
  Minor: $95,000, $105,000 (every $5K)
  Micro: $101,000, $102,000 (every $1K)

For most assets:
  Stops cluster within 0.1-0.3% of major round numbers.

Significance: LOW to MODERATE
  Round numbers add confluence to other liquidity sources.
  A swing high AT a round number has more liquidity than a swing
  high between round numbers (stops from pattern + round number).

  Round number liquidity alone is not sufficient for a trade.
  It is a supporting factor only.

================================================================================
7.3  LIQUIDITY EVENT CLASSIFICATION
================================================================================

When price reaches a liquidity level, the critical question is:
is this a SWEEP (price will reverse) or a RUN (price will continue)?

The system must classify every liquidity event into one of four
categories. Getting this wrong is among the most expensive errors
in ICT trading.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 1: SWEEP (1-2 candles beyond the level, then reversal)
────────────────────────────────────────
What happens:
  Price pushes beyond the liquidity level for a very brief time
  (1-2 candles on the timeframe being analyzed). The breach is
  primarily through WICKS — the body may or may not close beyond.
  Then price reverses with displacement in the opposite direction.

Characteristics in OHLC data:
  - Candle(s) at the level: wick extends beyond, body closes back
    OR: body barely closes beyond (within 0.1% of the level)
  - Volume on the breach candle: normal to moderate
    (not extreme — this is a controlled sweep, not a panic)
  - The candle after the breach: displacement in the opposite
    direction (range > 1.5× ATR, body > 60%, body in reversal
    direction)
  - The reversal creates an OB and likely an FVG

Detection algorithm:
  After price exceeds a liquidity level (BSL or SSL):
    Count candles that remain beyond the level: N_beyond
    If N_beyond ≤ 2:
      Check reversal candle:
        If reversal candle shows displacement → SWEEP confirmed
        If reversal candle is weak → wait one more candle
        If no reversal within 3 candles → reclassify as Raid or Run

Significance for trading:
  SWEEP is the highest-probability reversal signal in ICT.
  The sequence: liquidity level → sweep → displacement → OB/FVG
  This IS Entry Model B (Liquidity Sweep + FVG Entry).
  When a sweep occurs at a HTF POI (OB, FVG), the probability
  of successful reversal is at its maximum.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 2: RAID (3-8 candles beyond, then reversal)
────────────────────────────────────────
What happens:
  Price pushes beyond the liquidity level and STAYS beyond for
  a short period (3-8 candles). Body closes beyond the level.
  It looks like a genuine breakout. Then price reverses with
  displacement back through the level.

Characteristics in OHLC data:
  - Multiple candles close beyond the level (3-8)
  - Volume may be moderate to high during the breach
  - No strong follow-through (price stalls after initial breach)
  - Eventually: a displacement candle reverses back through the level
  - The reversal is often violent (large candle, high volume)

Detection algorithm:
  After price exceeds a liquidity level:
    Count candles beyond: N_beyond
    If 3 ≤ N_beyond ≤ 8:
      Monitor for reversal displacement
      If displacement reversal occurs:
        → RAID confirmed
      If price continues beyond with new BOS:
        → Reclassify as Run

Significance for trading:
  Raids are trickier than sweeps because the extended time beyond
  the level makes traders believe the breakout is real. Many traders
  enter in the breakout direction during the raid period and get
  trapped when the reversal comes.

  Trading raids:
  - DO NOT enter the reversal immediately at the first sign of
    pullback (it might be just a pullback within a real breakout)
  - Wait for DISPLACEMENT reversal (displacement back through the
    level = confirmation that this was a raid, not a breakout)
  - After confirmed raid: treat it like a sweep for entry purposes
    but with slightly lower confidence (the extended stay beyond
    the level suggests less institutional conviction)
  - Consider 75% position size (vs 100% for a clean sweep)

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 3: PURGE (8-20+ candles beyond, then reversal)
────────────────────────────────────────
What happens:
  Price breaks the liquidity level and stays beyond for an extended
  period (8-20+ candles). Most traders accept the breakout as real.
  New structure forms beyond the level (new swings, possibly even
  a BOS in the breakout direction). Then a dramatic reversal occurs,
  often after sweeping ANOTHER liquidity level beyond the first one.

Characteristics in OHLC data:
  - Extended time beyond the level with multiple candle closes
  - New swing points form beyond the level
  - May show weakening momentum (smaller candles, lower volume)
  - Reversal is typically dramatic (climactic candle + displacement)
  - Often occurs at HTF level (Weekly/Daily OB or major liquidity)

Detection algorithm:
  After price exceeds a liquidity level:
    If N_beyond > 8 and no strong continuation trend develops:
      Monitor for signs of exhaustion:
        - Decreasing candle size
        - Decreasing volume
        - Bearish/bullish divergence forming
      If dramatic reversal displacement occurs:
        → PURGE confirmed (retroactively — hard to predict in advance)

Significance for trading:
  Purges are VERY difficult to trade in real-time because the
  extended period beyond the level is indistinguishable from a
  genuine breakout until the reversal occurs.

  Best approach:
  - Note the purge after it occurs
  - The zone where price reversed from the purge is a high-quality
    institutional zone (OB at the purge extreme)
  - Trade the AFTERMATH: the reversal move back through the
    original level and beyond
  - Do not try to predict purges in advance

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPE 4: RUN (genuine breakout — no reversal)
────────────────────────────────────────
What happens:
  Price breaks the liquidity level and CONTINUES in the breakout
  direction. This is a real structural break, not a sweep. The
  liquidity that was taken provides fuel for the trend.

Characteristics in OHLC data:
  - Strong body close beyond the level (body_pct > 60%)
  - Displacement characteristics on the breaking candle
    (range > 2× ATR, body > 70%, vol_ratio > 1.5)
  - Follow-through: next 3+ candles continue beyond the level
  - New swing points form in the breakout direction
  - Price does NOT return to the original level (or only briefly
    for a retest before continuing)
  - Volume remains elevated (not just a spike and fade)

Detection algorithm:
  After price exceeds a liquidity level:
    If breaking candle shows displacement:
      AND next 3+ candles continue beyond:
      AND volume remains above average:
      → Classify as RUN (breakout)

  Run confirmation strengthens with:
    - BOS in the breakout direction (new structure break)
    - FVGs forming in the breakout direction
    - OBs forming that support continuation

Significance for trading:
  A Run means the level is broken. Do NOT trade against it.
  Do NOT expect a reversal back through the level.

  Instead:
  - If the Run is with your HTF bias → trade WITH it
    (enter on pullback to the OB/FVG created by the Run)
  - If the Run is against your HTF bias → step aside and
    reassess. The bias may be changing.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE CRITICAL DISTINCTION ALGORITHM
────────────────────────────────────────
After any liquidity level is breached, the system applies this
decision tree:

  Step 1: BREAKING CANDLE ANALYSIS
    Was there displacement? (range > 2× ATR, body > 70%, vol > 1.5×)
      YES → leans toward RUN (but not confirmed yet)
      NO  → leans toward SWEEP (but wait for more data)

  Step 2: VOLUME PATTERN
    Volume > 2× average on the break AND sustained high volume after:
      → Strengthens RUN classification
    Volume spike on break but then drops immediately:
      → Strengthens SWEEP classification (climactic spike = reversal)
    Normal volume throughout:
      → Inconclusive, continue monitoring

  Step 3: FOLLOW-THROUGH (most important)
    Monitor next 3 candles:
      All 3 close beyond the level with no wick back through:
        → RUN highly likely
      1-2 close beyond then reversal candle appears:
        → SWEEP if reversal has displacement
        → RAID if reversal is slower
      More than 3 candles but weakening:
        → Possible RAID or PURGE, continue monitoring

  Step 4: STRUCTURAL CONTEXT
    Was the level a major structural swing (N=5+)?
      Breaking major structure with displacement = likely RUN
      Breaking major structure without displacement = likely RAID
    Was the level minor (N=2)?
      Breaking minor structure = could be inducement, watch closely

  Step 5: HTF CONTEXT (most authoritative)
    Is the break in the direction of HTF bias?
      YES → strongly favors RUN
      NO  → strongly favors SWEEP (counter-trend breaks are usually
            liquidity sweeps, not genuine breakouts)

  This is the single most important analytical judgment the system
  makes. Getting it right = entering the reversal after a sweep
  OR riding the continuation after a run. Getting it wrong =
  fading a genuine breakout or chasing a false one.

  WHEN IN DOUBT: wait. Do not classify until the evidence is clear.
  A premature classification is worse than no classification.

================================================================================
7.4  THE COMPREHENSIVE LIQUIDITY MAP
================================================================================

The system maintains a complete map of all identified liquidity
levels across all timeframes. This map is the primary tool for
answering "Where is price going next?"

────────────────────────────────────────
MAP STRUCTURE
────────────────────────────────────────
For each identified liquidity level:
  {
    type: "BSL" | "SSL"
    source: "SWING" | "EQH" | "EQL" | "PDH" | "PDL" |
            "PWH" | "PWL" | "ASIAN_H" | "ASIAN_L" |
            "LONDON_H" | "LONDON_L" | "TRENDLINE" | "ROUND"
    price: exact price level
    timeframe: which TF the swing/level belongs to
    significance: "MAJOR" | "MODERATE" | "MINOR"
    swing_quality: quality score from SWING_DETECTION (if applicable)
    eqh_eql_count: number of touches (if EQH/EQL)
    status: "UNSWEPT" | "SWEPT" | "RUN_THROUGH"
    distance_from_current: absolute and percentage
    in_trend_direction: true | false (based on Daily bias)
    sweep_probability: estimated probability of sweep vs run
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
BUILDING THE MAP
────────────────────────────────────────
Step 1: COLLECT all liquidity sources
  From each timeframe (W, D, 4H, 1H, 15m):
    All swing highs → BSL entries
    All swing lows → SSL entries
    All EQH clusters → BSL entries (upgraded significance)
    All EQL clusters → SSL entries (upgraded significance)
  From session data:
    PDH, PDL, PWH, PWL → BSL/SSL entries
    Asian H/L → BSL/SSL entries
    London H/L → BSL/SSL entries

Step 2: CLASSIFY significance
  MAJOR:
    Weekly/Daily swing + quality 20+
    EQH/EQL with 3+ touches
    PWH/PWL
  MODERATE:
    4H swing + quality 15+
    EQH/EQL with 2 touches
    PDH/PDL
    Asian H/L
  MINOR:
    1H swing (any quality)
    15m swing (quality 15+)
    Round numbers

Step 3: CALCULATE distance and direction
  For each level:
    distance = |level.price - current_price|
    distance_pct = distance / current_price × 100
    if level is BSL (above):
      in_trend_direction = true if Daily bias is bullish
    if level is SSL (below):
      in_trend_direction = true if Daily bias is bearish

Step 4: ESTIMATE sweep probability
  Base probability by type:
    EQH/EQL triple+ = 85%
    EQH/EQL double = 70%
    Major swing = 60%
    Moderate swing = 50%
    Session H/L = 55%

  Adjustments:
    Against HTF trend: +15% sweep probability (counter-trend breaks
      are more likely to be sweeps than genuine breakouts)
    With HTF trend: -15% sweep probability (with-trend breaks are
      more likely to be genuine breakouts)
    At HTF POI (OB/FVG): +10% (institutional zone = reaction likely)
    In Kill Zone: +5% (institutional activity = more likely sweep)

Step 5: SORT by relevance
  Primary sort: distance from current price (nearest first)
  Secondary sort: significance (MAJOR first)
  Tertiary sort: sweep probability (highest first)

────────────────────────────────────────
USING THE MAP
────────────────────────────────────────
The liquidity map answers three critical questions:

QUESTION 1: "Where is price going next?" (DRAW ON LIQUIDITY)
  Look at the nearest UNSWEPT liquidity in the trend direction:
    If bias is bullish → nearest UNSWEPT BSL above current price
    If bias is bearish → nearest UNSWEPT SSL below current price
  This is the DRAW ON LIQUIDITY (DoL) — the most probable target.
  DoL typically becomes TP2 for your trades.

QUESTION 2: "Where should I look for entries?"
  Look at the nearest UNSWEPT liquidity AGAINST the trend direction:
    If bias is bullish → nearest UNSWEPT SSL below
      (price may sweep this SSL, then reverse up toward BSL target)
    If bias is bearish → nearest UNSWEPT BSL above
      (price may sweep this BSL, then reverse down toward SSL target)
  An SSL sweep in a bullish context = entry opportunity for longs.

QUESTION 3: "Is there danger between my entry and my target?"
  Check for UNSWEPT liquidity between your entry and TP1/TP2:
    If UNSWEPT SSL exists below your long entry but above your SL:
      → Price may sweep this SSL and stop you out before reaching TP
      → Either: adjust SL below this SSL, or wait until it's swept
    If UNSWEPT BSL exists above your short entry but below your SL:
      → Same risk in reverse

  RULE: Do not enter a trade if there is significant UNSWEPT
  liquidity between your entry and your stop loss. This liquidity
  may be swept, and the sweep could trigger your stop before the
  trade has a chance to work.

  Example:
    You want to go long at $99,000, SL at $98,500
    There is an UNSWEPT SSL at $98,700 (EQL, double bottom)
    This SSL is between your entry and your SL
    → RISK: price may sweep $98,700, potentially to $98,600,
      which would trigger your SL at $98,500
    → SOLUTION: either wait for $98,700 SSL to be swept first
      (then enter long on the reversal), OR place SL below the
      $98,700 level ($98,400 area) with a wider stop

================================================================================
7.5  LIQUIDITY VOID
================================================================================

A Liquidity Void is a zone where price moved extremely quickly in
one direction with minimal two-sided trading, creating a "vacuum"
in the price history.

────────────────────────────────────────
DETECTION FROM OHLC
────────────────────────────────────────
A Liquidity Void exists when:
  3 or more consecutive candles are in the same direction (all bullish
  or all bearish) with:
  - Each candle has body_pct > 60% (committed moves, not wicks)
  - The cumulative range of the sequence is > 3× ATR
  - Volume is moderate to high throughout

The void ZONE:
  For a bullish void (price shot up):
    Void_bottom = low of the first candle in the sequence
    Void_top = close of the last candle in the sequence
    The entire range between these = the void

  For a bearish void (price shot down):
    Void_top = high of the first candle
    Void_bottom = close of the last candle

────────────────────────────────────────
WHAT A VOID MEANS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Within the void, there was no efficient price discovery.
  Price moved through so fast that orders were not properly matched.
  The market tends to return to voids to "fill" them — similar to
  FVGs but on a larger scale.

  A void is NOT an entry zone (it's too large and imprecise).
  A void IS a target zone (price is drawn back to fill it).

  Within a void, look for:
  - FVGs (the specific imbalances within the broader void)
  - OBs (the specific institutional zones within the void)
  These specific zones within the void are the actionable levels.

────────────────────────────────────────
USING VOIDS
────────────────────────────────────────
  As targets:
    If price shot up creating a void, and then reversed,
    the void below is a target for the downside move.
    TP2 might be within or at the bottom of the void.

  As context:
    A void tells you that price moved here without resistance.
    If price returns, it may move through the void quickly again
    (in either direction) because there are no significant levels
    within it — no stops, no orders, no structure.

  As warning:
    If your SL is within a void, it may be hit quickly because
    price can move through voids with little resistance.
    Place SL at the EDGE of a void (where structure exists),
    not within it.

================================================================================
7.6  DRAW ON LIQUIDITY (DoL) — THE DIRECTIONAL COMPASS
================================================================================

Draw on Liquidity is the concept that unifies all of ICT methodology.
Every price move is a delivery from one liquidity pool to the next.
Identifying the DoL tells you WHERE price is headed.

────────────────────────────────────────
DETERMINING THE DoL
────────────────────────────────────────
Step 1: Identify the Daily Bias (from MARKET_STRUCTURE)
  Bullish Daily Bias → price is being drawn toward BSL (upside liquidity)
  Bearish Daily Bias → price is being drawn toward SSL (downside liquidity)

Step 2: From the liquidity map, find the nearest UNSWEPT target
  in the bias direction:

  Bullish: nearest UNSWEPT BSL above current price
    Priority: MAJOR > MODERATE > MINOR
    If multiple at similar distance: highest significance wins

  Bearish: nearest UNSWEPT SSL below current price

Step 3: The DoL = this target level
  DoL is where price is "trying to get to"
  It is the most probable TP2 for your trades
  Every pullback, every manipulation, every intraday move is
  in service of eventually reaching the DoL

────────────────────────────────────────
DoL CHANGES
────────────────────────────────────────
DoL is not static. It updates when:
  1. The current DoL is reached (liquidity swept):
     → Next UNSWEPT liquidity in the same direction becomes new DoL
     → OR: if the sweep leads to reversal → DoL flips to opposite side

  2. Daily Bias changes (CHoCH on Daily):
     → DoL changes from BSL to SSL (or vice versa)

  3. A new, more significant liquidity level forms:
     → If a triple EQH forms closer than the current DoL → new DoL

────────────────────────────────────────
DoL AND TRADE PLANNING
────────────────────────────────────────
The DoL determines:
  - Trade DIRECTION (with the DoL, not against it)
  - TP2 (the DoL level itself or PD Arrays along the way)
  - Whether the current pullback is an entry opportunity
    (pullback against the DoL direction = chance to enter
    with the DoL for the ride toward the target)

Example:
  Daily Bias: Bullish
  Current Price: $99,000
  Nearest UNSWEPT BSL: $101,500 (4H EQH, triple top)
  Nearest UNSWEPT SSL: $97,800 (4H swing low, moderate)

  DoL = $101,500 (BSL, because bias is bullish)

  If price pulls back to $98,200 (Daily OB zone):
    → This pullback is an ENTRY OPPORTUNITY
    → Enter long at $98,200 zone (with LTF confirmation)
    → TP1: nearest valid unswept structural level; report actual R:R
    → TP2: the DoL at $101,500

  If instead price drops to $97,800 and sweeps the SSL:
    → SSL is now SWEPT → check for reversal
    → If displacement reversal occurs → enter long
    → New TP2 = $101,500 (the DoL is still the BSL above)
    → This sweep + reversal setup is even better because
      SSL liquidity has been taken (fuel for the up move)

================================================================================
7.7  UNSWEPT LIQUIDITY SAFETY CHECK
================================================================================

Before entering any trade, the system must perform this safety check:

────────────────────────────────────────
FOR LONG ENTRIES
────────────────────────────────────────
Check: is there any significant UNSWEPT SSL between my entry
and my stop loss?

  If YES:
    Risk: price may sweep this SSL, which would likely trigger my SL
    Options:
      a) Wait for the SSL to be swept first, then enter on reversal
      b) Place SL below the unswept SSL (wider stop, smaller position)
      c) Skip the trade entirely

    DO NOT: enter with SL above the unswept SSL and hope it won't
    be swept. If there is liquidity, institutions may target it.

  If NO:
    Proceed with the trade. No hidden liquidity traps between
    entry and SL.

────────────────────────────────────────
FOR SHORT ENTRIES
────────────────────────────────────────
Check: is there any significant UNSWEPT BSL between my entry
and my stop loss?

  Same logic in reverse.

────────────────────────────────────────
BETWEEN ENTRY AND TP1
────────────────────────────────────────
Also check: is there any significant opposing PD Array or
liquidity between my entry and TP1?

  If there is a Bearish OB between my long entry and my TP1:
    Risk: price may reverse at that OB before reaching TP1
    → TP1 should be BELOW that OB (at the OB or before it)
    → Or: confidence is reduced because there's a hurdle

  If the path to TP1 is "clean" (no significant opposing zones):
    → Higher confidence that TP1 will be reached

================================================================================
7.8  LIQUIDITY AND THE MARKET CYCLE
================================================================================

Liquidity maps directly onto the ICT market cycle
(Accumulation → Manipulation → Distribution):

────────────────────────────────────────
ACCUMULATION (Phase 1)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Liquidity builds on BOTH sides of a range:
  - BSL forms above range highs (shorts' stops + breakout buys)
  - SSL forms below range lows (longs' stops + breakdown sells)
  - The longer the range, the more liquidity accumulates
  - EQH/EQL frequently form during accumulation

────────────────────────────────────────
MANIPULATION (Phase 2)
────────────────────────────────────────
  ONE SIDE of the liquidity is swept:
  - In bullish scenario: SSL is swept (price drops below range low)
    → Retail longs get stopped out (their stops = sell orders
      = liquidity for institutions to buy)
    → Breakout shorts enter (more sell orders = more liquidity)
    → Institutions absorb ALL of this selling to build their
      long position at the manipulated low price
  - Then: displacement REVERSAL back into the range

────────────────────────────────────────
DISTRIBUTION (Phase 3)
────────────────────────────────────────
  The OTHER SIDE's liquidity is the TARGET:
  - In bullish scenario: BSL above the range is the target
  - Price rallies from the manipulation low toward the BSL
  - BSL is eventually taken (may be the next day/session)
  - At BSL: institutions distribute (sell) their accumulated
    position INTO the buy orders triggered at the BSL

  The full cycle:
    SSL swept (manipulation) → rally → BSL reached (distribution)
    Price moved FROM one liquidity pool TO the other.
    This IS ICT methodology in its purest form.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE SYSTEM
────────────────────────────────────────
When building the liquidity map, always ask:
  1. Are we in accumulation? (range forming, liquidity building on both sides)
  2. Has manipulation occurred? (one side swept recently with displacement)
  3. If yes → which side was swept? (SSL or BSL?)
  4. The OPPOSITE side is the DoL (the target for distribution)

If SSL was just swept with displacement → DoL = BSL above
If BSL was just swept with displacement → DoL = SSL below

This is the most powerful framework for determining trade direction.

================================================================================
7.9  LIQUIDITY MAP STATE MANAGEMENT
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
UPDATE ON EACH NEW CANDLE
────────────────────────────────────────
For each liquidity level in the map:

  If level is BSL:
    If candle.high > level.price:
      If candle.close > level.price AND displacement follows:
        → status = "RUN_THROUGH" (genuine breakout)
      If candle.high > level.price BUT candle.close < level.price:
        → status = "SWEPT" (wick sweep)
      If candle.close > level.price but no displacement:
        → status tentatively "SWEPT" or "RUN_THROUGH"
        → monitor next 3 candles for confirmation

  If level is SSL:
    Mirror logic using candle.low and candle.close

  When status changes to SWEPT:
    Record: sweep_candle_index, sweep_type (SWEEP/RAID/PURGE/RUN)
    Check for displacement reversal
    If displacement reversal → flag as entry opportunity
    Update DoL if necessary

────────────────────────────────────────
ADDING NEW LEVELS
────────────────────────────────────────
  New swing forms → new BSL/SSL entry in the map
  New session starts → update session H/L levels
  New day/week → update PDH/PDL, PWH/PWL

────────────────────────────────────────
REMOVING IRRELEVANT LEVELS
────────────────────────────────────────
  SWEPT levels: keep in map with SWEPT status (for reference)
    but do not use for future targeting
  RUN_THROUGH levels: keep but mark as broken structure
  Very old MINOR levels (beyond IPDA 60-day range): consider removing
  MAJOR levels: retain regardless of age (Weekly swing highs/lows
    from months ago are still relevant)

================================================================================
7.10  COMMON LIQUIDITY MISTAKES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: ASSUMING EVERY LEVEL BREAK IS A SWEEP
────────────────────────────────────────
Not every break of a swing high/low is a sweep. Some are genuine
breakouts (Runs). Automatically fading every level break leads to
significant losses when the trend is strong.

Fix: Use the classification algorithm (section 7.3). Check for
displacement, volume, follow-through, and HTF context. When in
doubt, wait for confirmation before entering.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: IGNORING UNSWEPT LIQUIDITY BETWEEN ENTRY AND SL
────────────────────────────────────────
Entering a long trade with an SSL sitting between entry and SL
is like walking through a minefield. The SSL is a magnet for price,
and it will likely be swept — triggering your stop in the process.

Fix: Always perform the safety check (section 7.7). No trade should
have significant unswept liquidity between entry and SL.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: TRADING AGAINST THE DoL
────────────────────────────────────────
The Draw on Liquidity tells you where price is headed. Trading
against it is fighting the current.

Scenario: DoL is BSL above at $101,500 (bullish). You see a
"beautiful" short setup on 15m. You enter short.
Result: price may dip briefly but then continues toward the BSL.
Your short gets stopped out.

Fix: ALL trades should be in the direction of the DoL. If DoL is
BSL above → only look for long entries. If DoL is SSL below →
only look for short entries. The DoL IS your directional bias.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: NOT UPDATING THE LIQUIDITY MAP
────────────────────────────────────────
A liquidity level that was swept 50 candles ago is no longer
relevant as a target. Yet the system still has it as UNSWEPT.
This leads to analyzing levels that no longer exist.

Fix: Real-time status tracking. Every candle updates every level.
SWEPT levels are retained for reference but not for targeting.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: EQUAL WEIGHT TO ALL LIQUIDITY SOURCES
────────────────────────────────────────
A triple EQH on the 4H chart is fundamentally different from a
minor 15m swing low. Yet some analyses treat them equally.

Fix: Use the significance classification (MAJOR/MODERATE/MINOR).
Major liquidity drives the market cycle. Minor liquidity drives
intraday wiggles. Trade based on Major liquidity; use Minor
liquidity for fine-tuning.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 6: EXPECTING SWEEPS IN STRONG TRENDS
────────────────────────────────────────
In a strong bullish trend, BSL levels (swing highs) will be
broken as genuine breakouts (Runs), not sweeps. Expecting a
reversal at every swing high in a strong uptrend leads to
repeatedly shorting into strength.

Fix: Context matters. In a strong trend, liquidity breaks IN the
trend direction are usually Runs. Liquidity breaks AGAINST the
trend direction are usually Sweeps. Trade accordingly.

Bullish trend:
  BSL breaks = Runs (don't short) → trade the pullback to OB/FVG
  SSL breaks = Sweeps (entry opportunities for longs!)

Bearish trend:
  SSL breaks = Runs (don't buy) → trade the pullback to OB/FVG
  BSL breaks = Sweeps (entry opportunities for shorts!)

================================================================================
7.11  INTERNAL RANGE LIQUIDITY (IRL) vs EXTERNAL RANGE LIQUIDITY (ERL)
================================================================================
Added from official ICT foundational source material (ICT Core Content
Months 1-12, "Reinforcing Liquidity Concepts and Price Delivery"):
every trading range/leg splits its liquidity into exactly two
categories based on WHERE the level sits relative to the current
range. This is not a new concept beyond sections 7.1-7.10 above - it
is the organizing framework that ties BSL/SSL, OB, and FVG together
into one coherent map, and it directly drives partial-profit strategy.

DEFINITIONS (mechanically precise, from raw OHLC):

  Given the current trading range [range_low, range_high] (defined by
  the two most recent significant opposing swings - see the
  significant swing detection used elsewhere in this system):

  EXTERNAL RANGE LIQUIDITY (ERL):
    Any liquidity level OUTSIDE the current range:
      - BSL sitting above range_high (prior swing highs beyond it)
      - SSL sitting below range_low (prior swing lows beyond it)
    These are the "big" targets - where the range itself will
    eventually break to reach.

  INTERNAL RANGE LIQUIDITY (IRL):
    Any PD Array or inefficiency INSIDE the current range:
      - Order Blocks located between range_low and range_high
      - Fair Value Gaps located between range_low and range_high
      - Liquidity Voids inside the range
    These are the "small" targets - levels the market is likely to
    revisit WHILE the range holds, before ever reaching ERL.

WHY THIS MATTERS - THE CORE RULE:
  "You can trade IRL to ERL, or ERL to IRL." Every trade has a
  starting reference point (where you entered, relative to IRL or
  ERL) and a target (the opposite category). A trade that enters at
  an IRL zone (OB/FVG inside the range) and targets ERL (liquidity
  outside the range) is the highest-probability, most "complete"
  version of an ICT setup - it uses the small internal levels as a
  precision entry, and the big external levels as the ultimate draw.

PARTIAL PROFIT RULE (directly actionable for position management):
  Take partial profits when price reaches IRL levels encountered
  along the way (they are real reactions, even mid-trade).
  Reserve full trade closure for when price reaches ERL (the range's
  external liquidity) - this is the "complete" objective of the move.

  This refines (does not replace) the existing [DUAL_TARGET_MANAGEMENT]
  section: TP1 should generally correspond to the nearest IRL level
  reached in the trade's direction, and TP2 (the fuller target) should
  correspond to the ERL level that represents the actual Draw on
  Liquidity for the move. If no ERL exists beyond TP1 in the trade's
  direction (rare - usually there is always a further external pool),
  treat TP1 itself as a partial and trail the remainder.

MECHANICAL DETECTION:
  1. Identify range_low and range_high from the two most recent
     significant opposing swings (reuse existing significant-swing
     detection - do not invent a new swing algorithm for this).
  2. Any detected OB/FVG/Liquidity Void with a midpoint between
     range_low and range_high -> tag as IRL.
  3. Any detected swing high/low, EQH/EQL, or liquidity pool beyond
     range_high or below range_low -> tag as ERL.
  4. When building the trade plan (Stage 5 / Entry TF synthesis),
     explicitly state which liquidity category is being used at each
     step (e.g. "this entry uses an IRL zone targeting an ERL zone"),
     rather than treating all PD Arrays and liquidity levels as
     interchangeable.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT CHANGE:
  IRL/ERL is a CLASSIFICATION layer on top of concepts already fully
  defined above (BSL/SSL in 7.1, Order Blocks in section 5, FVG in
  section 6) - it does not redefine how OBs, FVGs, or liquidity pools
  are detected. It only labels WHICH SIDE of the current range each
  one sits on, and gives a clear partial-vs-full profit rule based on
  that label.

================================================================================
7.12  SMT DIVERGENCE — CROSS-ASSET NON-CONFIRMATION
================================================================================
⚠️ ROOT-CAUSE ADDITION (July 2026, after deep external research into
Michael Huddleston's actual methodology): SMT (Smart Money Technique)
Divergence was completely absent from the constitution before this
update, despite being one of ICT's core confirmation tools for exactly
the moment a trade at a higher-timeframe PD Array needs its final
timing trigger. The system already computes SMT mechanically (see
detect_smt_divergence in authenticity_engine.py, using BTC/ETH as the
crypto-native correlated pair) — but the model receiving that computed
result had no deep explanation of WHAT it means, WHY it matters, or
HOW to weigh it. This section is that missing explanation.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE CORE IDEA IN PLAIN LANGUAGE
────────────────────────────────────────
Two assets that are normally tightly correlated (they usually make
their highs and lows together, moving as if tied by a rubber band)
occasionally "disagree" for a moment at exactly the kind of turning
point that matters most: one asset pushes to a new extreme (a new
high or a new low) while the other, correlated asset FAILS to follow
it there. That failure-to-confirm is the SMT Divergence.

Why does this matter? Because if both assets were being pushed purely
by genuine, broad buying or selling pressure, they should move
together — that is the entire meaning of "correlated." When one
moves further than the other at a key level, it suggests the move in
the leading asset was NOT backed by the same broad conviction that
usually drives both — it may be a liquidity engineering move (a
sweep) in that one asset specifically, rather than a real market-wide
directional shift. SMT divergence is therefore evidence that the
apparent new high/low in the leading asset is suspect, and a reversal
there is more likely than usual.

────────────────────────────────────────
BULLISH SMT DIVERGENCE — EXACT DEFINITION
────────────────────────────────────────
Given two positively correlated assets (in this system: BTC/USDT and
ETH/USDT — see CRYPTO_MARKET_DATA and authenticity_engine.py's
_SMT_COUNTERPART mapping), both approaching a swing LOW at
approximately the same time:

  Bullish SMT Divergence exists when:
    Asset A makes a LOWER LOW (a new low beyond its own prior swing low)
    Asset B makes a HIGHER LOW (fails to make a new low — its low
      stays ABOVE its own prior swing low)

  Numeric example (BTC vs ETH, both approaching a swing low):
    BTC prior swing low: 82,000.  BTC current low: 81,400.
      81,400 < 82,000 → BTC made a genuine LOWER LOW.
    ETH prior swing low: 1,950.   ETH current low: 1,962.
      1,962 > 1,950 → ETH did NOT make a new low — HIGHER LOW.

  → This is Bullish SMT Divergence. BTC's push to a new low was NOT
    confirmed by ETH. The asset failing to confirm (ETH here) is
    called "the non-manipulating leg" — its refusal to make a new low
    is the signal. The interpretation: BTC's new low may be a
    liquidity sweep (an engineered move to grab sell-side liquidity)
    rather than genuine broad-market weakness, since if the weakness
    were real and broad, ETH should have made a new low too. This
    favors a bullish reversal in BOTH assets from this level.

────────────────────────────────────────
BEARISH SMT DIVERGENCE — EXACT DEFINITION (mirror image)
────────────────────────────────────────
Given the same two assets, both approaching a swing HIGH:

  Bearish SMT Divergence exists when:
    Asset A makes a HIGHER HIGH (a new high beyond its own prior swing high)
    Asset B makes a LOWER HIGH (fails to make a new high)

  Numeric example:
    BTC prior swing high: 98,000.  BTC current high: 98,750.
      98,750 > 98,000 → BTC made a genuine HIGHER HIGH.
    ETH prior swing high: 3,650.   ETH current high: 3,610.
      3,610 < 3,650 → ETH did NOT make a new high — LOWER HIGH.

  → This is Bearish SMT Divergence. BTC's push to a new high was not
    confirmed by ETH. This favors a bearish reversal in both assets.

────────────────────────────────────────
HOW MUCH WEIGHT TO GIVE SMT — IT IS A TIMING TRIGGER, NOT A STANDALONE BIAS
────────────────────────────────────────
A critical mistake to avoid: SMT divergence, by itself, floating in
open space with no other context, is a weak, easily-misread signal.
Its real power only appears when it fires AT a location that already
matters for other reasons:

  SMT is at its STRONGEST when it occurs simultaneously with:
    - Price sitting AT a higher-timeframe PD Array (an OB or FVG
      already identified from the Daily/4H top-down analysis) — the
      PD Array tells you WHERE to expect a reaction; SMT tells you
      WHEN the reaction is actually happening right now, not just a
      hopeful guess about the zone.
    - A liquidity sweep having just occurred on the leading asset
      (the one making the "fake" new high/low) — this ties SMT
      directly into the same sweep-then-reversal mechanics used
      throughout LIQUIDITY_MAPPING and MARKET_STRUCTURE.
    - The correlated asset's failure being a CLEAR, unambiguous
      failure — a lower low/higher high that misses by a meaningful
      margin, not a razor-thin difference that could be normal noise
      or a data/exchange discrepancy between the two instruments.

  SMT should be treated as ONE additional piece of evidence to weigh
  in the overall narrative — never as an automatic override of your
  own independent reading of the structure. If SMT divergence is
  present but the leading asset's own structure shows no liquidity
  sweep and no PD Array reaction at all, the divergence alone is not
  sufficient grounds for a reversal call — say so explicitly rather
  than treating the mere presence of divergence as decisive.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY BTC/ETH SPECIFICALLY (THE CRYPTO-NATIVE CHOICE)
────────────────────────────────────────
ICT originally introduced SMT using NQ vs ES (the two major US index
futures) and later GBP/USD vs EUR/USD in forex — in both cases, two
instruments driven by the same broad macro forces (US equity risk
appetite; European economic conditions) that should therefore move
together outside of asset-specific manipulation. In crypto, BTC and
ETH play the exact same structural role: both are driven by the same
broad risk-on/risk-off crypto market sentiment, both react to the
same macro catalysts, and they are historically the two most tightly
correlated large-cap crypto assets. When analyzing BTC, ETH is the
correlated counterpart (and vice versa) — this is a direct, sound
substitution of the ICT concept's underlying logic, not an arbitrary
crypto-specific invention.

────────────────────────────────────────
HIDDEN / FAILURE MODE TO WATCH FOR: SMT ON WEAKLY-CORRELATED MOVES
────────────────────────────────────────
Correlation between BTC and ETH is usually strong but is NOT constant
— it can weaken meaningfully during asset-specific news (e.g. an
Ethereum-specific protocol upgrade, or a Bitcoin-specific ETF flow
event) that affects one asset without affecting the other for
legitimate, non-manipulative reasons. If independent news specific to
one asset explains why it diverged from the other, that divergence is
NOT genuine SMT in the ICT sense (a liquidity/manipulation signal) —
it is simply two assets legitimately decoupling for a real
fundamental reason. When available context suggests an asset-specific
catalyst, flag this honestly rather than treating every raw high/low
mismatch as institutional manipulation evidence.


================================================================================
END OF [LIQUIDITY_MAPPING]
================================================================================




================================================================================
[PREMIUM_DISCOUNT]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Premium and Discount zones answer a simple but critical question:
"Is the current price expensive or cheap relative to the recent range?"

Institutions do not buy at expensive prices. They do not sell at cheap
prices. They buy in discount (cheap) and sell in premium (expensive).
This is not theory — it is the fundamental logic of profitable
position building. An institution accumulating a $500M long position
does so when price is in discount. An institution distributing that
position does so when price is in premium.

The Premium/Discount framework provides a FILTER for every other ICT
concept. An Order Block, an FVG, a liquidity sweep — all of these are
stronger or weaker depending on whether they occur in the correct zone:
  - Bullish OB in Discount = institutional logic (strong)
  - Bullish OB in Premium = against institutional logic (weak)
  - Bearish OB in Premium = institutional logic (strong)
  - Bearish OB in Discount = against institutional logic (weak)

This section defines how to calculate Premium/Discount zones from OHLC
data, how to identify the Optimal Trade Entry (OTE) zone, and how to
apply these concepts dynamically as structure evolves.

================================================================================
8.1  THE RANGE AND EQUILIBRIUM
================================================================================

Premium/Discount is calculated relative to a RANGE — the distance
between a significant swing high and swing low.

────────────────────────────────────────
IDENTIFYING THE RELEVANT RANGE
────────────────────────────────────────
The range is defined by the most recent STRUCTURAL swing high and
swing low on the timeframe being analyzed.

For analysis purposes:
  Range High = the most recent significant swing high
               (MODERATE+ quality, N=3+ confirmation)
  Range Low  = the most recent significant swing low
               (MODERATE+ quality, N=3+ confirmation)

Which swings to use:
  - The swings that define the CURRENT structural movement
  - In an uptrend: the last confirmed HH and the last confirmed HL
  - In a downtrend: the last confirmed LH and the last confirmed LL
  - In a range: the range boundaries (highest SH and lowest SL
    within the consolidation)

IMPORTANT: the range changes every time a new structural swing forms.
  When a new HH is made → Range High updates → all zones shift
  When a new HL is made → Range Low updates → all zones shift
  This makes Premium/Discount a DYNAMIC concept, not static.

────────────────────────────────────────
EQUILIBRIUM (EQ)
────────────────────────────────────────
  EQ = (Range_High + Range_Low) / 2

  Equilibrium is the "fair price" — the midpoint between the
  extremes of the current structural range.

  EQ is not a trading level by itself. It is the dividing line
  between premium and discount.

  Price at EQ = no edge. Price is neither cheap nor expensive.
  There is no institutional advantage to buying or selling at EQ.

────────────────────────────────────────
PREMIUM ZONE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Premium = everything ABOVE EQ (from EQ to Range_High)

  In this zone, price is EXPENSIVE relative to the range.
  Institutional behavior in premium:
    - Institutions SELL in premium (distribute positions)
    - Institutions DO NOT BUY in premium (too expensive)
    - Retail traders buy in premium (chasing, fear of missing out)
    - This is where institutions sell TO retail

  For the system:
    - Bearish setups in Premium = aligned with institutional logic
    - Bullish setups in Premium = against institutional logic
    - Bearish OBs, Bearish FVGs in Premium = strongest
    - Bullish OBs in Premium = weakest (likely to fail)

────────────────────────────────────────
DISCOUNT ZONE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Discount = everything BELOW EQ (from Range_Low to EQ)

  In this zone, price is CHEAP relative to the range.
  Institutional behavior in discount:
    - Institutions BUY in discount (accumulate positions)
    - Institutions DO NOT SELL in discount (too cheap)
    - Retail traders sell in discount (panic, fear)
    - This is where institutions buy FROM retail

  For the system:
    - Bullish setups in Discount = aligned with institutional logic
    - Bearish setups in Discount = against institutional logic
    - Bullish OBs, Bullish FVGs in Discount = strongest
    - Bearish OBs in Discount = weakest (likely to fail)

================================================================================
8.2  FIBONACCI LEVELS WITHIN THE RANGE
================================================================================

ICT uses specific Fibonacci retracement levels within the
Premium/Discount framework. These are not arbitrary — they
correspond to institutional order placement tendencies observed
across decades of market data.

────────────────────────────────────────
CALCULATION
────────────────────────────────────────
From Range_High and Range_Low:

  Range = Range_High - Range_Low

  Key levels (measured from Range_High downward for a bullish range,
  i.e., measuring how far price has pulled back from the high):

  0% level   = Range_High (no retracement — price at the high)

  Premium Zone:
  23.6% level = Range_High - (Range × 0.236) → shallow premium
  38.2% level = Range_High - (Range × 0.382) → mid premium

  Equilibrium:
  50.0% level = Range_High - (Range × 0.500) = EQ

  Discount Zone:
  61.8% level = Range_High - (Range × 0.618) → shallow discount
  70.5% level = Range_High - (Range × 0.705) → mid discount (OTE start)
  78.6% level = Range_High - (Range × 0.786) → deep discount (OTE end)

  100% level  = Range_Low (full retracement — price at the low)

For a BEARISH range (measuring from Range_Low upward):
  The levels flip:
  0% = Range_Low
  61.8% = Range_Low + (Range × 0.618) → premium OTE zone for shorts
  78.6% = Range_Low + (Range × 0.786) → deep premium
  100% = Range_High

────────────────────────────────────────
ZONE BOUNDARIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  EXTREME PREMIUM:  0% to 23.6%   (near the high — overextended)
  PREMIUM:          23.6% to 50%  (above EQ — expensive)
  EQUILIBRIUM:      ~50%          (fair price — no edge)
  DISCOUNT:         50% to 76.4%  (below EQ — cheap)
  EXTREME DISCOUNT: 76.4% to 100% (near the low — deeply cheap)

  The system tracks which zone the current price is in:
    current_zone_pct = (Range_High - current_price) / Range × 100

    if current_zone_pct < 23.6: zone = "EXTREME_PREMIUM"
    elif current_zone_pct < 50:  zone = "PREMIUM"
    elif current_zone_pct < 52:  zone = "EQUILIBRIUM" (±2% buffer)
    elif current_zone_pct < 76.4: zone = "DISCOUNT"
    else:                         zone = "EXTREME_DISCOUNT"

================================================================================
8.3  OPTIMAL TRADE ENTRY (OTE)
================================================================================

The OTE zone is the sweet spot within the discount (for longs) or
premium (for shorts) where institutional orders are most densely
concentrated. It is derived from the Fibonacci retracement levels
between 62% and 79%.

────────────────────────────────────────
OTE FOR BULLISH ENTRIES
────────────────────────────────────────
When looking for a long entry after a bullish impulse:

  The impulse created a new swing high (Range_High)
  Price is pulling back (retracing) from this high

  OTE Zone:
    OTE_top    = Range_High - (Range × 0.618)  (61.8% retracement)
    OTE_bottom = Range_High - (Range × 0.786)  (78.6% retracement)

  The OTE zone is in the DISCOUNT area of the range.
  This is where institutions placed their buy orders during the
  impulse leg, and where unfilled orders remain.

  Example:
    Range_High = $101,000 (recent HH)
    Range_Low  = $98,000 (recent HL)
    Range = $3,000

    OTE_top    = 101000 - (3000 × 0.618) = 101000 - 1854 = $99,146
    OTE_bottom = 101000 - (3000 × 0.786) = 101000 - 2358 = $98,642

    OTE zone for long entry: $98,642 to $99,146

  If a bullish OB exists within the OTE zone → this is the highest-
  probability long entry available.

  If an FVG's CE falls within the OTE zone → exceptional entry point.

────────────────────────────────────────
OTE FOR BEARISH ENTRIES
────────────────────────────────────────
When looking for a short entry after a bearish impulse:

  The impulse created a new swing low (Range_Low)
  Price is rallying (retracing) from this low

  OTE Zone:
    OTE_bottom = Range_Low + (Range × 0.618)  (61.8% retracement up)
    OTE_top    = Range_Low + (Range × 0.786)  (78.6% retracement up)

  The OTE zone is in the PREMIUM area for shorts.

  Example:
    Range_High = $101,000 (recent LH)
    Range_Low  = $98,000 (recent LL)
    Range = $3,000

    OTE_bottom = 98000 + (3000 × 0.618) = 98000 + 1854 = $99,854
    OTE_top    = 98000 + (3000 × 0.786) = 98000 + 2358 = $100,358

    OTE zone for short entry: $99,854 to $100,358

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY OTE WORKS
────────────────────────────────────────
The 62-79% retracement zone is not magic. It works because:

1. Shallow pullbacks (23-38%) do not give institutions enough
   liquidity. Not enough stops have been triggered, not enough
   counter-trend traders have entered. The pullback needs to go
   deeper to gather sufficient orders.

2. Deep pullbacks (beyond 79%) start threatening the structural
   swing (approaching the Range_Low for longs). If price retraces
   more than 79%, the odds increase that the swing will be broken
   entirely (CHoCH), invalidating the long thesis.

3. The 62-79% zone is the mathematical sweet spot:
   - Deep enough to gather liquidity and trigger stops
   - Not so deep that it threatens the structure
   - This is where institutional algorithms cluster their orders

4. Empirically: across thousands of documented ICT examples,
   the highest-success-rate entries occur within this zone.
   This is not a guaranteed reversal zone — nothing is — but it
   is the statistically optimal entry area.

────────────────────────────────────────
OTE + OB + FVG = THE TRIFECTA
────────────────────────────────────────
When all three align at the same price:
  - OTE zone (62-79% retracement) → optimal institutional zone
  - Order Block within the OTE → specific unfilled orders
  - FVG overlapping the OB → confirmed imbalance

This trifecta is the highest-probability setup in ICT:
  Three independent institutional references → same zone.
  Confidence adjustment: +15% when all three align.

The system should specifically check for this alignment:
  1. Calculate OTE zone
  2. Check: is there an OB within the OTE?
  3. Check: does an FVG overlap with that OB?
  4. If all three → flag as "TRIFECTA_SETUP" with maximum priority

================================================================================
8.4  DYNAMIC PREMIUM/DISCOUNT
================================================================================

Premium/Discount zones are NOT static. They shift every time the
structural range changes. The system must recalculate dynamically.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN THE RANGE UPDATES
────────────────────────────────────────

Event 1: NEW HIGHER HIGH (in bullish structure)
  Range_High updates to the new HH.
  Range_Low stays at the last confirmed HL.
  All zones shift upward.

  What was Premium becomes closer to EQ.
  What was EQ becomes Discount.

  Practical impact:
    An OB that was at 55% retracement (near EQ, weak zone for longs)
    may now be at 65% retracement (OTE zone, strong zone for longs)
    after the range extends with a new HH.

  The system must RECALCULATE zone positions for all active OBs
  and FVGs after every structural range update.

Event 2: NEW HIGHER LOW (in bullish structure)
  Range_Low updates to the new HL.
  Range_High stays at the last confirmed HH.
  The range contracts (HL moves up, shrinking the distance).

  Zones compress. What was deep discount is now moderate discount.

Event 3: BOS creates entirely new range
  After a BOS, the system must identify the NEW structural
  Range_High and Range_Low.
  All zones are recalculated from scratch.

Event 4: CHoCH (potential range reset)
  After a CHoCH, the old range may no longer be relevant.
  The system should:
    - Use the CHoCH swing as one boundary
    - Use the extreme that preceded the CHoCH as the other
    - Recalculate from this new range

────────────────────────────────────────
MULTI-TIMEFRAME PREMIUM/DISCOUNT
────────────────────────────────────────
Each timeframe has its own range and its own P/D zones:

  Weekly P/D: based on Weekly swing H/L (the broadest view)
  Daily P/D: based on Daily swing H/L
  4H P/D: based on 4H swing H/L
  1H P/D: based on 1H swing H/L (the most granular for intraday)

These can CONFLICT:
  Price may be in Daily Discount but 4H Premium.
  This happens when: the Daily pullback is shallow but the 4H
  has already made a significant rally within the pullback.

RESOLUTION:
  Higher TF P/D takes priority for BIAS decisions.
  Lower TF P/D is used for ENTRY precision.

  Example:
    Daily: price in Discount (good for longs — institutional logic)
    4H: price in Premium (within the Daily discount zone, 4H has
        rallied and is now in its own premium)

    Assessment: the Daily discount is the dominant framework.
    The 4H being in premium means: wait for 4H to pull back into
    its own discount (which will still be within the Daily discount).
    When 4H pulls back to its OTE within the Daily OTE → entry zone.

  This multi-layer approach is how the TOP_DOWN_WORKFLOW section
  narrows zones from broad (Daily P/D) to precise (4H P/D within
  Daily zones).

================================================================================
8.5  PRACTICAL APPLICATION RULES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
RULE 1: NO BUYING IN PREMIUM
────────────────────────────────────────
If the current price is in the Premium zone (above EQ):
  - Do NOT enter new long positions
  - Bullish OBs in this zone are weak (expect them to fail)
  - Bullish FVGs in this zone are weak
  - The price is expensive — institutions are selling here, not buying

Exception:
  If the range just shifted (new HH was just made) and what was
  Premium is now closer to EQ in the new range → reassess.

────────────────────────────────────────
RULE 2: NO SELLING IN DISCOUNT
────────────────────────────────────────
If the current price is in the Discount zone (below EQ):
  - Do NOT enter new short positions
  - Bearish OBs in this zone are weak
  - Bearish FVGs in this zone are weak
  - The price is cheap — institutions are buying here, not selling

────────────────────────────────────────
RULE 3: NO EDGE AT EQUILIBRIUM
────────────────────────────────────────
If the current price is within ±2% of EQ:
  - Neither buyer nor seller has an advantage
  - Wait for price to move into a definitive zone
  - Setups at EQ are lower confidence (-5% adjustment)
  - An OB at exactly EQ is ambiguous (neither P nor D advantage)

────────────────────────────────────────
RULE 4: OTE IS THE SWEET SPOT
────────────────────────────────────────
The OTE zone (62-79% retracement) is the primary entry area.
  - Bullish entries: OTE in discount = best zone
  - Bearish entries: OTE in premium = best zone
  - An OB within OTE = high-priority entry
  - FVG CE within OTE = optimal entry point
  - OTE + OB + FVG = trifecta (maximum confidence)

────────────────────────────────────────
RULE 5: EXTREME ZONES ARE REVERSAL TERRITORIES
────────────────────────────────────────
Extreme Premium (0-23.6%):
  Price is near the high of the range. Overextended.
  If the trend is bullish: expect a pullback (don't chase)
  If a bearish setup appears here: higher confidence (selling high)

Extreme Discount (76.4-100%):
  Price is near the low of the range. Deeply cheap.
  If the trend is bearish: expect a bounce (don't chase shorts)
  If a bullish setup appears here: higher confidence (buying low)

  Extreme zones coinciding with HTF OBs or HTF FVGs =
  some of the highest-probability reversal setups available.

────────────────────────────────────────
RULE 6: P/D AFFECTS CONFIDENCE SCORING
────────────────────────────────────────
For the CONFIDENCE_SCORING section, P/D zone contributes:

  Entry in correct zone (discount for longs, premium for shorts):
    In OTE zone (62-79%): +8%
    In discount/premium but outside OTE: +5%

  Entry at EQ: +0% (neutral)

  Entry in wrong zone (premium for longs, discount for shorts):
    -10% (significant penalty — against institutional logic)

================================================================================
8.6  P/D CALCULATION EXAMPLES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
EXAMPLE 1: BULLISH STRUCTURE ON 4H
────────────────────────────────────────
  Last confirmed HH: $102,400 (4H swing high, quality 19)
  Last confirmed HL: $99,200 (4H swing low, quality 17)

  Range = 102400 - 99200 = 3200

  EQ = (102400 + 99200) / 2 = $100,800

  Premium Zone: $100,800 to $102,400 (above EQ)
  Discount Zone: $99,200 to $100,800 (below EQ)

  OTE Zone (for longs — pullback from HH):
    OTE_top    = 102400 - (3200 × 0.618) = 102400 - 1978 = $100,422
    OTE_bottom = 102400 - (3200 × 0.786) = 102400 - 2515 = $99,885

  Current price: $100,200

  Zone check: (102400 - 100200) / 3200 = 68.75% retracement
  → DISCOUNT zone ✓
  → Within OTE range (between 61.8% and 78.6%) ✓
  → This is the optimal zone for long entries

  If there is a 4H Bullish OB at $100,100-$100,300:
    → OB is within OTE ✓
    → OB is in Discount ✓
    → P/D criterion score = 5/5
    → This is a high-priority entry zone

────────────────────────────────────────
EXAMPLE 2: RANGE SHIFT AFTER NEW HH
────────────────────────────────────────
  Old range: HH = $102,400, HL = $99,200
  Price rallies and makes new HH at $104,000

  New range: HH = $104,000, HL = $99,200 (HL hasn't updated yet)

  New EQ = (104000 + 99200) / 2 = $101,600

  Old EQ was $100,800. New EQ is $101,600.

  A Bullish OB at $101,000:
    Old range: at 43.75% retracement → near EQ, neutral zone
    New range: at 62.5% retracement → OTE zone! Now high-priority

  This is why P/D must be recalculated every time the range changes.
  The same OB went from "neutral" to "optimal entry zone" simply
  because a new HH extended the range.

────────────────────────────────────────
EXAMPLE 3: CONFLICTING MULTI-TF P/D
────────────────────────────────────────
  Daily range: HH = $105,000, HL = $95,000
    EQ = $100,000
    Current price: $98,000
    Daily zone: 70% retracement → OTE/DISCOUNT ✓

  4H range (within the Daily pullback):
    Recent 4H HH = $99,500, Recent 4H HL = $97,000
    4H EQ = $98,250
    Current price: $98,000
    4H zone: 60% retracement → near EQ/shallow discount

  Assessment:
    Daily says: "You're in the OTE — look for longs"
    4H says: "You're near EQ — wait for deeper pullback"

    Resolution: Daily OTE confirms the area is correct for longs.
    4H near EQ means: wait for 4H to pull back to ITS OTE
    (4H OTE = $98,250 - (2500 × 0.618) to (2500 × 0.786)
     = $97,955 to $97,535)

    → Refined entry zone: $97,535 to $97,955
       (4H OTE within Daily OTE = tightly refined zone)

================================================================================
8.7  P/D STATE TRACKING
================================================================================

The system maintains P/D state for each analyzed timeframe:

  {
    timeframe: "4H"
    range_high: price (last significant SH)
    range_high_index: candle index
    range_low: price (last significant SL)
    range_low_index: candle index
    range: range_high - range_low
    eq: (range_high + range_low) / 2

    ote_long_top: range_high - (range × 0.618)
    ote_long_bottom: range_high - (range × 0.786)
    ote_short_bottom: range_low + (range × 0.618)
    ote_short_top: range_low + (range × 0.786)

    current_price: price
    current_retracement_pct: (range_high - current_price) / range × 100
    current_zone: "EXTREME_PREMIUM" | "PREMIUM" | "EQUILIBRIUM" |
