                  "DISCOUNT" | "EXTREME_DISCOUNT"
    in_ote: true/false (is current price within OTE zone?)

    last_update: candle index when range was last recalculated
  }

This state is recalculated whenever:
  - A new swing high or low is confirmed on the timeframe
  - A BOS or CHoCH changes the structural reference points
  - At the start of each new analysis

================================================================================
8.8  COMMON P/D MISTAKES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: USING A STATIC RANGE
────────────────────────────────────────
Calculating P/D once and never updating. As structure evolves
(new HH, new HL, BOS), the range changes. Zones that were
discount become premium and vice versa.

Fix: Recalculate every time a new structural swing is confirmed
or a BOS/CHoCH creates a new range reference.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: BUYING IN PREMIUM BECAUSE "THE OB LOOKS GOOD"
────────────────────────────────────────
An OB can have perfect formation, strong displacement, high volume,
and be completely wrong for a long entry if it sits in Premium.
The OB quality criteria (section 5.4, criterion 5) already accounts
for this, but it bears repeating: P/D context overrides visual quality.

Fix: Always check the zone BEFORE committing to a trade direction.
A "beautiful" bullish OB in premium is a trap for retail buyers.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: TREATING EQ AS AN ENTRY ZONE
────────────────────────────────────────
EQ is the midpoint — neither cheap nor expensive. Entering at EQ
provides no institutional advantage. The edge comes from entering
in discount (for longs) or premium (for shorts).

Fix: Treat EQ as a "no man's land." Wait for price to move into
a definitive zone before considering entries.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: IGNORING MULTI-TF P/D CONFLICTS
────────────────────────────────────────
Entering a long because the 1H shows discount, while the Daily
shows premium. The Daily context is dominant — being in 1H discount
within Daily premium means you're buying high on the bigger picture.

Fix: Always check the HIGHER TF P/D first. HTF P/D overrides LTF P/D
for directional bias. LTF P/D refines the entry within the HTF context.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: OTE AS A GUARANTEE
────────────────────────────────────────
Assuming price MUST reverse at OTE. The OTE zone is the optimal
entry area, but it is not guaranteed to hold. If the structural
swing below the OTE breaks (price retraces beyond 79% and closes
below the Range_Low), the entire thesis is invalidated (CHoCH).

Fix: OTE is a probability zone, not a certainty zone. Always have
a SL below the structural level. If price goes through OTE and
breaks structure → the trade thesis was wrong. Accept the loss.

================================================================================
END OF [PREMIUM_DISCOUNT]
================================================================================





================================================================================
[ICT_TIME_AND_SESSIONS]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
In ICT methodology, TIME is not secondary to price — it is equally
important. The best price setup in the world is worthless if it occurs
at the wrong time. Institutions operate on schedules. They execute
during specific windows. Their algorithms activate at specific hours.
The manipulation-distribution cycle follows predictable daily patterns.

ICT says: "Time is more important than price."

This is not an exaggeration. A mediocre setup during NY AM Kill Zone
has higher probability than a perfect setup during NY Lunch dead zone.
The reason is simple: institutional algorithms are active during Kill
Zones and dormant during dead zones. Without institutional participation,
even a technically perfect setup will not receive the displacement and
follow-through it needs to reach targets.

This section defines every time-based concept in ICT, how to detect
each from candle timestamps, and how time affects every trading decision.

All times in this section are NEW YORK TIME (Eastern).
The system converts UTC timestamps to NY time using DST rules defined
in OHLC_PROCESSING section 2.7.

================================================================================
9.1  THE TRADING DAY STRUCTURE
================================================================================

A complete ICT trading day is divided into distinct phases. Each phase
has a specific character, a specific purpose in the institutional cycle,
and specific rules for the system.

────────────────────────────────────────
ASIAN RANGE: 18:00 - 00:00 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  Low volatility. Tight range. Low volume. Price consolidates.
  This is the "calm before the storm."

Institutional purpose:
  During Asian Range, institutions are NOT actively pushing price.
  They are BUILDING liquidity:
  - Retail traders see the "support" and "resistance" of the range
  - They place buy orders at range lows and sell orders at range highs
  - Stop losses accumulate above the range high and below the range low
  - By the time London opens, a dense pool of liquidity exists on
    both sides of the Asian Range

What the system does:
  1. Identify Asian Range High and Asian Range Low:
     Asian_High = max(high) for all candles 18:00-00:00 NY
     Asian_Low  = min(low) for all candles 18:00-00:00 NY
  2. These are LIQUIDITY TARGETS for London and NY sessions
  3. DO NOT enter trades during Asian Range
  4. Use Asian Range to prepare for the trading sessions ahead

Trading: NO NEW ENTRIES
  Confidence adjustment: -10%
  Exception: none for intraday on FOREX/gold/indices. For CRYPTO
  (BTC/ETH) specifically, see section 9.1B below for the
  crypto-adapted schedule - the underlying "do not trade the
  low-participation range-building window" principle is identical,
  but the exact clock window used to identify that range differs
  (00:00-08:30 NY "Overnight Range" replaces this literal
  18:00-00:00 NY Asian Range for crypto).

────────────────────────────────────────
ASIAN MANIPULATION WINDOW: 00:00 - 02:00 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  Transitional period. Sometimes quiet (continuation of Asian calm).
  Sometimes a small sweep occurs as early institutional algorithms
  begin probing one side of the Asian Range.

What the system does:
  1. Monitor for sweeps of Asian H/L during this window
  2. If a sweep occurs:
     - Record it: which side was swept (high or low)?
     - Does it have displacement? (if yes → strong signal for London)
     - This gives an early indication of London's likely direction:
       Asian Low swept → London likely to move UP
       Asian High swept → London likely to move DOWN
  3. If no sweep occurs → Asian Range remains intact for London

Trading: NO NEW ENTRIES (monitoring only)
  The sweep during this window, if it occurs, provides CONTEXT
  for London session decisions. Not an entry signal by itself.

Frequency of sweeps during this window:
  ~40% of days: nothing happens (flat)
  ~35% of days: small sweep of one side
  ~15% of days: clear sweep with early displacement
  ~10% of days: significant move (usually news-driven)

────────────────────────────────────────
LONDON KILL ZONE: 02:00 - 05:00 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  The first major institutional session. Volume increases dramatically.
  This is where the day's direction often reveals itself — but through
  MANIPULATION first, then direction.

Institutional purpose:
  London open is where the AMD (Accumulation-Manipulation-Distribution)
  cycle begins for the European/US trading day.

  Typical London sequence:
    02:00-02:30: Initial volatility spike
    02:30-03:30: MANIPULATION phase (Judas Swing)
      Price sweeps one side of the Asian Range
      This is a FALSE move designed to trap retail traders
    03:30-05:00: DISTRIBUTION phase
      The REAL move begins in the opposite direction of the Judas Swing
      Displacement candles appear
      FVGs and OBs form in the true direction

What the system does:
  1. Identify the Judas Swing direction (see section 9.3)
  2. Wait for the sweep of Asian H/L
  3. Look for displacement reversal AFTER the sweep
  4. Enter on LTF confirmation in the reversal direction
  5. This is one of the highest-probability intraday setups

Trading: ACTIVE WINDOW
  Confidence adjustment: +5%
  Best time for entries: 03:00-04:30 NY
  Silver Bullet #1 window: 03:00-04:00 NY (see section 9.5)

────────────────────────────────────────
PRE-NEW YORK: 05:00 - 08:30 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  Transition between London and NY. Often a pullback or consolidation
  of the London move. Lower volume as London winds down and NY
  has not yet opened.

What the system does:
  1. Assess what London accomplished:
     - Did London sweep Asian H/L? Which side?
     - Is the London move likely to continue or reverse?
     - Where is price relative to key levels?
  2. Identify potential NY setups forming

Trading: CAUTION
  Confidence adjustment: -3%
  Can enter IF London established a clear direction AND price
  is pulling back to a key level (OB/FVG in the London direction).
  Otherwise: wait for NY AM.

────────────────────────────────────────
NEW YORK AM KILL ZONE: 08:30 - 11:00 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  The MOST important trading window of the day. Highest volume.
  Largest moves. Most institutional participation. The US market
  opens (equities at 09:30, but CME futures and forex react
  from 08:30 with economic data releases).

Institutional purpose:
  NY AM is where institutions execute the bulk of their daily orders.
  Two common scenarios:

  Scenario A: NY CONTINUES London's direction
    London established the direction (after the Judas Swing reversal).
    NY AM picks up where London left off.
    The continuation often targets the next liquidity pool beyond
    London's reach.

  Scenario B: NY REVERSES London
    Sometimes London's move was itself the manipulation (a larger
    Judas Swing). NY AM reverses London's direction.
    This is less common but very powerful when it occurs.
    Look for: London's move into a HTF OB/FVG → NY reversal from
    that level.

  NY AM AMD within the session:
    08:30-09:15: Accumulation (initial range)
    09:15-09:45: Manipulation (sweep one side of the range)
    09:45-11:00: Distribution (the real intraday move)

What the system does:
  1. At 08:30: assess current position relative to London move
  2. Identify NY session accumulation range (first 30-45 minutes)
  3. Watch for manipulation (sweep of accumulation range)
  4. Enter on the distribution move with LTF confirmation
  5. This window provides the highest-confidence entries of the day

Trading: HIGHEST PRIORITY WINDOW
  Confidence adjustment: +8%
  Best time for entries: 09:00-10:30 NY
  Silver Bullet #2 window: 10:00-11:00 NY

────────────────────────────────────────
NY LUNCH: 12:00 - 13:30 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  DEAD ZONE. Institutional traders are literally at lunch.
  Price action becomes choppy, directionless, and full of
  false signals. Stop hunts occur frequently but without
  follow-through. Spreads may widen.

Institutional purpose:
  None. Institutions are not actively executing during this period.
  Any movement is noise — thin market conditions allowing random
  spikes that reverse immediately.

What the system does:
  1. CLOSE any open analysis for new entries
  2. Do NOT enter any new trades
  3. If already in a trade: maintain position but do not adjust
  4. Any "setup" that appears during NY Lunch is statistically
     unreliable and should be ignored

Trading: ABSOLUTELY NO NEW ENTRIES
  Confidence adjustment: -10%
  This is not a guideline — it is a rule. Losses during NY Lunch
  are almost always avoidable and represent system discipline failure.

────────────────────────────────────────
NEW YORK PM KILL ZONE: 13:30 - 16:00 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  Second institutional window. Often a continuation of the AM move,
  but sometimes a reversal (profit-taking from the AM move creates
  a counter-move).

Institutional purpose:
  This is where:
  - The AM move either completes (reaches its target) or
  - Institutional profit-taking creates a retracement
  - Sometimes a "second leg" forms, extending the AM move further

  PM behavior depends on whether the AM reached its target:
    AM reached TP (DoL swept): PM may reverse (profit-taking)
    AM did NOT reach TP: PM continues toward the target
    AM was choppy: PM may establish the real direction

What the system does:
  1. Assess: did the AM move reach its liquidity target?
     - Yes → expect potential reversal or slowdown in PM
     - No → PM likely continues toward the target
  2. If continuing: look for pullback to AM's OB/FVG for entry
  3. If reversing: look for AM high/low sweep + displacement

Trading: SECONDARY WINDOW
  Confidence adjustment: +3%
  Less reliable than AM but still valid.
  Silver Bullet #3 window: 14:00-15:00 NY
  Be cautious of "end of day" dynamics after 15:00.

────────────────────────────────────────
POST-MARKET / TRANSITION: 16:00 - 18:00 NY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character:
  US markets closing. Volume drops. Day's narrative is concluded.
  Price may drift or consolidate.

Trading: NO NEW ENTRIES
  Confidence adjustment: -15%
  Use this time for analysis, not execution.
  Review the day's price action and prepare for the next day.

────────────────────────────────────────
WEEKLY DEAD PERIODS
────────────────────────────────────────
WEEKEND (Saturday-Sunday):
  Even in crypto (which trades 24/7), weekends are dangerous:
  - Volume drops 40-70% compared to weekdays
  - Moves are easily manipulated by smaller players
  - Institutional Smart Money is not active
  - Weekend moves are frequently reversed on Monday
  - "Sunday night gap" in traditional markets creates noise

  Confidence adjustment: -15% for ANY weekend analysis
  Recommendation: do not trade Saturday or Sunday. Analyze, plan,
  but do not execute.

FRIDAY AFTERNOON (after 12:00 NY):
  Weekly position squaring. Institutional traders close positions
  before the weekend. This creates counter-trend moves that can
  trap traders. A strong trend all week can reverse on Friday
  afternoon purely due to profit-taking, not structural change.

  Confidence adjustment: -5% after 12:00 NY Friday
  Avoid new entries after 14:00 NY Friday.

================================================================================
9.1B  CRYPTO-SPECIFIC SESSION ADAPTATION — RECONCILING 24/7 MARKETS WITH ICT TIME THEORY
================================================================================
⚠️ ROOT-CAUSE CORRECTION (July 2026, after deep external research into
how the ICT community actually adapts time theory for BTC/ETH): the
session windows in 9.1 above (Asian Range 18:00-00:00 NY, Asian
Manipulation 00:00-02:00 NY, London Killzone 02:00-05:00 NY as the
Judas Swing window) were written using the LITERAL forex session
schedule. This produces a genuine, documented contradiction for crypto
specifically: BTC and ETH have no real "London open" or "Asian
session" in the institutional sense forex does (a physical exchange
opening/closing at those exact hours) — crypto trades continuously,
and the actual concentration of institutional participation (via
NQ/ES-correlated desks, US-based market makers, and equity-hours
trading firms that also run crypto books) clusters around **US
equity-market hours**, not the literal London/Asian clock.

This does NOT mean sections 9.1-9.10 above are wrong or should be
ignored — they remain fully correct and directly applicable for
forex/gold/indices, and the underlying LOGIC of every concept
(accumulation before manipulation, manipulation sweeps liquidity
before the real move, kill zones concentrate institutional activity)
is identical for crypto too. What changes for crypto is ONLY the
CLOCK TIMES themselves — the mechanism, sequence, and reasoning stay
the same.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE CRYPTO-ADAPTED SESSION MAP (use these times for BTC/ETH; use 9.1's
original times for forex/gold/indices)
────────────────────────────────────────
  OVERNIGHT RANGE: 00:00 - 08:30 NY
    This REPLACES the forex "Asian Range (18:00-00:00 NY)" concept for
    crypto specifically. Rationale: crypto has no genuine low-volume
    "Asian session" distinct from the rest of the day the way forex
    does (forex Asian session is quiet specifically because London
    and NY desks are closed; crypto desks never fully close). The
    empirically useful substitute is the range price builds from
    midnight NY until the US equity-hours institutional window opens
    at 08:30 NY — this plays the SAME structural role (a range whose
    high/low become the liquidity pool the next active window targets)
    even though the underlying cause (desk closures) does not apply.
    Compute exactly as: overnight_high = max(high) for all candles
    00:00-08:30 NY: overnight_low = min(low) for the same window.
    Trading: NO NEW ENTRIES during this window, same as forex Asian
    Range — this window is for identifying the range, not trading it.

  CRYPTO JUDAS WINDOW: 08:30 - 11:00 NY (= forex NY AM Killzone)
    This REPLACES the forex "Asian Manipulation + London Killzone
    (00:00-05:00 NY)" window as the search window for a Judas Swing on
    crypto. Rationale: the sweep of the overnight range (defined
    above) that then reverses into the day's real direction
    statistically clusters around the US equity open for crypto, not
    the London hours — because this is when the correlated NQ/ES
    institutional flow (which crypto desks track closely — see
    ICT_MARKET_MAKER_MODEL and the BTC/NQ correlation logic in
    LIQUIDITY_MAPPING) actually activates. Detection logic is
    otherwise IDENTICAL to forex's Judas Swing (9.3 below): does price
    wick beyond the overnight_high or overnight_low, opposite to the
    already-established Daily Bias, then fail to close beyond it and
    reverse? If yes, that is the crypto Judas Swing.

  PRIMARY CRYPTO KILLZONE: 08:30 - 11:00 NY
    The single highest-probability execution window for BTC/ETH —
    equivalent in importance to forex's NY AM Killzone (9.1 already
    covers this correctly for both markets; no change needed here,
    this row is included only for a complete crypto-specific summary).

  SECONDARY CRYPTO KILLZONE: 13:30 - 16:00 NY
    Equivalent to forex's NY PM Killzone — still meaningful for
    crypto due to continued equity-hours institutional participation,
    but statistically less powerful than the AM window above.

  LOWER-PROBABILITY WINDOW: 02:00 - 05:00 NY
    This is where forex places its PRIMARY Judas Swing / London
    Killzone. For crypto, this window still sees genuine volatility
    occasionally (particularly during high-volatility weeks or
    Europe-specific crypto news), but it is NOT the primary window —
    treat any setup here with the same caution 9.1 assigns to a
    lower-confidence window, not with London Killzone's forex-level
    confidence.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE JUDAS SWING SPECIFICALLY (a concrete example
of getting this wrong)
────────────────────────────────────────
Consider a bullish Daily Bias on BTC. Using the FOREX schedule
literally, the system would watch 00:00-05:00 NY for a fake bearish
sweep of the Asian range before the real rally. Using the CORRECT
crypto-adapted schedule, the system instead watches 08:30-11:00 NY for
a fake bearish sweep of the OVERNIGHT range (00:00-08:30 NY) before
the real rally. These produce genuinely different trade windows and
different reference ranges — using the wrong one means either missing
the real crypto Judas Swing entirely (because it happens at 09:15 NY,
outside the 00:00-05:00 forex window the system was checking) or
falsely flagging ordinary overnight noise as a "Judas Swing" during a
period where crypto institutional participation is statistically low.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHAT STAYS EXACTLY THE SAME FOR CRYPTO (do not re-derive from scratch)
────────────────────────────────────────
  - The core AMD/Power of Three logic (9.2): accumulation, then a
    manipulation sweep, then the real distribution move. Only the
    clock anchor changes (see above); the phase LOGIC is identical.
  - The Weekend / Friday-afternoon cautions (9.1, already crypto-aware
    where written — "even in crypto... weekend moves are 60-70% lower
    volume, frequently false"). No further adjustment needed.
  - The Macro Times (9.4) and Day-of-Week profiles (9.7) — these
    reference specific clock minutes tied to US economic data release
    schedules and NYSE hours, both of which affect crypto through the
    BTC/NQ correlation regardless of crypto's own 24/7 nature. Use as
    written.
  - The Silver Bullet windows (9.5) — kept as continuation-timing
    windows within the crypto-adapted killzones above, same logic.


================================================================================
9.2  POWER OF 3 (AMD)
================================================================================

The Power of 3 — Accumulation, Manipulation, Distribution — is
the heartbeat of ICT methodology. It describes how institutions
engineer price movement within EVERY session, EVERY day.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE THREE PHASES
────────────────────────────────────────

PHASE 1: ACCUMULATION
  What happens:
    Price consolidates in a tight range. Volume is moderate to low.
    This range builds liquidity on BOTH sides (stops above and below).
    Institutions are quietly building their position within this range.

  Detection from OHLC:
    First 4-8 candles of a Kill Zone:
    - Range is tight: (highest_high - lowest_low) < 1.5× ATR
    - No displacement candles
    - Volume is average or below average
    - Candles alternate between bullish and bearish (no clear direction)
    - Inside bars may form

  What the system records:
    Accumulation_High = max(high) of these initial candles
    Accumulation_Low  = min(low) of these initial candles
    Accumulation_Range = Accumulation_High - Accumulation_Low

PHASE 2: MANIPULATION (JUDAS SWING)
  What happens:
    Price breaks ONE SIDE of the accumulation range. This looks like
    a breakout. Retail traders enter in the breakout direction.
    Their entries + the triggered stops from the other side provide
    the liquidity institutions need.

    THIS IS A FALSE MOVE. The breakout will fail.

  Detection from OHLC:
    After accumulation range is established:
    - Price breaks above Accumulation_High OR below Accumulation_Low
    - The break may be 1-3 candles beyond the range
    - Volume may spike (stop triggers)
    - BUT: no strong follow-through (no sustained displacement
      in the breakout direction)
    - Key indicator: the body of the breaking candle(s) has
      significant wicks or the next candle reverses direction

  Direction prediction:
    The manipulation goes OPPOSITE to the real move:
    - If HTF bias is bullish → manipulation goes DOWN first
      (sweeps SSL below accumulation, then reverses UP)
    - If HTF bias is bearish → manipulation goes UP first
      (sweeps BSL above accumulation, then reverses DOWN)

    This is not guaranteed, but occurs in the majority of sessions.

PHASE 3: DISTRIBUTION
  What happens:
    After the manipulation sweep, price REVERSES with displacement
    in the true direction. This is the REAL move of the session.
    Institutions have filled their orders during manipulation and
    now allow price to move toward the target (Draw on Liquidity).

  Detection from OHLC:
    After manipulation:
    - Displacement candle(s) in the opposite direction of manipulation
      (range > 2× ATR, body > 70%, vol_ratio > 1.5)
    - BOS or CHoCH confirms the reversal
    - FVGs form in the distribution direction
    - OBs form at the reversal point
    - Volume is elevated and sustained

  This is where entries occur:
    The displacement creates OBs and FVGs in the true direction.
    Enter on the first pullback to these OBs/FVGs.
    Target: the liquidity on the opposite side of the accumulation
    range (and beyond).

────────────────────────────────────────
AMD DETECTION ALGORITHM
────────────────────────────────────────
Step 1: Identify Kill Zone start time from candle timestamp

Step 2: Collect first 4-8 candles of the KZ
  Calculate: range, average volume, average candle size

Step 3: Check for accumulation characteristics:
  If range < 1.5× ATR AND no displacement candle → ACCUMULATION

Step 4: Monitor for manipulation:
  Does price break above or below the accumulation range?
  If yes:
    Record: manipulation_direction (UP or DOWN)
    Record: manipulation_extreme (highest/lowest price reached)
    Count candles beyond the range

Step 5: Monitor for distribution:
  After manipulation (1-5 candles beyond):
  Does a displacement candle form in the OPPOSITE direction?
  If yes:
    → AMD confirmed
    → Distribution direction = opposite of manipulation
    → Entry opportunity at the OB/FVG formed by the displacement

Step 6: If no manipulation occurs by mid-KZ:
  The session may not follow classic AMD.
  Fall back to structure-based analysis (BOS/CHoCH + OB/FVG).

────────────────────────────────────────
BULLISH AMD EXAMPLE (from OHLC)
────────────────────────────────────────
London KZ starts at 02:00 NY:

  Candles 02:00-03:00 (4 × 15m candles):
    Range: $99,800 - $100,200 (tight, 400 points)
    No displacement. Average volume.
    → ACCUMULATION identified
    Acc_High = $100,200, Acc_Low = $99,800

  Candle 03:15 (manipulation):
    O:99820 H:99850 L:99550 C:99600
    Broke below Acc_Low ($99,800) → manipulation DOWN
    Volume spike (stops triggered below $99,800)
    Manipulation extreme = $99,550

  Candle 03:30 (distribution begins):
    O:99600 H:100500 L:99580 C:100450
    Displacement UP: range=920, body=850, body_pct=92%
    vol_ratio = 2.3
    → DISTRIBUTION confirmed, direction = UP
    This candle creates a Bullish OB at the manipulation low
    and likely an FVG

  Candles 03:45-05:00:
    Continuation upward. Price targets BSL above Acc_High.
    Eventually reaches $100,800 (BSL from prior session high)

  The trade:
    Entry: pullback to the OB/FVG created at 03:30 candle
    SL: below manipulation extreme ($99,550 - buffer)
    TP1: above Acc_High ($100,200 area) → likely 3:1+
    TP2: BSL at $100,800 (Draw on Liquidity)

================================================================================
9.3  JUDAS SWING
================================================================================

The Judas Swing IS the Manipulation phase of AMD. It is called
"Judas" because it betrays traders who follow it — it leads them
in the wrong direction before reversing.

⚠️ WHICH RANGE AND WINDOW TO USE: everything below refers to the
"Asian Low/High" and the "00:00-05:00 NY window" - this is CORRECT
AS WRITTEN for forex/gold/indices. For CRYPTO (BTC/ETH), substitute
mentally: "Asian Low/High" -> "Overnight Range low/high (00:00-08:30
NY)", and "00:00-05:00 NY window" -> "08:30-11:00 NY window" - see
section 9.1B for the full crypto adaptation and why. The DIRECTIONAL
LOGIC below (which side gets swept based on HTF bias, then reverses)
is 100% identical for both markets; only the clock and reference
range change.

────────────────────────────────────────
PREDICTING JUDAS SWING DIRECTION
────────────────────────────────────────
The system can anticipate the Judas Swing direction BEFORE it occurs
by using HTF bias:

  HTF Bias BULLISH (Daily/4H uptrend):
    → Judas Swing will likely go DOWN first
    → It will sweep the Asian Low or accumulation low
    → Then reverse UP (the true session direction)

  HTF Bias BEARISH (Daily/4H downtrend):
    → Judas Swing will likely go UP first
    → It will sweep the Asian High or accumulation high
    → Then reverse DOWN

  HTF Bias UNCLEAR:
    → Judas direction is unpredictable
    → Wait for the sweep and displacement to determine direction
    → This reduces to reactive AMD trading (not predictive)

────────────────────────────────────────
JUDAS AND ASIAN RANGE
────────────────────────────────────────
The most common Judas Swing scenario:

  Asian Range forms during 18:00-00:00 NY
  London opens at 02:00 NY

  Bullish day expected (Daily bias bullish):
    London initially pushes DOWN → sweeps Asian Low
    This triggers stops below Asian Low (SSL)
    Institutions fill long orders using this liquidity
    Then London reverses UP with displacement
    Price targets Asian High first, then higher levels

  Bearish day expected:
    London initially pushes UP → sweeps Asian High
    Triggers stops above Asian High (BSL)
    Institutions fill shorts
    London reverses DOWN
    Targets Asian Low first, then lower

Detection from OHLC:
  1. Know Asian H/L from previous section
  2. At London open, monitor:
     Does price take out Asian Low? (wick below or brief close below)
     If yes AND HTF bias is bullish:
       → Judas Swing DOWN confirmed
       → Expect reversal UP
       → Wait for displacement candle reversing upward
       → Enter on the displacement's OB/FVG

────────────────────────────────────────
JUDAS SWING FAILURE
────────────────────────────────────────
Sometimes what looks like a Judas Swing is actually the real move:
  Daily bias was bullish, London pushes down (expected Judas).
  But instead of reversing, price CONTINUES DOWN with sustained
  displacement and multiple BOS in the down direction.

This means: the Daily bias may be CHANGING (CHoCH developing),
or this was a deeper manipulation than expected.

How to tell the difference:
  Judas (will reverse):
    - Sweep is brief (1-3 candles below the level)
    - No sustained displacement in the sweep direction
    - Volume spikes on the sweep candle then drops
    - Price quickly reverses with displacement in opposite direction

  Real move (will continue):
    - Sustained movement beyond the level (5+ candles)
    - Displacement in the move direction (not the reversal direction)
    - Volume stays elevated
    - BOS in the move direction
    - No reversal displacement

  The system should NOT enter until displacement in the expected
  reversal direction is confirmed. If it does not come within
  5 candles of the sweep → the move may be genuine, not a Judas.
  Step aside and reassess.

================================================================================
9.4  MACRO TIMES
================================================================================

Within Kill Zones, ICT has identified specific times when the most
significant price movements tend to occur. These are called Macro
times — moments when institutional algorithms are known to execute.

────────────────────────────────────────
MACRO WINDOWS (NY TIME)
────────────────────────────────────────
  09:30 - 09:50 NY → NYSE Open macro (equities + correlated crypto)
  09:50 - 10:10 NY → Post-open macro (initial direction established)
  10:50 - 11:10 NY → Mid-morning macro (often a reversal point)
  11:50 - 12:10 NY → Pre-lunch macro (last good move before dead zone)
  13:10 - 13:40 NY → Post-lunch macro (NY PM begins)
  14:50 - 15:10 NY → Afternoon macro (often a reversal or acceleration)
  15:15 - 15:45 NY → Last hour macro (final institutional push)

────────────────────────────────────────
HOW THE SYSTEM USES MACRO TIMES
────────────────────────────────────────
Macro times are NOT entry signals. They are WINDOWS OF ATTENTION.

When a macro time approaches:
  1. Check: is price near a significant level (OB, FVG, liquidity)?
  2. If yes → heightened probability of a reaction AT that level
     DURING the macro window
  3. Watch for displacement/LTF confirmation during the macro
  4. If confirmation appears → enter

When a macro time passes without event:
  → The opportunity may come at the next macro window
  → Or the session may not have a clean setup today (HOLD)

Macro times provide TIMING CONTEXT, not trade signals.
They answer "WHEN might the move happen?" not "WHAT should I trade?"

────────────────────────────────────────
MACRO TIMES AND CRYPTO
────────────────────────────────────────
Crypto is influenced by macro times because:
  - Many crypto traders also trade equities
  - Institutional crypto desks operate during market hours
  - CME Bitcoin Futures follow NYSE hours
  - BTC correlates with equities during macro events

BTC/crypto-specific timing considerations:
  - 08:30 NY: US economic data releases → immediate BTC reaction
  - 09:30 NY: NYSE open → BTC often moves with/against equities
  - 16:00 NY: NYSE close → crypto-specific moves may begin
  - 00:00 UTC: Binance daily candle close → volume spike
  - 08:00 UTC: Binance futures funding timestamp → position adjustments

================================================================================
9.5  SILVER BULLET
================================================================================

The Silver Bullet is a specific ICT entry model tied to TIME windows.
It looks for a single condition: an FVG that forms with displacement
during a specific 1-hour window.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE THREE SILVER BULLET WINDOWS
────────────────────────────────────────

SILVER BULLET #1: 03:00 - 04:00 NY (London)
  Context: early London session, often during or after the Judas Swing
  Character: the strongest Silver Bullet because it catches the
    beginning of the London distribution phase
  The FVG formed here is typically:
    - Created by displacement after an Asian Range sweep
    - In the direction of the Daily Bias
    - Large and clean (London open provides strong displacement)
  Best timeframe for detection: 5m (15m may only show 1 candle
    in the window, not enough for FVG formation)
  Practical note: this SB requires fast recognition because London
    displacement moves quickly. The FVG may fill within 15-30 minutes.

SILVER BULLET #2: 10:00 - 11:00 NY (NY AM)
  Context: mid-morning NY, after the initial open volatility settles
  Character: the most PRACTICAL Silver Bullet for the system because:
    - Larger FVGs (NY AM has the highest volume)
    - More time for the FVG to be recognized before it fills
    - Follows the NY open manipulation phase
    - The "second chance" entry for the NY AM move
  Best timeframe for detection: 15m (larger FVGs visible on 15m)
  This is the recommended default Silver Bullet for the system.

SILVER BULLET #3: 14:00 - 15:00 NY (NY PM)
  Context: early NY PM session
  Character: the weakest Silver Bullet but still valid
    - Often a re-entry for the AM direction
    - Or a reversal setup if AM target was reached
    - Smaller moves typically (PM has less energy than AM)
  Best timeframe for detection: 15m
  Trade with caution: reduce position size to 75%.

────────────────────────────────────────
SILVER BULLET ENTRY CRITERIA
────────────────────────────────────────
ALL must be met:

  1. Current time is within one of the 3 SB windows
  2. A displacement candle forms during the window:
     range > 2× ATR, body_pct > 70%, vol_ratio > 1.5
  3. The displacement creates a FVG
  4. The FVG direction aligns with HTF bias (Daily Bias)
  5. The displacement is IN the trade direction
     (bullish displacement for long, bearish for short)

Entry:
  At FVG CE (Consequent Encroachment — midpoint)
  This is a limit order placed at CE, waiting for price to return
  and fill the FVG.

SL:
  Below/above the FVG extreme (with buffer)

TP1:
  Nearest valid unswept structural level / PD Array; report actual R:R

TP2:
  Draw on Liquidity / HTF target

────────────────────────────────────────
SILVER BULLET DETECTION FROM OHLC
────────────────────────────────────────
Step 1: Convert candle timestamp to NY time
Step 2: Check if candle falls within a SB window
Step 3: Check if candle meets displacement criteria
Step 4: Check if an FVG was created by this candle:
  (candle[i-1].high < candle[i+1].low for bullish, or inverse)
Step 5: Check alignment with Daily Bias
Step 6: If all pass → Silver Bullet setup detected

================================================================================
9.6  TRUE DAY
================================================================================

The ICT True Day begins at 00:00 New York Time (midnight Eastern).
This is different from the UTC day (which starts 4-5 hours earlier
depending on DST).

────────────────────────────────────────
OPENING PRICE
────────────────────────────────────────
True Day Opening Price = the OPEN of the first candle at or after
00:00 NY time.

For the system: convert each candle's timestamp to NY time.
The first candle where NY_hour = 0 and NY_minute = 0 (or the
closest candle after midnight NY) → its open = True Day Opening Price.

────────────────────────────────────────
USING TRUE DAY OPENING PRICE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Price currently ABOVE the True Day Open:
    → Intraday bullish bias
    → The day is "net positive" so far
    → Supports long setups

  Price currently BELOW the True Day Open:
    → Intraday bearish bias
    → The day is "net negative" so far
    → Supports short setups

  Price oscillating around the True Day Open:
    → Indecision day
    → Lower confidence on any setup

This is a SUPPORTING indicator, not a primary one.
  True Day Open agrees with Daily Bias → +3% confidence
  True Day Open disagrees with Daily Bias → -3% confidence
    (price is above opening but Daily bias is bearish = caution)

================================================================================
9.7  DAY OF WEEK PROFILES
================================================================================

Different days of the week have statistically different characters
based on institutional scheduling and market rhythms.

────────────────────────────────────────
MONDAY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character: establishment day. Institutions set the week's direction.
  Often a continuation of the previous week's momentum.
  Can be slow as institutions assess weekend developments.
  "Monday sets the range for the week."

For crypto: Monday often reverses weekend moves (weekend moves
  were low-volume and often manipulated).

Confidence adjustment: +0% (neutral)
Best approach: observe and prepare. Monday setups are valid but
  may lack the follow-through of mid-week setups.

────────────────────────────────────────
TUESDAY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character: the BEST trading day of the week (statistically).
  Monday established the direction. Tuesday commits to it.
  Highest follow-through on setups.
  London and NY KZ are both typically strong.
  "If you only trade one day a week, make it Tuesday."

Confidence adjustment: +5%

────────────────────────────────────────
WEDNESDAY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character: continuation day OR mid-week reversal.
  If Tuesday's move was strong → Wednesday often continues
  If Tuesday's move was weak → Wednesday may reverse
  FOMC and other major events often fall on Wednesdays
  High-impact news creates additional volatility

Confidence adjustment: +5% (same as Tuesday)
Note: check economic calendar. FOMC days are special (high
  volatility but unpredictable direction pre-announcement).

────────────────────────────────────────
THURSDAY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character: extension day.
  The week's move often reaches its peak on Thursday.
  Weekly targets (PWH/PWL sweeps) frequently complete on Thursday.
  Less reliable for NEW setups than Tue/Wed.

Confidence adjustment: +3%

────────────────────────────────────────
FRIDAY
────────────────────────────────────────
Character: profit-taking and position squaring.
  Institutional traders close weekly positions.
  Counter-trend moves are common (profit-taking creates reversals).
  After 12:00 NY → the week is effectively over for Smart Money.
  "Don't start new trades on Friday afternoon."

Confidence adjustment: -5%
  After 12:00 NY Friday: additional -5% (total -10%)
  New entries after 14:00 NY Friday: NOT RECOMMENDED

────────────────────────────────────────
SATURDAY - SUNDAY
────────────────────────────────────────
For crypto markets:
  Volume drops 40-70%
  Moves are unreliable
  Weekend "breakouts" frequently reverse Monday
  Smart Money is not active

Confidence adjustment: -15%
Recommendation: analysis only, no execution.

================================================================================
9.8  TIME-BASED CONFIDENCE ADJUSTMENTS (SUMMARY)
================================================================================

These adjustments apply AUTOMATICALLY to the confidence score:

SESSION TIMING:
  London KZ (02:00-05:00 NY):         +5%
  NY AM KZ (08:30-11:00 NY):          +8%
  London + NY overlap (08:30-10:00):  +10%
  NY PM KZ (13:30-16:00 NY):          +3%
  Pre-NY (05:00-08:30):               -3%
  NY Lunch (12:00-13:30 NY):          -10%
  Asian Session (18:00-02:00 NY):     -10%
  Dead Zone (16:00-18:00 NY):         -15%

DAY OF WEEK:
  Tuesday:                             +5%
  Wednesday:                           +5%
  Thursday:                            +3%
  Monday:                              +0%
  Friday (before 12:00 NY):           -5%
  Friday (after 12:00 NY):            -10%
  Saturday-Sunday:                     -15%

SPECIAL:
  During Silver Bullet window:         +3%
  During Macro Time window:            +2%
  First 3 days of new quarter:         -5%
  FOMC/major news within 1 hour:       -10%

These adjustments are CUMULATIVE but capped:
  Maximum positive time adjustment: +15%
  Maximum negative time adjustment: -25%

Example: Tuesday NY AM KZ during Silver Bullet #2 window:
  +8% (NY AM) + 5% (Tuesday) + 3% (Silver Bullet) = +16% → capped at +15%

Example: Saturday Asian session:
  -10% (Asian) + (-15%) (Weekend) = -25% → at cap

================================================================================
9.9  TIME DETECTION FROM CANDLE DATA
================================================================================

The system receives candle timestamps in UTC milliseconds. The
conversion pipeline:

Step 1: UTC datetime from timestamp
  utc_dt = timestamp_ms / 1000 → Unix seconds → datetime UTC

Step 2: Determine DST status
  if date falls between:
    Second Sunday of March 02:00 local →
    First Sunday of November 02:00 local:
      offset = -4 hours (EDT)
  else:
      offset = -5 hours (EST)

Step 3: NY datetime
  ny_dt = utc_dt + offset

Step 4: Extract components
  ny_hour = ny_dt.hour (0-23)
  ny_minute = ny_dt.minute
  ny_weekday = ny_dt.weekday() (0=Monday, 6=Sunday)

Step 5: Determine active session
  Based on ny_hour and ny_minute:
    Map to the session windows defined in section 9.1

Step 6: Determine Kill Zone
  Is the current candle within London KZ, NY AM KZ, NY PM KZ,
  or a dead zone?

Step 7: Check Silver Bullet windows
  Is the current candle within 03:00-04:00, 10:00-11:00,
  or 14:00-15:00 NY?

Step 8: Check Macro Time windows
  Is the current candle within any of the Macro windows
  defined in section 9.4?

Step 9: Calculate confidence adjustments
  Sum all applicable adjustments from section 9.8
  Apply caps (+15% max, -25% min)

This pipeline runs for EVERY candle analyzed, ensuring that
time context is always current and accurate.

================================================================================
9.10  COMMON TIME-BASED MISTAKES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: TRADING DURING NY LUNCH
────────────────────────────────────────
"But the setup looks perfect!" — No. Even a perfect-looking setup
during 12:00-13:30 NY lacks institutional backing. Without
institutions, there is no displacement, no follow-through, and
no reliable price delivery. The "setup" is a mirage.

Fix: Hard rule. No entries 12:00-13:30 NY. No exceptions.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: IGNORING THE JUDAS SWING
────────────────────────────────────────
London opens and pushes down. Trader sees bearish momentum and
shorts. But the down move was the Judas Swing — it reverses UP
with displacement after sweeping the Asian Low. The short gets
destroyed.

Fix: At London open, do NOT trade the INITIAL move. Wait for the
sweep of Asian H/L, THEN wait for displacement in the opposite
direction. The real trade is AFTER the Judas, not during it.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: SAME STRATEGY ALL HOURS
────────────────────────────────────────
Applying the same confidence and position sizing at 03:00 NY
(London KZ, institutions active) and at 22:00 NY (dead zone,
nobody home). The statistical profiles are completely different.

Fix: Use the time-based confidence adjustments. They exist because
probability profiles genuinely differ by session.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: TRADING EVERY KILL ZONE
────────────────────────────────────────
London KZ gives a signal. Trader enters. NY AM KZ gives another
signal. Trader enters again. NY PM gives another. Three trades
in one day, each eating risk budget.

Fix: Typically 1-2 entries per day is optimal. The BEST entry of
the day is usually during NY AM KZ. If London provided a good
entry, NY AM may be a continuation (manage existing trade, not
a new entry). Quality over quantity.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: WEEKEND CRYPTO TRADING
────────────────────────────────────────
"Crypto is 24/7, weekends are fine." Technically true. Practically
false. Weekend crypto moves are 60-70% lower volume, frequently
manipulated, and often reversed on Monday. Weekend "breakdowns"
and "breakouts" are traps more often than genuine moves.

Fix: Confidence -15% on weekends. Ideally: no execution Saturday-Sunday.

================================================================================
END OF [ICT_TIME_AND_SESSIONS]
================================================================================




================================================================================
[ICT_MARKET_MAKER_MODEL]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
The Market Maker Model (MMM) is the macro framework that ties every
ICT concept together. While individual concepts (OB, FVG, liquidity,
structure) are tools, the MMM is the BLUEPRINT that shows how these
tools fit into a complete institutional cycle.

The MMM describes how institutions move price through a predictable
sequence of phases to accumulate positions, deliver price to targets,
and distribute positions. This cycle repeats on every timeframe — from
a Weekly cycle lasting months to a 15m cycle lasting hours. The same
logic, the same phases, the same institutional behavior.

Understanding the MMM allows the system to answer the most valuable
question in trading: "Which PHASE are we currently in?" Because each
phase has specific characteristics, specific opportunities, and specific
dangers:
  - Phase 1: no trade (building)
  - Phase 2: high-risk reversal entry (early, aggressive)
  - Phase 3: no new entry (already moving, too late for safe entry)
  - Phase 4: THE best entry (pullback after confirmed direction)
  - Phase 5: no trade (exit time)

Knowing the phase = knowing what to do.

This section also integrates Wyckoff methodology, which describes the
same institutional cycle with different terminology. ICT's MMM is
essentially a modernized, price-action-focused version of Wyckoff's
accumulation-distribution theory.

This 3-phase skeleton (Accumulation -> Manipulation/Range Expansion ->
Distribution) is what ICT's official material calls "Power of Three"
(PO3, sometimes referenced as OHLC/OLHC daily range behavior) - the
same concept as the 5-phase MMM above, just at its simplest 3-stage
framing. If the name "Power of Three" or "PO3" appears in any
instruction or context, treat it as referring to this exact MMM cycle -
there is no separate methodology to apply.

================================================================================
10.1  THE BUY MODEL (BULLISH CYCLE)
================================================================================

The Buy Model describes how institutions engineer price from bearish
to bullish — how they accumulate a long position and then deliver
price upward to distribute it.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 1: ORIGINAL CONSOLIDATION
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff equivalent: Preliminary Support (PS), Selling Climax (SC),
  Automatic Rally (AR), Secondary Test (ST)

What institutions are doing:
  Building the first pieces of their long position quietly.
  They cannot buy all at once (would spike price). They absorb
  selling gradually during a range-bound period.

What it looks like in OHLC data:
  - Price is in a RANGE after a decline
  - Multiple swing highs at similar prices (EQH forming → BSL)
  - Multiple swing lows at similar prices (EQL forming → SSL)
  - Volume is declining overall (selling pressure exhausting)
  - Candle ranges are getting SMALLER (compression)
  - No displacement candles
  - Inside bars may form
  - ATR is decreasing

Detection criteria:
  On the analysis timeframe:
    trend_direction = "RANGING" or "BEARISH_WEAKENING"
    Last 10-20 candles show:
      range_ratio < 0.8 for majority of candles (small ranges)
      vol_ratio trending downward
      No candle meets displacement criteria
      Swing highs within 0.5% of each other (EQH)
      Swing lows within 0.5% of each other (EQL)

What the system should do:
  - Identify the consolidation range (high and low)
  - Map the liquidity building on both sides
  - Note: "Phase 1 detected — consolidation forming"
  - DO NOT TRADE. There is no edge during accumulation.
    Price could break either way. Wait for Phase 2.

Duration:
  Weekly: weeks to months
  Daily: days to weeks
  4H: 1-3 days
  1H: 4-12 hours
  15m: 1-4 hours

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 2: SMART MONEY REVERSAL (THE SPRING)
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff equivalent: Spring, Terminal Shakeout, Last Point of Support

This is the MOST important phase. It is where the manipulation occurs
and where the most aggressive (and profitable) entries exist — but
also the riskiest.

What institutions are doing:
  Engineering price to sweep the SSL below the consolidation.
  This accomplishes three things simultaneously:
    1. Triggers stop losses of traders who bought during the range
       (their stops are below the range lows → sell orders → liquidity)
    2. Attracts breakout short sellers who see "support broken"
       (their sell entries → more liquidity for institutions to buy)
    3. Allows institutions to accumulate their remaining long position
       at the LOWEST possible price (after the sweep drops price)

What it looks like in OHLC data:
  - Price BREAKS BELOW the consolidation range low
  - The break may be:
    Quick (1-3 candles): a clean sweep → fastest reversal
    Extended (3-8 candles): a raid → slower reversal
    Deep (8+ candles): a purge → difficult to trade
  - Volume SPIKES on the break (stop triggers + breakout entries)
  - Then: DISPLACEMENT in the opposite direction (UP)
    Large bullish candle(s): range > 2× ATR, body > 70%
    This displacement is the evidence that institutions absorbed
    the selling and are now letting price rise
  - CHoCH occurs: price breaks above a recent LH within or near
    the consolidation range
  - This CHoCH is the FIRST structural signal of the reversal

Detection criteria:
  prerequisite: Phase 1 consolidation identified
  then:
    Price breaks below consolidation low (SSL swept)
    Within 1-8 candles: displacement UP occurs
    CHoCH on the analysis TF or one TF lower
    Volume pattern: spike on break → decrease → spike on reversal

What the system should do:
  - Record: "Phase 2 detected — Smart Money Reversal (Spring)"
  - If entering here: THIS IS MODEL F (CHoCH Reversal Entry)
    → Highest R:R possible (entry near the absolute low)
    → Highest RISK (the CHoCH may fail, this could be a Run)
    → Requires: displacement + CHoCH + HTF support
    → Position: 50% size (due to uncertainty)
  - If NOT entering here: wait for Phase 3 confirmation,
    then enter during Phase 4 (safer)

CRITICAL WARNING:
  Not every break of the consolidation low is a Spring.
  Sometimes it is a genuine breakdown (the beginning of a new
  downtrend). The DISPLACEMENT reversal is what confirms the Spring.
  Without displacement reversal → this is NOT Phase 2 → it may be
  a continuation breakdown.

  Do not anticipate the Spring. React to the evidence:
    Break below → displacement up → CHoCH → CONFIRMED Spring
    Break below → continued selling → more BOS down → NOT a Spring

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 3: SMART MONEY RALLY (MARKUP)
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff equivalent: Sign of Strength (SOS), Jump Across the Creek,
  Markup Phase

What institutions are doing:
  They have accumulated their full position. Now they LET price rise.
  They are not actively buying anymore — they already bought during
  Phases 1 and 2. They simply stop absorbing selling, and the
  natural demand imbalance pushes price up.

What it looks like in OHLC data:
  - BOS (bullish) — price breaks above a significant swing high
    from the consolidation or from the Phase 2 reversal
  - Series of HH + HL (bullish structure confirmed)
  - Displacement candles appear on impulse legs
  - FVGs form in the upward direction
  - OBs form on each impulse (Propulsion Blocks)
  - Volume is higher on impulse legs, lower on pullback legs
  - Momentum: each impulse leg covers more distance than the
    pullback that follows

Detection criteria:
  prerequisite: Phase 2 reversal detected
  then:
    BOS above the consolidation high (or Phase 2 high)
    Confirmed bullish structure: 2+ HH and 2+ HL
    Displacement present on impulse legs
    FVGs and OBs forming

What the system should do:
  - Record: "Phase 3 confirmed — Smart Money Rally (Markup)"
  - DO NOT enter new positions during Phase 3 impulse legs
    (price is already moving, chasing is dangerous)
  - WAIT for the Phase 4 pullback
  - The OBs and FVGs created during Phase 3 are the entry zones
    for Phase 4

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 4: RE-ACCUMULATION
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff equivalent: Back-Up to the Edge of the Creek,
  Last Point of Support (LPS)

This is THE BEST AND SAFEST entry phase.

What institutions are doing:
  After the initial rally (Phase 3), price pulls back. This is NOT
  institutions selling — it is:
    - Retail profit-taking (early buyers selling for quick gains)
    - New short sellers entering (thinking the rally is over)
    - Institutions adding to their position at lower prices
    The pullback provides institutional buyers with more liquidity
    to increase their position size before the next leg up.

What it looks like in OHLC data:
  - Pullback AGAINST the Phase 3 rally
  - Price drops toward the OB and/or FVG created during Phase 3
  - The pullback does NOT break the Phase 3 structure
    (the last HL holds — if it breaks, Phase 3 may have ended)
  - Volume DECREASES on the pullback (healthy — not aggressive selling)
  - Candle sizes decrease (less volatility)
  - Price enters the OTE zone (62-79% retracement of the Phase 3 leg)
  - Then: LTF CHoCH/BOS in the bullish direction (resuming the rally)
  - Displacement UP from the OB/FVG zone

Detection criteria:
  prerequisite: Phase 3 markup in progress
  then:
    Pullback: price retraces toward Phase 3 OB/FVG
    Pullback characteristics:
      vol_ratio < 1.0 on pullback candles (low volume)
      candle ranges < ATR (small candles)
      No displacement in the pullback direction
    Price reaches OTE zone AND/OR touches Phase 3 OB/FVG
    LTF shows: CHoCH bullish → BOS bullish → confirmation
    This is Entry Model A (OTE + OB) or Model C (BOS Pullback)

What the system should do:
  - Record: "Phase 4 detected — Re-Accumulation pullback"
  - THIS IS THE PRIMARY ENTRY OPPORTUNITY
  - Apply Entry Model A, B, or C:
    Model A: OTE + OB (if price reaches OTE zone at an OB)
    Model B: if a liquidity sweep occurs during the pullback
             (sweeps a minor swing low then reverses)
    Model C: BOS pullback (price pulls back to OB/FVG after BOS)
  - Full position size (this is the highest-confidence phase)
  - SL: below the Phase 3 structural HL
  - TP1: above Phase 3 high (next BSL)
  - TP2: the major Draw on Liquidity above

WHY PHASE 4 IS THE BEST ENTRY:
  1. Direction is CONFIRMED (Phase 3 proved the bullish thesis)
  2. Entry is at DISCOUNT (pullback to OB/FVG in OTE zone)
  3. Volume profile SUPPORTS the trade (low vol on pullback = healthy)
  4. Structure is INTACT (Phase 3 HL holds = bullish)
  5. Risk is DEFINED (SL below the HL = clear invalidation)
  6. R:R is FAVORABLE (entry at pullback, target at/above Phase 3 high)

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 5: DISTRIBUTION (EXIT)
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff equivalent: Preliminary Supply (PSY), Buying Climax (BC),
  Automatic Reaction (AR), Distribution Phase

What institutions are doing:
  They accumulated in Phases 1-2 and added in Phase 4. Now they are
  SELLING (distributing) their position to retail buyers who are
  buying the "strong uptrend" at the TOP.

What it looks like in OHLC data:
  - Price reaches a major BSL target (HTF liquidity pool)
  - BSL is swept (or multiple BSL levels are taken)
  - Volume CLIMAX: extremely high volume on the final push up
    (highest volume of the entire cycle — retail FOMO buying)
  - Exhaustion candles: large range but large wicks (body < 50%)
    High volume but rejection at the highs
  - Structure begins to weaken:
    HH barely exceeds previous HH (weak momentum)
    Subsequent pullbacks are DEEPER than earlier pullbacks
  - Eventually: CHoCH occurs (price breaks below the last HL)
  - This CHoCH is the first sign that distribution is complete

Detection criteria:
  Signs of Phase 5 developing:
    Price has been in Phase 3/4 cycle for extended period
    Reaches major BSL (HTF swing high, EQH, PWH)
    Exhaustion characteristics appear:
      Candle with range > 2× ATR BUT body_pct < 50% (wick > body)
      vol_ratio > 2.5 (climactic volume)
      buy_sell_ratio starts DROPPING despite price rising
        (taker sells increasing = institutions distributing)
    Structure weakening:
      Last HH barely exceeded previous HH (< 0.3% difference)
      Pullback after last HH is deeper than pullback after previous HH
    CHoCH: price breaks below last HL

What the system should do:
  - Record: "Phase 5 warning — potential distribution"
  - DO NOT enter new long positions
  - If currently in a long: TIGHTEN trailing stop aggressively
  - If trailing stop hits → exit and do not re-enter long
  - The cycle is ending. The next cycle may be:
    a) Another consolidation (Phase 1 at a higher level)
    b) A bearish cycle beginning (Sell Model Phase 2)

================================================================================
10.2  THE SELL MODEL (BEARISH CYCLE)
================================================================================

The Sell Model is the exact mirror of the Buy Model. Institutions
accumulate a SHORT position during consolidation, manipulate price
UP (Spring up = UTAD), then distribute downward.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 1: ORIGINAL CONSOLIDATION
────────────────────────────────────────
Same as Buy Model Phase 1 but after a RALLY (not a decline).
Price ranges at highs. Institutions are quietly selling.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 2: SMART MONEY REVERSAL (UTAD)
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff equivalent: Upthrust After Distribution (UTAD)

Price BREAKS ABOVE the consolidation high (BSL swept).
  - Triggers buy stops above the range → liquidity for institutions to sell
  - Attracts breakout long traders → more buy orders for institutions
  - Institutions sell INTO all this buying at premium prices

Then: DISPLACEMENT DOWN
  - Large bearish candle(s) confirm the Spring was a UTAD
  - CHoCH: price breaks below a recent HL within the consolidation

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 3: SMART MONEY DECLINE (MARKDOWN)
────────────────────────────────────────
BOS bearish. Series of LH + LL.
Displacement on impulse legs down.
FVGs and OBs form in the downward direction.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 4: RE-DISTRIBUTION
────────────────────────────────────────
THE BEST SHORT ENTRY.
Price rallies against the decline → approaches Phase 3 bearish OB/FVG.
Volume low on the rally (healthy corrective move, not genuine buying).
OTE zone for shorts = 62-79% retracement upward.
LTF confirms: CHoCH bearish → BOS bearish → enter short.

Entry Models A, B, C apply in reverse.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 5: ACCUMULATION (EXIT)
────────────────────────────────────────
Price reaches major SSL targets. Volume climax on the final drop.
Exhaustion candles at the lows. Structure weakens.
CHoCH bullish → distribution of shorts is complete.
Exit short positions. Cycle may restart as bullish.

================================================================================
10.3  PHASE IDENTIFICATION FROM OHLC DATA
================================================================================

The system must determine the current phase on each timeframe.
This is done by evaluating multiple signals simultaneously.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 1 INDICATORS (CONSOLIDATION)
────────────────────────────────────────
  □ Price is ranging (no clear HH/HL or LH/LL)
  □ ATR is declining (volatility compressing)
  □ Volume trending downward
  □ Candle ranges < 1× ATR on average
  □ EQH and/or EQL forming (range boundaries becoming clear)
  □ No displacement candles in recent history
  □ Inside bars appearing

  If 4+ of these conditions are present → likely Phase 1
  Confidence in Phase 1 assessment: moderate to high

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 2 INDICATORS (REVERSAL / SPRING / UTAD)
────────────────────────────────────────
  □ Phase 1 consolidation existed recently
  □ Price just broke below/above the consolidation boundary
  □ Volume spiked on the break (stops triggered)
  □ Displacement in the OPPOSITE direction followed within 1-8 candles
  □ CHoCH occurred in the reversal direction
  □ The break was a sweep (wick beyond, body returning) not a run

  If all core conditions met → Phase 2 confirmed
  If break occurred but no displacement yet → Phase 2 PENDING
    (do not trade until displacement confirms)

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 3 INDICATORS (MARKUP / MARKDOWN)
────────────────────────────────────────
  □ BOS confirmed in the reversal direction
  □ Confirmed trend: 2+ HH/HL (bull) or 2+ LH/LL (bear)
  □ Displacement candles on impulse legs
  □ FVGs forming in the trend direction
  □ OBs forming (Propulsion Blocks after the Phase 2 CHoCH)
  □ Volume higher on impulse legs than pullback legs

  If all present → Phase 3 confirmed
  This means: the trend is active, wait for Phase 4 pullback

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 4 INDICATORS (RE-ACCUMULATION / RE-DISTRIBUTION)
────────────────────────────────────────
  □ Phase 3 trend is in progress
  □ Price is currently pulling back against the Phase 3 direction
  □ Pullback volume is LOW (vol_ratio < 1.0)
  □ Pullback candles are SMALL (range < ATR)
  □ No displacement in the pullback direction
  □ Price is approaching Phase 3 OB/FVG
  □ Price is entering OTE zone (62-79% retracement)
  □ Phase 3 structural swing (HL for bull, LH for bear) is INTACT

  If 5+ conditions met → Phase 4 in progress
  THIS IS THE ENTRY PHASE → apply Entry Models

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 5 INDICATORS (DISTRIBUTION / ACCUMULATION EXIT)
────────────────────────────────────────
  □ Price has been trending for extended period (Phases 3+4 repeated)
  □ Price reaches a MAJOR HTF liquidity target
  □ Volume climax (vol_ratio > 2.5 on extreme candles)
  □ Exhaustion candles (range > 2× ATR, body < 50%, high volume)
  □ buy_sell_ratio diverging from price:
    Price making new highs but buy_sell_ratio < 0.5 = bearish divergence
    Price making new lows but buy_sell_ratio > 0.6 = bullish divergence
  □ Structure weakening:
    New extreme barely exceeds previous (< 0.3%)
    Pullbacks getting deeper
  □ CHoCH occurs against the trend

  Early warning (3+ indicators) → "Phase 5 DEVELOPING — tighten management"
  Confirmed (CHoCH + climax + exhaustion) → "Phase 5 CONFIRMED — exit"

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE ASSESSMENT OUTPUT
────────────────────────────────────────
For each analyzed timeframe, the system outputs:

  {
    timeframe: "4H"
    current_phase: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5
    model_type: "BUY_MODEL" | "SELL_MODEL" | "UNDETERMINED"
    phase_confidence: percentage
    phase_details: {
      // Phase-specific data
      // Phase 1: consolidation range, EQH/EQL levels
      // Phase 2: sweep direction, displacement candle, CHoCH level
      // Phase 3: BOS levels, trend strength, OBs/FVGs created
      // Phase 4: pullback depth, OB/FVG being approached, OTE status
      // Phase 5: exhaustion candles, divergence, CHoCH level
    }
    recommended_action: "WAIT" | "AGGRESSIVE_ENTRY" | "NO_NEW_ENTRY" |
                        "PRIMARY_ENTRY" | "EXIT_AND_REVERSE"
  }

================================================================================
10.4  WYCKOFF ↔ ICT TRANSLATION
================================================================================

The system should understand both terminologies because historical
trading literature uses Wyckoff terms while ICT content uses ICT terms.
The underlying concepts are identical.

────────────────────────────────────────
BUY MODEL (ACCUMULATION → MARKUP)
────────────────────────────────────────
Wyckoff Term          │  ICT Term               │  What It Is
──────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────
Preliminary Support   │  Phase 1 early          │  First signs of buying
 (PS)                 │   consolidation         │   in a decline
                      │                         │
Selling Climax (SC)   │  SSL sweep with         │  Aggressive final drop
                      │   displacement down     │   on high volume
                      │                         │
Automatic Rally (AR)  │  Initial displacement   │  Bounce after climax
                      │   up from SC            │   (supply exhausted)
                      │                         │
Secondary Test (ST)   │  Retest of              │  Returns to SC area,
                      │   consolidation low     │   holds above it
                      │                         │
Phase B               │  Phase 1                │  Extended consolidation
 (Building Cause)     │   (consolidation)       │   range building
                      │                         │
Spring               │  Phase 2                │  Breaks below range,
                      │   (SSL Sweep)           │   sweeps stops, reverses
                      │                         │
Test of Spring        │  Retest of Phase 2      │  Returns to spring low,
                      │   sweep zone            │   holds (confirms)
                      │                         │
Sign of Strength      │  Phase 3 BOS            │  Breaks above range high
 (SOS)                │                         │   with displacement
                      │                         │
Last Point of         │  Phase 4                │  Pullback to OB/FVG
 Support (LPS)        │   (Re-Accumulation)     │   after SOS
                      │                         │
Markup                │  Phase 3 continuation   │  Sustained uptrend
                      │   + Phase 4 repeats     │
                      │                         │
Preliminary Supply    │  Phase 5 early          │  First signs of selling
 (PSY)                │   warning               │   at highs
                      │                         │
Buying Climax (BC)    │  BSL sweep with         │  Aggressive final push
                      │   exhaustion candle     │   up on climax volume
                      │                         │
Distribution          │  Phase 5                │  Institutions sell to
                      │   (Distribution)        │   retail at the top

────────────────────────────────────────
SELL MODEL (DISTRIBUTION → MARKDOWN)
────────────────────────────────────────
The mirror image. Key translation:

  UTAD (Upthrust After Distribution) = BSL Sweep in Phase 2 (Sell Model)
  Sign of Weakness (SOW) = Bearish BOS in Phase 3
  Last Point of Supply (LPSY) = Phase 4 Re-Distribution pullback
  Markdown = Phase 3 bearish continuation

================================================================================
10.5  VOLUME ANALYSIS PER PHASE
================================================================================

Volume is one of the most reliable indicators of which phase the
market is in. Each phase has a characteristic volume profile.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 1 VOLUME PROFILE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Overall: declining
  Pattern: volume decreases over the consolidation period
  Why: selling pressure (in a Buy Model) or buying pressure (in a
    Sell Model) is gradually exhausting
  buy_sell_ratio: oscillates around 0.5 (balanced)
  num_trades: average to below average

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 2 VOLUME PROFILE
────────────────────────────────────────
  At the sweep: SPIKE (climactic)
    vol_ratio > 2.0 on the break candle
    num_trades spikes (many stops triggering)
    buy_sell_ratio < 0.4 on the sweep candle (for Buy Model — heavy selling)

  During displacement reversal: volume STAYS elevated
    vol_ratio > 1.5 on the reversal candle
    buy_sell_ratio FLIPS to > 0.55 (buyers now dominating)
    num_trades remains high

  Key: volume spike on the sweep followed by sustained volume on
  the reversal = institutions actively participating in both events.
  Volume spike on sweep followed by volume DROP on reversal =
  possible that the sweep is genuine (Run), not a Spring.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 3 VOLUME PROFILE
────────────────────────────────────────
  On impulse legs: elevated (vol_ratio > 1.2)
  On pullback legs: declining (vol_ratio < 1.0)

  This divergence is HEALTHY:
    High volume on impulse = institutions supporting the move
    Low volume on pullback = the pullback is corrective, not institutional

  buy_sell_ratio on impulse: > 0.55 (Buy Model) or < 0.45 (Sell Model)
  buy_sell_ratio on pullback: closer to 0.5 (balanced, no aggression)

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 4 VOLUME PROFILE
────────────────────────────────────────
  On the pullback: LOW volume (the most reliable Phase 4 indicator)
    vol_ratio < 0.8 on pullback candles
    num_trades below average
    buy_sell_ratio may slightly favor the pullback direction
      (but not aggressively — this is retail, not institutional selling)

  At the reversal from pullback: volume INCREASES
    vol_ratio > 1.2 on the first bullish displacement candle
    buy_sell_ratio flips back to trend direction
    This confirms: institutions are stepping back in at this level

  LOW VOLUME PULLBACK → VOLUME INCREASE ON REVERSAL = Phase 4 confirmed

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 5 VOLUME PROFILE
────────────────────────────────────────
  CLIMACTIC: the highest volume of the entire cycle
    vol_ratio > 2.5 on extreme candles
    This is retail FOMO (buying at top or panic selling at bottom)

  Divergence: volume is extreme but price barely makes new extremes
    Volume making "new highs" while price barely exceeds previous high
    → This is exhaustion (supply being absorbed by institutions
       who are selling INTO the buying)

  buy_sell_ratio DIVERGES from price:
    Buy Model Phase 5: price at highs but buy_sell_ratio < 0.45
    (institutions are net sellers despite price going up)
    This is the most reliable early warning of Phase 5.

  num_trades: extremely high → institutions executing rapidly
    to distribute their position before the reversal

================================================================================
10.6  MMM ON MULTIPLE TIMEFRAMES
================================================================================

The MMM operates on EVERY timeframe simultaneously. A Phase 4 pullback
on the Daily might contain a complete Phase 1-5 cycle on the 1H.

────────────────────────────────────────
FRACTAL NESTING
────────────────────────────────────────
Example:
  Weekly: Phase 3 Markup (bullish trend confirmed)
  Daily: Phase 4 Re-Accumulation (pullback within Weekly markup)
  4H: Phase 2 Spring (sweep of Daily pullback low → reversal up)
  1H: Phase 3 Rally (impulse up after 4H Spring)
  15m: Phase 4 Re-Accumulation (pullback within 1H rally)
    → THIS IS WHERE THE ENTRY IS

The system should identify:
  HTF Phase (Daily/Weekly): determines the BIAS and the macro position
  MTF Phase (4H): determines the current structural context
  LTF Phase (1H/15m): determines the entry timing

IDEAL ALIGNMENT FOR ENTRIES:
  HTF in Phase 3 or 4 (trend confirmed, pullback or continuation)
  MTF in Phase 4 (pullback to key zone)
  LTF in Phase 2→3 transition (Spring/CHoCH confirmed, BOS emerging)
  → Enter at LTF Phase 4 (pullback to LTF OB/FVG)

  This multi-timeframe alignment is the HIGHEST PROBABILITY setup.

────────────────────────────────────────
CONFLICTING PHASES
────────────────────────────────────────
When timeframes show conflicting phases:

  Daily Phase 3 (bullish markup) + 4H Phase 5 (distribution signals):
    This may mean:
    a) The Daily trend is ending (4H Phase 5 is the leading indicator)
    b) The 4H Phase 5 is just a larger pullback within the Daily trend
    Resolution: check Weekly. If Weekly is Phase 3 bullish → Daily
    trend likely continues → 4H "Phase 5" is just a deep pullback.

  Rule: HIGHER TF PHASE OVERRIDES LOWER TF PHASE for bias.
  Lower TF phases provide entry timing within the HTF framework.

================================================================================
10.7  MMM AND ENTRY MODEL MAPPING
================================================================================

Each phase maps to specific Entry Models:

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE → ENTRY MODEL MAPPING
────────────────────────────────────────

Phase 1 (Consolidation):
  → NO ENTRY. Wait.
  → Prepare: identify the range, map liquidity, determine HTF bias
    to predict which direction the breakout will go.

Phase 2 (Spring / UTAD):
  → Model F: CHoCH Reversal Entry
    Conditions: displacement + CHoCH confirmed at the Phase 2 extreme
    Risk: HIGH (this is counter-trend until Phase 3 confirms)
    R:R: HIGHEST (5:1 to 10:1 possible)
    Size: 50% of normal (due to uncertainty)
    Confidence: 60-70% (high reward compensates lower probability)

  → Also: Model B variant may apply if the Spring includes a
    clean liquidity sweep + FVG formation

Phase 3 (Markup / Markdown):
  → Model C: BOS Pullback Entry
    Conditions: BOS confirmed, pullback to OB/FVG, LTF confirms
    Risk: LOW (trend is confirmed)
    R:R: 3-5:1
    This is the SAFEST entry but ONLY on the pullbacks within Phase 3,
    not during the impulse legs.

  → NO ENTRY during impulse legs (chasing = danger)

Phase 4 (Re-Accumulation / Re-Distribution):
  → Model A: OTE + OB Entry (most common)
    Conditions: pullback reaches OTE zone, OB or FVG present
    Risk: LOW-MEDIUM (structure intact, correct zone)
    R:R: 3-6:1

  → Model B: Liquidity Sweep + FVG Entry
    Conditions: during pullback, a minor swing is swept + FVG forms
    Risk: LOW (everything aligns)
    R:R: 4-8:1 (best of Phase 4)

  → Model D: AMD Session Entry
    Conditions: Phase 4 pullback aligns with a KZ AMD pattern
    Risk: MEDIUM (time-dependent)
    R:R: 3-6:1

  Phase 4 is where the MAJORITY of entries should occur.

Phase 5 (Distribution / Accumulation Exit):
  → NO NEW ENTRY in the trend direction
  → EXIT existing positions
  → If Phase 5 CHoCH is confirmed with displacement:
    → Potentially Model F for the reversal (new cycle beginning)
    → But this is Phase 2 of the NEW cycle, not Phase 5 of the old one

================================================================================
10.8  COMMON MMM MISTAKES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: ENTERING DURING PHASE 1
────────────────────────────────────────
Consolidation looks boring. Trader tries to trade the range. Gets
chopped up by false breakouts and random sweeps within the range.

Fix: Phase 1 = patience. No edge exists during consolidation.
Wait for Phase 2 (aggressive) or Phase 4 (safe).

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: CHASING PHASE 3
────────────────────────────────────────
Trader sees the trend starting (Phase 3 impulse), panics about
missing out, enters at the top of the impulse. Price pulls back
(normal Phase 4 pullback) and stops the trader out.

Fix: Phase 3 impulse legs are for OBSERVATION, not entry. Mark the
OBs and FVGs being created. Wait for the pullback (Phase 4) to enter.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: CONFUSING PHASE 2 WITH A RUN
────────────────────────────────────────
Price breaks below consolidation (potential Phase 2 Spring). Trader
immediately buys expecting a reversal. But there is no displacement
reversal — price continues down. It was a genuine breakdown, not a
Spring.

Fix: NEVER enter Phase 2 without displacement reversal confirmation.
The break alone is not enough. Displacement + CHoCH = confirmed.
No displacement = not confirmed = do not enter.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: NOT RECOGNIZING PHASE 5
────────────────────────────────────────
The trend has been going for weeks. Every Phase 4 pullback has been
a great entry. Trader assumes the next pullback is another Phase 4.
But it's Phase 5 — the trend is ending. The "pullback" turns into
a reversal. Trader gets stopped out.

Fix: Watch for Phase 5 indicators. When volume becomes climactic,
exhaustion candles appear, and buy_sell_ratio diverges → the cycle
may be ending. Be more cautious with new entries and tighten trailing
stops on existing positions.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: WRONG TIMEFRAME PHASE
────────────────────────────────────────
Trader identifies Phase 4 on the 15m and enters, but the Daily is
in Phase 5 (the daily trend is ending). The 15m Phase 4 pullback
does bounce, but the larger Daily reversal overwhelms it.

Fix: Always check the HTF phase first. If HTF is Phase 5, do not
enter the LTF Phase 4 in the same direction. The macro cycle
overrides the micro cycle.

================================================================================
END OF [ICT_MARKET_MAKER_MODEL]
================================================================================



================================================================================
