[IPDA_DATA_RANGES]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
IPDA stands for Interbank Price Delivery Algorithm. ICT teaches that
institutional price delivery references specific lookback periods:
20 trading days, 40 trading days, and 60 trading days. These are not
arbitrary numbers — they correspond to approximately 1 calendar month,
2 calendar months, and 3 calendar months (one quarter) of trading data.

The IPDA concept answers a practical question: "How far back should I
look for relevant Order Blocks, Fair Value Gaps, and liquidity levels?"

Not all historical levels are equally relevant. A Daily OB from 15
trading days ago is far more likely to attract price than a Daily OB
from 90 trading days ago. Institutional algorithms reference recent
price history more heavily than distant history. The 20/40/60 day
framework provides a structured way to prioritize levels by recency.

This section is intentionally concise because IPDA is a PRIORITIZATION
TOOL, not an analytical method. It tells the system WHICH levels to
focus on, not HOW to analyze them (that is covered in OB, FVG, and
LIQUIDITY sections).

================================================================================
11.1  THE THREE DATA RANGES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
20-DAY RANGE (PRIORITY 1 — HIGHEST)
────────────────────────────────────────
Lookback: last 20 trading days of Daily candles

Calculation:
  range_20_high = max(high) of last 20 Daily candles
  range_20_low  = min(low) of last 20 Daily candles

What this represents:
  Approximately 1 calendar month of price action.
  This is the MOST RELEVANT data range for current trading.
  Institutional algorithms most heavily reference the last 20 days
  for order placement, liquidity targeting, and fair value assessment.

OBs and FVGs within this range:
  Any Daily/4H OB or FVG created within the last 20 trading days
  is at MAXIMUM relevance. These are the zones most likely to
  attract price on a return visit.

Liquidity within this range:
  The highs and lows of the 20-day range itself are significant
  liquidity targets:
    range_20_high = major BSL (stops from 1 month of shorts)
    range_20_low  = major SSL (stops from 1 month of longs)

Importance multiplier: 3×
  When scoring OB/FVG quality, levels within the 20-day range
  receive 3× the weight of levels outside the 60-day range.

────────────────────────────────────────
40-DAY RANGE (PRIORITY 2 — MODERATE)
────────────────────────────────────────
Lookback: last 40 trading days of Daily candles

Calculation:
  range_40_high = max(high) of last 40 Daily candles
  range_40_low  = min(low) of last 40 Daily candles

What this represents:
  Approximately 2 calendar months of price action.
  OBs and FVGs from 21-40 days ago are still relevant but less
  immediately than those within 20 days. Institutions reference
  this range for broader context and larger positional decisions.

OBs and FVGs in this range (but outside 20-day):
  Moderate relevance. These zones may still attract price, but
  the probability decreases compared to 20-day levels. Some of
  the institutional orders from 2 months ago may have been
  cancelled or repositioned.

Liquidity:
  range_40_high and range_40_low are significant if they exceed
  the 20-day range (meaning there are highs/lows from 21-40 days
  ago that haven't been revisited).

Importance multiplier: 2×

────────────────────────────────────────
60-DAY RANGE (PRIORITY 3 — LOWER)
────────────────────────────────────────
Lookback: last 60 trading days of Daily candles

Calculation:
  range_60_high = max(high) of last 60 Daily candles
  range_60_low  = min(low) of last 60 Daily candles

What this represents:
  Approximately 1 quarter (3 calendar months) of price action.
  This is the outer boundary of primary institutional reference.
  OBs and FVGs from 41-60 days ago are at the edge of relevance
  for intraday trading purposes.

OBs and FVGs in this range (but outside 40-day):
  Lower relevance. Still worth noting on Daily and Weekly timeframes,
  but 4H and lower TF levels from this period are effectively expired.

Liquidity:
  The 60-day high/low represents the quarterly extreme.
  If price is near the 60-day extreme, a significant reaction is
  likely (quarterly boundary often triggers institutional repositioning).

Importance multiplier: 1×

────────────────────────────────────────
BEYOND 60 DAYS
────────────────────────────────────────
OBs and FVGs older than 60 trading days:
  For INTRADAY trading: effectively expired. Do not use.
  For SWING trading on Daily/Weekly: still potentially relevant
  if they are on the Weekly timeframe and have never been tested.

Exception: Weekly and Monthly OBs can remain relevant for 6+ months.
  These are structural zones, not algorithmic reference points.
  The IPDA 60-day cutoff applies to Daily and lower TF levels,
  not to Weekly/Monthly levels which follow their own lifecycle.

================================================================================
11.2  LOOK LEFT LOGIC
================================================================================

When the system identifies an OB, FVG, or liquidity level, it must
determine which IPDA range the level falls within. This is called
"Look Left" because you are looking back in time to assess when the
level was created.

────────────────────────────────────────
ALGORITHM
────────────────────────────────────────
For each identified OB, FVG, or liquidity level:

  Step 1: Determine the creation date
    When was this level formed?
    creation_age = current_date - creation_date (in trading days)

  Step 2: Classify into IPDA range
    if creation_age ≤ 20:
      → PRIORITY_1 (highest relevance)
      → importance_multiplier = 3
    elif creation_age ≤ 40:
      → PRIORITY_2 (moderate relevance)
      → importance_multiplier = 2
    elif creation_age ≤ 60:
      → PRIORITY_3 (lower relevance)
      → importance_multiplier = 1
    else:
      → EXPIRED for intraday (importance_multiplier = 0)
      → Exception: Weekly/Monthly levels retain importance_multiplier = 1

  Step 3: Apply to quality scoring
    The IPDA multiplier affects how the system prioritizes levels:
    When two OBs of similar quality exist, the one within the
    20-day range is prioritized over the one in the 40-day range.

    Practical impact on quality scoring:
      Within 20-day: add +3 to OB/FVG quality score
      Within 40-day: add +2
      Within 60-day: add +1
      Beyond 60-day: add +0 (or remove from active consideration
        for intraday trading)

────────────────────────────────────────
PRACTICAL APPLICATION
────────────────────────────────────────
Scenario: Two untested Bullish OBs on the Daily chart
  OB_A: created 12 days ago, quality score 22 (A-tier)
  OB_B: created 45 days ago, quality score 24 (A-tier)

  Without IPDA: OB_B scores higher (24 vs 22)
  With IPDA:
    OB_A: 22 + 3 (within 20-day) = effective 25
    OB_B: 24 + 1 (within 60-day) = effective 25
    → Equal. But OB_A should be slightly preferred because recency
      correlates with institutional relevance.

  Another scenario:
    OB_C: created 8 days ago, quality 20 (B-tier)
    OB_D: created 55 days ago, quality 26 (S-tier)

    With IPDA:
      OB_C: 20 + 3 = effective 23
      OB_D: 26 + 1 = effective 27
    → OB_D still wins because its inherent quality is much higher.
      IPDA adjusts priority but does not override fundamental quality.
      An S-tier OB from 55 days ago is still better than a B-tier OB
      from 8 days ago. IPDA provides nuance, not override.

================================================================================
11.3  IPDA RANGE AS LIQUIDITY FRAMEWORK
================================================================================

The boundaries of the IPDA ranges themselves are significant levels:

────────────────────────────────────────
20-DAY HIGH AND LOW
────────────────────────────────────────
  range_20_high = the highest price in the last 20 trading days
  range_20_low  = the lowest price in the last 20 trading days

  These are the boundaries of the current month's price action.
  They attract institutional attention because:
  - Stops cluster above the 20-day high (BSL from recent shorts)
  - Stops cluster below the 20-day low (SSL from recent longs)
  - Breaking the 20-day extreme often signals a shift in institutional
    positioning (a new 20-day high means bullish momentum is strong
    enough to exceed a full month of resistance)

  Use as liquidity targets:
    If bias is bullish and 20-day high is nearby → probable target
    If bias is bearish and 20-day low is nearby → probable target

────────────────────────────────────────
40-DAY AND 60-DAY EXTREMES
────────────────────────────────────────
  range_40_high/low: 2-month extremes
  range_60_high/low: quarterly extremes

  These are MAJOR liquidity pools:
  - A 60-day high that hasn't been exceeded in 3 months represents
    a massive concentration of sell stops above it
  - Breaking it = significant event (potential quarterly breakout)
  - Sweeping it and reversing = potential quarterly reversal

  Use:
    As MAJOR TP2 targets for swing trades
    As warning levels (if price approaches 60-day extreme, expect
    increased volatility and potential reversal)

────────────────────────────────────────
EXTENDED POSITION CHECK
────────────────────────────────────────
The system checks if price is near the extreme of any IPDA range:

  For the 20-day range:
    distance_to_high = range_20_high - current_price
    distance_to_low  = current_price - range_20_low
    range_20 = range_20_high - range_20_low

    position_in_20 = (current_price - range_20_low) / range_20 × 100

    if position_in_20 > 90%:
      → EXTENDED in premium of 20-day range
      → Price is near the 20-day high
      → Reversal probability increases
      → Caution with new longs

    if position_in_20 < 10%:
      → EXTENDED in discount of 20-day range
      → Price is near the 20-day low
      → Reversal probability increases
      → Caution with new shorts

  Same check for 40-day and 60-day ranges.

  When price is extended in MULTIPLE ranges simultaneously
  (near the 20-day, 40-day, AND 60-day high):
    → Very high probability of reversal
    → Confidence for continuation trades: -10%
    → Confidence for reversal trades (if HTF supports): +10%

  When price is extended in NO ranges (in the middle of all ranges):
    → Normal positioning, no adjustment needed

================================================================================
11.4  QUARTERLY SHIFT
================================================================================

ICT has noted that institutional positioning often shifts at the
start of each calendar quarter:
  Q1: January, Q2: April, Q3: July, Q4: October

────────────────────────────────────────
WHAT HAPPENS AT QUARTERLY BOUNDARIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - Institutional portfolios are rebalanced
  - New quarterly allocations are made
  - The IPDA 20-day range resets (new quarter = new month)
  - Volatility typically increases during the first week
  - The direction established in the first week of a quarter
    often sets the tone for the following weeks

────────────────────────────────────────
PRACTICAL IMPACT FOR THE SYSTEM
────────────────────────────────────────
  First 3 trading days of January, April, July, October:
    Confidence adjustment: -5%
    Reason: increased unpredictability during repositioning

    The system should note: "Quarterly shift period — exercise
    additional caution with new entries."

  After the first week of a new quarter:
    Assess: what direction did the first week establish?
    This direction is likely to continue for weeks 2-4.

  This is a minor adjustment, not a major trading rule.
  For intraday trading, the quarterly shift is context, not
  a decision driver. It becomes more significant for swing
  trading (multi-day to multi-week holds).

================================================================================
11.5  IPDA INTEGRATION WITH OTHER SECTIONS
================================================================================

IPDA does not operate in isolation. It modifies how other sections
prioritize their levels:

────────────────────────────────────────
WITH ORDER_BLOCKS (Section 5)
────────────────────────────────────────
  OB quality scoring criterion 4 (freshness) already captures
  test count, but IPDA adds TIME-BASED recency:
    An untested OB from 5 days ago (within 20-day) is more
    relevant than an untested OB from 50 days ago (within 60-day),
    even though both have tests = 0.

  Application: when multiple OBs exist as potential entry zones,
  prefer the one within the tighter IPDA range (20 > 40 > 60).

────────────────────────────────────────
WITH FAIR_VALUE_GAPS (Section 6)
────────────────────────────────────────
  FVGs also have time-based relevance:
    5m/15m FVGs: relevant for hours, not days (IPDA doesn't apply
    at this resolution — use the time decay rules in FVG section)
    1H FVGs: relevant for 1-5 days
    4H FVGs: relevant within 20-day range primarily
    Daily FVGs: IPDA fully applies (20/40/60 day prioritization)
    Weekly FVGs: remain relevant beyond 60 days

────────────────────────────────────────
WITH LIQUIDITY_MAPPING (Section 7)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Liquidity levels from within the 20-day range are the primary
  targets for current price delivery. Levels from the 40-60 day
  range are secondary targets.

  The Draw on Liquidity (DoL) should preferentially target
  liquidity within the 20-day range unless a stronger level
  exists in the 40/60-day range.

────────────────────────────────────────
WITH PREMIUM_DISCOUNT (Section 8)
────────────────────────────────────────
  The IPDA ranges can be used as an additional P/D reference:
    Price in the upper 10% of the 60-day range = quarterly premium
    Price in the lower 10% of the 60-day range = quarterly discount

  This provides a WIDER context than the structural P/D (which uses
  the most recent swing H/L). Structural P/D is primary for entry
  decisions. IPDA P/D is secondary context.

================================================================================
11.6  IPDA STATE TRACKING
================================================================================

The system maintains IPDA data and updates it daily:

  {
    range_20: {
      high: price,
      low: price,
      range: price distance,
      current_position_pct: 0-100
    },
    range_40: { same fields },
    range_60: { same fields },

    extended_status: "NONE" | "EXTENDED_HIGH" | "EXTENDED_LOW"
    extended_ranges: list of which ranges price is extended in

    quarterly_shift: true/false (are we in first 3 days of quarter?)
    quarter: "Q1" | "Q2" | "Q3" | "Q4"

    ob_priority_adjustments: {
      within_20: +3 to quality score
      within_40: +2
      within_60: +1
      beyond_60: +0
    }
  }

Update frequency: once per day (at the start of analysis or when
a new Daily candle closes). IPDA ranges are Daily-resolution
concepts and do not need intraday updates.

================================================================================
END OF [IPDA_DATA_RANGES]
================================================================================






================================================================================
[TOP_DOWN_WORKFLOW]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
This section is the OPERATIONAL ENGINE of the entire system. Every
concept defined in sections 1-11 comes together here in a specific,
sequential workflow that the system executes for every analysis.

Top-down analysis is not optional. It is not a suggestion. It is the
ONLY correct way to apply ICT methodology. Analyzing a 15m chart
without first understanding the Weekly, Daily, 4H, and 1H context
is like reading the last page of a book and trying to understand the
plot. The 15m data only makes sense within the framework established
by higher timeframes.

The workflow is strictly sequential: Weekly → Daily → 4H → 1H → Entry TF.
Each step produces specific outputs that feed into the next step.
Skipping a step or starting from the bottom up will produce unreliable
analysis.

This section defines every step, what to extract, what decisions to
make, and how to proceed to the next step.

================================================================================
12.1  THE COMPLETE WORKFLOW
================================================================================

The system processes timeframes in this exact order:

  STEP 1: WEEKLY   → NARRATIVE (the big picture)
  STEP 2: DAILY    → BIAS (today's direction)
  STEP 3: 4H       → CONTEXT (refined zones and structure)
  STEP 4: 1H       → TACTICAL (is an entry forming?)
  STEP 5: ENTRY TF → EXECUTION (the precise trade)

Each step has:
  - INPUTS: what data to analyze
  - PROCESS: what to extract and evaluate
  - OUTPUTS: specific conclusions that feed the next step
  - GATE: a decision point that determines whether to proceed

If any GATE says STOP → the system outputs HOLD.
The system only reaches Step 5 if all four preceding gates pass.

================================================================================
12.2  STEP 1: WEEKLY ANALYSIS → NARRATIVE
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
INPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Weekly OHLCV candles (last 52-104 candles = 1-2 years)
  Previously identified Weekly swing points
  Previously identified Weekly OBs and FVGs

────────────────────────────────────────
PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
1. STRUCTURE:
   Identify Weekly swing sequence using N=5
   Determine: bullish (HH/HL), bearish (LH/LL), or ranging
   Assess strength: strong trend or weakening

2. KEY LEVELS:
   Active Weekly OBs (apply detection from Section 5)
   Active Weekly FVGs (apply detection from Section 6)
   Major BSL: unswept Weekly swing highs
   Major SSL: unswept Weekly swing lows
   EQH/EQL on Weekly

3. CURRENT POSITION:
   Where is price relative to Weekly structure?
   Is price near a Weekly OB or FVG?
   Is price near a major Weekly liquidity level?
   What is the IPDA 60-day position?

4. DRAW ON LIQUIDITY (MACRO):
   Based on Weekly structure and unswept levels:
   Where is price being drawn on the macro scale?
   Which major liquidity pool is the next target?

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  WEEKLY_NARRATIVE = {
    trend: "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "RANGING"
    strength: "STRONG" | "MODERATE" | "WEAK"
    key_levels: [list of major Weekly OBs, FVGs, liquidity]
    current_position: description of where price sits
    macro_dol: the major Draw on Liquidity target
    at_weekly_poi: true/false (is price AT a Weekly level now?)
    weekly_poi_type: "OB" | "FVG" | "LIQUIDITY" | null
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
GATE 1
────────────────────────────────────────
  Weekly analysis does NOT gate the workflow (it always passes).
  Weekly provides CONTEXT, not a go/no-go decision.
  Even if Weekly is unclear, the system proceeds to Daily.

  However, Weekly information MODIFIES confidence throughout:
  - Clear Weekly trend: +5% to overall confidence
  - Weekly ranging/unclear: -3%
  - Price at a Weekly POI: +10% (very significant)

================================================================================
12.3  STEP 2: DAILY ANALYSIS → BIAS
================================================================================

This is the MOST IMPORTANT step for intraday trading.
The Daily Bias is the COMMANDER. All trades must serve it.

────────────────────────────────────────
INPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Daily OHLCV candles (last 60-120 candles = 2-4 months)
  WEEKLY_NARRATIVE from Step 1
  Previously identified Daily swing points, OBs, FVGs

────────────────────────────────────────
PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
1. STRUCTURE:
   Identify Daily swing sequence using N=5
   Determine Daily trend direction and strength
   Identify last Daily BOS and CHoCH events

2. DAILY BIAS DETERMINATION:
   The Daily Bias answers: "Is TODAY likely bullish or bearish?"

   Determination method:

   a) Structure-based:
      Daily making HH/HL → BULLISH bias
      Daily making LH/LL → BEARISH bias
      Daily ranging → UNCLEAR bias → HOLD

   b) Context-based (refines the structure assessment):
      Last Daily candle closed bullish in an uptrend → confirms bullish
      Last Daily candle closed bearish in an uptrend → pullback
        → still bullish if HL holds
      Daily CHoCH just occurred → bias is SHIFTING
        → cautious until new direction confirms

   c) Level-based:
      Price just reached a Daily demand zone (OB/FVG) in uptrend
        → bullish continuation expected → BULLISH
      Price just reached a Daily supply zone in downtrend
        → bearish continuation expected → BEARISH
      Price at EQ of Daily range → UNCLEAR

   d) Weekly alignment check:
      Daily agrees with Weekly → HIGH confidence in bias
      Daily disagrees with Weekly → MODERATE confidence
        (Daily may be in a corrective phase within Weekly trend)
      Both unclear → HOLD

3. PD ARRAYS (Points of Interest):
   All active Daily OBs with quality assessment
   All active Daily FVGs with quality assessment
   Daily Premium/Discount zones
   Daily OTE zone
   IPDA 20/40/60 day ranges and current position

4. DAILY LIQUIDITY:
   PDH/PDL (Previous Day High/Low)
   PWH/PWL (Previous Week High/Low)
   Nearest unswept Daily BSL and SSL
   Session levels (Asian H/L from last night)

5. DRAW ON LIQUIDITY (DAILY):
   Based on Daily bias:
   If BULLISH → DoL = nearest unswept BSL above
   If BEARISH → DoL = nearest unswept SSL below
   This DoL is the primary target for today's trades.

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  DAILY_BIAS = {
    direction: "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "UNCLEAR"
    confidence: percentage (how certain is the bias)
    weekly_alignment: "ALIGNED" | "DIVERGENT" | "BOTH_UNCLEAR"
    last_event: last BOS or CHoCH (type + when)
    daily_pois: [list of active Daily OBs and FVGs with zones]
    pd_zone: current Premium/Discount status
    ote_zone: {top, bottom} for the relevant direction
    dol: {price, type, significance} of today's Draw on Liquidity
    pdh: price, pdl: price
    asian_high: price, asian_low: price
    nearest_bsl: {price, significance}
    nearest_ssl: {price, significance}
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
GATE 2
────────────────────────────────────────
  IF DAILY_BIAS.direction == "UNCLEAR":
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "Daily Bias is unclear. No directional edge today."
    → STOP. Do not proceed to Step 3.

  IF DAILY_BIAS.confidence < 50%:
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "Daily Bias confidence too low for execution."
    → STOP.

  IF DAILY_BIAS passes:
    → Proceed to Step 3 with the established bias direction.
    → ALL subsequent analysis must align with this direction.

================================================================================
12.4  STEP 3: 4H ANALYSIS → CONTEXT
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
INPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  4H OHLCV candles (last 100-200 candles = ~2-4 weeks)
  DAILY_BIAS from Step 2
  WEEKLY_NARRATIVE from Step 1

────────────────────────────────────────
PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
1. STRUCTURE:
   4H swing sequence using N=3
   4H trend direction
   Last 4H BOS/CHoCH

   ALIGNMENT CHECK:
   Is 4H aligned with Daily Bias?

   ALIGNED: 4H trend = same direction as Daily Bias
     → Ideal. Look for entry within the current 4H impulse
       or on the next 4H pullback.

   PULLBACK: 4H trend = opposite of Daily Bias
     → 4H is in a corrective move against the Daily trend.
     → This is NORMAL and often creates the best entries.
     → The question: where will the 4H pullback END?
     → Answer: at a Daily POI (OB, FVG) in the OTE zone.
     → Wait for 4H to realign with Daily before entering.

   TRANSITION: 4H just had a CHoCH in the Daily Bias direction
     → 4H was against Daily (pullback) and is now flipping back.
     → This is the SETUP FORMING signal.
     → Proceed to 1H with heightened attention.

2. REFINED ZONES:
   Within the Daily POIs identified in Step 2:
   Are there 4H OBs that narrow the zone?
   Are there 4H FVGs that overlap with Daily OBs?

   The goal: take the broad Daily zone and refine it to a
   specific 4H zone. This narrows the entry area.

   Example:
     Daily OB: $98,500 - $99,200 (700 point zone)
     Within this Daily OB, there is a 4H OB: $98,700 - $98,900
     Refined zone: $98,700 - $98,900 (200 point zone)
     → Much tighter. Better R:R. Higher precision.

3. 4H LIQUIDITY:
   Nearest 4H BSL and SSL
   Any EQH/EQL on 4H
   Relationship to Daily liquidity levels

4. MARKET MAKER MODEL PHASE:
   What phase is the 4H in? (from Section 10)
   Phase 1 → no entry yet
   Phase 2 → aggressive entry possible (Model F)
   Phase 3 → wait for pullback
   Phase 4 → primary entry phase → proceed to 1H
   Phase 5 → caution, possible cycle end

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  FOUR_H_CONTEXT = {
    trend: "BULLISH" | "BEARISH" | "RANGING" | "TRANSITIONAL"
    daily_alignment: "ALIGNED" | "PULLBACK" | "TRANSITION" | "CONFLICT"
    mmm_phase: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5
    refined_zones: [list of 4H OBs/FVGs within Daily POIs]
    nearest_4h_bsl: {price, significance}
    nearest_4h_ssl: {price, significance}
    price_at_zone: true/false (is price currently at a refined zone?)
    zone_details: {type, top, bottom, quality} if at a zone
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
GATE 3
────────────────────────────────────────
  IF daily_alignment == "CONFLICT" AND mmm_phase not in [2, 4]:
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "4H conflicts with Daily without a clear entry phase."
    → STOP.

  IF mmm_phase == 1 or 5:
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "4H in Phase 1 (consolidation) or Phase 5 (distribution).
       No entry opportunity."
    → STOP.

  IF mmm_phase == 3 AND price_at_zone == false:
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "4H in impulse phase, not at entry zone. Wait for pullback."
    → STOP.

  PASS CONDITIONS (proceed to Step 4):
    daily_alignment in ["ALIGNED", "PULLBACK", "TRANSITION"]
    AND mmm_phase in [2, 3, 4] (with 3 requiring price_at_zone)
    AND refined_zones is not empty OR price is approaching a zone

================================================================================
12.5  STEP 4: 1H ANALYSIS → TACTICAL
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
INPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  1H OHLCV candles (last 200-500 candles = ~1-3 weeks)
  FOUR_H_CONTEXT from Step 3
  DAILY_BIAS from Step 2

────────────────────────────────────────
PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
1. STRUCTURE:
   1H swing sequence using N=3
   1H trend direction
   Last 1H BOS/CHoCH events

   KEY QUESTION: Has 1H shown a structural shift in the trade direction
   at or near a HTF zone?

   1H CHoCH in the Daily Bias direction AT a 4H/Daily zone:
     → ENTRY_READY signal
     → Proceed to Entry TF

   1H BOS confirming the CHoCH:
     → ENTRY_CONFIRMED signal
     → Higher confidence entry

   1H still moving against Daily Bias (internal pullback):
     → WAIT
     → The pullback is still developing
     → Do not proceed to Entry TF yet

2. PROXIMITY TO HTF ZONES:
   Is price currently AT or NEAR a refined zone from Step 3?

   At zone (within 0.2% of the zone boundary):
     → Ready for reaction. Look for LTF confirmation.

   Approaching zone (within 0.5%):
     → Getting close. Monitor for arrival and reaction.

   Far from zone (> 1%):
     → Not yet. Either wait or look for intermediate zones on 1H.

3. 1H LEVEL REFINEMENT:
   Within the 4H zones from Step 3:
   Any 1H OBs or FVGs that further narrow the zone?

   Example chain:
     Daily OB: $98,500 - $99,200
     4H OB within it: $98,700 - $98,900
     1H FVG within the 4H OB: $98,750 - $98,850
     → Refined entry zone: $98,750 - $98,850 (100 points)
     → CE of this zone: $98,800 (exact entry candidate)

4. TIME CHECK:
   What session/Kill Zone is active right now?
   Apply time-based confidence adjustments from Section 9.

   In a Kill Zone → good timing
   In a Dead Zone → do not proceed even if structure looks ready

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  ONE_H_TACTICAL = {
    trend: direction
    structural_shift: "CHoCH_DETECTED" | "BOS_CONFIRMED" | "NONE"
    shift_direction: direction of the shift (should match Daily Bias)
    at_htf_zone: true/false
    zone_details: the specific zone price is at/near
    refined_entry_zone: narrowest zone from the HTF chain
    entry_ready: true/false
    session: current Kill Zone or session
    time_confidence_adj: percentage adjustment from timing
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
GATE 4
────────────────────────────────────────
  IF entry_ready == false:
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "1H has not confirmed entry readiness.
       Waiting for structural shift at HTF zone."
    → STOP.

  IF session is "DEAD_ZONE" or "NY_LUNCH" or "ASIAN":
    → OUTPUT: HOLD
    → Reason: "Timing does not support entry.
       Setup may be forming but execution window is wrong."
    → STOP. (Re-evaluate at the next Kill Zone)

  PASS CONDITIONS (proceed to Step 5):
    entry_ready == true
    AND session is an active trading window
    AND structural_shift matches Daily Bias direction

================================================================================
12.6  STEP 5: ENTRY TF ANALYSIS → EXECUTION
================================================================================

This is where the trade is constructed. All higher timeframes have
confirmed: direction, zone, structure, and timing. Now the system
finds the exact entry, stop loss, and targets.

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY TIMEFRAME SELECTION
────────────────────────────────────────
The entry TF depends on the structural SL distance needed:

  Calculate the structural SL from the HTF zone:
    For a long: SL would be below the OB/FVG/swing that defines the zone
    For a short: SL would be above it

  SL distance from potential entry:
    If > 0.5% → use 1H for entry
    If 0.3-0.5% → use 15m or 30m
    If < 0.3% → use 5m (sniper entry)

  Choose the TF that provides:
    1. Clear structural confirmation (CHoCH/BOS on Entry TF)
    2. An OB or FVG for precise entry placement
    3. SL above the minimum threshold for that TF
    4. The best possible R:R to TP1

────────────────────────────────────────
INPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Entry TF OHLCV candles (e.g., 15m: last 200-500 candles)
  ONE_H_TACTICAL from Step 4
  The refined entry zone from the HTF chain
  The Daily Bias direction
  Kill Zone and time context

────────────────────────────────────────
PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
1. LTF STRUCTURE WITHIN THE HTF ZONE:
   On the Entry TF, look for:

   a) Market Structure Shift (MSS = CHoCH on Entry TF):
      The first candle sequence where the Entry TF breaks a swing
      in the trade direction while price is AT the HTF zone.

      Example (long entry):
        Price enters the HTF bullish zone (4H OB)
        On 15m: price was making LH/LL (bearish internal structure)
        Then: 15m prints a candle that closes above the last 15m LH
        → This is the MSS/CHoCH on 15m → first confirmation

   b) BOS on Entry TF:
      After the MSS, price continues and breaks the next swing
      in the trade direction.
      → BOS confirms the MSS → higher confidence

   c) OB/FVG formation from the MSS/BOS:
      The displacement that caused the MSS/BOS creates:
      - A new OB on the Entry TF (the last opposing candle before
        the displacement)
      - Possibly an FVG from the displacement candle
      → These are the EXACT entry levels

2. ENTRY DETERMINATION:
   Method 1 (CE Entry — default):
     If an FVG formed: enter at FVG CE
     If an OB formed but no FVG: enter at OB CE
     If FVG + OB overlap: enter at overlap CE (best)

   Method 2 (Aggressive):
     Enter at the edge of the OB/FVG (closer to the zone)
     Tighter SL possible, higher R:R, higher NO_FILL risk

   Method 3 (Conservative):
     Wait for a rejection candle at the OB/FVG
     Then enter on the next candle's open
     Wider SL, lower R:R, but highest confirmation

3. STOP LOSS:
   SL is placed beyond the structural level that would invalidate
   the trade thesis:

   For longs:
     Below the HTF zone bottom (the lowest point of the OB/FVG chain)
     Plus buffer: max(0.3 × ATR on Entry TF, 0.2% of price)

   For shorts:
     Above the HTF zone top + buffer

   SL VALIDATION:
     Check: SL distance ≥ minimum for the Entry TF
       (1H ≥ 0.5%, 15m ≥ 0.3%, 5m ≥ 0.15%)
     Check: SL distance ≥ 1.5 × ATR on Entry TF
     If either check fails:
       Option A: use a wider structural level (next swing out)
       Option B: switch to a lower Entry TF for tighter entry
       Option C: skip the trade

4. TARGET DETERMINATION:

   TP1 — THE CORRECT PROCESS:
     Step A: identify the nearest OPPOSING PD Array or liquidity level
       in the trade direction from the entry point.

       For longs, look UP for:
         Nearest bearish OB (supply zone)
         Nearest unfilled bearish FVG
         Nearest BSL (swing high, EQH)
         Nearest session level (PDH, London High)
         Nearest Breaker Block

       The NEAREST of these = TP1 candidate

     Step B: place TP1 slightly before the level
       TP1 = level - (0.15% of price) for longs
       TP1 = level + (0.15% of price) for shorts
       Why: price often reverses just before reaching the exact level

     Step C: verify R:R
       R:R = |TP1 - Entry| / |Entry - SL|
       TP1 is valid when the level is real, unswept, ahead of entry and achievable.
       Report its actual R:R; do not skip nearer liquidity to cherry-pick a prettier ratio.
         NEVER set TP1 at an arbitrary price just to achieve 3:1

   TP2 — DRAW ON LIQUIDITY:
     The major target from the Daily analysis:
       DAILY_BIAS.dol (the Draw on Liquidity)
       OR: the next major HTF OB/FVG beyond TP1
       OR: the next major BSL/SSL beyond TP1

     TP2 does not have a minimum R:R requirement.
     It represents the full potential of the move.
     50% of the position targets TP2 after TP1 is reached.

5. ENTRY MODEL CLASSIFICATION:
   Based on the setup characteristics, classify which Entry Model
   this trade represents (see Section 13 for full details):

   Model A: entry at OTE zone + OB → check if OTE conditions met
   Model B: liquidity swept before entry → check if sweep occurred
   Model C: BOS on HTF + pullback to OB/FVG → check if BOS preceded
   Model D: AMD session pattern → check if AMD was identified
   Model E: Silver Bullet → check if within SB time window + FVG
   Model F: CHoCH reversal → check if this is a Phase 2 reversal

6. CONFLUENCE COUNT:
   Count all independent confluences supporting this trade:
   (each of these counts as 1 confluence if present)

   □ HTF trend alignment (Weekly and/or Daily in trade direction)
   □ Price at HTF PD Array (OB/FVG on 4H or higher)
   □ Liquidity swept before entry
   □ LTF structure shift confirms (CHoCH/BOS on Entry TF)
   □ Price in correct Premium/Discount zone
   □ Price in OTE zone (62-79% retracement)
   □ Kill Zone active
   □ Volume confirms (vol_ratio > 1.5 on confirmation candle)
   □ Rejection candle at key level
   □ FVG + OB overlap
   □ Session liquidity target clear (PDH/PDL as target)
   □ True Day bias agrees
   □ Day of week supports (Tue/Wed/Thu)
   □ IPDA range supports
   □ MMM phase is optimal (Phase 4)
   □ No unswept liquidity between entry and SL

   Minimum: 3 confluences required (Rule 7 from SYSTEM_CORE)
   If < 3 → HOLD regardless of how good it looks

7. CONFIDENCE CALCULATION:
   Apply the CONFIDENCE_SCORING methodology (Section 16):
   Base 50% + additions - deductions
   If final confidence < 60% → HOLD

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTPUTS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TRADE_SIGNAL = {
    signal: "BUY" | "SELL" | "HOLD"
    pair: symbol
    entry_tf: "5m" | "15m" | "30m" | "1H"
    entry_model: "A" | "B" | "C" | "D" | "E" | "F"
    entry_price: exact price
    sl_price: exact price
    tp1_price: exact price
    tp2_price: exact price
    position_split: "50% TP1, 50% TP2"
    rr_to_tp1: actual calculated R:R (informational unless user policy says otherwise)
    rr_to_tp2: calculated R:R
    risk_percent: based on confidence level
    confidence: final percentage
    confluence_count: number
    confluences: [list of each confluence present]

    htf_analysis: {
      weekly: WEEKLY_NARRATIVE summary
      daily: DAILY_BIAS summary
      h4: FOUR_H_CONTEXT summary
      h1: ONE_H_TACTICAL summary
    }

    zone_chain: {
      daily_zone: {type, top, bottom}
      h4_refinement: {type, top, bottom}
      h1_refinement: {type, top, bottom}
      entry_tf_zone: {type, top, bottom, ce}
    }

    invalidation: specific price and condition that kills the trade
    management: "TP1 hit → SL to entry, trail behind structure"
    time_context: {session, kz, day_of_week, macro_window}
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
FINAL GATE
────────────────────────────────────────
  IF confluence_count < 3: → HOLD
  IF confidence < 60%: → HOLD
  IF rr_to_tp1 < 3.0: → HOLD
  IF SL distance < minimum for Entry TF: → HOLD
  IF unswept liquidity exists between entry and SL: → HOLD

  IF all checks pass: → OUTPUT the TRADE_SIGNAL

================================================================================
12.7  PD ARRAYS — THE UNIFIED FRAMEWORK
================================================================================

PD Arrays (Price Delivery Arrays) is ICT's unified term for ALL
institutional levels. Rather than thinking of OBs, FVGs, Breakers,
and other zones as separate tools, PD Arrays groups them as a
single category: "levels where institutional price delivery is
expected to react."

────────────────────────────────────────
DEMAND PD ARRAYS (for bullish reactions)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Bullish Order Block
  Bullish FVG
  Bullish Breaker Block
  Bullish Mitigation Block (tested OB, still holding)
  Bullish Rejection Block (long wick rejection at demand)
  Old Low (swing low that was swept and held)
  Liquidity Void below (unfilled area price may return to)

────────────────────────────────────────
SUPPLY PD ARRAYS (for bearish reactions)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Bearish Order Block
  Bearish FVG
  Bearish Breaker Block
  Bearish Mitigation Block
  Bearish Rejection Block
  Old High (swing high that was swept and held)
  Liquidity Void above

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY PD ARRAY THINKING MATTERS
────────────────────────────────────────
Instead of asking "Is there an OB here? Is there an FVG here?
Is there a Breaker here?" as separate questions, the system asks:
"What is the nearest PD Array in the trade direction?"

This simplifies the workflow:
  1. Identify ALL PD Arrays on each timeframe
  2. Sort by: distance from price → quality → timeframe
  3. The nearest high-quality PD Array = the primary entry zone
  4. If multiple PD Arrays cluster at the same level = confluence
  5. The nearest OPPOSING PD Array = TP1 target

The system does not need to decide "Should I use the OB or the FVG?"
If they overlap, use the overlap. If they don't, use whichever is
higher quality and better positioned (P/D, OTE, IPDA range).

================================================================================
12.8  WHEN TO OUTPUT HOLD
================================================================================

HOLD is not a failure. HOLD is the system correctly identifying that
conditions are not met for a high-probability trade. The system should
HOLD more often than it signals.

────────────────────────────────────────
HOLD CONDITIONS (comprehensive list)
────────────────────────────────────────

STRUCTURE:
  □ Daily Bias is unclear
  □ No clear HH/HL or LH/LL on Daily
  □ 4H and Daily in structural conflict (not a normal pullback)
  □ 1H has not shown structural shift at HTF zone
  □ No confirmed CHoCH or BOS on Entry TF

LEVELS:
  □ No HTF PD Array (OB/FVG) near current price
  □ Price is in "open space" (not at any institutional level)
  □ The nearest PD Array is too far (price needs to travel > 1%
    to reach it without a pullback setup in between)

CONFLUENCES:
  □ Fewer than 3 independent confluences
  □ Confluences are weak or uncertain

RISK:
  □ User-configured min_rr policy is not met (do not move TP1)
  □ SL distance below minimum threshold
  □ Unswept liquidity between entry and SL
  □ TP1 cannot be placed at a real structural level

TIMING:
  □ Dead Zone (NY Lunch, post-close, weekend)
  □ Asian Session (unless clear KZ transition setup)
  □ Friday after 14:00 NY
  □ Major news event within 60 minutes

MARKET:
  □ Extreme funding rate against trade direction (crypto)
  □ OI spike suggesting liquidation cascade risk (crypto)
  □ Price at IPDA extreme with no reversal confirmation

ANALYSIS:
  □ The data is insufficient to establish clear structure
  □ Multiple analytical elements are "uncertain" rather than "clear"
  □ The system would need to stretch or fabricate a confluence

When HOLD is the output, the system should provide:
  {
    signal: "HOLD"
    reason: specific reason(s) from the list above
    what_to_watch: "Wait for 1H CHoCH at the 4H OB zone ($98,700)"
    potential_setup: description of what WOULD create a valid signal
    reassess_at: next time/KZ to re-evaluate
  }

================================================================================
12.9  WORKFLOW EXECUTION EXAMPLE
================================================================================

Complete example showing the workflow from Weekly to Entry:

  STEP 1 — WEEKLY:
    Weekly structure: bullish (HH/HL confirmed, 3 sets)
    Weekly OB at $92,000-$93,500 (far below, not immediately relevant)
    Weekly FVG unfilled at $96,000-$97,200
    Macro DoL: BSL at $108,000 (previous major high)
    Current price: $99,500
    Weekly conclusion: bullish narrative, price mid-range
    → PASS (Weekly context established)

  STEP 2 — DAILY:
    Daily structure: bullish (2 HH + 2 HL confirmed)
    Last event: BOS bullish 5 days ago
    Daily OB: $98,100-$98,600 (last HL zone)
    Daily FVG: $98,400-$98,900
    P/D: current price $99,500, EQ at $99,000 → near EQ/slight premium
    OTE zone: $98,842 - $98,368 (for the current range)
    DoL: BSL at $101,200 (4H EQH triple top)
    PDH: $100,100, PDL: $98,800
    DAILY BIAS: BULLISH (structure + Weekly alignment)
    Confidence: 75%
    → PASS (clear bullish bias)

  STEP 3 — 4H:
    4H structure: was bullish, now pulling back (LH formed on 4H)
    4H alignment: PULLBACK (bearish 4H within bullish Daily)
    4H OB within Daily OB: $98,400-$98,600
    MMM Phase: Phase 4 (pullback to OB/FVG zone)
    Price: $99,500 (approaching the zone but not there yet)
    → Conditional PASS (zone identified, waiting for price to arrive)

  STEP 4 — 1H:
    Price drops to $98,700 (near the zone)
    1H structure: was bearish (pullback) → CHoCH just occurred
      (1H closed above last 1H LH at $98,900)
    At HTF zone: YES (within 4H OB zone)
    Session: NY AM KZ (09:30 NY, Tuesday)
    → ENTRY_READY → PASS

  STEP 5 — 15m ENTRY:
    15m shows: MSS (CHoCH) at $98,800 → BOS at $99,000
    15m FVG formed: $98,820-$98,920 (from the displacement up)
    15m OB: $98,750-$98,800
    FVG + OB overlap: $98,820-$98,800 → wait, no overlap here
    Best entry: 15m FVG CE = $98,870

    SL: below 4H OB bottom $98,400 - buffer = $98,300
    SL distance: $98,870 - $98,300 = $570 (0.58%) → above 15m minimum ✓

    TP1: BSL at $100,100 (PDH) → $100,100 - 0.15% = $99,950
    R:R to TP1: ($99,950 - $98,870) / ($98,870 - $98,300) = 1.89 → FAILS

    Next level: EQH at $101,200 → $101,050 TP1
    R:R to TP1: ($101,050 - $98,870) / ($98,870 - $98,300) = 3.82 → PASSES ✓

    But wait: there's opposing supply (Bearish FVG at $100,300-$100,600)
    between entry and TP1. Price may react there.

    Revised TP1: Bearish FVG bottom $100,300 - buffer = $100,150
    R:R to TP1: ($100,150 - $98,870) / ($98,870 - $98,300) = 2.25 → FAILS

    This means: the nearest clean target doesn't give 3:1.

    Options:
    a) Tighten entry: use 5m OB within the 15m zone for tighter entry
       5m OB at $98,830-$98,850, entry at $98,840
       New SL: still $98,300 (HTF structure)
       R:R to $100,150: ($100,150-$98,840)/($98,840-$98,300) = 2.43 → still fails

    b) Move SL tighter: below the 15m swing low at $98,650 + buffer = $98,580
       R:R to $100,150: ($100,150-$98,870)/($98,870-$98,580) = 4.41 → PASSES ✓
       But: is this SL structural? The 4H OB extends to $98,400.
       SL at $98,580 is within the 4H OB zone → price may wick there
       and not invalidate the thesis. Risk of SL_TOO_TIGHT.

       Compromise: SL below the 1H swing low at $98,500 + buffer = $98,420
       R:R: ($100,150-$98,870)/($98,870-$98,420) = 2.84 → close but fails

    c) Accept the wider SL ($98,300) and use TP1 at EQH $101,050:
       The Bearish FVG at $100,300-$100,600 is a hurdle but not
       necessarily a reversal zone (it's tested once, B-tier quality).
       If the thesis is correct, price should push through it.
       R:R: 3.82 → PASSES ✓

    Decision: Option C. TP1 at $101,050 (EQH target, higher certainty).
    Accept that the Bearish FVG is a potential resistance but the
    bullish momentum from the setup should carry through.

    TP2: Weekly DoL at $108,000 (very far — use trailing instead)
    Or: Daily FVG at $103,000 as TP2 → more realistic.

    Confluences:
    1. Weekly bullish ✓
    2. Daily bullish + BOS confirmed ✓
    3. Price at Daily OB zone ✓
    4. 4H pullback to OTE zone (price at ~64% retracement) ✓
    5. 1H CHoCH confirmed with displacement ✓
    6. 15m MSS + BOS ✓
    7. NY AM Kill Zone (Tuesday) ✓
    8. PDL swept yesterday (SSL taken) ✓
    Count: 8 confluences → well above minimum of 3

    Confidence: 50 + 15(trend) + 10(OB quality) + 10(sweep) + 8(KZ)
      + 5(OTE) + 5(volume) - 0(no deductions) = 103 → cap at ~85%

    Final Signal:
    BUY BTC/USDT at $98,870, SL $98,300, TP1 $101,050, TP2 $103,000
    R:R 3.82:1 to TP1, Confidence 83%, 8 confluences
    Entry Model: A (OTE + OB), NY AM Tuesday

================================================================================
END OF [TOP_DOWN_WORKFLOW]
================================================================================





================================================================================
[ENTRY_MODELS]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Entry Models are the final execution layer of the ICT system. After the
top-down workflow (Section 12) has established the bias, identified the
zone, and confirmed entry readiness, the Entry Model defines EXACTLY how
to enter the trade: where, how, and under what specific conditions.

There are 6 Entry Models. Each one describes a specific market condition
and the precise way to execute within that condition. They are not
interchangeable — each applies to a different scenario. The system must
identify which model matches the current setup and apply that model's
specific rules.

The models are ordered by frequency of use and reliability balance:
  Model A: OTE + OB (most common — the workhorse)
  Model B: Liquidity Sweep + FVG (strongest — highest confluence)
  Model C: BOS Pullback (safest — lowest risk)
  Model D: AMD Session (time-dependent — Kill Zone specific)
  Model E: Silver Bullet (time-window — very specific)
  Model F: CHoCH Reversal (highest R:R — highest risk)

Every model shares the same core requirements:
  - HTF bias established (Daily + at least one other TF agrees)
  - Price at or near an institutional zone (PD Array)
  - LTF confirmation present (structure shift on Entry TF)
  - Minimum 3 confluences
  - Actual R:R to structural TP1 is disclosed (optional user filter only)
  - Active Kill Zone (or at minimum, not in a Dead Zone)

The difference between models is HOW the setup forms and WHAT
specific evidence triggers the entry.

================================================================================
13.1  MODEL A: OTE + ORDER BLOCK ENTRY
================================================================================

THE WORKHORSE. This is the most frequently occurring entry model.
It captures the classic ICT entry: price pulls back to an OB in the
OTE zone during a confirmed trend, and the system enters on LTF
confirmation at that zone.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN MODEL A APPLIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - A trend is CONFIRMED on HTF (2+ BOS in the trend direction)
  - Price has made an impulse and is now PULLING BACK
  - The pullback reaches an OB that sits within the OTE zone
    (62-79% retracement of the impulse leg)
  - LTF confirms that the pullback is ending (CHoCH/BOS in trend dir)

  This corresponds to MMM Phase 3 (pullback within markup/markdown)
  and Phase 4 (re-accumulation/re-distribution).

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS (ALL must be met)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. HTF TREND: Daily or 4H showing confirmed trend (2+ HH/HL or LH/LL)
     The trend must be established, not just beginning.

  2. IMPULSE LEG: a clear impulse move in the trend direction occurred
     on 4H or 1H, creating OBs and FVGs along the way.

  3. PULLBACK: price is currently retracing against the impulse.
     Pullback characteristics:
       - Volume LOWER than impulse volume (vol_ratio < 1.0)
       - Candle sizes SMALLER than impulse candles
       - No displacement in the pullback direction

  4. OB IN OTE: the pullback has reached (or is reaching) an OB
     that is within the OTE zone of the impulse leg.
     OTE zone = 62-79% retracement measured from the impulse extreme.
     The OB must be B-tier or higher quality.

  5. LTF CONFIRMATION: on the Entry TF (15m/5m), price shows:
     - CHoCH in the trend direction (MSS), OR
     - BOS in the trend direction, OR
     - Rejection candle (wick > 2× body) at the OB zone
     with displacement characteristics

  6. TIMING: active Kill Zone or at minimum not in a Dead Zone.

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  PREFERRED: FVG CE within the OB zone (if FVG overlaps with OB)
  ALTERNATIVE: OB CE (if no FVG overlap)
  AGGRESSIVE: OB edge (closest to the zone boundary)

  For a LONG entry at a Bullish OB in OTE:
    entry = FVG_CE or OB_CE (whichever is available)
    The entry is a limit order placed at this price, waiting
    for the pullback to reach it.

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Below the OB bottom + buffer
  Buffer = max(0.3 × ATR on Entry TF, 0.2% of price)

  If the OB is within a larger HTF OB:
    SL should be below the HTF OB bottom (not just the Entry TF OB)
    The HTF structural level is what truly invalidates the thesis.

  SL must pass minimum distance check (see RISK_ENGINE section).

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGETS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1: nearest valid unswept opposing PD Array or liquidity; report actual R:R
    For longs: nearest BSL, Bearish OB, Bearish FVG, session high
    Place TP1 0.1-0.2% before the level

  TP2: Draw on Liquidity from the Daily analysis
    Or: next major BSL/SSL beyond TP1
    Or: leave on trailing stop

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPICAL CHARACTERISTICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Frequency: NO PRESET QUOTA — valid conditions alone determine occurrence.
  Win probability: UNVERIFIED — calculate only from a complete out-of-sample journal.
  Expected R:R: VARIABLE — determined by entry, structural SL and real target.
  Best timeframe for entry: 15m (standard), 5m (sniper)
  MMM Phase: 3 (pullback within impulse) or 4 (re-accumulation)
  Confidence score: heuristic evidence label, NOT a win probability.

================================================================================
13.2  MODEL B: LIQUIDITY SWEEP + FVG ENTRY
================================================================================

THE STRONGEST MODEL. This is the highest-confluence entry because it
combines a liquidity event (sweep) with an institutional footprint
(FVG), both confirming the same directional thesis.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN MODEL B APPLIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - A clear liquidity level exists (BSL/SSL, EQH/EQL, PDH/PDL)
  - Price SWEEPS this level (wick beyond, body returns — section 7.3)
  - Displacement occurs AWAY from the swept level
  - The displacement creates an FVG
  - The FVG aligns with HTF bias

  This is the ICT bread-and-butter setup. Sweep → Displacement → FVG.

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS (ALL must be met)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. IDENTIFIED LIQUIDITY: a clear, significant liquidity level exists.
     BSL above (for bearish setup) or SSL below (for bullish setup).
     Significance: MODERATE or MAJOR (not MINOR).
     Preferably EQH/EQL (densest liquidity) or session level (PDH/PDL).

  2. SWEEP CONFIRMED: price breaches the level and returns.
     Sweep characteristics (from Section 7.3):
       - Wick extends beyond the level
       - Body closes back inside (or barely beyond)
       - 1-3 candles beyond maximum
       - Volume spike on the breach candle (stops triggered)

  3. DISPLACEMENT AFTER SWEEP: within 1-5 candles of the sweep:
     A displacement candle forms in the OPPOSITE direction:
       range > 2× ATR, body > 70%, vol_ratio > 1.5

  4. FVG CREATED: the displacement creates a Fair Value Gap.
     The FVG is in the reversal direction (opposite of sweep).
     The FVG must be of B-tier quality or higher.

  5. HTF BIAS ALIGNMENT: the reversal direction matches HTF bias.
     If HTF is bullish: SSL was swept → displacement UP → Bullish FVG
     If HTF is bearish: BSL was swept → displacement DOWN → Bearish FVG

  6. TIMING: active Kill Zone strongly preferred.
     Sweeps during Kill Zones have highest probability of genuine
     reversal (institutional participation).

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  PRIMARY: FVG CE (Consequent Encroachment of the FVG created by
  the post-sweep displacement)

  This is a limit order:
    For bullish: buy limit at FVG CE (waiting for price to pull
    back into the FVG after the displacement up)
    For bearish: sell limit at FVG CE

  AGGRESSIVE: FVG edge (nearer to the sweep extreme)
  CONSERVATIVE: wait for price to enter FVG and show rejection candle

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Beyond the sweep extreme + buffer

  For bullish (SSL swept):
    SL = sweep low - buffer
    The sweep low is the lowest wick during the sweep.
    If price returns below this point, the sweep has failed
    (it was a Run, not a Sweep) → thesis invalidated.

  Buffer = max(0.3 × ATR, 0.2% of price)

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGETS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1: nearest valid unswept opposing PD Array or liquidity; report actual R:R
    Often the opposite side of a range:
    If SSL was swept (below range) → TP1 toward BSL (above range)
    This creates natural 4:1+ R:R because the sweep extreme
    provides a tight SL while the opposite liquidity is far.

  TP2: the major BSL/SSL on the other side (the Draw on Liquidity)

────────────────────────────────────────
SUB-VARIANT: TURTLE SOUP (PDH/PDL SWEEP)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Turtle Soup is a specific version of Model B that targets
  Previous Day High/Low specifically.

  Additional conditions:
    The swept level MUST be PDH (for shorts) or PDL (for longs)
    Best timing: first 30-60 minutes of London KZ or NY AM KZ
    (institutions target PDH/PDL at session opens)

  Why Turtle Soup is special:
    PDH/PDL are universally visible → everyone places stops there
    → maximum liquidity concentration → highest-probability sweeps
    R:R typically 4:1 to 8:1 (very tight SL from sweep extreme,
    target on opposite side of previous day range)

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPICAL CHARACTERISTICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Frequency: NO PRESET QUOTA — valid conditions alone determine occurrence.
  Win probability: UNVERIFIED — calculate only from a complete out-of-sample journal.
  Expected R:R: VARIABLE — determined by entry, structural SL and real target.
  Best timeframe for entry: 15m (FVG detection) or 5m (tighter)
  MMM Phase: 2 (spring/UTAD) or 4 (pullback sweep of minor swing)
  Confidence score: heuristic evidence label, NOT a win probability.

================================================================================
13.3  MODEL C: BOS PULLBACK ENTRY
================================================================================

THE SAFEST MODEL. This model enters AFTER a trend is confirmed by BOS,
on the first pullback to the zone that created the BOS. It sacrifices
some R:R for maximum confirmation.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN MODEL C APPLIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - A BOS has occurred on 4H or 1H (confirming the trend)
  - The BOS impulse created an OB and/or FVG
  - Price is pulling back toward this OB/FVG
  - The pullback has NOT broken the new structural swing
    (the HL/LH created by the BOS is intact)

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS (ALL must be met)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. CONFIRMED BOS: a BOS event on 4H or 1H with quality score ≥ 14
     (Standard or Strong BOS). The BOS must have displacement.

  2. OB/FVG FROM THE BOS: the BOS impulse leg created identifiable
     zones. At minimum, an OB should exist (the last opposing candle
     before the BOS impulse). FVG is ideal but not required.

  3. PULLBACK IN PROGRESS: price is retracing toward the BOS zone.
     Pullback volume is LOW (healthy corrective, not aggressive).
     No displacement in the pullback direction.

  4. STRUCTURE INTACT: the swing that defines the new structure
     (the HL after bullish BOS, or LH after bearish BOS) has NOT
     been broken. If it breaks, the BOS may have been false.

  5. LTF CONFIRMATION: on Entry TF, confirmation that the pullback
     is ending: CHoCH/BOS in the trend direction, or rejection candle.

  6. HTF ALIGNMENT: the BOS direction matches Daily Bias.

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  At the OB/FVG created by the BOS impulse.
  Preferred: FVG CE if FVG exists, else OB CE.

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Below the structural swing (HL for longs, LH for shorts)
  that was created by the BOS.
  Plus buffer.

  This is often wider than Model A or B SL, which is why R:R
  is typically lower. The tradeoff: wider SL gives more room,
  fewer SL_TOO_TIGHT losses.

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGETS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1: the BOS level itself (the swing that was broken).
    For bullish BOS: the swing high that was broken = TP1 area.
    R:R check: this often gives exactly 3:1 (entry at pullback OB,
    SL below HL, TP1 at the broken high).

  TP2: next liquidity level beyond the BOS high.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPICAL CHARACTERISTICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Frequency: NO PRESET QUOTA — valid conditions alone determine occurrence.
  Win probability: UNVERIFIED — calculate only from a complete out-of-sample journal.
  Expected R:R: VARIABLE — determined by entry, structural SL and real target.
  Best timeframe for entry: 15m or 1H
  MMM Phase: 3 (after BOS, on the pullback) or 4
  Confidence score: heuristic evidence label, NOT a win probability.

================================================================================
13.4  MODEL D: AMD SESSION ENTRY
================================================================================

TIME-DEPENDENT MODEL. This model is specifically designed around the
Accumulation-Manipulation-Distribution cycle within a Kill Zone session.
It requires the AMD pattern to be identified in real-time.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN MODEL D APPLIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - A Kill Zone is active (London or NY AM strongly preferred)
  - The session has shown a clear AMD sequence:
    Accumulation range formed → Manipulation sweep occurred →
    Distribution displacement followed
  - The distribution phase creates entry opportunities

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS (ALL must be met)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. ACTIVE KILL ZONE: London KZ (02:00-05:00 NY) or NY AM KZ
     (08:30-11:00 NY). NY PM KZ is acceptable but lower probability.

  2. ACCUMULATION RANGE IDENTIFIED: first 4-8 candles of the KZ
     form a tight range (< 1.5× ATR total range).
     Record: Accumulation_High and Accumulation_Low.

  3. MANIPULATION OCCURRED: price broke one side of the accumulation
     range. Which side was broken determines the trade direction:
     Broke below (swept SSL) → real move is UP
     Broke above (swept BSL) → real move is DOWN
     The break should be brief (1-5 candles).

  4. DISTRIBUTION CONFIRMED: displacement in the OPPOSITE direction
     of the manipulation. This is the real session move.
     Displacement candle(s) must meet criteria from Section 2.3.

  5. HTF ALIGNMENT: the distribution direction matches Daily Bias.
     If Daily is bullish and distribution is UP → confirmed.
     If Daily is bullish but distribution is DOWN → CONFLICT → SKIP.

  6. FVG/OB FROM DISTRIBUTION: the displacement creates tradeable zones.

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  On the FVG/OB created by the distribution displacement.
  Entry at FVG CE or OB CE.

  The entry occurs when price briefly pulls back from the distribution
  displacement to fill the FVG (normal post-displacement behavior).

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Below/above the manipulation extreme.
  The manipulation extreme is the lowest point (for bullish) or
  highest point (for bearish) reached during the manipulation phase.
  Plus buffer.

  This is typically a tight SL because the manipulation extreme
  represents the absolute extreme of the false move.

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGETS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1: the opposite side of the accumulation range.
    If manipulation swept below → TP1 at or above Accumulation_High.
    This is often a quick target (within the same session).

  TP2: the session's Draw on Liquidity (PDH/PDL, Asian H/L,
    or HTF liquidity beyond the accumulation range).

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPICAL CHARACTERISTICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Frequency: NO PRESET QUOTA — valid conditions alone determine occurrence.
  Win probability: UNVERIFIED — calculate only from a complete out-of-sample journal.
  Expected R:R: VARIABLE — determined by entry, structural SL and real target.
  Best timeframe for entry: 5m or 15m
  MMM Phase: session-level Phase 2→3 (manipulation→distribution)
  Confidence score: heuristic evidence label, NOT a win probability.

================================================================================
13.5  MODEL E: SILVER BULLET ENTRY
================================================================================

TIME-WINDOW MODEL. Silver Bullet is the most time-specific entry model.
It looks for one thing: an FVG created by displacement during a specific
1-hour window.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN MODEL E APPLIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - Current time is within a Silver Bullet window:
    SB#1: 03:00-04:00 NY (London)
    SB#2: 10:00-11:00 NY (NY AM — best for the system)
    SB#3: 14:00-15:00 NY (NY PM)
  - A displacement candle forms DURING the window
  - The displacement creates an FVG
  - The FVG direction aligns with HTF bias

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS (ALL must be met)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. TIME WINDOW: candle timestamp converts to NY time within one
     of the three SB windows.

  2. DISPLACEMENT IN WINDOW: a candle within the window meets
     displacement criteria (range > 2× ATR, body > 70%, vol > 1.5×).

  3. FVG CREATED: the displacement creates a detectable FVG
     (3-candle check from Section 6.1/6.2).

  4. DIRECTION ALIGNMENT: FVG direction matches Daily Bias.
     Bullish FVG during bullish Daily Bias → valid.
     Bearish FVG during bearish Daily Bias → valid.
     FVG against Daily Bias → INVALID (skip).

  5. HTF CONTEXT: price should be at or near a HTF PD Array.
     Silver Bullet is strongest when the displacement occurs
     AT a HTF zone (the zone causes the displacement).
     Silver Bullet in open space (no HTF level) → lower confidence.

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  At FVG CE. Always.
  This is the defining characteristic of Silver Bullet:
  displacement creates FVG → enter at CE when price returns.

  Limit order placed at CE immediately after the FVG is detected.

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Below/above the FVG extreme + buffer.
  For bullish SB: SL below FVG_bottom - buffer.
  For bearish SB: SL above FVG_top + buffer.

  If the FVG is large (> 0.5% of price), the SL may be too wide.
  In this case: use the CE of the FVG as entry and SL at the
  opposite edge of the FVG (tighter, but risks partial fill).

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGETS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1: nearest valid unswept structural level/liquidity; report actual R:R.
  TP2: session Draw on Liquidity.

  Silver Bullet trades are typically INTRADAY and complete within
  the current session. TP2 should be reachable within 4-8 hours.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPICAL CHARACTERISTICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Frequency: NO PRESET QUOTA — valid conditions alone determine occurrence.
  Win probability: UNVERIFIED — calculate only from a complete out-of-sample journal.
  Expected R:R: VARIABLE — determined by entry, structural SL and real target.
  Best timeframe for entry: 15m (SB#2, SB#3) or 5m (SB#1)
  Confidence score: heuristic evidence label, NOT a win probability.

================================================================================
13.6  MODEL F: CHoCH REVERSAL ENTRY
================================================================================

THE HIGHEST R:R AND HIGHEST RISK MODEL. This model enters at the very
beginning of a potential trend reversal, immediately after a CHoCH at
a major HTF level. It catches the bottom (for longs) or top (for shorts)
of a move, which gives exceptional R:R but carries the highest risk
because the reversal is not yet confirmed by BOS.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHEN MODEL F APPLIES
────────────────────────────────────────
  - Price is at a MAJOR HTF level (Daily/Weekly OB, major FVG)
  - A CHoCH has occurred at this level with DISPLACEMENT
  - This is likely MMM Phase 2 (Spring or UTAD)
  - The CHoCH is the first signal of a potential cycle reversal

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS (ALL must be met — STRICT)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. MAJOR HTF LEVEL: price must be at a Daily or Weekly PD Array.
     4H level is NOT sufficient for Model F.
     The reversal thesis requires major institutional significance.

  2. CHoCH WITH DISPLACEMENT: the CHoCH candle(s) must show
     displacement (Grade B or higher from Section 2.3).
     CHoCH without displacement → too uncertain for Model F.

  3. FIRST STRUCTURAL RESPONSE: after the CHoCH, the first
     swing in the new direction forms:
     For bullish reversal: first HL forms after the CHoCH
     For bearish reversal: first LH forms after the CHoCH

  4. LIQUIDITY SWEPT: the move that preceded the CHoCH should have
     swept a significant liquidity level:
     For bullish reversal: SSL was swept at/below the HTF level
     For bearish reversal: BSL was swept at/above the HTF level
     The sweep provides the institutional logic (liquidity was taken
     to fill reversal orders).

  5. LTF BOS CONFIRMS: on the Entry TF, a BOS in the new direction
     must confirm the CHoCH. CHoCH alone is not enough.

  6. POSITION SIZE: 50% of normal. This is mandatory for Model F.
     The trade is counter to the prior trend until Phase 3 confirms.

  For HTF REVERSAL TRADE (against Daily Bias):
     Additional requirements from SYSTEM_CORE Rule 4:
     - Weekly/Monthly OB (not just Daily)
     - Confidence ≥ 75%
     - Actual R:R disclosed; any min_rr is an explicit user policy
     - Tagged as HTF_REVERSAL_TRADE
     - Frequency ≤ 10% of total trades

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  On the OB/FVG created by the CHoCH displacement.
  After the CHoCH, the first pullback (forming the HL/LH) targets
  the zone where the CHoCH displacement originated.

  Entry: OB CE or FVG CE from the CHoCH displacement candle(s).

────────────────────────────────────────
STOP LOSS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Beyond the extreme of the move that preceded the CHoCH.

  For bullish reversal (CHoCH up after a downtrend):
    SL below the lowest point of the entire decline that ended
    at the HTF level. This is the "if price goes below HERE,
    the entire reversal thesis is wrong."

  This can be a WIDE SL. That is why the R:R must be ≥ 4:1.

────────────────────────────────────────
TARGETS
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1: the first significant structural level in the new direction.
    For bullish reversal: the swing high that defined the last LH
    before the reversal (the "old resistance" that becomes the first target).

  TP2: the major Draw on Liquidity in the new direction.
    If this is a genuine reversal from a Weekly OB, TP2 can be very far
    (the weekly DoL). R:R to TP2 can exceed 10:1 or even 20:1.

────────────────────────────────────────
TYPICAL CHARACTERISTICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Frequency: NO PRESET QUOTA — valid conditions alone determine occurrence.
  Win probability: UNVERIFIED — calculate only from a complete out-of-sample journal.
  Expected R:R: VARIABLE — determined by entry, structural SL and real target.
  Best timeframe for entry: 15m or 1H (5m is too noisy for reversals)
  MMM Phase: 2 (Spring/UTAD)
  Confidence score: heuristic evidence label, NOT a win probability.

  PROFITABILITY NOTE:
  Despite the lower win rate, Model F is highly profitable because
  the R:R compensates:
    55% win rate × 7:1 average R:R = excellent expected value
    The few losses are small (2% risk × 50% size = 1% per loss)
    The wins are large (7× the risk amount)

================================================================================
13.7  MODEL COMPARISON AND SELECTION
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
QUICK REFERENCE TABLE
────────────────────────────────────────

  Model │ Frequency │ Win%    │ R:R      │ Risk    │ Best Phase
  ──────┼───────────┼─────────┼──────────┼─────────┼───────────
    A   │ High      │ 65-75%  │ 3-6:1    │ Medium  │ Phase 3/4
    B   │ Moderate  │ 70-80%  │ 4-8:1    │ Low-Med │ Phase 2/4
    C   │ High      │ 65-70%  │ 3-5:1    │ Low     │ Phase 3
    D   │ Moderate  │ 70-75%  │ 3-6:1    │ Medium  │ Session AMD
    E   │ Low-Mod   │ 65-72%  │ 3-5:1    │ Medium  │ SB Window
    F   │ Low       │ 55-65%  │ 5-15:1   │ High    │ Phase 2

────────────────────────────────────────
SELECTION LOGIC
────────────────────────────────────────
The system does not "choose" a model arbitrarily. The market conditions
determine which model applies:

  Was liquidity just swept with displacement?
    YES → Model B (or Model B Turtle Soup if PDH/PDL)
    NO → continue

  Is there a confirmed BOS with a pullback to OB/FVG?
    YES → Is the OB in OTE zone?
      YES → Model A
      NO → Model C
    NO → continue

  Is a Kill Zone active with clear AMD pattern?
    YES → Model D
    NO → continue

  Is current time within a Silver Bullet window with FVG?
    YES → Model E
    NO → continue

  Is price at a major HTF level with CHoCH + displacement?
    YES → Model F
    NO → HOLD (no model applies)

  In practice, multiple models may apply simultaneously:
    A sweep occurs (Model B) at an OB in OTE zone (Model A)
    during NY AM KZ AMD (Model D) within SB#2 window (Model E).

    When this happens: the setup has MAXIMUM confluence.
    Classify under the PRIMARY model (Model B in this case,
    since the sweep is the most defining feature) and note
    the others as supporting confluences. Confidence is elevated.

================================================================================
13.8  HOLD CONDITIONS — WHEN NO MODEL APPLIES
================================================================================

If the workflow reaches this section and no model matches:
the answer is HOLD.

────────────────────────────────────────
COMPREHENSIVE HOLD CHECKLIST
────────────────────────────────────────

CONFLUENCE FAILURES:
  □ Fewer than 3 independent confluences verified
  □ Confluences are weak or ambiguous
  □ Multiple "maybe" signals instead of clear "yes" signals

STRUCTURAL FAILURES:
  □ No clear HTF trend (Daily ranging or unclear)
  □ No confirmed BOS or CHoCH on any relevant TF
  □ LTF has not confirmed (no MSS/BOS on Entry TF)
  □ Structure is transitional (between phases)

ZONE FAILURES:
  □ Price is not at any institutional level (open space)
  □ The nearest OB/FVG is too far for current entry
  □ Available OBs/FVGs are C-tier or below quality
  □ Price is at EQ (50%) — no P/D advantage

RISK FAILURES:
  □ User-configured min_rr policy is not met (do not move TP1)
  □ SL distance below minimum threshold
  □ Unswept liquidity between entry and SL
  □ TP1 path blocked by major opposing PD Array

TIMING FAILURES:
  □ NY Lunch Dead Zone (12:00-13:30 NY)
  □ Post-Market (16:00-18:00 NY)
  □ Asian Session (18:00-02:00 NY) without London transition setup
  □ Weekend (Saturday-Sunday)
  □ Friday after 14:00 NY
  □ Major news event imminent (within 60 minutes)

CONFIDENCE FAILURES:
  □ Calculated confidence < 60%
  □ The system is uncertain and would need to stretch analysis
  □ Multiple analytical elements are "probable" rather than "confirmed"

DATA FAILURES:
  □ Insufficient candle data to establish structure
  □ Missing timeframe data
  □ Data quality issues noted

────────────────────────────────────────
THE HOLD OUTPUT
────────────────────────────────────────
When HOLD is the result, the system provides actionable context:

  {
    signal: "HOLD",
    reason: "1H has not confirmed structural shift at 4H OB zone.
             Price is at the zone but no CHoCH/BOS on Entry TF yet.",
    what_to_watch: "Monitor 15m for CHoCH above $99,150 (last LH).
                    If this occurs during NY AM KZ, re-evaluate.",
    potential_setup: "Model A (OTE + OB) forming at 4H OB $98,700.
                      Needs LTF confirmation to activate.",
    nearest_levels: {
      demand: "$98,700-$98,900 (4H OB, A-tier, fresh)",
      supply: "$101,200-$101,400 (4H Bearish FVG)",
      bsl: "$101,500 (EQH triple)",
      ssl: "$97,800 (4H swing low)"
    },
    reassess_at: "Next candle close, or when price enters the OB zone",
    confidence_if_confirmed: "~78% (strong setup pending confirmation)"
  }

This HOLD output tells the user exactly:
  - WHY no signal now
  - WHAT would change the assessment
  - WHERE the key levels are
  - WHEN to check again

HOLD is not "I don't know." HOLD is "I know exactly what needs to
happen, and it hasn't happened yet."

================================================================================
13.9  ENTRY MODEL EXECUTION CHECKLIST
================================================================================

Before outputting ANY signal, the system runs this final checklist:

  ── PRE-SIGNAL VERIFICATION ──

  □ 1. Daily Bias is clear and matches signal direction
  □ 2. At least one HTF (4H or Daily) PD Array supports the zone
  □ 3. Entry TF shows structural confirmation (CHoCH/BOS)
  □ 4. Entry Model conditions are ALL met (not partial)
  □ 5. Entry price is at a specific, justified level
  □ 6. SL is at a structural invalidation level + buffer
  □ 7. SL passes minimum distance check
  □ 8. SL has no unswept liquidity between it and entry
  □ 9. TP1 is at a real structural/liquidity level
  □ 10. TP1 at a real unswept level; actual R:R calculated and disclosed
  □ 11. TP2 is identified (DoL or HTF target)
  □ 12. Confluence count ≥ 3
  □ 13. Confidence ≥ 60%
  □ 14. Timing is appropriate (not Dead Zone)
  □ 15. Position size is calculated per confidence level
  □ 16. No rule from SYSTEM_CORE is violated

  All 16 must pass → OUTPUT SIGNAL
  Any single failure → HOLD

  This checklist is not bureaucratic overhead. It is the final
  quality gate that prevents the system from outputting a signal
  that violates any principle of the methodology.

================================================================================
END OF [ENTRY_MODELS]
================================================================================






================================================================================
[DUAL_TARGET_MANAGEMENT]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
This section defines the complete trade management system from entry to
exit. The dual-target approach splits every trade into two portions:
50% targets TP1 (the secure profit) and 50% targets TP2 (the runner).

This design solves the classic trading dilemma: "Should I take profit
now or let it run?" The answer is BOTH. Take half at the first logical
target to lock in profit, then let the other half run with a trailing
stop to capture larger moves when they occur.

The system is mechanical after entry. There is no discretion in the
management rules. This eliminates emotional decision-making during
the trade — the plan is set at entry and executed without deviation.

================================================================================
14.1  THE DUAL-TARGET PHILOSOPHY
================================================================================

WHY TWO TARGETS:

Single target systems have fundamental weaknesses:
