  - Single TP too close: captures small wins but misses big moves.
    On a day when price moves 5:1 in your favor, you closed at 3:1.
  - Single TP too far: aims for big wins but gives back many trades.
    Price reaches 4:1 in your favor but reverses, hitting BE or SL.

The dual-target system captures BOTH outcomes:
  TP1 (close): locks in guaranteed profit on 50% of the position.
  TP2 (far): captures the extended move on the remaining 50%.

The mathematical advantage:
  If TP1 hits but TP2 doesn't → you still profit (WIN_PARTIAL)
  If both TP1 and TP2 hit → maximum profit (WIN_FULL)
  If neither hits → standard loss (LOSS)

  The WIN_PARTIAL outcome is the key innovation. It converts what
  would be a breakeven or loss (in a single-target system) into a
  profitable outcome. Over 100 trades, this significantly improves
  the equity curve.

THE SPLIT:
  50% of position → TP1 (first target)
  50% of position → TP2 (second target, managed with trailing stop)

  Why 50/50? It is the simplest and most balanced approach:
  - 60/40 (more on TP1): captures more profit early but reduces
    the runner. Better for choppy markets.
  - 40/60 (more on TP2): maximizes runners but takes less profit
    early. Better for trending markets.
  - 50/50: works in all conditions. This is the default.

  The system uses 50/50 universally. Adjusting the split based on
  market conditions adds complexity without proven benefit for
  an automated analytical system.

================================================================================
14.2  TP1: THE FIRST TARGET
================================================================================

TP1 secures partial profit at the nearest VALID, UNSWEPT structural level in
the trade direction: BSL/SSL, EQH/EQL, a session extreme, an opposing active
PD Array, or a genuine swing. Select the level first, then compute and disclose
R:R. Never ignore nearby liquidity or stretch TP1 merely to manufacture 3R.

VALIDATION:
  - Level is identifiable in the available OHLC data.
  - It has not already been swept/invalidated.
  - It is ahead of entry in the trade direction.
  - It is appropriate to the expected holding horizon.
  - A small before-level execution margin is a configurable bot policy, not a
    universal Michael rule for every instrument.

If no valid level exists, do not invent TP1 and do not issue a trade plan. A
user may configure min_rr as a separate execution policy; failure of that
policy means skip the trade, not move the target.

================================================================================
14.3  TP2: THE SECOND TARGET
================================================================================

TP2 is the RUNNER target. It captures the extended move when the market
continues beyond TP1. TP2 is less precise than TP1 — it represents the
ideal outcome, and reaching it is a bonus, not an expectation.

────────────────────────────────────────
TP2 SELECTION
────────────────────────────────────────
TP2 is based on the Draw on Liquidity or a major HTF target:

  Option A: DRAW ON LIQUIDITY (preferred)
    The DoL identified in the Daily analysis (Section 12.3).
    For longs: the major BSL above (EQH, major swing high, PWH)
    For shorts: the major SSL below (EQL, major swing low, PWL)

  Option B: HTF PD ARRAY
    A major opposing PD Array on Daily or Weekly:
    For longs: Daily/Weekly Bearish OB or FVG above
    For shorts: Daily/Weekly Bullish OB or FVG below

  Option C: OPEN (trailing stop only)
    If no clear TP2 level exists, leave TP2 as "trailing."
    The 50% runner stays open with a trailing stop that follows
    structure until stopped out. This captures the maximum possible
    move without a predetermined exit level.

TP2 has no fixed R:R requirement. It must be distinct and beyond TP1 with
independent structural confirmation. If evidence is insufficient, use
OPEN_TRAILING rather than a numeric TP2.

────────────────────────────────────────
TP2 PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP2 = the DoL or HTF level - margin (same 0.1-0.2% as TP1)
  Or: TP2 = OPEN (trailing stop manages exit)

  If using a fixed TP2 level:
    Place the exit order at the level.
    Also maintain a trailing stop in case price reverses before TP2.
    Whichever hits first → exits the runner.

================================================================================
14.4  BREAKEVEN (BE) MECHANISM
================================================================================

The BE mechanism is the core risk elimination tool. After TP1 is hit,
the stop loss for the remaining 50% moves to the entry price. This
transforms the trade from "at risk" to "risk-free."

────────────────────────────────────────
BE TRIGGER
────────────────────────────────────────
  WHEN: immediately after TP1 is filled.
  ACTION: move SL for the remaining 50% to ENTRY PRICE.

  Not to entry + 1 pip. Not to entry - buffer. To ENTRY PRICE exactly.
  The runner is now completely free:
    If price continues to TP2 → WIN_FULL (maximum profit)
    If price reverses to entry → WIN_PARTIAL (TP1 profit secured)
    In both cases: no additional risk on the remaining portion.

────────────────────────────────────────
CRITICAL: DO NOT MOVE TO BE BEFORE TP1
────────────────────────────────────────
  Moving SL to BE before TP1 hits is a common mistake.

  "Price moved halfway to TP1, let me secure profits by moving SL to BE."

  This sounds prudent but is mathematically destructive:
    Price often pulls back to the entry zone before reaching TP1.
    This is normal market behavior (impulse → pullback → continuation).
    A premature BE stop gets triggered during a normal pullback,
    resulting in a $0 trade on a setup that would have hit TP1.

  Over 100 trades, premature BE moves convert approximately 15-25%
  of winning trades into breakeven trades. That is 15-25 wins
  destroyed by premature management.

  THE RULE:
    Before TP1: SL stays at its ORIGINAL position. Do not move it.
    After TP1: SL moves to entry. Then trailing begins.
    No exceptions. No "but this time it looks like it might reverse."

  The ONLY exception to this rule:
    If a CHoCH with displacement occurs AGAINST the trade direction
    on the 1H timeframe while the trade is open and TP1 has not hit:
      → The structural thesis may be failing
      → The system may note: "1H CHoCH against trade — consider
        manual exit or moving SL to reduce risk"
      → This is a DISCRETIONARY override, not a standard rule
      → It should be rare and documented when used

================================================================================
14.5  TRAILING STOP FOR THE RUNNER
================================================================================

After TP1 hits and SL moves to BE, the remaining 50% needs active
management to capture as much of the move as possible while protecting
the secured position.

────────────────────────────────────────
METHOD 1: STRUCTURE TRAIL (DEFAULT — RECOMMENDED)
────────────────────────────────────────
The trailing stop follows structural swing points on the Entry TF
or one TF higher.

For LONG trades:
  After TP1 hits, as price continues up:
    Every time a new CONFIRMED Higher Low forms on the Entry TF:
      Move trailing SL to just below this new HL
      (below HL price - buffer)

    As price makes new HH → new HL forms → trail SL below new HL
    The SL follows the trend structure, staying below each
    successive HL.

    When the trend eventually breaks (price drops below the last HL):
      The trailing SL is hit → runner exits with profit
      The exit level = the last HL - buffer

For SHORT trades:
  Mirror: trail above each new confirmed Lower High.

Why structure trail is best:
  - It keeps you in the trade as long as the trend structure holds
  - It exits you when the trend actually breaks (CHoCH)
  - It is based on market structure, not arbitrary distances
  - It gives maximum room for normal pullbacks while protecting
    against genuine reversals

How to identify "confirmed HL/LH" for trailing:
  Use N=2 on the Entry TF (faster confirmation for trailing)
  The HL must be confirmed with 2 candles after it before
  moving the trailing SL.

────────────────────────────────────────
METHOD 2: ATR TRAIL (ALTERNATIVE)
────────────────────────────────────────
For LONG trades:
  trailing_sl = highest_price_since_entry - (1.5 × ATR on Entry TF)

  As price makes new highs:
    highest_price updates
    trailing_sl moves up (following the highest price minus ATR buffer)
    trailing_sl only moves UP, never down

  When price drops 1.5× ATR from its highest point:
    trailing_sl is hit → runner exits

For SHORT trades:
  trailing_sl = lowest_price_since_entry + (1.5 × ATR)
  Moves down only, never up.

Why 1.5× ATR:
  1.0× ATR: too tight, gets hit by normal volatility
  2.0× ATR: too loose, gives back too much profit
  1.5× ATR: balanced — allows normal pullbacks while catching reversals

When to use ATR trail instead of structure trail:
  - When structure is unclear or forming too slowly
  - When the Entry TF swings are unreliable (very volatile market)
  - When the system needs a simpler, more mechanical approach

────────────────────────────────────────
METHOD 3: CANDLE TRAIL (SIMPLE)
────────────────────────────────────────
For LONG trades:
  After each confirmed candle close on the Entry TF:
    trailing_sl = previous_candle.low - buffer

  As new candles form:
    trailing_sl = the low of the most recently closed candle - buffer
    Only moves up (if new candle's low is above current trailing_sl)
    Stays at current level if new candle's low is below

For SHORT trades:
  trailing_sl = previous_candle.high + buffer

When to use candle trail:
  - Fastest moving trail (tightest)
  - Best for very fast-moving markets (strong displacement phases)
  - Captures less of the move than structure trail but locks in
    profit faster

────────────────────────────────────────
DEFAULT RECOMMENDATION
────────────────────────────────────────
  Use STRUCTURE TRAIL as default for all trades.
  Switch to ATR TRAIL if structure is unclear or too slow to form.
  Use CANDLE TRAIL only in extremely fast-moving conditions
  (rare — typically during high-impact news moves).

================================================================================
14.6  OUTCOME CLASSIFICATION
================================================================================

Every completed trade receives exactly ONE classification. This
classification drives statistics, learning, and system improvement.

────────────────────────────────────────
WIN_FULL 🏆
────────────────────────────────────────
  Definition: TP1 AND TP2 both hit.

  What happened:
    Entry filled → price moved toward TP1 → TP1 hit (50% closed)
    → SL moved to entry → price continued → TP2 hit (50% closed)

  P/L calculation:
    Profit = (50% × |TP1 - Entry|) + (50% × |TP2 - Entry|)
    This is the maximum planned profit.

  Statistics: counts as WIN
  Learning: analyze what made this setup run to completion.
    Was the trend exceptionally strong?
    Was the DoL particularly clear?
    Replicate these conditions.

────────────────────────────────────────
WIN_TRAIL ✅
────────────────────────────────────────
  Definition: TP1 hit, trailing stop hit before TP2.

  What happened:
    TP1 hit → SL to entry → price continued past TP1 → trailing stop
    followed the move → price eventually reversed → trailing stop hit
    → runner exited at a level between TP1 and TP2.

  P/L calculation:
    Profit = (50% × |TP1 - Entry|) + (50% × |Trail_Exit - Entry|)
    Trail_Exit > Entry (always, by definition of trailing up from BE)
    Total profit is between WIN_PARTIAL and WIN_FULL.

  Statistics: counts as WIN
  Learning: was the trailing method appropriate?
    Trail too tight → exited too early (price continued after stop)
    Trail too loose → gave back unnecessary profit
    Adjust trailing method if pattern repeats.

────────────────────────────────────────
WIN_PARTIAL ✅ ← CRITICAL CLASSIFICATION
────────────────────────────────────────
  Definition: TP1 hit, price returned to entry, BE stop hit.

  What happened:
    TP1 hit → 50% closed at profit → SL moved to entry (BE)
    → price reversed → came all the way back → hit BE stop
    → remaining 50% closed at entry (zero on this portion)

  P/L calculation:
    Profit = (50% × |TP1 - Entry|) + (50% × $0) = 50% × TP1 profit
    NET RESULT IS POSITIVE.

  ⚠️ THIS IS NOT A LOSS. THIS IS NOT A LOSS. THIS IS NOT A LOSS.

  In every statistic, report, and assessment:
    WIN_PARTIAL counts as a WIN
    WIN_PARTIAL adds to gross profit (not gross loss)
    WIN_PARTIAL contributes positively to profit factor
    WIN_PARTIAL is evidence that the SYSTEM WORKED:
      The entry was correct (price moved to TP1)
      TP1 was correctly placed (it was reached)
      Risk management worked (SL moved to BE, no loss on runner)
      The only "failure" was TP2 ambition (not a real failure)

  A high WIN_PARTIAL rate (>40% of all wins):
    May indicate TP2 targets are consistently too ambitious
    OR: may indicate the market is choppy (reaching TP1 then reversing)
    Either way: the system is PROFITABLE despite not reaching TP2
    Consider adjusting: TP2 closer, or trailing method tighter

────────────────────────────────────────
BREAKEVEN ➖
────────────────────────────────────────
  Definition: trade closed at entry without TP1 hitting.

  What happened:
    Two possible scenarios:

    Scenario A: discretionary BE move
      The system detected concerning signs (1H CHoCH against trade)
      SL was moved to entry as a precaution before TP1
      Price hit the BE stop → trade closed flat

    Scenario B: time-based exit
      The trade has been open for an extended period (>24 hours for
      intraday setups) without reaching TP1 or SL
      The setup context has changed (new day, different conditions)
      Trade closed at entry manually

  P/L: $0

  Statistics: counts as NEUTRAL (not win, not loss)
  Learning:
    Follow-up analysis: did the trade eventually reach TP1/TP2?
    If yes → the BE was premature (patience issue)
    If no → the BE was correct (market changed)
    Track the follow-up to improve future management decisions.

────────────────────────────────────────
LOSS ❌
────────────────────────────────────────
  Definition: original SL hit before TP1.
  This is the ONLY classification where real capital is lost.

  What happened:
    Entry filled → price moved AGAINST the trade → hit original SL
    → full risk amount lost

  P/L: negative (= Account × Risk% for this trade)

  Statistics: counts as LOSS
  Learning: MANDATORY detailed analysis (see SELF_LEARNING section)
    Every loss must be classified into one of these error types:
      WRONG_DIRECTION: HTF bias was misread
      SL_TOO_TIGHT: correct direction but SL placed too close
      STOP_HUNT: SL swept, then price went in our direction
      BAD_TIMING: right setup, wrong time
      FABRICATED_SETUP: setup was not genuinely present
      COUNTER_TREND: traded against Daily Bias (rule violation)
      NO_CONFIRMATION: entered without LTF confirmation
      EARLY_ENTRY: entered before price reached the zone
      OVEREXTENDED: entered too late in the move (chased)

────────────────────────────────────────
NO_FILL ⏭️
────────────────────────────────────────
  Definition: price never reached the entry level.

  What happened:
    Signal given with a limit order at a specific price.
    Price moved in the anticipated direction without pulling back
    to the entry level. The order was never executed.

  P/L: $0

  Statistics: counts as NEUTRAL
  Learning:
    Was the entry too conservative? (placed too deep in the zone)
    Would CE entry have filled where edge entry didn't?
    Track NO_FILL rate:
      > 30% of signals → entries are consistently too ambitious
      < 10% of signals → entries may be too aggressive (consider
        deeper entries for better R:R)
      10-25% → normal range

================================================================================
14.7  THE COMPLETE TRADE LIFECYCLE
================================================================================

Every trade passes through these phases in order:

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 1: ANALYSIS → SIGNAL
────────────────────────────────────────
  Top-down workflow (Section 12) produces a signal.
  All checks pass. Entry Model is identified.
  Signal includes: entry, SL, TP1, TP2, confidence, confluences.

  Duration: the analysis itself (minutes).

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 2: ORDER PLACEMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
  Limit order placed at the entry price.
  SL order placed at the SL price.
  TP1 order placed for 50% of position at TP1 price.

  Duration: until entry fills or the setup expires.
  Setup expiration: if the setup context changes (new BOS against,
  CHoCH against, or price structure evolves past the zone), cancel
  the order. Typically: 24 hours maximum for intraday setups.

  If order doesn't fill within the valid window → NO_FILL.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 3: ENTRY → PRE-TP1 (HANDS OFF)
────────────────────────────────────────
  Entry filled. Trade is live. SL is set. TP1 is set.

  RULES DURING THIS PHASE:
    DO NOT move SL (not to BE, not wider, not tighter)
    DO NOT close early (even if uncomfortable)
    DO NOT add to position
    DO NOT remove TP1

  The ONLY action during this phase:
    Monitor for 1H CHoCH with displacement against the trade.
    If this occurs → discretionary early exit is acceptable
    (but should be rare and documented).

  Duration: minutes to hours (typically 1-8 hours for intraday).

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 4: TP1 HIT → BREAKEVEN
────────────────────────────────────────
  TP1 fills. 50% of position closed at profit.

  IMMEDIATE ACTION:
    Move SL for remaining 50% to ENTRY PRICE.
    Activate trailing stop method (Structure Trail default).

  If TP2 is a fixed level: ensure the TP2 order is active for
  the remaining 50%.

  Duration: immediate (one action, then move to Phase 5).

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 5: POST-TP1 → TRAILING
────────────────────────────────────────
  50% running toward TP2 with trailing stop active.

  RULES DURING THIS PHASE:
    Trail SL forward following the chosen method
    SL only moves in the profit direction (toward TP2), never back
    If price reaches TP2 → close remaining 50% → WIN_FULL
    If trailing SL hits above entry → close → WIN_TRAIL
    If price returns to entry (BE) → close → WIN_PARTIAL

  Duration: hours to days (depending on TP2 distance).

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 6: EXIT → RECORD
────────────────────────────────────────
  Trade is closed (by TP2, trailing stop, or BE).

  IMMEDIATE ACTIONS:
    Record all trade data (see TRADE_JOURNAL section 18)
    Classify the outcome (WIN_FULL/WIN_TRAIL/WIN_PARTIAL/LOSS/etc.)
    Calculate actual R:R achieved
    Calculate actual P/L

  Duration: immediate.

────────────────────────────────────────
PHASE 7: POST-TRADE ANALYSIS → LEARN
────────────────────────────────────────
  After recording, the system performs post-trade analysis:

    What happened after exit? (follow-up)
      Did price continue in the trade direction?
      Did it reverse? How far?

    Was the management optimal?
      Could a different trailing method have captured more?
      Was TP1 placement correct (was it reached easily or barely)?
      Was TP2 realistic?

    If LOSS: full error analysis (SELF_LEARNING section)
    If WIN: what made it work? Can conditions be replicated?
    If WIN_PARTIAL: was TP2 too ambitious or trail too loose?
    If NO_FILL: was entry too conservative?

  This analysis feeds into the system's evolving knowledge base.

================================================================================
14.8  MANAGEMENT IN BACKTEST MODE
================================================================================

During backtesting, the system simulates the dual-target management
using future candle data (which is available in backtest but not in
live trading).

────────────────────────────────────────
BACKTEST EVALUATION PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
Given: entry_price, sl_price, tp1_price, tp2_price, direction
Given: future_candles (candles after the entry point)

Step 1: ENTRY FILL CHECK
  Scan future candles for entry fill:
    For LONG: does any candle.low ≤ entry_price? (price reached entry)
    For SHORT: does any candle.high ≥ entry_price?

  Maximum wait: 30 candles on the entry TF.
  If not filled within 30 candles → NO_FILL.

  Record: entry_fill_candle_index

Step 2: FROM ENTRY, SIMULATE TRADE
  Starting from entry_fill_candle:
  Scan each subsequent candle:

  For LONG trade:
    For each candle after entry:
      Check SL first: if candle.low ≤ sl_price → LOSS
        (SL is always checked before TP within same candle)
      Check TP1: if candle.high ≥ tp1_price → TP1 HIT
        After TP1: sl becomes entry_price (BE)
        Continue scanning for TP2:
          Check BE: if candle.low ≤ entry_price → WIN_PARTIAL
          Check TP2: if candle.high ≥ tp2_price → WIN_FULL

  For SHORT trade: mirror (check SL with high, TP with low)

  Special case: within a SINGLE candle, if both SL and TP could
  have been hit (candle range covers both levels):
    The system assumes the WORST case:
      Before TP1: assume SL hit first → LOSS
      After TP1 (BE active): assume BE hit first → WIN_PARTIAL
    This prevents optimistic bias in backtesting.

Step 3: RECORD METRICS
  MFE (Maximum Favorable Excursion):
    The furthest price moved in the trade direction before exit.
    For longs: max(high) of all candles while trade was open
    MFE_RR = (MFE - entry) / (entry - original_sl)

  MAE (Maximum Adverse Excursion):
    The furthest price moved against the trade before exit.
    For longs: min(low) of all candles while trade was open
    MAE_RR = (entry - MAE) / (entry - original_sl)

  Actual R:R: the R:R achieved at the actual exit point
  Duration: number of candles from entry to exit

Step 4: CLASSIFICATION
  Based on what was hit:
    SL before TP1 → LOSS
    TP1 then TP2 → WIN_FULL
    TP1 then BE → WIN_PARTIAL
    TP1 then trailing (simulated) → WIN_TRAIL
    Nothing within 100 candles → TIME_EXPIRED (treat as BE)

────────────────────────────────────────
TRAILING STOP SIMULATION IN BACKTEST
────────────────────────────────────────
After TP1 is hit in backtest:
  Apply structure trail using confirmed swings in the future data:
    Identify each new HL (for longs) in the post-TP1 candles
    Move simulated trailing SL below each new HL
    If price drops below a trailing SL level → exit there
    Record: trail_exit_price and trail_exit_candle

  If structure trail is complex to simulate:
    Use ATR trail as fallback:
    trailing_sl = running_max(high) - 1.5 × ATR
    If candle.low ≤ trailing_sl → exit

================================================================================
14.9  POSITION MANAGEMENT RULES SUMMARY
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
BEFORE TP1 (50% risk phase):
────────────────────────────────────────
  ✓ SL at original structural level — DO NOT MOVE
  ✓ TP1 at designated level — DO NOT MOVE
  ✗ No moving SL to BE
  ✗ No adding to position
  ✗ No closing early (except: 1H CHoCH against = discretionary exit)
  ✗ No widening SL
  ✗ No removing TP1

────────────────────────────────────────
AT TP1 (transition moment):
────────────────────────────────────────
  ✓ 50% closes automatically at TP1
  ✓ SL for remaining 50% moves to entry (BE) immediately
  ✓ Trailing stop activates
  ✓ TP2 order is set (if fixed level) or trailing manages exit

────────────────────────────────────────
AFTER TP1 (risk-free phase):
────────────────────────────────────────
  ✓ Trailing SL follows structure (or ATR/candle method)
  ✓ SL only moves FORWARD (toward profit)
  ✗ No moving SL backward (below entry = adding risk back)
  ✗ No closing early unless TP2 is reached or trailing SL is hit
  ✓ If TP2 reached → close remaining 50%
  ✓ If trailing SL hit → close remaining 50%
  ✓ If BE hit → close remaining 50% (WIN_PARTIAL, not loss)

================================================================================
END OF [DUAL_TARGET_MANAGEMENT]
================================================================================





================================================================================
[RISK_ENGINE]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Risk management is not a component of the trading system. It IS the
trading system. Every other section — structure, OBs, FVGs, entries —
determines WHERE and WHEN to trade. The Risk Engine determines HOW MUCH
to risk and ensures that no single trade, no single day, and no single
week can cause irreversible damage to the account.

The best analysis in the world is worthless without proper risk
management. A system with 80% win rate and 5:1 R:R will still destroy
an account if each trade risks 20% of capital. Conversely, a system
hypothetical combinations of win rate and R:R do not guarantee growth; if each
trade risks 1-2% of capital.

This section defines position sizing, stop loss validation, loss limits,
risk adjustment by confidence, and leverage management. These rules are
mechanical and absolute — they do not bend for "perfect setups" or
"obvious trades."

================================================================================
15.1  POSITION SIZING
================================================================================

Position sizing determines HOW MUCH of the asset to buy/sell based on
the risk parameters. The goal: ensure that if the stop loss is hit, the
loss equals exactly the intended risk amount — no more, no less.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE FORMULA
────────────────────────────────────────

  Step 1: Determine RISK PERCENTAGE
    Base: 2% of account balance (maximum, from SYSTEM_CORE Rule 2)
    Adjusted by confidence (see section 15.5):
      85%+ confidence: 2.0%
      75-84%:          1.5%
      65-74%:          1.0%
      60-64%:          0.75%
      <60%:            0% (HOLD — do not trade)

    For HTF_REVERSAL_TRADE (Model F against Daily Bias):
      Maximum: 1.0% (50% of normal maximum, regardless of confidence)

  Step 2: Calculate RISK AMOUNT in dollars
    risk_amount = account_balance × risk_percentage

    Example: $10,000 account, 75% confidence → 1.5% risk
    risk_amount = $10,000 × 0.015 = $150

  Step 3: Calculate SL DISTANCE
    sl_distance = |entry_price - sl_price|
    sl_distance_pct = sl_distance / entry_price × 100

    Example: entry at $99,000, SL at $98,400
    sl_distance = $600
    sl_distance_pct = 0.606%

  Step 4: Calculate POSITION SIZE
    position_size = risk_amount / sl_distance

    Example: $150 / $600 = 0.25 BTC

    Verification: if SL hits:
    0.25 BTC × $600 loss = $150 = 1.5% of $10,000 ✓

  Step 5: Split for DUAL TARGETS
    tp1_portion = position_size × 0.50 = 0.125 BTC (closes at TP1)
    tp2_portion = position_size × 0.50 = 0.125 BTC (runs to TP2)

────────────────────────────────────────
POSITION SIZE LIMITS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Maximum position per trade:
    Never exceed 10% of account in a single position (notional value)
    Example: $10,000 account → max notional $1,000 at 1× leverage
    With 10× leverage: max notional $10,000 (= $1,000 margin)
    This prevents a single trade from dominating the account.

  Maximum concurrent positions:
    Maximum 3 open positions simultaneously
    Total risk across all open positions: ≤ 5%
    Example: 3 trades at 1.5% each = 4.5% total risk ✓
             3 trades at 2.0% each = 6.0% → reduce one or more

  If a new signal appears while 3 positions are open:
    → Close the weakest performing position OR
    → Skip the new signal
    → Never exceed 3 simultaneous positions

================================================================================
15.2  STOP LOSS VALIDATION
================================================================================

The stop loss is determined by structure (below/above the OB, FVG, or
swing that defines the trade thesis). But the structural SL must pass
validation checks before the trade is approved.

────────────────────────────────────────
MINIMUM SL DISTANCE BY ASSET AND TIMEFRAME
────────────────────────────────────────
These minimums prevent stops from being placed too close to the entry,
where normal market noise would trigger them. They are based on typical
volatility characteristics.

BTC/USDT:
  Entry on 1H:   SL ≥ 0.5% from entry
  Entry on 30m:  SL ≥ 0.4%
  Entry on 15m:  SL ≥ 0.3%
  Entry on 5m:   SL ≥ 0.15%

ETH/USDT:
  Entry on 1H:   SL ≥ 0.6%
  Entry on 30m:  SL ≥ 0.5%
  Entry on 15m:  SL ≥ 0.35%
  Entry on 5m:   SL ≥ 0.2%

OTHER ALTCOINS:
  Multiply the BTC minimums by 1.5×
  (Altcoins are more volatile → wider noise → tighter stops fail more)

FOREX (if applicable):
  Major pairs (EUR/USD, GBP/USD): SL ≥ 10 pips on 15m
  Minor/exotic pairs: SL ≥ 15-20 pips on 15m

────────────────────────────────────────
MAXIMUM SL DISTANCE - HARD CAP: 2.5% (ALL ASSETS, ALL TIMEFRAMES)
────────────────────────────────────────
This is a mandatory account-level risk cap (user-mandated, backed by
professional risk management consensus - see rationale below). No SL
may exceed 2.5% of entry price, regardless of how well-justified the
structural level appears.

Why 2.5% specifically (not arbitrary):
  Professional risk management sources (CME Group Education "2% Rule",
  and general industry consensus) converge on capping risk-per-trade
  at 1-2% of account equity. When SL distance grows, either position
  size must shrink dramatically (to preserve the same dollar risk) or
  leverage must increase dangerously to compensate - both erode edge.
  A 2.5% ceiling keeps SL distance compatible with reasonable position
  sizing without forcing excessive leverage.

  ICT methodology itself is naturally aligned with tight stops: because
  ICT entries are refined through Kill Zone timing, OTE (62-79% Fib),
  and Judas Swing/MSS confirmation, the resulting stop distances are
  typically very tight relative to the profit target - this is exactly
  what produces the high R:R ratios (1:5, 1:10) ICT is known for. A SL
  wider than 2.5% usually signals an entry that was NOT properly
  refined through the full Top-Down workflow (Weekly→Daily→4H→1H→Entry)
  - the entry is too far from the actual invalidation point.

THE CRITICAL RULE - WHAT TO DO WHEN THE CAP IS EXCEEDED:
  If the correct structural SL (below the OB bottom / swing low / FVG
  edge that defines the trade thesis) results in a distance > 2.5%:

  ✗ WRONG: Arbitrarily move the SL closer to entry to force it under
    2.5%. This breaks the entire point of a "structural" stop - it
    becomes a number with no relationship to actual invalidation,
    exactly the "Option D" mistake forbidden elsewhere in this section
    (SL must always have a structural basis, never just a distance
    target). A documented real failure: an SL moved this way ended up
    on the WRONG SIDE of entry entirely (a "stop" that would trigger
    on your very first tick of profit) - collapsing the trade logic.

  ✓ RIGHT (in order of preference):
    1. Keep the genuine structural invalidation; never tighten merely for a prettier ratio. If a closer level independently invalidates the same thesis, document why -
       a closer Order Block edge, a nearer Fair Value Gap boundary, or
       a more recent (smaller) swing point that still represents real
       invalidation of the trade thesis. Re-derive the entry itself if
       needed to sit closer to this tighter structural level.
    2. Drop to a lower Entry TF (if currently on 1H, try 15m) - a more
       precise entry on a smaller timeframe means the same structural
       level is now further away in percentage terms, or a tighter
       valid structural level becomes visible that wasn't on the
       higher timeframe.
    3. If neither produces a valid SL within 2.5% while still meeting
       the configured risk policy - the setup
       does not qualify right now. Output HOLD. Do not force a trade
       that requires breaking either the minimum or maximum SL rules.

────────────────────────────────────────
ATR-BASED VALIDATION
────────────────────────────────────────
In addition to the percentage minimums:
  SL distance must be ≥ 1.5 × ATR(14) on the Entry TF

  Why 1.5×?
    1.0× ATR: the average candle range → a normal candle can sweep it
    1.5× ATR: beyond normal fluctuation → needs genuine adverse move
    2.0× ATR: very safe but often too wide for good R:R

  If the structural SL is < 1.5× ATR:
    The SL is within normal noise range.
    A single volatile candle (not even displacement) could trigger it.
    This leads to SL_TOO_TIGHT losses.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE VALIDATION PROCESS
────────────────────────────────────────
After determining the structural SL:

  Check 1: sl_distance_pct ≥ minimum for asset and Entry TF?
    YES → pass
    NO → SL is too tight

  Check 2: sl_distance ≥ 1.5 × ATR(14) on Entry TF?
    YES → pass
    NO → SL is too tight

  If BOTH checks pass → SL is validated → proceed

  If EITHER check fails → SL is too tight → options:

    Option A: WIDEN SL to the next structural level
      Use the next swing out (wider swing low for longs, wider swing
      high for shorts). This increases SL distance but also increases
      R:R denominator → recalculate and disclose actual R:R.

    Option B: DROP TO LOWER ENTRY TF
      If currently on 15m → try 5m for a tighter entry
      A tighter entry means the same SL is now FURTHER in percentage
      terms relative to the entry. This preserves the structural SL
      while improving the distance percentage.

    Option C: SKIP THE TRADE
      If neither A nor B provides a valid solution → HOLD.
      The trade does not meet risk criteria. Move on.

    Option D is NOT allowed:
      Setting SL at the minimum distance without structural basis.
      "SL must be at 0.3% so I'll put it there" → NO.
      SL must be at a structural level. If no structural level gives
      adequate distance → skip.

================================================================================
15.3  STOP LOSS PLACEMENT RULES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
WHERE TO PLACE SL
────────────────────────────────────────
SL goes at the level where the trade thesis is INVALIDATED:

  For LONG entries:
    Below the OB bottom that justified the entry
    OR: below the swing low that defines the current structure (HL)
    OR: below the FVG bottom if using FVG as entry zone
    Whichever is the STRUCTURAL invalidation level.

    If multiple levels cluster (OB bottom near swing low near FVG
    bottom): SL below ALL of them. The widest one defines the
    structural invalidation.

  For SHORT entries:
    Above the OB top / swing high (LH) / FVG top + buffer.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE BUFFER
────────────────────────────────────────
  SL is not placed AT the structural level — it is placed BEYOND it
  with a buffer. This protects against sweep wicks that briefly
  pierce the level before reversing (which is a BULLISH event for
  an OB, not invalidation).

  Buffer calculation:
    buffer = max(0.3 × ATR(14) on Entry TF, 0.2% of price)
    Use whichever is LARGER.

  For LONG: SL = structural_level - buffer
  For SHORT: SL = structural_level + buffer

  Example:
    Bullish OB bottom: $98,400
    ATR(14) on 15m: $300
    Buffer option A: 0.3 × $300 = $90
    Buffer option B: 0.2% × $98,400 = $196.80
    Use larger: $196.80

    SL = $98,400 - $196.80 = $98,203.20 → round to $98,200

────────────────────────────────────────
SL PLACEMENT MISTAKES TO AVOID
────────────────────────────────────────
  ✗ SL at a round number ($98,000 exactly)
    Round numbers attract stop clusters. Institutions know this.
    Place SL slightly off-round: $97,980 or $98,020.

  ✗ SL at the exact level (no buffer)
    Wicks frequently sweep 0.1-0.2% past levels. Without buffer,
    the stop gets hit on a normal sweep that would have reversed.

  ✗ SL inside the OB zone
    SL must be OUTSIDE the zone. Inside the zone is not invalidation
    — price is supposed to enter the zone (that is the entry logic).
    Only a close THROUGH the zone is invalidation.

  ✗ SL based on dollar amount or percentage alone
    "I only want to lose $100 so I'll put SL $100 away" → NO.
    SL is structure-first. Position size adjusts to make the risk
    equal the desired dollar amount. Not the other way around.

================================================================================
15.4  LOSS LIMITS
================================================================================

Loss limits prevent catastrophic drawdowns by stopping trading activity
after a threshold of losses is reached.

────────────────────────────────────────
DAILY LOSS LIMIT: 4% OF ACCOUNT
────────────────────────────────────────
  If cumulative losses for the day reach 4% of account balance:
    → STOP TRADING for the rest of the day.
    → No new entries. No exceptions.
    → Review what went wrong at end of day.

  4% = two losses at 2% risk each.
  After two full-risk losses in a day, the market is not cooperating
  with the analysis. Continuing to trade is emotional, not strategic.

  Tracking:
    daily_loss_total = sum of all losses today
    daily_loss_pct = daily_loss_total / account_balance_start_of_day
    if daily_loss_pct ≥ 0.04: stop_trading = true

────────────────────────────────────────
WEEKLY LOSS LIMIT: 8% OF ACCOUNT
────────────────────────────────────────
  If cumulative losses for the week reach 8%:
    → STOP TRADING for the rest of the week.
    → Full review of all trades this week.
    → Identify if there is a systemic issue (wrong bias, bad timing,
      forced entries, etc.)

  8% = four full-risk losses in a week.
  A week with 4+ losses suggests something is fundamentally off.

────────────────────────────────────────
CONSECUTIVE LOSS PROTOCOL
────────────────────────────────────────
  3 consecutive losses:
    → Reduce position size to 50% of normal for next 3 trades
    → Review: are the losses from the same error pattern?
    → If same error repeats → identify and correct before continuing

  5 consecutive losses:
    → STOP TRADING for 24 hours minimum
    → Full system review
    → Potential knowledge file adjustment if pattern is clear
    → Resume with 50% size for first 3 trades back

  The consecutive loss protocol is about recognizing that the system
  may be in a hostile market environment. Reducing exposure protects
  the account while the system adapts.

────────────────────────────────────────
DRAWDOWN LIMITS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Maximum drawdown from peak equity: 15%

  If account drops 15% from its highest balance:
    → STOP TRADING entirely
    → Full system audit required
    → Review every trade in the drawdown period
    → Identify: is the strategy flawed, or was this variance?
    → Resume only after root cause identified and corrected

  15% drawdown at 2% risk per trade requires 7-8 consecutive losses.
  This should statistically happen less than once per year with a
  60%+ win rate system. If it happens, something is wrong.

================================================================================
15.5  RISK ADJUSTMENT BY CONFIDENCE
================================================================================

Not all trades deserve the same risk. A signal with 85% confidence
backed by 8 confluences deserves more risk capital than a signal with
62% confidence backed by 3 confluences.

────────────────────────────────────────
CONFIDENCE → RISK MAPPING
────────────────────────────────────────

  Confidence 85%+:   Risk = 2.0% (maximum — full conviction)
  Confidence 80-84%: Risk = 1.75%
  Confidence 75-79%: Risk = 1.5%
  Confidence 70-74%: Risk = 1.25%
  Confidence 65-69%: Risk = 1.0%
  Confidence 60-64%: Risk = 0.75% (minimum tradeable)
  Confidence <60%:   Risk = 0% (HOLD — do not trade)

  For HTF_REVERSAL_TRADE (Model F against Daily):
    Cap risk at 1.0% regardless of confidence
    Even at 85% confidence → max 1.0% (50% of normal max)

  This scaling ensures:
    High-conviction trades get full capital allocation
    Medium-conviction trades get proportionally less
    Low-conviction trades are skipped entirely
    Over time, more capital flows to the best setups

────────────────────────────────────────
PRACTICAL IMPACT
────────────────────────────────────────
  $10,000 account, BTC entry at $99,000, SL at $98,400:
  SL distance = $600 (0.606%)

  At 85% confidence (2.0% risk):
    Risk = $200, Position = $200/$600 = 0.333 BTC

  At 70% confidence (1.25% risk):
    Risk = $125, Position = $125/$600 = 0.208 BTC

  At 62% confidence (0.75% risk):
    Risk = $75, Position = $75/$600 = 0.125 BTC

  The SL location is THE SAME in all three cases. Only the position
  size changes. This is critical: risk management adjusts SIZE,
  not STOP PLACEMENT. The stop is always structural.

================================================================================
15.6  LEVERAGE
================================================================================

Leverage is a risk management tool, not a strategy tool. It does NOT
change the analysis, the entry, the SL, or the TP. It changes only
the capital efficiency.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE TRUTH ABOUT LEVERAGE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Common misconception: "Higher leverage = more risk"

  Reality: Risk$ = Account × Risk%. Period.

  Whether you use 1× or 100× leverage:
    If your risk is 2% of $10,000 = $200
    If SL is $600 away
    Position size = $200/$600 = 0.333 BTC

    At 1× leverage: you need $33,000 margin (0.333 × $99,000)
      → you cannot take this trade with $10,000 at 1×

    At 10× leverage: you need $3,300 margin
      → trade is possible, $200 risk unchanged

    At 50× leverage: you need $660 margin
      → trade is possible, $200 risk STILL unchanged

  The risk is determined by position size × SL distance.
  Leverage determines how much MARGIN (collateral) is needed.
  Higher leverage = less margin needed = same risk.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE REAL DANGER OF HIGH LEVERAGE
────────────────────────────────────────
  The danger is not risk per trade (which is controlled by position
  sizing). The danger is LIQUIDATION.

  Liquidation occurs when the unrealized loss equals the margin.

  At 10× leverage:
    Margin = $3,300
    Liquidation ≈ when price moves ~10% against the trade
    (varies by exchange, funding, etc.)
    SL at 0.6% → SL is hit LONG before liquidation → safe

  At 100× leverage:
    Margin = $330
    Liquidation ≈ when price moves ~1% against the trade
    SL at 0.6% → SL MIGHT be hit before liquidation
    But a fast wick could skip the SL and hit liquidation directly
    → DANGEROUS

────────────────────────────────────────
LEVERAGE LIMITS
────────────────────────────────────────
  The system recommends (but does not enforce — the user decides):

  Entry on 1H (SL typically 0.5-1.0%):
    Maximum leverage: 10×
    Liquidation distance at 10×: ~10% → SL at 0.5-1% is safe

  Entry on 15m/30m (SL typically 0.3-0.5%):
    Maximum leverage: 15×
    Liquidation distance at 15×: ~6.7% → SL at 0.3-0.5% is safe

  Entry on 5m (SL typically 0.15-0.3%):
    Maximum leverage: 20×
    Liquidation distance at 20×: ~5% → SL at 0.15-0.3% is safe

────────────────────────────────────────
LIQUIDATION SAFETY CHECK
────────────────────────────────────────
  RULE: Liquidation price must be > 2.0× the SL distance from entry.

  Calculation:
    liquidation_distance = entry_price / leverage (approximate)
    sl_distance = |entry_price - sl_price|
    safety_ratio = liquidation_distance / sl_distance

    If safety_ratio < 2.0:
      → Leverage is too high for this SL distance
      → Reduce leverage until safety_ratio ≥ 2.0

  Example:
    Entry: $99,000, SL: $98,400, SL distance: $600
    Leverage: 20×
    Liquidation distance: $99,000 / 20 = $4,950
    Safety ratio: $4,950 / $600 = 8.25 → safe ✓

    Same trade at 100× leverage:
    Liquidation distance: $99,000 / 100 = $990
    Safety ratio: $990 / $600 = 1.65 → UNSAFE (< 2.0) ✗
    → Reduce to max 50× (safety ratio ≈ 3.3)

────────────────────────────────────────
MARGIN MODE
────────────────────────────────────────
  ALWAYS use ISOLATED MARGIN, not Cross Margin.

  Isolated Margin:
    Only the margin allocated to this specific trade is at risk.
    If liquidated: lose the margin for THIS trade only.
    Other positions and the rest of the account are unaffected.

  Cross Margin:
    The ENTIRE account balance is used as margin.
    If liquidated: the entire account can be lost.
    A single bad trade can wipe out everything.

  There is no scenario where Cross Margin is appropriate for this
  system. Isolated Margin always. This is not a recommendation —
  it is a requirement.

================================================================================
15.7  RISK PER TRADE DOCUMENTATION
================================================================================

Every signal output must include the complete risk profile:

  {
    risk_percent: the percentage being risked (e.g., 1.5%)
    risk_amount: the dollar amount at risk
    position_size: the calculated size (in base asset units)
    sl_distance_pct: the SL distance as percentage
    sl_distance_abs: the SL distance in price units
    sl_atr_ratio: sl_distance / ATR (should be ≥ 1.5)
    tp1_distance_pct: TP1 distance as percentage
    rr_to_tp1: actual calculated R:R to TP1
    rr_to_tp2: calculated R:R to TP2
    max_leverage_recommended: based on SL distance
    liquidation_warning: true if safety_ratio < 3.0
    daily_risk_remaining: how much daily risk budget remains
    weekly_risk_remaining: how much weekly risk budget remains
    concurrent_positions: number of other open positions
    total_open_risk: combined risk of all open positions
  }

================================================================================
15.8  RISK SCENARIOS
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
SCENARIO 1: STANDARD TRADE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Account: $10,000
  Signal: BUY BTC at $99,000, SL $98,400, TP1 $100,800
  Confidence: 78% → Risk: 1.5%

  Risk amount: $150
  SL distance: $600 (0.606%)
  Position: 0.25 BTC ($24,750 notional)
  Leverage needed: $24,750 / $10,000 = 2.48× minimum
  Recommended: 5× (comfortable margin, safety ratio high)

  At TP1: 50% closes = 0.125 BTC × ($100,800-$99,000) = $225 profit
  If WIN_PARTIAL: total profit = $225 (net +$225 = 2.25% of account)
  If LOSS: total loss = $150 (net -$150 = 1.5% of account)

────────────────────────────────────────
SCENARIO 2: LOW CONFIDENCE TRADE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Account: $10,000
  Signal: SELL BTC at $101,000, SL $101,500, TP1 $99,500
  Confidence: 63% → Risk: 0.75%

  Risk amount: $75
  SL distance: $500 (0.495%)
  Position: 0.15 BTC ($15,150 notional)

  Smaller position = smaller exposure for a less certain trade.
  If it wins: profit is smaller but positive.
  If it loses: loss is half the standard amount ($75 vs $150).

────────────────────────────────────────
SCENARIO 3: HTF REVERSAL TRADE
────────────────────────────────────────
  Account: $10,000
  Signal: BUY at Weekly OB (Model F, against Daily bearish bias)
  Confidence: 77% → normally 1.5%, but HTF REVERSAL cap = 1.0%

  Risk amount: $100
  SL distance: $800 (0.8% — wider because reversal SL is beyond
    the entire prior move's extreme)
  Position: 0.125 BTC

  The reduced risk acknowledges the higher uncertainty of trading
  against the current Daily trend. Even though the setup meets all
  HTF Reversal Trade conditions, the position is deliberately smaller.

────────────────────────────────────────
SCENARIO 4: MULTIPLE POSITIONS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Account: $10,000
  Position 1: BTC long, risk 1.5% ($150)
  Position 2: ETH long, risk 1.25% ($125)
  Total open risk: 2.75%

  New signal: SOL short, confidence 72% → risk would be 1.25%
  Total if entered: 2.75% + 1.25% = 4.0%

  4.0% < 5.0% maximum → trade is ALLOWED

  If the SOL risk were 2.5%: total would be 5.25% → REJECTED.
  → Reduce SOL risk to max 2.25% (5.0% - 2.75%) or skip.

================================================================================
15.9  COMMON RISK MISTAKES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 1: RISKING MORE ON "PERFECT" SETUPS
────────────────────────────────────────
"This setup is S-tier, 8 confluences, perfect alignment. I'll risk 5%."
→ NO. Maximum is 2%. Even the best setup can lose. One 5% loss requires
multiple wins to recover. The edge is in CONSISTENCY, not in individual
trade sizing.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 2: ADJUSTING SL TO FIT POSITION SIZE
────────────────────────────────────────
"I want a 0.5 BTC position but the structural SL gives me only 0.25.
Let me tighten the SL to fit 0.5."
→ NO. SL is determined by structure. Position size adjusts to SL.
Never the reverse. Tightening SL to fit a desired position leads to
SL_TOO_TIGHT losses.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 3: NOT COUNTING OPEN RISK
────────────────────────────────────────
Two positions open at 2% each = 4% at risk. Adding a third at 2%
= 6% total. One bad session could hit all three stops = 6% loss.

Fix: always calculate total open risk before adding new positions.
Maximum 5% total at any time.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 4: IGNORING DAILY LOSS LIMITS
────────────────────────────────────────
Two losses in a row. Trader is frustrated. Third trade is taken out
of revenge, not analysis. Fourth trade is desperation. End of day:
8% drawdown that could have been 4%.

Fix: hard daily limit of 4%. After reaching it, trading is DONE for
the day. This is a discipline rule, not a guideline.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 5: CROSS MARGIN ON LEVERAGED TRADES
────────────────────────────────────────
Using Cross Margin because it prevents liquidation on small moves.
True — but it also means a single catastrophic move can liquidate
the ENTIRE account. One flash crash, one exchange glitch, one
unexpected news event → account gone.

Fix: Isolated Margin. Always. The margin for each trade is the
maximum at risk for that trade. Account balance is protected.

────────────────────────────────────────
MISTAKE 6: SAME RISK REGARDLESS OF CONFIDENCE
────────────────────────────────────────
Risking 2% on every trade regardless of whether confidence is 62%
or 88%. This over-allocates to weak setups and under-utilizes the
benefit of strong setups (in relative terms).

Fix: use the confidence → risk mapping. It ensures more capital
flows to higher-quality signals and less to marginal ones.

================================================================================
END OF [RISK_ENGINE]
================================================================================






================================================================================
[CONFIDENCE_SCORING]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
The confidence score is a numerical assessment of how many independent
factors support a trade signal and how strongly they support it. It is
NOT a prediction of win probability. It is a measure of SETUP QUALITY.

A confidence score of 80% does NOT mean "this trade has 80% chance of
winning." It means "this setup has 80% of the possible supporting
factors aligned, making it one of the better setups I can identify."

Even an 85% confidence trade can lose. And a 62% confidence trade can
win. The confidence score determines POSITION SIZE (how much to risk),
not whether the trade will succeed. The edge comes from consistently
taking well-scored setups at appropriate sizes — over 100+ trades, the
math works because higher-scored setups DO win more often, and the R:R
advantage compounds.

The scoring must be HONEST. Inflating confidence to justify a trade the
system "wants" to take is the fastest path to system failure. Every
point added or deducted must be traceable to a specific, verifiable
condition in the OHLC data or time context.

================================================================================
16.1  THE SCORING FRAMEWORK
================================================================================

  CONFIDENCE = BASE + ADDITIONS - DEDUCTIONS

  BASE:     50% (every analysis starts here)
  MAXIMUM:  95% (no signal can exceed this — certainty doesn't exist)
  MINIMUM FOR SIGNAL: 60% (below 60% = HOLD)

  The system starts at 50% — a coin flip. From there, each verified
  positive factor ADDS to confidence, and each negative factor
  DEDUCTS from it. The final number reflects the cumulative weight
  of evidence.

  Every addition and deduction must be listed in the signal output
  as a CONFIDENCE BREAKDOWN. This breakdown is mandatory — it shows
  exactly WHY the confidence is what it is and allows post-trade
  review to assess whether each factor was correctly identified.

================================================================================
16.2  ADDITIONS (POSITIVE FACTORS)
================================================================================

Each factor below adds to confidence when VERIFIED in the data.
"Verified" means the system can point to specific OHLC evidence.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 1: TREND ALIGNMENT
────────────────────────────────────────

  Weekly + Daily + 4H ALL in trade direction:          +15
    The full stack agrees. This is the strongest directional
    confirmation possible. All three major timeframes show
    the same trend (HH/HL for longs, LH/LL for shorts).

  Daily + 4H in trade direction (Weekly unclear):      +10
    The two most relevant timeframes agree. Weekly is ranging
    or unclear but not opposing.

  Daily in trade direction (4H in pullback):           +7
    Daily supports but 4H is temporarily against (pullback).
    This is normal and can still be a good setup if the pullback
    reaches a key zone.

  Only one TF clearly supports:                        +3
    Minimal trend support. Not sufficient alone but contributes.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 2: INSTITUTIONAL ZONE QUALITY
────────────────────────────────────────

  S-Tier OB (score 26-30):                             +12
    The highest quality institutional zone. Strong displacement,
    BOS confirmed, fresh, correct P/D zone, HTF timeframe.

  A-Tier OB (score 21-25):                             +10
    Strong zone with most quality criteria met.

  B-Tier OB (score 16-20):                             +7
    Good zone but with some weaknesses.

  C-Tier OB (score 11-15):                             +3
    Weak zone. Needs exceptional additional confluence.

  OB + FVG Overlap:                                    +5 (additional)
    Two independent institutional footprints at the same zone.
    This is on TOP of the OB tier score above.

  OB + FVG + OTE Trifecta:                             +8 (additional)
    Three references converging. Replaces the +5 overlap bonus.

  FVG only (no OB, A-tier or higher):                  +6
    A strong FVG without OB overlap is still valuable.

  FVG only (B-tier):                                   +4

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 3: LIQUIDITY
────────────────────────────────────────

  Liquidity swept before entry:                        +10
    SSL swept for longs, BSL swept for shorts.
    The sweep must be confirmed (displacement after the sweep).
    This is one of the most powerful confluences because:
    - Stops have been triggered (weak hands removed)
    - Institutions filled orders (using the triggered stops)
    - The sweep direction is now exhausted
    - Fuel has been loaded for the move in trade direction

  EQH/EQL swept (triple+):                            +12
    Sweeping a triple equal high/low is even more significant
    than a standard swing sweep. Maximum liquidity was taken.

  Session liquidity swept (PDH/PDL, Asian H/L):        +8
    Time-specific liquidity provides strong intraday confirmation.

  No unswept liquidity between entry and SL:           +3
    The path from entry to SL is "clean" — no hidden stop
    clusters that could trigger before the trade works.

  Clear Draw on Liquidity (DoL) identified:            +3
    The target for the trade (TP2) is a clearly identifiable
    liquidity pool. Price has a logical destination.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 4: STRUCTURE CONFIRMATION
────────────────────────────────────────

  BOS confirmed on analysis TF (4H or 1H):             +8
    Break of Structure in the trade direction. The trend is
    not just probable — it is structurally confirmed.

  CHoCH + HL/LH + BOS sequence complete:               +10
    The full reversal confirmation sequence has played out.
    Maximum structural evidence of the new trend.

  LTF MSS/CHoCH on Entry TF:                           +5
    Market Structure Shift on the execution timeframe confirms
    that the HTF zone is being respected.

  LTF BOS on Entry TF:                                 +3 (additional)
    BOS after the MSS adds further confirmation. On top of MSS score.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 5: PRICE CONTEXT
────────────────────────────────────────

  Price in OTE zone (62-79% retracement):              +5
    The optimal institutional entry zone for the current range.

  Price in Discount for longs / Premium for shorts:    +5
    Correct P/D zone alignment. Institutional logic supports.

  Price at HTF OB (Daily or Weekly):                   +5
    The zone is on a high timeframe, adding significance.

  MMM Phase 4 (Re-accumulation/Re-distribution):       +5
    The market cycle phase is optimal for entry.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 6: VOLUME AND DATA CONFIRMATION
────────────────────────────────────────

  Displacement candle (Grade A or B) at the zone:      +5
    Institutional force confirmed by the candle characteristics.

  Volume confirms (vol_ratio > 1.5 on confirmation):   +3
    Above-average volume on the candle that confirms the entry.

  buy_sell_ratio supports direction:                   +2
    Taker buy/sell data shows aggression in the trade direction.
    (> 0.55 for longs, < 0.45 for shorts on the confirmation candle)

  num_trades spike on confirmation:                    +2
    High number of individual trades = genuine participation.

  Rejection candle at key level:                       +3
    Wick > 2× body at the HTF zone = clear institutional rejection.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 7: TIME AND SESSIONS
────────────────────────────────────────
  (These come from ICT_TIME_SESSIONS section 9.8)

  London KZ active:                                    +5
  NY AM KZ active:                                     +8
  London + NY overlap:                                 +10
  NY PM KZ active:                                     +3
  Silver Bullet window:                                +3
  Macro Time window:                                   +2
  Tuesday or Wednesday:                                +5
  Thursday:                                            +3
  True Day bias agrees:                                +3

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 8: ADDITIONAL FACTORS
────────────────────────────────────────

  R:R to TP1 ≥ 5:1:                                   +3
    Exceptional R:R provides a mathematical edge bonus.

  R:R to TP1 ≥ 8:1:                                   +5
    Replaces the +3 above. Extraordinary R:R.

  IPDA 20-day range supports (level within 20 days):   +3
    The institutional zone is within the most relevant lookback.

  Breaker Block at the zone:                           +3
    Failed zone that flipped provides trapped-trader logic.

  Propulsion Block (first OB after CHoCH):             +3
    First institutional commitment to the new direction.

================================================================================
16.3  DEDUCTIONS (NEGATIVE FACTORS)
================================================================================

Each factor below deducts from confidence when present.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 1: STRUCTURAL CONCERNS
────────────────────────────────────────

  Trading against HTF trend (not HTF Reversal Trade):  -20
    If the trade direction opposes Weekly or Daily without meeting
    the strict HTF Reversal Trade conditions → severe penalty.
    This should effectively prevent the trade (50 + adds - 20 =
    unlikely to reach 60%).

  Trading against Daily Bias specifically:             -15
    Even if 4H and 1H look good, opposing Daily is dangerous.

  No clear HTF structure (Daily ranging):              -10
    Without Daily direction, all intraday setups are lower quality.

  No OB or FVG at the entry zone:                      -10
    Entering without an institutional zone = entering based on
    hope, not institutional logic.

  CHoCH without displacement (weak reversal signal):   -5
    The structural shift occurred but without force.

  Structure is in transition (between phases):         -5
    Not clearly trending, not clearly reversing.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 2: LIQUIDITY CONCERNS
────────────────────────────────────────

  Unswept liquidity between entry and SL:              -10
    A hidden stop cluster that could trigger and take out the trade.
    This should ideally prevent the trade entirely (see Section 7.7).

  No clear Draw on Liquidity (target unclear):         -5
    If the system cannot identify WHERE price is heading,
    the trade thesis is incomplete.

  Entering on the same side as crowded retail:          -5
    If Long/Short ratio shows > 70% on the trade direction side,
    retail is crowded → institutions may target them.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 3: ZONE QUALITY CONCERNS
────────────────────────────────────────

  OB/FVG is C-tier or below:                          -5
    Weak zone. High risk of failure.

  OB/FVG has been tested 2 times:                     -5
    Weakened by multiple tests. May not hold a third time.

  Entry in wrong P/D zone:                             -10
    Buying in premium or selling in discount.
    Against institutional logic.

  Price at Equilibrium (50% — no edge):                -5
    Neither cheap nor expensive. No P/D advantage.

  OB/FVG outside IPDA 40-day range:                   -3
    Older level, reduced relevance.

  OB/FVG outside IPDA 60-day range:                   -5
    Old level, low relevance for intraday.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 4: TIME CONCERNS
────────────────────────────────────────
  (From ICT_TIME_SESSIONS section 9.8)

  Asian Session:                                       -10
  NY Lunch Dead Zone:                                  -10
  Post-Market Dead Zone:                               -15
  Weekend (Saturday/Sunday):                           -15
  Friday after 12:00 NY:                               -10
  Friday before 12:00 NY:                              -5
  Monday:                                              +0 (neutral)
  Pre-NY transition (05:00-08:30):                     -3
  First 3 days of new quarter:                         -5
  Major news event within 60 minutes:                  -10

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 5: DATA CONCERNS
────────────────────────────────────────

  Volume below average on confirmation (vol_ratio < 0.7): -5
    Low participation reduces reliability of the signal.

  Extreme funding rate against trade direction:        -5
    (Crypto: funding > 0.05% for longs, < -0.03% for shorts)

  OI spike detected (liquidation cascade risk):        -5
    Open Interest anomaly suggests potential forced liquidations.

  Insufficient data to establish structure:            -8
    If the OHLC dataset is too small to identify reliable swings
    and structure on the analysis timeframe.

────────────────────────────────────────
CATEGORY 6: INSTANT REJECTION
────────────────────────────────────────

  R:R to TP1: informational after selecting the structural level.
    It does not alter the target. A configured user risk policy may decline execution.

  Fewer than 3 confluences:                            → REJECT
    Same: instant rejection, not a deduction.

  SL below minimum distance:                           → REJECT
    Same: instant rejection.

================================================================================
16.4  CONFIDENCE LEVELS AND ACTIONS
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
THE TIERS
────────────────────────────────────────

  90-95%: EXCEPTIONAL (A+ tier)
    Extremely rare. Everything aligns perfectly.
    Full position size (2% risk)
    Frequency: no preset quota; measure only from the complete live journal.
    Every confluence fires. Every timeframe agrees. Perfect timing.

  80-89%: STRONG (A tier)
    Excellent setup with minor imperfections.
    Full position size (2% at 85%+, 1.75% at 80-84%)
    Frequency: no preset quota; conditions decide.

  70-79%: GOOD (B+ tier)
    Solid setup with some factors missing or weak.
    Reduced position (1.5% at 75-79%, 1.25% at 70-74%)
    Frequency: no preset quota; conditions decide.
    Most trades should fall in this range.

  60-69%: ADEQUATE (B tier)
    Minimum tradeable quality. Multiple factors uncertain.
    Small position (1.0% at 65-69%, 0.75% at 60-64%)
    Frequency: no preset quota; conditions decide.
    Acceptable only with strong HTF alignment.

  50-59%: INSUFFICIENT (C tier)
    Below trading threshold. Too many weaknesses.
    → HOLD. Do not trade.
    The system should output HOLD with the specific factors
    that prevented the score from reaching 60%.

  Below 50%: ADVERSE (D tier)
    The deductions outweigh the additions. This is actively
    a BAD setup — more factors argue against it than for it.
    → HOLD with strong warning.

────────────────────────────────────────
DISTRIBUTION EXPECTATIONS
────────────────────────────────────────
Over 100 analyses, the expected distribution:
  90%+:   2-5% of analyses
  80-89%: 10-15%
  70-79%: 20-30%
  60-69%: 15-25%
  Below 60%: 30-50%

  This means: 30-50% of analyses should result in HOLD.
  This is CORRECT. A system that signals on >70% of analyses
  is not selective enough. The market does not produce high-
  quality setups on every analysis.

  If the system is producing 80%+ confidence on >25% of analyses:
    → The scoring is likely inflated
    → Review: are factors being claimed without clear OHLC evidence?
    → Tighten the verification criteria

  If the system is producing HOLD on >70% of analyses:
    → The scoring may be too strict
    → OR: the market is genuinely ranging/unclear (which is valid)
    → Check: are real setups being scored below 60% due to
      excessive deductions? If so, recalibrate.

================================================================================
16.5  WHAT CONFIDENCE IS AND IS NOT
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
CONFIDENCE IS:
────────────────────────────────────────
  A measure of HOW MANY independent factors support the trade.
  A measure of HOW STRONG each factor is.
  An input to POSITION SIZING (more confident = more capital).
  A tool for POST-TRADE ANALYSIS (did high-confidence trades
    outperform low-confidence trades?).
  An honest assessment of setup quality based on verifiable data.

────────────────────────────────────────
CONFIDENCE IS NOT:
────────────────────────────────────────
  A prediction of win probability.
    75% confidence ≠ 75% chance of winning.
    A 75% confidence trade might have 65% actual win rate.
    The relationship is: higher confidence → higher win rate,
    but the absolute numbers don't match.

  A guarantee of anything.
    Even a 92% confidence trade can lose. And it WILL lose
    sometimes. That is probability, not system failure.

  An excuse to override rules.
    95% confidence does not allow breaking the 2% risk cap.
    95% confidence does not allow trading in a Dead Zone.
    Rules are rules. Confidence adjusts position size, not rules.

  A number to inflate for emotional satisfaction.
    The system's value depends on confidence scores being
    CALIBRATED: high-confidence signals should win more often
    and produce better R:R than low-confidence signals.
    If every signal is 80%+ because the system inflates scores,
    the confidence becomes meaningless — it no longer distinguishes
    good setups from mediocre ones.

================================================================================
16.6  THE CONFIDENCE BREAKDOWN FORMAT
================================================================================

Every signal must include a detailed breakdown showing exactly how
the confidence was calculated. This is mandatory — no signal without
a breakdown.

────────────────────────────────────────
FORMAT
────────────────────────────────────────

  confidence_breakdown: {
    base: 50,

    additions: [
      {factor: "Weekly+Daily+4H trend alignment", value: +15,
       evidence: "Weekly HH/HL confirmed, Daily BOS bullish 3 days ago,
                  4H making HH/HL within Daily trend"},
      {factor: "A-Tier OB at entry zone", value: +10,
       evidence: "4H Bullish OB at $98,700-$98,900, score 23/30,
                  fresh (0 tests), impulse ratio 3.2"},
      {factor: "SSL swept before entry", value: +10,
       evidence: "4H swing low at $98,500 swept by wick to $98,420,
                  displacement up followed (candle range 2.3×ATR)"},
      {factor: "LTF MSS confirmed", value: +5,
       evidence: "15m CHoCH above $98,950 (last LH), displacement candle"},
      {factor: "NY AM Kill Zone", value: +8,
       evidence: "Current time: 09:45 NY, Tuesday"},
      {factor: "Price in OTE zone", value: +5,
       evidence: "67% retracement of 4H impulse leg"},
      {factor: "Volume confirms", value: +3,
       evidence: "Confirmation candle vol_ratio: 1.8"},
      {factor: "Tuesday", value: +5,
       evidence: "Best trading day of the week"}
    ],

    deductions: [
      {factor: "Bearish FVG between entry and TP1", value: -3,
       evidence: "Bearish 4H FVG at $100,300-$100,600, B-tier,
                  tested once — may cause resistance"}
    ],

    total_additions: +61,
    total_deductions: -3,
    raw_score: 50 + 61 - 3 = 108,
    capped_score: 95,  // maximum cap
    final_confidence: 95,

    // But realistically, this would score lower because
    // not every factor would be present simultaneously.
    // This is an illustrative example of the FORMAT.
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
REALISTIC EXAMPLE
────────────────────────────────────────

  confidence_breakdown: {
    base: 50,

    additions: [
      {factor: "Daily+4H trend alignment", value: +10,
       evidence: "Daily bullish structure, 4H in pullback (bearish
                  internal, bullish external)"},
      {factor: "B-Tier OB at zone", value: +7,
       evidence: "4H Bullish OB at $98,700-$98,900, score 18/30,
                  fresh, impulse ratio 2.1"},
      {factor: "LTF MSS", value: +5,
       evidence: "15m CHoCH bullish at $98,950"},
      {factor: "NY AM KZ", value: +8,
       evidence: "09:30 NY, Wednesday"},
      {factor: "Volume confirms", value: +3,
       evidence: "vol_ratio 1.6 on MSS candle"},
      {factor: "Wednesday", value: +5,
       evidence: "Second best trading day"}
    ],

    deductions: [
      {factor: "Weekly structure unclear", value: -3,
       evidence: "Weekly is ranging, no clear direction"},
      {factor: "No liquidity swept", value: 0,
       evidence: "No major sweep occurred — not a deduction,
                  but absence of the +10 bonus"}
    ],

    total_additions: +38,
    total_deductions: -3,
    raw_score: 50 + 38 - 3 = 85,
    final_confidence: 85
  }

  Note the difference between the two examples:
  First example: nearly every factor present → 95% (unrealistic)
  Second example: good setup with some gaps → 85% (realistic)

  The second is more typical. Real setups rarely have everything
  perfectly aligned. An 85% signal is already very strong.

================================================================================
16.7  CALIBRATION AND SELF-CHECK
================================================================================

The system should periodically verify that confidence scores
correlate with actual outcomes.

────────────────────────────────────────
EXPECTED CORRELATIONS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Over 50+ trades:
    Trades with confidence 80%+ should win at a HIGHER RATE
    than trades with confidence 60-69%.

    If they don't → the scoring is not calibrated:
      Possibility A: high-confidence factors are being claimed
        without genuine verification → tighten scoring
      Possibility B: certain factors don't actually contribute
        to outcomes → reassess factor weights
      Possibility C: sample size too small → wait for more data

  Expected win rates by tier (approximate):
    85%+ confidence: 70-80% win rate
    75-84%: 65-75%
    65-74%: 55-65%
    60-64%: 50-60%

  If actual win rates diverge significantly from these expectations:
    → Recalibrate factor weights in the SELF_LEARNING section

────────────────────────────────────────
ANTI-INFLATION CHECK
────────────────────────────────────────
  The system should flag when confidence seems inflated:

  Check 1: if more than 30% of signals are above 80% confidence
    → Possible inflation. Review factor verification.

  Check 2: if average confidence of LOSING trades is above 75%
    → Factors are being credited when they shouldn't be.
    → Review which factors were claimed on losses and verify
      whether they were genuinely present in the OHLC data.

  Check 3: if confidence and outcomes show NO correlation
    → The scoring system is not measuring anything useful.
    → Full recalibration needed (review every factor and weight).

================================================================================
END OF [CONFIDENCE_SCORING]
================================================================================






================================================================================
[SELF_LEARNING]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
The self-learning system transforms every trade outcome — win, loss,
partial, no-fill — into knowledge that improves future performance.
Without learning, the system makes the same errors indefinitely. With
learning, error frequency decreases over time, and the system converges
toward a state where remaining losses are due to genuinely unpredictable
market events rather than analytical mistakes.

The learning system operates on a simple principle:
  Every outcome contains information. Extract it. Apply it.

A loss is not just money gone. It is data about what went wrong and
why. A win is not just profit. It is data about what worked and whether
it was skill or luck. A WIN_PARTIAL reveals whether TP2 targeting is
calibrated. A NO_FILL reveals whether entry placement is realistic.

This section defines the complete learning loop: how to analyze each
outcome type, how to classify errors, how to identify patterns across
multiple trades, how to modify the knowledge base when warranted, and
how to protect against learning the wrong lessons.

================================================================================
17.1  POST-TRADE ANALYSIS: LOSSES
================================================================================

Every LOSS receives mandatory detailed analysis. No loss goes
unexamined. The analysis follows a structured process.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE 9 QUESTIONS
────────────────────────────────────────
For every loss, the system must answer these 9 questions:

Q1: WAS THE DAILY BIAS CORRECT?
  Check: what was the Daily Bias at the time of entry?
  Follow-up: did the Daily move in the biased direction that day?
  If Daily Bias was wrong → error class: WRONG_DIRECTION
  If Daily Bias was right → the direction wasn't the problem

Q2: WAS THE HTF ZONE CORRECT?
  Check: did price actually reach and react to the identified zone?
  If price blew through the zone without reacting → the zone was
  misidentified or too weak (wrong OB/FVG quality assessment)
  Error class: FABRICATED_SETUP or zone quality overestimated

Q3: WAS THE SL PLACED CORRECTLY?
  Check: was SL at a structural invalidation level?
  What happened at the SL level?
  Did price sweep the SL and then reverse in the trade direction?
  If yes → SL_TOO_TIGHT (the SL was inside the noise zone)
  If price continued through SL without reversing → SL was correct,
  the thesis was wrong

Q4: WAS THERE LTF CONFIRMATION?
  Check: did the Entry TF actually show CHoCH/BOS?
  Was the confirmation genuine (displacement) or marginal?
  If no real confirmation → NO_CONFIRMATION (rule violation)
  If confirmation was there but weak → reassess confirmation criteria

Q5: WAS THE TIMING APPROPRIATE?
  Check: which Kill Zone was active? What day of the week?
  Was there a news event that disrupted the setup?
  If Dead Zone or news → BAD_TIMING
  If timing was appropriate → not a timing issue

Q6: WHAT HAPPENED AFTER THE SL WAS HIT?
  This is critical follow-up:
  After the SL triggered, did price:
  a) Continue against the trade → WRONG_DIRECTION (thesis was wrong)
  b) Reverse and go to TP1/TP2 → STOP_HUNT (SL was swept, thesis
     was correct but SL placement was poor)
  c) Chop around the SL area → inconclusive, market was indecisive

  If (b): the loss was PREVENTABLE with better SL placement.
  Track: how much further past SL did price go before reversing?
  This data calibrates the SL buffer over time.

Q7: WAS THERE UNSWEPT LIQUIDITY BETWEEN ENTRY AND SL?
  Check: was there a significant liquidity level (SSL/BSL/EQH/EQL)
  between the entry and the SL?
  If yes → the liquidity was likely swept, triggering the SL.
  Error class: LIQUIDITY_TRAP (a predictable and preventable loss)

Q8: WAS THE SETUP GENUINELY PRESENT OR FABRICATED?
  Honest assessment: looking back at the OHLC data at the time of
  entry, was the setup clearly present? Or was the system stretching
  the interpretation to justify an entry?
  If fabricated → FABRICATED_SETUP (the most serious error type)
  This requires brutal honesty. If there is any doubt, classify as
  fabricated and add safeguards.

Q9: WAS THIS A RULE VIOLATION?
  Check every SYSTEM_CORE rule:
  □ Was actual R:R disclosed and any explicit user min_rr policy followed?
