  □ Was risk ≤ 2%? If not → RULE_VIOLATION
  □ Was SL present? Always yes (mandatory)
  □ Was trade against Daily Bias without HTF Reversal conditions?
    If yes → COUNTER_TREND
  □ Was there LTF confirmation? If not → NO_CONFIRMATION
  □ Were there ≥ 3 confluences? If not → RULE_VIOLATION
  □ Was timing appropriate? If Dead Zone → BAD_TIMING
  Rule violations are the MOST preventable errors.

────────────────────────────────────────
ERROR CLASSIFICATIONS (9 types)
────────────────────────────────────────

WRONG_DIRECTION:
  Description: the HTF bias was misread. The market moved opposite.
  Severity: HIGH (fundamental analytical error)
  Corrective action:
    Review the Daily/Weekly structure analysis at the time.
    Was there a CHoCH or BOS that was missed?
    Was the trend weaker than assessed (approaching Phase 5)?
    Was there a conflicting signal that was ignored?
  Prevention: stricter trend confirmation requirements.

SL_TOO_TIGHT:
  Description: the trade direction was correct but SL was hit before
  price moved to TP1. After SL hit, price reversed and went to target.
  Severity: MODERATE (execution error, not analytical error)
  Corrective action:
    Measure: how far past SL did price go? (the "overshoot")
    If overshoot < 0.2% → SL was marginally too tight
    If overshoot < 0.5% → SL needs more buffer
    If overshoot > 0.5% → SL placement was fundamentally wrong
  Prevention: increase SL buffer, or use wider structural level.

STOP_HUNT:
  Description: a rapid wick swept the SL then immediately reversed.
  The sweep was institutional stop-hunting, not genuine selling.
  Severity: MODERATE (market microstructure issue)
  Corrective action:
    Was the SL at an obvious level where many stops cluster?
    Was the sweep during a known manipulation window (Judas, AMD)?
    Future: place SL further from obvious clusters.
    Consider: waiting for the sweep BEFORE entering (Model B approach)
    instead of being positioned and getting swept.
  Prevention: wider buffer at obvious levels, or wait for sweep.

BAD_TIMING:
  Description: the setup was valid but the timing was wrong.
  Trade was taken during low-probability window (Dead Zone, news,
  Asian session) and lacked follow-through.
  Severity: LOW (preventable, not analytical)
  Corrective action:
    Was a time rule violated? If yes → enforce rules more strictly.
    Was the KZ assessment incorrect? Recalibrate time identification.
  Prevention: strict adherence to Kill Zone and Dead Zone rules.

FABRICATED_SETUP:
  Description: the setup was not genuinely present in the data.
  The system stretched interpretation to justify an entry.
  Severity: CRITICAL (the most serious error type)
  Corrective action:
    Identify exactly which confluence was fabricated.
    Was an OB claimed where displacement was < 2× ATR?
    Was a "CHoCH" called on a minor swing break (inducement)?
    Was volume "confirming" when it was actually average?
    Add explicit checks to prevent this specific fabrication.
  Prevention: stricter verification requirements. Add a pre-signal
  check: "Can I point to specific candle indices for every claim?"

COUNTER_TREND:
  Description: the trade was against Daily Bias without meeting the
  strict HTF Reversal Trade conditions.
  Severity: HIGH (rule violation)
  Corrective action:
    Why was the Daily Bias overridden?
    Was it a deliberate choice (bad judgment) or a misreading of
    the Daily structure (analytical error)?
    Enforce Rule 4 more strictly.
  Prevention: add a hard check — if signal direction ≠ Daily Bias
  AND HTF Reversal conditions not all met → automatic HOLD.

NO_CONFIRMATION:
  Description: entry was taken without LTF confirmation. Price was
  at an HTF zone but the Entry TF did not show CHoCH/BOS.
  Severity: HIGH (rule violation — patience failure)
  Corrective action:
    Why was the entry taken early?
    Was there pressure to "not miss" the trade?
    Reinforce: the entry AFTER confirmation may be slightly worse
    but is significantly more reliable.
  Prevention: hard requirement — no entry without Entry TF event.

EARLY_ENTRY:
  Description: entered before price reached the intended zone.
  Price was APPROACHING the zone but had not arrived. The entry
  was premature.
  Severity: MODERATE (patience error)
  Corrective action:
    How far from the zone was the actual entry?
    Would waiting for the zone have resulted in a better entry?
    Often yes — and the SL would have been tighter.
  Prevention: use limit orders at the zone, not market orders
  based on "it's getting close."

OVEREXTENDED:
  Description: entered too late, after the move had already begun.
  Price had already moved significantly in the trade direction
  before entry. The entry was a "chase."
  Severity: MODERATE (FOMO-driven error)
  Corrective action:
    What triggered the late entry? Was it seeing the move and
    wanting to participate?
    The setup was valid earlier but was missed. Chasing it is
    a new decision with worse R:R and higher risk.
  Prevention: if the Entry TF OB/FVG has already been left behind
  (price has moved away by > 0.3%), the entry is TOO LATE.
  Wait for the next pullback or the next setup entirely.

================================================================================
17.2  POST-TRADE ANALYSIS: WINS
================================================================================

Wins also deserve analysis. Not every win is evidence of correct
analysis — some wins are luck.

────────────────────────────────────────
WAS IT SKILL OR LUCK?
────────────────────────────────────────
For every winning trade, assess:

SKILL indicators (the win was EARNED):
  □ All confluences that were claimed actually played out
  □ The HTF zone reacted as expected
  □ The LTF confirmation was genuine (displacement, not marginal)
  □ Price followed the expected path (to TP1, possibly TP2)
  □ The setup matched one of the Entry Models cleanly
  □ Timing was appropriate (Kill Zone, correct day)

LUCK indicators (the win may have been fortunate):
  □ Price barely reached entry before reversing → the fill was lucky
  □ Price went against the trade significantly before recovering
    (MAE was > 70% of SL distance) → almost stopped out
  □ A news event moved price in the trade direction unexpectedly
  □ One or more claimed confluences were marginal or questionable
  □ The trade hit TP1 but the analysis behind it was weak

If SKILL: document what made it work. Reinforce those conditions.

If LUCK: treat with caution. The win does not validate the analysis.
  If the same setup occurs again, the outcome may be different.
  Do not increase confidence for similar setups based on this win.

────────────────────────────────────────
WIN QUALITY ASSESSMENT
────────────────────────────────────────
For each win, score the quality of the outcome:

  CLEAN WIN: price moved smoothly to TP1 with minimal drawdown.
    MAE < 30% of SL distance. Setup was clearly correct.
    → High quality. Replicate these conditions.

  MESSY WIN: price oscillated significantly before reaching TP1.
    MAE > 50% of SL distance. Was close to stopping out.
    → Lower quality. The entry or SL may need adjustment.
    → If messy wins are frequent → entries may be too early or
      SLs may be too tight.

  PARTIAL WIN (WIN_PARTIAL): TP1 hit but TP2 not reached.
    Was TP2 realistic? Was the trailing method appropriate?
    → If WIN_PARTIAL is >40% of wins → TP2 may be consistently
      too ambitious. Consider closer TP2 or tighter trailing.

================================================================================
17.3  POST-TRADE ANALYSIS: OTHER OUTCOMES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
WIN_PARTIAL ANALYSIS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Questions:
    Was TP1 placement correct? (Did price reach it cleanly?)
    Was TP2 realistic? (How far did price go after TP1?)
    How much further past TP1 did price go before returning to BE?
      If it went 80% of the way to TP2 → TP2 was realistic,
        trailing was just too loose
      If it barely passed TP1 before returning → TP2 was too far,
        or the move exhausted at TP1

  Pattern tracking:
    Track WIN_PARTIAL rate across models:
      Model A WIN_PARTIAL rate > 40%? → OTE pullbacks may not
        extend as far as expected. Adjust TP2 or trailing.
      Model B WIN_PARTIAL rate > 40%? → post-sweep reversals
        may not carry as far. Tighten TP2.

────────────────────────────────────────
NO_FILL ANALYSIS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Questions:
    Was the entry level too deep in the zone? (too conservative)
    Would a CE entry have filled? (vs edge entry)
    Would a market entry after LTF BOS have filled?
    Did price eventually reach the zone later? (missed timing)

  If NO_FILL rate > 30% of signals:
    Entries are consistently too conservative.
    Adjustment: use CE instead of edge, or consider market
    entry after confirmation instead of limit orders.

  If NO_FILL rate < 10%:
    Entries may be too aggressive (filling easily but possibly
    getting worse prices). Not necessarily a problem — just note.

────────────────────────────────────────
BREAKEVEN ANALYSIS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Questions:
    Why was SL moved to BE early (before TP1)?
    Was the discretionary BE justified by a 1H CHoCH?
    Follow-up: did price reach TP1 after the BE exit?
      If yes → the BE was premature (patience failure)
      If no → the BE was correct (market was changing)

  Track premature BE rate:
    If >50% of BEs would have reached TP1 → the system is
    being too defensive. Let the SL do its job.

================================================================================
17.4  FOLLOW-UP ANALYSIS
================================================================================

After every trade exit, the system tracks what happened NEXT:

────────────────────────────────────────
THE FOLLOW-UP WINDOW
────────────────────────────────────────
  Monitor price for 50-100 candles after exit (on the Entry TF).

  Record:
    After LOSS: did price eventually reach TP1?
      If yes → the analysis was correct but execution was flawed
      How many candles later? How far past SL did it go first?
      This data is critical for SL calibration.

    After WIN_PARTIAL: did price eventually reach TP2?
      If yes → the trailing was too tight or the reversal was
      temporary (price came back to BE but then continued)
      How long did it take?

    After WIN_FULL: did price continue past TP2?
      If significantly → TP2 may have been too conservative.
      Could more profit have been captured?

    After NO_FILL: did price reach the entry level later?
      If yes → how much later? Was the setup still valid at that point?
      If the same setup appeared 4 hours later and would have worked,
      the issue was timing, not analysis.

────────────────────────────────────────
WHY FOLLOW-UP MATTERS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Follow-up transforms a loss into a lesson:
    "LOSS at $98,400 SL. Price went to $98,350 then reversed to
     $100,800 (TP1 level). Overshoot was $50 (0.05%). If SL had
     been $98,300 instead of $98,400, the trade would have been
     WIN_FULL."
    → Lesson: SL buffer was $100 short. Increase buffer by 0.05%
      for similar setups.

  Follow-up confirms WIN quality:
    "WIN_FULL. After TP2 at $101,200, price continued to $103,500.
     Additional $2,300 was available beyond TP2."
    → Lesson: TP2 was conservative. For this type of setup
      (Model B sweep at Daily OB), consider trailing without
      fixed TP2 to capture more.

================================================================================
17.5  ERROR PATTERN DETECTION
================================================================================

Individual trade analysis identifies single errors. Pattern detection
identifies SYSTEMIC issues — errors that repeat across multiple trades.

────────────────────────────────────────
THE REVIEW CYCLE
────────────────────────────────────────
Every 20 trades, the system performs a comprehensive review:

  1. ERROR FREQUENCY:
     Count each error type across the last 20 trades.
     SL_TOO_TIGHT: X times
     WRONG_DIRECTION: Y times
     FABRICATED_SETUP: Z times
     etc.

     Any error type appearing 3+ times in 20 trades = PATTERN.
     A pattern requires corrective action.

  2. MODEL PERFORMANCE:
     Win rate by Entry Model:
       Model A: X wins / Y total = Z%
       Model B: ...
       If any model has win rate < 50% over 20 trades:
         → Investigate: are the model conditions being applied
           correctly? Is the model unsuitable for current conditions?

  3. TIMEFRAME PERFORMANCE:
     Win rate by Entry TF:
       15m entries: X%
       5m entries: Y%
       1H entries: Z%
     If one TF consistently underperforms → investigate:
       5m entries losing? → possibly too noisy, use 15m
       1H entries losing? → possibly too wide SL, refine on 15m

  4. TIME PERFORMANCE:
     Win rate by Kill Zone:
       London KZ: X%
       NY AM KZ: Y%
       NY PM KZ: Z%
     Win rate by day of week:
       Monday through Friday

     If a specific KZ or day consistently underperforms:
       → Reduce confidence for that KZ/day
       → Or avoid it entirely until conditions change

  5. PAIR PERFORMANCE (if trading multiple assets):
     Win rate by trading pair:
       BTC/USDT: X%
       ETH/USDT: Y%
     If one pair consistently underperforms → the system may not
     be calibrated for that asset's characteristics.

  6. CONFIDENCE CALIBRATION:
     Average confidence of wins vs average confidence of losses.
     Expected: wins have higher average confidence than losses.
     If not → confidence scoring is not predictive → recalibrate.

────────────────────────────────────────
PATTERN → ACTION
────────────────────────────────────────
When a pattern is detected (same error 3+ times in 20 trades):

  Step 1: DOCUMENT the pattern
    "SL_TOO_TIGHT has occurred 4 times in the last 20 trades.
     Average overshoot past SL before reversal: 0.12%.
     Average SL buffer used: 0.15%. All 4 cases were on 15m entries
     with BTC during NY AM KZ."

  Step 2: IDENTIFY the root cause
    "The SL buffer of 0.15% is insufficient for BTC on 15m during
     NY AM KZ, where volatility is typically 20-30% higher than
     average. The ATR-based buffer (0.3 × ATR) was used, but ATR
     was calculated from a calm period before the KZ, understating
     the actual KZ volatility."

  Step 3: FORMULATE the correction
    "For 15m BTC entries during NY AM KZ: increase SL buffer to
     0.4 × ATR instead of 0.3 × ATR. This should accommodate
     the higher KZ volatility."

  Step 4: TEST the correction
    Apply to the next 10 trades of this type.
    Track: does the correction reduce SL_TOO_TIGHT frequency
    without significantly reducing R:R?

  Step 5: IMPLEMENT if successful
    If the correction works (0 or 1 SL_TOO_TIGHT in 10 trades):
      → Add as a learned rule (see section 17.6)
    If the correction doesn't work or creates new problems:
      → Discard and try a different approach

================================================================================
17.6  KNOWLEDGE MODIFICATION
================================================================================

When a pattern is confirmed and a correction is tested and validated,
the system modifies its knowledge base. This is the mechanism for
genuine, data-driven improvement.

────────────────────────────────────────
CONDITIONS FOR MODIFICATION (ALL must be met)
────────────────────────────────────────
  1. The same error has occurred 3+ times in 20 trades
  2. A clear root cause has been identified
  3. A correction has been formulated
  4. The correction has been tested on 10+ trades
  5. The correction showed measurable improvement
  6. The correction did not create new problems

  If ANY condition is not met → do not modify.
  Premature modification based on 1-2 occurrences or untested
  corrections can make the system worse, not better.

────────────────────────────────────────
MODIFICATION FORMAT
────────────────────────────────────────
Each modification is recorded as a LEARNED RULE:

  [LEARNED_RULE_001]
  Date: 2025-07-15
  Source: 20-trade review, trades #42-61
  Pattern: SL_TOO_TIGHT × 4 on 15m BTC entries during NY AM KZ
  Root Cause: SL buffer (0.3×ATR) insufficient for KZ volatility
  Correction: increase buffer to 0.4×ATR for 15m BTC during NY AM
  Test Period: trades #62-71 (10 trades)
  Test Result: 0 SL_TOO_TIGHT occurrences, R:R decreased by 0.2 avg
  Status: ACTIVE
  Confidence Impact: none (execution improvement, not scoring change)

  ORIGINAL_RULE: "Buffer = max(0.3 × ATR, 0.2% of price)"
  MODIFIED_RULE: "Buffer = max(0.3 × ATR, 0.2% of price)
                  EXCEPTION: 15m BTC during NY AM KZ: use 0.4 × ATR"

────────────────────────────────────────
PRESERVING THE ORIGINAL
────────────────────────────────────────
  The ORIGINAL knowledge base is NEVER overwritten.
  It is preserved as [ORIGINAL_KB].

  Why? Because:
  1. Modifications can be wrong despite testing (overfitting to
     recent market conditions)
  2. Market conditions change — a correction that works in July
     may not work in December
  3. If multiple modifications accumulate and performance degrades,
     the system can REVERT to the original and start fresh

  Structure:
    data/trading_knowledge.txt      → ACTIVE (with modifications)
    data/trading_knowledge_ORIG.txt → ORIGINAL (never modified)
    data/learned_rules.json         → all learned rules with status

────────────────────────────────────────
REVERTING A MODIFICATION
────────────────────────────────────────
  If a learned rule does not improve performance after 20 trades:
    Status: REVERTED
    The original rule is restored.
    Document: why the modification failed.

  If overall performance degrades after multiple modifications:
    Consider: FULL REVERT to original knowledge base.
    Then: re-assess from scratch with fresh data.

================================================================================
17.7  LEARNING FROM SUCCESSES
================================================================================

Success patterns are as valuable as error patterns.

────────────────────────────────────────
EVERY 20 TRADES: SUCCESS PATTERN SEARCH
────────────────────────────────────────

  1. BEST PERFORMING CONDITIONS:
     Which combination of factors produces the highest win rate?
     Example discovery: "Model B entries during NY AM KZ on
     Tuesday/Wednesday with ≥7 confluences have won 9 of 10 times."
     → Tag this as a HIGH_PRIORITY condition set.
     → When these conditions align in the future → flag for attention.

  2. HIGHEST R:R TRADES:
     Which setups produced R:R > 5:1?
     What did they have in common?
     Was there a pattern in the Entry Model, timeframe, or zone type?
     → If a pattern exists → optimize position sizing for these
       setups (consider full 2% risk even at lower confidence).

  3. CLEANEST EXECUTION:
     Which trades had the lowest MAE (minimal drawdown before winning)?
     These are the most precisely entered trades.
     What entry method was used? (CE, edge, market after confirmation)
     → If CE entries consistently have lower MAE → reinforce CE
       as the default entry method.

  4. HIGHEST WIN RATE CONDITIONS:
     Any specific conditions that show >80% win rate:
     → Document as a GOLDEN SETUP.
     → When these conditions appear → note in the signal output:
       "This matches a Golden Setup pattern (historical win rate X%)"

================================================================================
17.8  PERFORMANCE METRICS
================================================================================

The system tracks these metrics continuously:

────────────────────────────────────────
CORE METRICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Total trades: count
  Win rate: wins / total (where wins = WIN_FULL + WIN_TRAIL + WIN_PARTIAL)
  Loss rate: losses / total
  HOLD rate: holds / total analyses (should be 30-50%)

  Average R:R achieved: average of actual R:R on winning trades
  Profit factor: gross_profit / gross_loss (should be > 2.0)
  Maximum drawdown: largest peak-to-trough equity decline
  Average win: dollar amount of average winning trade
  Average loss: dollar amount of average losing trade

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTCOME DISTRIBUTION
────────────────────────────────────────
  WIN_FULL rate: what % of trades reach both targets
  WIN_TRAIL rate: what % reach TP1 and trail exit
  WIN_PARTIAL rate: what % reach TP1 but return to BE
  BREAKEVEN rate: what % exit at entry
  LOSS rate: what % hit original SL
  NO_FILL rate: what % never fill

  Healthy distribution targets:
    WIN_FULL: 15-25% of trades
    WIN_TRAIL: 10-20%
    WIN_PARTIAL: 15-25%
    BREAKEVEN: 5-10%
    LOSS: 20-35%
    NO_FILL: 5-15%

────────────────────────────────────────
SEGMENTED METRICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  By Entry Model: win rate, avg R:R, frequency for each Model A-F
  By Entry Timeframe: same metrics for 5m, 15m, 30m, 1H
  By Kill Zone: same metrics for London, NY AM, NY PM
  By Day of Week: same metrics for Mon-Fri
  By Confidence Tier: same metrics for 85%+, 75-84%, 65-74%, 60-64%
  By Asset: same metrics for BTC, ETH, etc.

  These segmented metrics reveal WHERE the system performs best
  and worst, guiding optimization efforts.

────────────────────────────────────────
ERROR TRACKING
────────────────────────────────────────
  Running count of each error type:
    WRONG_DIRECTION: total and rate
    SL_TOO_TIGHT: total and rate
    STOP_HUNT: total and rate
    BAD_TIMING: total and rate
    FABRICATED_SETUP: total and rate
    COUNTER_TREND: total and rate
    NO_CONFIRMATION: total and rate
    EARLY_ENTRY: total and rate
    OVEREXTENDED: total and rate

  Most frequent error: the error that appears most often.
  This is the #1 improvement target.

  Trend: is the most frequent error becoming MORE or LESS common
  over time? If less → the learning system is working. If more
  or stable → the corrections are not effective.

================================================================================
17.9  PERIODIC REVIEW REPORT
================================================================================

Every 20 trades, the system generates a comprehensive review:

────────────────────────────────────────
REPORT STRUCTURE
────────────────────────────────────────

  SECTION 1: PERFORMANCE SUMMARY
    Total trades: 20
    Wins: X (WIN_FULL: A, WIN_TRAIL: B, WIN_PARTIAL: C)
    Losses: Y
    BE: Z
    NO_FILL: W
    Win rate: X%
    Profit factor: X.XX
    Average R:R achieved: X.X:1

  SECTION 2: ERROR ANALYSIS
    Errors this period:
      [list each error type and count]
    Most frequent error: [type]
    Comparison to previous period:
      [improving / stable / worsening]
    New patterns detected:
      [any error appearing 3+ times]

  SECTION 3: SUCCESS ANALYSIS
    Best performing model: [Model X, Y% win rate]
    Best performing KZ: [KZ, Y% win rate]
    Best performing day: [Day, Y% win rate]
    Golden setups identified: [any new ones]

  SECTION 4: CONFIDENCE CALIBRATION
    Average confidence of wins: X%
    Average confidence of losses: Y%
    Correlation: [positive/negative/none]
    Calibration status: [good / needs adjustment]

  SECTION 5: LEARNED RULES
    New rules added this period: [list]
    Rules tested this period: [list with results]
    Rules reverted this period: [list with reasons]

  SECTION 6: RECOMMENDATIONS
    Priority 1: [most impactful improvement to make]
    Priority 2: [second most impactful]
    Priority 3: [third]
    These recommendations drive the next period's focus.

  SECTION 7: OVERALL TRAJECTORY
    Is the system improving over time?
    Compare: win rate, profit factor, error rate across
    the last 3-5 review periods.
    Trend: [improving / stable / degrading]
    If degrading: [emergency actions recommended]

================================================================================
END OF [SELF_LEARNING]
================================================================================




================================================================================
[TRADE_JOURNAL]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
The Trade Journal is the permanent record of every signal the system
produces and every trade outcome that follows. It serves three functions:

1. ACCOUNTABILITY: every decision is documented and cannot be revised
   after the fact. The system cannot claim "I would have done X" when
   the record shows it actually did Y.

2. DATA SOURCE: the SELF_LEARNING section (17) draws all its analysis
   from the journal. Without accurate, complete records, learning is
   impossible.

3. VERIFICATION: the journal allows manual chart verification. Every
   trade includes the exact date, time, pair, and price levels so that
   a human can open TradingView, navigate to that point, and verify
   whether the system's analysis was correct.

Every signal — BUY, SELL, or HOLD — is recorded. Every outcome is
tracked to completion. Nothing is omitted.

================================================================================
18.1  SINGLE TRADE RECORD STRUCTURE
================================================================================

Each trade is recorded with the following complete data set:

────────────────────────────────────────
IDENTIFICATION
────────────────────────────────────────
  trade_id:         unique identifier (sequential: T001, T002, ...)
  date_utc:         UTC datetime of signal generation
  date_ny:          NY datetime of signal generation
  pair:             trading pair (e.g., "BTC/USDT")
  direction:        "BUY" | "SELL"

────────────────────────────────────────
ENTRY DETAILS
────────────────────────────────────────
  entry_tf:         entry timeframe ("5m" | "15m" | "30m" | "1H")
  entry_model:      which model ("A" | "B" | "B_TURTLE" | "C" | "D" | "E" | "F")
  entry_price:      exact planned entry price
  entry_method:     "LIMIT_CE" | "LIMIT_EDGE" | "MARKET_AFTER_CONFIRM"
  actual_entry:     actual fill price (may differ from planned)
  entry_filled:     true | false
  fill_delay:       candles waited before fill (0 = immediate)

────────────────────────────────────────
RISK PARAMETERS
────────────────────────────────────────
  sl_price:         stop loss price
  sl_distance_pct:  SL distance as percentage of entry
  sl_distance_atr:  SL distance as multiple of ATR
  sl_structural_level: description of what the SL is below/above
  sl_buffer_used:   the buffer amount added beyond the structural level
  tp1_price:        first target price
  tp1_level:        what structural level TP1 is at
  tp1_rr:           actual R:R to structural TP1
  tp2_price:        second target price (or "TRAILING")
  tp2_level:        what structural level TP2 is at
  tp2_rr:           R:R to TP2
  position_size:    calculated position (in base asset)
  risk_percent:     actual risk % used (based on confidence)
  risk_amount:      dollar amount at risk

────────────────────────────────────────
ANALYSIS CONTEXT
────────────────────────────────────────
  confidence:       final confidence score (60-95)
  confidence_breakdown: {
    base: 50,
    additions: [{factor, value, evidence}, ...],
    deductions: [{factor, value, evidence}, ...],
    total: final score
  }
  confluence_count: number of confluences
  confluences:      [list of each confluence with evidence]

  htf_analysis: {
    weekly:  {trend, strength, key_level_nearby}
    daily:   {bias, last_event, dol}
    h4:      {direction, alignment, mmm_phase, zone}
    h1:      {status, structural_event, entry_ready}
  }

  zone_chain: {
    daily_zone:   {type, top, bottom, quality}
    h4_zone:      {type, top, bottom, quality}
    h1_zone:      {type, top, bottom, quality}
    entry_zone:   {type, top, bottom, ce, quality}
  }

  pd_context: {
    range_high:    price
    range_low:     price
    eq:            price
    current_zone:  "PREMIUM" | "DISCOUNT" | "EQ"
    in_ote:        true | false
    retracement_pct: percentage
  }

  liquidity_context: {
    nearest_bsl:   {price, type, significance}
    nearest_ssl:   {price, type, significance}
    swept_before:  true | false (was liquidity swept before entry?)
    swept_details: description if yes
    unswept_between_entry_sl: true | false
  }

  time_context: {
    kill_zone:     which KZ was active
    day_of_week:   "Monday" through "Sunday"
    session:       "LONDON" | "NY_AM" | "NY_PM" | etc.
    sb_window:     true | false (Silver Bullet window?)
    macro_window:  true | false
    time_adj:      confidence adjustment from timing
  }

  market_data: {
    funding_rate:    value (if crypto)
    open_interest:   value and change
    ls_ratio:        Long/Short ratio
    buy_sell_ratio:  taker buy/sell on entry candle
  }

  invalidation:     exact condition that kills the trade
  management_plan:  "TP1 → SL to entry → trail structure"

────────────────────────────────────────
OUTCOME
────────────────────────────────────────
  outcome:          "WIN_FULL" | "WIN_TRAIL" | "WIN_PARTIAL" |
                    "BREAKEVEN" | "LOSS" | "NO_FILL"
  tp1_hit:          true | false
  tp1_hit_candle:   index/timestamp when TP1 was reached
  tp2_hit:          true | false
  tp2_hit_candle:   index/timestamp
  sl_hit:           true | false
  sl_hit_candle:    index/timestamp
  actual_exit:      exit price
  actual_rr:        R:R actually achieved
  pnl_dollar:       profit/loss in dollars
  pnl_percent:      profit/loss as % of account
  duration_candles: number of candles from entry to exit
  duration_time:    actual time duration

────────────────────────────────────────
POST-TRADE METRICS
────────────────────────────────────────
  mfe:              Maximum Favorable Excursion (best price reached)
  mfe_rr:           MFE expressed as R:R
  mae:              Maximum Adverse Excursion (worst price reached)
  mae_rr:           MAE expressed as R:R (how close to SL)

  mfe_after_tp1:    MFE after TP1 hit (for the runner portion)
  trail_exit_price: where trailing stop exited (if applicable)

────────────────────────────────────────
ANALYSIS AND LEARNING
────────────────────────────────────────
  what_went_right:  [list of factors that worked as expected]
  what_went_wrong:  [list of factors that did not work]
  error_class:      error classification if LOSS (from section 17.1)
  success_class:    "SKILL" | "LUCK" | "MIXED" if WIN
  lesson_learned:   text description of the key takeaway
  would_take_again: true | false (knowing what we know now,
                    would we take this trade again?)

  chart_reference: {
    go_to: "Open BTC/USDT on 15m, navigate to 2025-07-15 09:30 NY"
    what_to_look_for: "Look for the 4H OB at $98,700, the SSL sweep
                       at $98,450, and the 15m CHoCH at $98,950"
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
FOLLOW-UP
────────────────────────────────────────
  follow_up_done:     true | false
  after_exit_price:   where price went after exit (50-100 candles)
  would_have_hit_tp1: true | false (for LOSS trades)
  would_have_hit_tp2: true | false (for WIN_PARTIAL trades)
  overshoot_past_sl:  how far past SL price went before reversing
                      (for LOSS trades where price later reversed)
  missed_opportunity: true | false (for NO_FILL or HOLD)
  kb_update_made:     true | false (was knowledge file modified?)
  kb_update_ref:      reference to the LEARNED_RULE if applicable

================================================================================
18.2  HOLD RECORD STRUCTURE
================================================================================

HOLD signals are also recorded, though with less detail:

  {
    record_id:       unique identifier (H001, H002, ...)
    date_utc:        UTC datetime
    date_ny:         NY datetime
    pair:            trading pair analyzed
    result:          "HOLD"
    reason:          specific reason for HOLD
    what_to_watch:   what would change the assessment
    potential_setup:  description of forming setup (if any)
    nearest_levels:  key levels identified
    reassess_at:     when to check again
    confidence_if_confirmed: estimated confidence if setup completes

    follow_up: {
      did_setup_form:  true | false (did the described setup materialize?)
      outcome_if_taken: estimated outcome had it been traded
      missed_opportunity: true | false
    }
  }

HOLD follow-up is important for calibration:
  If the system HOLDs and the described setup DOES form and
  WOULD have been profitable → was the HOLD premature?
  If this happens frequently → the system may be too conservative.

  If the system HOLDs and the setup does NOT form or would have
  lost → the HOLD was correct. This reinforces conservative behavior.

================================================================================
18.3  SUMMARIES
================================================================================

────────────────────────────────────────
DAILY SUMMARY
────────────────────────────────────────
Generated at end of each trading day (after 17:00 NY):

  {
    date: "2025-07-15"
    signals_generated: count
    holds: count
    trades_entered: count
    outcomes: {
      win_full: count, win_trail: count, win_partial: count,
      breakeven: count, loss: count, no_fill: count
    }
    daily_pnl: dollar and percentage
    daily_risk_used: percentage of daily limit consumed
    errors: [list of error types that occurred]
    best_trade: trade_id with highest R:R achieved
    worst_trade: trade_id with worst outcome
    key_lesson: primary takeaway from the day
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
WEEKLY SUMMARY
────────────────────────────────────────
Generated at end of each trading week (Friday after close):

  {
    week: "2025-W29"
    total_signals: count
    total_holds: count
    total_trades: count
    outcomes: same breakdown as daily
    weekly_pnl: dollar and percentage
    win_rate: percentage
    avg_rr_achieved: average R:R on wins
    profit_factor: gross_profit / gross_loss
    best_day: which day performed best
    worst_day: which day performed worst
    best_model: which Entry Model had highest win rate
    worst_model: which had lowest
    error_summary: {type: count} for each error type
    cumulative_lessons: [lessons from each daily summary]
    trend: "improving" | "stable" | "degrading" vs previous week
  }

────────────────────────────────────────
MONTHLY SUMMARY
────────────────────────────────────────
Generated at end of each month:

  Same structure as weekly but aggregated across the month.
  Additional fields:
    monthly_return: percentage return on account
    max_drawdown_this_month: percentage
    learned_rules_added: count and list
    learned_rules_reverted: count and list
    confidence_calibration: are scores predicting outcomes?
    overall_assessment: free text assessment of the month
    goals_for_next_month: [list of improvement priorities]

================================================================================
18.4  COMPREHENSIVE REPORT
================================================================================

Generated on demand or at major milestones (every 100 trades):

  SECTION 1: LIFETIME STATISTICS
    Total trades, win rate, profit factor, average R:R
    Total return, maximum drawdown
    Sharpe ratio equivalent (if calculable)

  SECTION 2: OUTCOME BREAKDOWN
    Distribution across all 6 outcome types
    Comparison to target distribution
    Trend over time (improving or degrading)

  SECTION 3: MODEL ANALYSIS
    Each Entry Model: win rate, frequency, avg R:R, best/worst
    Recommendation: which models to prioritize, which to reduce

  SECTION 4: TIME ANALYSIS
    Performance by KZ, day of week, session
    Best and worst time windows

  SECTION 5: ERROR EVOLUTION
    Error frequency over time (chart-like data)
    Most common error now vs 50 trades ago
    Errors that have been eliminated by learned rules

  SECTION 6: LEARNING LOG
    All learned rules, their source, and their impact
    Rules that worked vs rules that were reverted
    Net impact of the learning system on performance

  SECTION 7: BEST AND WORST TRADES
    Top 5 best trades: what made them work
    Top 5 worst trades: what went wrong
    Common factors in best trades → replicate
    Common factors in worst trades → avoid

  SECTION 8: FORWARD OUTLOOK
    Based on current performance and trends:
    Areas of strength to maintain
    Areas of weakness to improve
    Specific action items for next 50 trades

================================================================================
END OF [TRADE_JOURNAL]
================================================================================



================================================================================
[CRYPTO_MARKET_DATA]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
When analyzing cryptocurrency markets, the system has access to data
beyond standard OHLCV: Funding Rate, Open Interest, Long/Short Ratio,
and Order Book data. These are available through the DataManager's
Binance Futures API integration.

These data sources provide SUPPLEMENTARY context about market
positioning and sentiment. They are NOT primary decision makers.
Price structure (OBs, FVGs, liquidity, market structure) always takes
priority. Crypto market data adds or removes small amounts of
confidence and provides early warning of extreme conditions.

The key principle: crypto market data confirms or warns. It never
overrides price structure. A perfect setup with adverse funding rate
is still a valid setup — the funding rate simply reduces confidence
by a few percent, potentially reducing position size.

================================================================================
19.1  DATA PRIORITY HIERARCHY
================================================================================

Not all crypto data is equally reliable or useful. The system
prioritizes them in this order:

────────────────────────────────────────
PRIORITY 1: FUNDING RATE (most reliable)
────────────────────────────────────────
Source: get_funding_rate() from DataManager
Update frequency: every 8 hours (Binance standard)

What it is:
  A periodic payment between long and short position holders.
  Positive funding: longs pay shorts (market is net long)
  Negative funding: shorts pay longs (market is net short)

What it tells us:
  The funding rate reveals the IMBALANCE in perpetual futures
  positioning. Extreme readings indicate crowded trades.

Interpretation:
  NORMAL range: -0.01% to +0.01%
    No significant imbalance. Neutral impact.

  MODERATELY POSITIVE: +0.01% to +0.05%
    More longs than shorts. Market is leaning bullish.
    Not extreme — no action needed.

  HIGHLY POSITIVE: > +0.05%
    Market is heavily long. Longs are paying significant fees.
    This creates DOWNWARD pressure over time (expensive to hold longs).
    Contrarian signal: crowded longs → institutions may push price
    down to liquidate longs and collect funding.

    Impact on confidence:
      If going LONG: -5% (you're joining the crowd → risky)
      If going SHORT: +3% (you're against the crowd → potential edge)

  MODERATELY NEGATIVE: -0.01% to -0.03%
    More shorts than longs. Market leaning bearish.
    Not extreme.

  HIGHLY NEGATIVE: < -0.03%
    Market heavily short. Shorts paying significant fees.
    Contrarian signal: crowded shorts → institutions may push price
    up to liquidate shorts.

    Impact on confidence:
      If going SHORT: -5% (joining the crowd)
      If going LONG: +3% (against the crowd)

  EXTREME (> +0.1% or < -0.1%):
    Very rare. Market is in euphoria or panic.
    Strong contrarian warning.
    Impact: -8% confidence for trades WITH the crowd.

Usage in analysis:
  Check funding rate as one of the last steps before signal output.
  It does not change the direction or the entry — only the confidence.

────────────────────────────────────────
PRIORITY 2: OPEN INTEREST + TAKER BUY/SELL (important)
────────────────────────────────────────

OPEN INTEREST:
Source: get_open_interest() from DataManager

What it is:
  Total number of outstanding futures contracts (not closed/settled).

What it tells us:
  Whether new money is entering or leaving the market.

Key patterns:
  OI RISING + PRICE RISING:
    New longs opening. Money flowing IN to bullish bets.
    Trend is supported by new capital → trend is healthy.
    Confidence: +3% for long trades.

  OI RISING + PRICE FALLING:
    New shorts opening. Money flowing IN to bearish bets.
    Trend is supported by new capital → downtrend is healthy.
    Confidence: +3% for short trades.

  OI FALLING + PRICE MOVING:
    Positions being CLOSED (not new positions opening).
    The move is driven by exits, not conviction.
    Less reliable. May reverse when the closing is done.
    Confidence: -3% (move lacks conviction).

  OI SPIKE (sudden large increase):
    Large number of new positions opened quickly.
    Warning: these positions create liquidation fuel.
    If price moves against them → cascade of liquidations.
    Confidence: -5% (increased volatility risk).

TAKER BUY/SELL RATIO:
Source: from OHLCV extended data (taker_buy_volume / volume)
Available: per candle (much more granular than funding rate)

What it is:
  The proportion of volume initiated by aggressive buyers
  (taker buys = market buy orders hitting the ask side).

Interpretation:
  > 0.55: buyers are aggressing (more market buys than sells)
    Price movement up is supported by active buying.
    Confidence: +2% for longs.

  < 0.45: sellers are aggressing (more market sells than buys)
    Price movement down is supported by active selling.
    Confidence: +2% for shorts.

  0.45-0.55: balanced. No clear aggressor.
    Neutral. No adjustment.

  This data is available PER CANDLE from Binance extended fields,
  making it more useful than funding rate (which updates every 8h).
  Check the buy_sell_ratio on the confirmation candle specifically:
    If the confirmation candle (the one that triggered entry)
    has buy_sell_ratio > 0.6 for a long entry → strong support.
    If < 0.45 for a long entry → the candle moved up but sellers
    were more aggressive → warning (move may not sustain).

────────────────────────────────────────
PRIORITY 3: LONG/SHORT RATIO (context only)
────────────────────────────────────────
Source: get_long_short_ratio() from DataManager
Available types: Top Traders Positions, Top Traders Accounts,
  Global Accounts, Taker Buy/Sell Volume

What it is:
  The ratio of long to short positions across different trader groups.

Which to use:
  TOP TRADERS POSITIONS: most useful (professional/large traders)
  TOP TRADERS ACCOUNTS: useful (number of accounts, not size)
  GLOBAL ACCOUNTS: least useful (includes all retail noise)
  TAKER BUY/SELL: already covered as buy_sell_ratio above

Interpretation:
  Top Traders L/S > 1.5:
    Professional traders heavily long.
    Two possible readings:
      a) Smart money is bullish → supports longs
      b) Crowded → may be setting up for a squeeze
    Context-dependent. Not actionable alone.
    Confidence: +2% for longs IF aligned with structure.

  Top Traders L/S < 0.7:
    Professional traders heavily short.
    Same dual reading.
    Confidence: +2% for shorts IF aligned with structure.

  EXTREME Top Traders L/S > 2.0 or < 0.5:
    Very crowded. Contrarian warning.
    Confidence: -3% for trades WITH the crowd.

  Crowded retail positioning (Global Accounts):
    When > 70% of retail accounts are on one side:
    This is NOT "retail is wrong." It is:
    "Retail is crowded → institutions have a liquidity target."
    Smart Money provides exit liquidity to crowded retail.
    The crowd's stop losses are the target.
    Confidence: -2% for trades WITH the retail crowd.

────────────────────────────────────────
PRIORITY 4: ORDER BOOK (weakest — context only)
────────────────────────────────────────
Source: get_order_book() from DataManager
Fields: bid_ask_ratio, biggest_bid, biggest_ask, spread_pct

What it is:
  The current limit orders visible on the exchange.

Why it is the LEAST reliable:
  - Changes every second (stale by the time analysis is complete)
  - Spoofing is common (large fake orders placed to manipulate,
    then cancelled before execution)
  - Reflects VISIBLE orders only (dark pools, iceberg orders hidden)

Limited useful signals:
  bid_ask_ratio > 1.3:
    More bids than asks visible → buying interest.
    Very weak signal. Confidence: +1% maximum.

  bid_ask_ratio < 0.7:
    More asks than bids → selling interest.
    Confidence: +1% for shorts maximum.

  Largest bid/ask walls:
    Note but do not rely on. They may be spoofed.
    If a large bid wall exists at the SL level → slightly reassuring
    (support at that level). But it can disappear instantly.

  spread_pct:
    Indicates market liquidity:
    < 0.01%: very liquid (tight spread) → normal conditions
    > 0.05%: widening spread → lower liquidity, increased slippage risk
    > 0.1%: poor liquidity → caution with market orders

  Order book data should NEVER change a trading decision.
  Maximum total confidence impact from order book: ±1%.

================================================================================
19.2  CRYPTO-SPECIFIC VOLUME WARNINGS
================================================================================

Volume data in crypto requires special handling due to known issues:

────────────────────────────────────────
WASH TRADING
────────────────────────────────────────
  Some exchanges and tokens have inflated volume from wash trading
  (same entity buying and selling to themselves to fake activity).

  How to identify potential wash trading:
    High volume but low num_trades → few large trades creating volume
    Volume spikes without price movement → artificial activity
    Volume significantly higher than similar assets → suspicious

  Protection:
    Use RELATIVE volume (vol_ratio) not absolute volume.
    Use num_trades as a secondary check (harder to fake).
    A candle with vol_ratio 2.0 but trades_ratio 0.5 → suspicious
    (high volume from few trades = possible manipulation).

────────────────────────────────────────
LIQUIDATION CASCADES
────────────────────────────────────────
  In crypto, forced liquidations create volume that looks like
  institutional activity but is not deliberate trading.

  Characteristics:
    Extreme volume spike (vol_ratio > 5×)
    Rapid price movement in one direction
    OI drops sharply simultaneously (positions being forced closed)

  This is NOT the same as a selling climax (Wyckoff SC):
    SC: deliberate selling that exhausts supply → reversal expected
    Liquidation cascade: forced selling from leverage → may continue
    or reverse depending on how much leverage remains

  How to distinguish:
    Check OI change during the volume spike:
      OI drops significantly → liquidation cascade (forced)
      OI stable or rising → genuine selling/buying (deliberate)

  Impact:
    During a liquidation cascade → confidence -5% for ANY trade.
    The market is in forced-liquidation mode, which is unpredictable.
    Wait for the cascade to complete (OI stabilizes, volume normalizes)
    before entering.

────────────────────────────────────────
24/7 MARKET CONSIDERATIONS
────────────────────────────────────────
  Crypto trades 24/7 but institutional participation varies:
    Weekday Kill Zones: institutional algorithms active → reliable
    Asian hours: lower institutional participation → less reliable
    Weekends: minimal institutional activity → unreliable

  Volume during weekends is 40-70% lower than weekday averages.
  Volume ratios calculated on weekend data will appear inflated
  if compared to weekday SMA(20) that includes weekdays.

  The system should be aware that weekend vol_ratio may not be
  comparable to weekday vol_ratio. A vol_ratio of 1.5 on a Saturday
  might represent activity that would be 0.7 on a Tuesday.

================================================================================
19.3  CONFIDENCE IMPACT SUMMARY
================================================================================

Maximum total confidence adjustment from ALL crypto market data: ±10%

This cap exists because crypto data is SUPPLEMENTARY. Even if every
crypto indicator is adverse, the maximum impact is -10%. A trade with
85% confidence from price structure cannot drop below 75% from crypto
data alone — which is still tradeable.

Conversely, favorable crypto data adds at most +10%, which cannot
push a sub-60% setup into tradeable territory (50% + 10% = 60%
barely, and the +10% requires ALL factors to be favorable simultaneously).

Breakdown:
  Funding Rate:      ±5% maximum
  OI pattern:        ±3% maximum
  Taker Buy/Sell:    ±2% maximum
  L/S Ratio:         ±2% maximum
  Order Book:        ±1% maximum
  Total possible:    ±13% but CAPPED at ±10%

These adjustments are applied in the CONFIDENCE_SCORING section as
part of the deductions/additions calculation.

================================================================================
END OF [CRYPTO_MARKET_DATA]
================================================================================



================================================================================
[SIGNAL_OUTPUT]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
This section defines the exact format of every output the system
produces. Standardized output ensures that every signal contains all
necessary information, that nothing is omitted, and that the output
can be programmatically parsed, stored in the Trade Journal, and
evaluated by the backtesting engine.

There are two output types: HOLD and TRADE (BUY/SELL). Both have
mandatory fields that must be present in every response. Missing
fields indicate an incomplete analysis and should trigger a re-analysis.

================================================================================
20.1  HOLD OUTPUT FORMAT
================================================================================

When the system determines that no high-probability trade exists:

{
  "signal": "HOLD",

  "reason": "Specific reason why no trade is taken. Must reference
             the exact gate that failed in the TOP_DOWN_WORKFLOW.
             Example: 'Daily Bias is unclear — Daily structure shows
             ranging with no confirmed BOS in either direction for
             the last 8 candles. Without Daily direction, no intraday
             bias can be established.'",

  "what_to_watch": "Specific condition that would change the assessment.
                    Example: 'Watch for Daily close above $101,500
                    (last swing high) for bullish BOS, or below $97,800
                    (last swing low) for bearish BOS.'",

  "potential_setup": "Description of any forming setup, if applicable.
                      Example: 'A 4H Bullish OB at $98,700-$98,900
                      (A-tier, fresh) exists within the Daily OTE zone.
                      If 1H shows CHoCH bullish at this zone during
                      NY AM KZ, a Model A entry may form.'
                      Or: null if no setup is forming.",

  "nearest_levels": {
    "demand": "Nearest bullish PD Array with quality.
               Example: '$98,700-$98,900 (4H Bullish OB, A-tier, fresh)'",
    "supply": "Nearest bearish PD Array.
               Example: '$101,200-$101,400 (4H Bearish FVG, B-tier)'",
    "bsl": "Nearest Buy-Side Liquidity.
            Example: '$101,500 (4H EQH triple, MAJOR, UNSWEPT)'",
    "ssl": "Nearest Sell-Side Liquidity.
            Example: '$97,800 (4H swing low, MODERATE, UNSWEPT)'"
  },

  "reassess_at": "When to check again. Example: 'Next 1H candle close,
                   or when price enters the $98,700-$98,900 zone,
                   whichever comes first.'",

  "confidence_if_confirmed": "Estimated confidence if the potential
                               setup completes. Example: '~78%
                               (pending LTF confirmation).'"
}

REQUIREMENTS:
  - "reason" must be SPECIFIC (not "no setup found")
  - "what_to_watch" must be ACTIONABLE (specific price or event)
  - Every field must be present (use null if not applicable)

================================================================================
20.2  TRADE OUTPUT FORMAT (BUY/SELL)
================================================================================

When the system identifies a valid, high-probability trade:

{
  "signal": "BUY" | "SELL",
  "pair": "BTC/USDT",

  "entry": {
    "timeframe": "15m",
    "model": "B",
    "model_name": "Liquidity Sweep + FVG Entry",
    "price": 98870,
    "method": "LIMIT_CE",
    "zone": "15m FVG CE within 4H Bullish OB"
  },

  "stop_loss": {
    "price": 98300,
    "distance_pct": 0.576,
    "distance_atr": 1.9,
    "structural_level": "Below 4H OB bottom ($98,400) with buffer",
    "buffer_used": 100,
    "minimum_check": "PASS (0.576% > 0.3% minimum for 15m)"
  },

  "tp1": {
    "price": 100150,
    "level": "Below 4H Bearish FVG at $100,300 (0.15% buffer)",
    "rr": 2.24,
    "note": "Nearest genuine level retained; actual R:R disclosed without extending the target."
  },

  "tp1_adjusted": {
    "price": 101050,
    "level": "Below 4H EQH triple top at $101,200 (0.15% buffer)",
    "rr": 3.82
  },

  "tp2": {
    "price": 103000,
    "level": "Daily FVG unfilled at $102,800-$103,200",
    "rr": 7.24,
    "method": "FIXED + TRAILING (whichever exits first)"
  },

  "position": {
    "split": "50% at TP1, 50% trailing to TP2",
    "risk_percent": 1.5,
    "risk_reason": "Confidence 78% → 1.5% risk tier",
    "management": "TP1 hit → SL moves to $98,870 (entry). Trail behind
                   each new 15m HL using structure method."
  },

  "confidence": {
    "score": 78,
    "tier": "B+",
    "breakdown": {
      "base": 50,
      "additions": [
        {"factor": "Daily+4H trend alignment", "value": 10,
         "evidence": "Daily HH/HL confirmed. 4H in pullback
                      (bearish internal within bullish external)."},
        {"factor": "A-Tier 4H OB at zone", "value": 10,
         "evidence": "4H Bullish OB $98,400-$98,600, score 22/30,
                      fresh, impulse ratio 2.8."},
        {"factor": "SSL swept before entry", "value": 10,
         "evidence": "4H swing low $98,500 swept by wick to $98,420.
                      Displacement candle followed: range 2.3×ATR,
                      body 84%, vol_ratio 2.1."},
        {"factor": "15m MSS confirmed", "value": 5,
         "evidence": "15m CHoCH above $98,950 (last 15m LH).
                      Displacement candle created FVG $98,820-$98,920."},
        {"factor": "NY AM Kill Zone (Tuesday)", "value": 13,
         "evidence": "09:45 NY time. Tuesday. NY AM KZ active.
                      (+8 NY AM, +5 Tuesday)"},
        {"factor": "Volume confirms", "value": 3,
         "evidence": "MSS candle vol_ratio: 1.8. buy_sell_ratio: 0.62."}
      ],
      "deductions": [
        {"factor": "Bearish FVG between entry and TP1", "value": -3,
         "evidence": "4H Bearish FVG at $100,300-$100,600 (B-tier,
                      tested once). May cause resistance before TP1."},
        {"factor": "Weekly unclear", "value": -3,
         "evidence": "Weekly structure is ranging. No clear W trend."},

         "evidence": "Price at 64% retracement — within OTE zone.
                      (This was an addition, not deduction.)
                      CORRECTION: OTE adds +5 not listed above."}
