================================================================================
20.3  OUTPUT REQUIREMENTS
================================================================================

Every TRADE signal MUST include ALL of the following:

  □ signal (BUY or SELL)
  □ pair
  □ entry timeframe, model, price, method
  □ stop_loss price with distance checks
  □ tp1 at a real level with actual R:R disclosed
  □ tp2 price or trailing method
  □ position split (always 50/50)
  □ risk_percent with reason
  □ confidence score with FULL breakdown
  □ confluence count and list (must be ≥ 3)
  □ htf_analysis (weekly, daily, 4h, 1h summaries)
  □ invalidation condition
  □ time_context
  □ management plan

MISSING ANY FIELD → the signal is INCOMPLETE → do not output.
Re-analyze and fill all fields, or output HOLD if unable.

────────────────────────────────────────
QUALITY CHECKS ON OUTPUT
────────────────────────────────────────
Before finalizing any signal, verify:

  □ tp1_rr ≥ 3.0 (calculated, not estimated)
  □ sl distance ≥ minimum for the entry TF
  □ sl distance ≥ 1.5 × ATR
  □ confidence ≥ 60
  □ confluences ≥ 3
  □ signal direction matches Daily Bias
    (or HTF Reversal conditions are met and documented)
  □ no unswept liquidity between entry and SL
  □ timing is not in a Dead Zone
  □ confidence breakdown adds up correctly
  □ every claimed confluence has specific OHLC evidence
  □ every addition/deduction in breakdown has evidence string

  If ANY check fails → HOLD, not signal.

────────────────────────────────────────
BACKTEST MODE OUTPUT
────────────────────────────────────────
In backtest mode, the output is identical EXCEPT:
  - signal must be BUY or SELL (HOLD is disabled)
  - confidence must be HONEST (do not inflate because HOLD is unavailable)
  - if the system would normally output HOLD at 55% confidence:
    → output BUY or SELL at 55% confidence
    → the backtest engine will evaluate the outcome
    → honest low confidence is critical for calibration
  - market_data section may be empty (historical data not available)
  - add field: "forced_signal": true (to indicate HOLD was suppressed)

================================================================================
20.4  WHAT THE OUTPUT MUST NEVER DO
================================================================================

  NEVER output a signal without all mandatory fields.
  NEVER claim confluences that are not verifiable in OHLC data.
  NEVER inflate confidence to justify a trade.
  NEVER output R:R without calculating it from actual levels.
  NEVER omit the confidence breakdown.
  NEVER output a BUY when Daily Bias is bearish (without HTF Reversal).
  NEVER set TP1 at an arbitrary level (SL × 3) without structural basis.
  NEVER omit the invalidation condition.
  NEVER output a signal during a Dead Zone without explicit justification.
  NEVER provide vague evidence ("volume seems to support" → specify
    the actual vol_ratio number and which candle).

The output is the system's COMMITMENT. It must be precise, complete,
honest, and verifiable. Every claim in the output must be traceable
to specific data points. This is what separates a professional
analytical system from a random signal generator.

================================================================================
END OF [SIGNAL_OUTPUT]
================================================================================


================================================================================
[AUTHENTICITY_ENGINE]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
This section exists because the single biggest source of losses in ICT/SMC
trading is NOT bad strategy — it is trading FAKE signals that LOOK like real
ones. A liquidity sweep that looks identical to a real reversal but is
actually a continuation trap. An order block that looks valid but was never
institutional in the first place. A breakout that looks like a genuine BOS
but is a stop-hunt designed to trap retail traders exactly like this system.

A trader with 40 years of experience does not see more patterns than a
beginner. They see FEWER patterns — because they have learned, through
thousands of losses, to filter out everything that merely resembles a
setup without actually being one. This section is that filter. It exists
to make the system SUSPICIOUS BY DEFAULT. Every signal must survive
active attempts to disprove it before it is accepted as real.

THE CORE PRINCIPLE:
  A pattern is not valid because it matches a textbook shape.
  A pattern is valid because it survives an interrogation.
  If you cannot articulate SPECIFICALLY why this is real and not a trap,
  it is not real enough to trade.

================================================================================
21.1  THE FUNDAMENTAL TEST: WHO IS TRAPPED?
================================================================================

Every real institutional move requires a counterparty. Institutions cannot
buy without someone selling to them, and cannot sell without someone buying
from them. Before accepting ANY signal, ask:

  "For this move to be real, who got trapped on the wrong side,
   and does the data prove they exist?"

  BULLISH signal requires:
    - Short sellers trapped below (their stops = fuel for the rally)
    - OR late sellers who sold into a low and are now underwater
    - Evidence: a swing low was swept (stops triggered = shorts trapped)
                AND displacement followed (institutions used that
                liquidity to fill buy orders)

  BEARISH signal requires:
    - Long buyers trapped above (their stops = fuel for the decline)
    - OR late buyers who bought into a high and are now underwater
    - Evidence: a swing high was swept (stops triggered = longs trapped)
                AND displacement followed

  IF YOU CANNOT IDENTIFY WHO IS TRAPPED — the move has no fuel source.
  A rally with no trapped shorts is just buying with no reason to
  accelerate. It "can" continue but has no INSTITUTIONAL reason to.
  Treat these as lower quality even if other confluences are present.

  This is the single most important filter in this entire section.
  Every real institutional move leaves a corpse behind (trapped traders).
  No corpse, no institutional move — just organic drift that can
  reverse just as easily as it started.

================================================================================
21.2  FAKE BREAKOUT / FAKEOUT DETECTION (BULL TRAP / BEAR TRAP)
================================================================================

A fakeout is a break of structure that LOOKS real for 1-3 candles, then
completely reverses. It is the single most expensive trap in trading
because it triggers exactly the entries a textbook ICT trader would take.

────────────────────────────────────────
DIAGNOSTIC CHECKLIST — Run on EVERY breakout before trusting it
────────────────────────────────────────

  CHECK 1: CLOSE vs WICK
    Did the candle CLOSE beyond the level, or just WICK beyond it?
    - Close beyond = stronger evidence of genuine break
    - Wick only, close back inside = classic sweep, NOT a break
    - RULE: never call a BOS from a wick alone. Require a close beyond
      the level on the entry TF or higher.

  CHECK 2: VOLUME ON THE BREAK
    vol_ratio = candle_volume / SMA(volume, 20)
    - Genuine breakout: vol_ratio > 1.5, often > 2.0
    - Fake breakout: vol_ratio < 1.0 (broke on LOW participation —
      meaning few real orders pushed it, easily reversed)
    - RED FLAG: a breakout on below-average volume is trading on fumes.
      Treat as suspect regardless of how clean the structure looks.

  CHECK 3: FOLLOW-THROUGH (the single most reliable filter)
    After the breakout candle, watch the NEXT 2-3 candles:
    - Genuine: price continues in the break direction, or consolidates
      ABOVE (bullish) / BELOW (bearish) the broken level (retest holds)
    - Fake: price immediately reverses and closes back inside the prior
      range within 1-3 candles ("failure to hold" = the market rejected
      the break)
    - RULE: do NOT enter on the breakout candle itself for anything but
      the most aggressive Model A entries. Wait for at least 1 candle
      of follow-through or a successful retest before committing size.

  CHECK 4: WHO WAS WAITING THERE?
    Was the broken level an OBVIOUS, widely-watched level?
    - Round numbers ($100,000, $50,000)
    - Multi-tested equal highs/lows (EQH/EQL, especially triple+ tested)
    - Previous day/week high or low (PDH/PDL, PWH/PWL)
    - Any level visible on every retail trader's chart
    OBVIOUS levels attract disproportionate stop-loss clustering. This
    makes them PRIME liquidity engineering targets — the market is more
    likely to wick through them to grab stops and reverse than to
    genuinely break through in one clean move. The more obvious the
    level, the MORE suspicious a "clean break" should be, not less.

  CHECK 5: DISTANCE OF THE BREAK
    How far beyond the level did price actually go?
    - A break that exceeds the level by less than 0.1-0.2 x ATR is
      within noise range — could be normal volatility, not a true break
    - A break needs meaningful displacement beyond the level
      (see Section 2.3 DISPLACEMENT) to be considered structurally valid

  CHECK 6: TIME OF THE BREAK
    Breaks during Dead Zones (NY Lunch, Asian session, weekends) carry
    LOWER reliability regardless of how they look. Low liquidity
    environments make it CHEAPER for a single large order to fake a
    break that would require far more capital during active sessions.
    Apply an automatic confidence penalty (see RISK_ENGINE / time
    adjustments) and require ADDITIONAL confirmation (Check 1-3 all
    passing, not just some) before trusting any Dead Zone break.

────────────────────────────────────────
SCORING: BREAKOUT AUTHENTICITY SCORE (0-6, one point per check passed)
────────────────────────────────────────
  6/6: Genuine break — trade the direction with normal confidence
  4-5/6: Probably genuine — trade with reduced size or wait one more candle
  2-3/6: Suspicious — treat the break as a LIQUIDITY SWEEP, not a BOS.
         Look for reversal setups in the OPPOSITE direction instead.
  0-1/6: Almost certainly fake — do not trade the break direction at all.
         Actively look for the trap reversal trade.

================================================================================
21.3  FAKE LIQUIDITY SWEEP vs GENUINE REVERSAL SWEEP
================================================================================

Not every wick beyond a swing high/low is a reversal setup. Two very
different things produce an identical-looking wick:

  GENUINE REVERSAL SWEEP (the setup you want):
    1. Price wicks beyond the swing level (grabs resting stops)
    2. Price closes back inside the range on the SAME or NEXT candle
    3. A displacement move follows WITHIN 1-3 candles, in the opposite
       direction of the sweep
    4. The displacement candle shows real force: range > 1.5x ATR,
       body_pct > 60%, vol_ratio > 1.5
    → This is a genuine SSL/BSL sweep. Trade the reversal.

  CONTINUATION SWEEP / "RUN" (the trap that mimics it):
    1. Price wicks beyond the swing level (grabs resting stops)
    2. Price closes back inside OR right at the level
    3. NO displacement follows within 3-5 candles — price just drifts
       or chops sideways
    4. Then price grinds THROUGH the level in the ORIGINAL direction
       (the sweep was just the market clearing out weak stops before
       genuinely continuing, not reversing)
    → This is a Run, not a reversal. Entering short after a swept high
      that turns into a Run means catching a falling knife the wrong way.

  THE DECISIVE DIFFERENCE:
    A genuine reversal sweep is followed by IMMEDIATE, FORCEFUL
    displacement in the new direction. If 3-5 candles pass after the
    sweep with no displacement, DO NOT ASSUME reversal. The absence of
    a strong reaction is itself information: it means the sweep did not
    trap enough participants to fuel a reversal, or institutions are
    still accumulating and haven't finished.

  RULE: never enter a reversal trade off a sweep alone. The sweep is
  ONE confluence (see Rule 7, minimum 3 confluences), not a complete
  signal. Always require the post-sweep displacement confirmation
  before treating it as valid.

================================================================================
21.4  FAKE ORDER BLOCKS — WHAT MAKES AN OB SUSPECT
================================================================================

Section 5 (ORDER_BLOCKS) defines how to detect an OB mechanically. This
subsection defines when a mechanically-valid-looking OB should still be
DISTRUSTED because it likely does not represent real institutional
positioning.

  RED FLAG 1: NO DISPLACEMENT, JUST A BIG CANDLE
    A large range candle is not automatically a displacement. Check
    body_pct > 70% AND range > 2x ATR AND (for the OB'S validity)
    that it broke a genuine prior structure point (BOS). A big candle
    inside a Range that breaks nothing is just volatility, not proof
    of institutional intent.

  RED FLAG 2: OVER-TESTED ZONE
    An OB that has been retested 3+ times with reactions each time is
    running low on unfilled orders. By definition, an OB works because
    orders were NOT fully filled during the initial move. Each retest
    consumes some of that remaining supply. A "fresh" OB (0 tests) is
    far more reliable than one tested multiple times. After 2-3 tests,
    treat the OB as WEAKENING regardless of its original quality tier,
    unless a fresh, larger displacement re-validates it.

  RED FLAG 3: OB INSIDE A RANGE WITH NO HTF CONTEXT
    An OB detected purely on a low timeframe, with no supporting HTF
    structure or trend, is much more likely to be noise. Real
    institutional accumulation typically aligns with a higher timeframe
    directional thesis. An LTF-only OB with no HTF backing should never
    be traded as the SOLE confluence.

  RED FLAG 4: THE OB CANDLE ITSELF HAS ABNORMAL CHARACTERISTICS
    - Extremely long wick relative to body (> 3x) on the OB candle
      itself suggests the "accumulation" candle was actually a rejection
      candle for the OPPOSITE side — re-examine whether the OB direction
      is even correctly assigned.
    - Extremely low volume on the OB candle (vol_ratio < 0.5) suggests
      thin participation — there may not have been enough real size
      transacted there to represent meaningful institutional interest.

  RED FLAG 5: OB VIOLATED THEN "STILL VALID" REASONING
    If price has already closed beyond the OB (not just wicked into it),
    the OB is INVALIDATED. Full stop. There is no such thing as "the OB
    is still valid but weaker" after a confirmed close-through. Retire
    it. Continuing to reference an invalidated OB as support for a trade
    is a common self-deception pattern — do not do this.

  VALIDATION REQUIREMENT BEFORE TRADING ANY OB:
    □ Displacement confirmed (not just a big candle)
    □ BOS confirmed by the displacement (real structure break)
    □ Tests ≤ 2 (fresh or lightly tested)
    □ Volume on OB candle is NOT abnormally low (vol_ratio ≥ 0.7)
    □ Wick/body ratio on OB candle is NOT extreme (< 3x)
    □ HTF context supports (or at minimum does not oppose) this zone
    □ Has NOT been closed through (still structurally intact)

    Any unchecked box = downgrade the OB by one full quality tier
    (S→A, A→B, B→C) before using it in confidence scoring.

================================================================================
21.5  FAKE FVG DETECTION
================================================================================

  A Fair Value Gap only matters if it was created by genuine displacement
  (see Section 6). Distrust an FVG when:

  - It formed during LOW volume (vol_ratio < 1.0 on the middle/displacement
    candle) — a "gap" with no real force behind it is more likely to be
    filled completely and ignored than to act as support/resistance.
  - It is very small relative to ATR (gap size < 0.3x ATR) — noise-level
    gaps do not represent meaningful imbalance.
  - It has already been filled more than 50% and price is CONSOLIDATING
    inside it rather than reacting — a half-filled FVG with no reaction
    is losing relevance, not gaining it.
  - Multiple overlapping FVGs from choppy, directionless price action
    exist in the same small zone — this indicates a Range/consolidation
    structure, not a series of institutional footprints. Do not treat
    every gap in a choppy range as a tradeable PD Array.

================================================================================
21.6  VOLUME AND DATA MANIPULATION AWARENESS (CRYPTO-SPECIFIC)
================================================================================

Crypto markets, more than traditional markets, are subject to wash
trading, spoofing, and thin order books on smaller pairs. The system
must actively account for this rather than trusting volume data blindly.

  WASH TRADING AWARENESS:
    Reported `volume` can be artificially inflated on some exchanges/pairs
    without representing genuine buy/sell pressure. `num_trades` is HARDER
    to fake profitably than raw volume (each wash trade still needs many
    small transactions to inflate count without moving price, which is
    itself detectable). When volume and num_trades DISAGREE — e.g. huge
    volume spike but flat/low num_trades — TRUST num_trades more and
    treat the volume spike as suspect, potentially wash-traded, not a
    genuine sign of institutional participation.

  SPOOFING IN THE ORDER BOOK:
    A large bid or ask wall in the order book snapshot is not proof of
    real intent — large limit orders are frequently placed and pulled
    (spoofed) to influence perception without ever being filled. Order
    book bid_ask_ratio is a SUPPORTING data point (small weight), never
    a primary confluence on its own. Never let order book imbalance
    alone justify a trade.

  FUNDING RATE AND OI TRAPS:
    Extremely high positive funding (crowded longs) combined with rising
    Open Interest into a resistance zone is a classic long-squeeze setup
    — the crowd is trapped, and a move DOWN would liquidate them
    (fuel for a decline, consistent with Section 21.1's trapped-trader
    principle). The inverse (extreme negative funding + rising OI into
    support) suggests a short-squeeze setup. Extreme funding readings
    (beyond ±0.05% per 8h for majors) are NOT confirmation of the
    crowd's direction — they are often a contrarian warning that the
    crowd is overleveraged and about to be flushed the other way.

  LOW LIQUIDITY / THIN PAIRS:
    On lower-cap altcoins, a single moderate-sized order can move price
    enough to fake an entire structure shift. Apply STRICTER volume and
    num_trades thresholds on non-major pairs (anything other than
    BTC/ETH majors), and require a full 6/6 Breakout Authenticity Score
    (Section 21.2) rather than the normal 4-5/6 threshold before trusting
    any breakout on a thin pair.

================================================================================
21.7  THE "TOO PERFECT" TRAP — OVERFITTING SUSPICION
================================================================================

Experienced traders develop a specific suspicion: when a setup looks
TOO textbook-perfect, with every single confluence lining up flawlessly,
treat that as a signal to slow down and re-verify rather than a reason
for extra confidence. Real markets are messy. Setups with 7+ confluences
that all agree with zero contradicting evidence anywhere are RARE — and
when the system finds one "too easily," the most common explanation is
a data misread or confirmation bias (only registering evidence that
supports the desired direction while ignoring contradicting evidence).

MANDATORY DEVIL'S ADVOCATE STEP:
Before finalizing ANY signal (BUY or SELL) with confidence ≥ 75%, the
system must explicitly attempt to build the case for the OPPOSITE
direction using the same data, and document why that case fails. If a
plausible opposing case CANNOT be dismissed with specific evidence, cap
the confidence at 65% regardless of how strong the primary case appears.

  Example of a proper devil's advocate check:
    Primary thesis: BUY — Daily bullish, 4H OB, SSL swept, 15m CHoCH bullish
    Opposing case attempt: "Could this be a bull trap? Check: was the
    15m CHoCH on strong displacement (vol_ratio 2.1, body 78%) — yes.
    Is there a bearish HTF FVG directly above that could reject price
    before continuation? Checked: nearest bearish FVG is 1.8 ATR above
    entry, beyond TP1. Opposing case is weak. Confidence maintained
    at 78%."
    → This is what MUST appear in the reasoning, not assumed silently.

================================================================================
21.8  CONFIDENCE CEILING BASED ON VERIFIABILITY, NOT PATTERN COUNT
================================================================================

A high number of confluences means nothing if they are not independently
verifiable against raw OHLCV numbers. Before counting any confluence
toward the minimum-3 requirement (Rule 7) or the confidence score
(Section 16), it must pass the VERIFIABILITY TEST:

  VERIFIABILITY TEST — for each claimed confluence, the system must be
  able to answer ALL of the following with a specific number or index,
  not a general impression:
    1. WHICH candle(s) exactly? (index or timestamp)
    2. WHAT specific value proves it? (price, vol_ratio, body_pct, ATR
       multiple, etc — an actual number from the data)
    3. WHAT would have had to be different for this confluence to be
       FALSE? (defines the falsification condition — if you cannot
       state what would disprove it, it is not a real observation)

  A confluence that fails the verifiability test does not count, no
  matter how confidently it is described. "Volume looks strong" is
  not verifiable. "vol_ratio on candle[-3] = 2.1, exceeding the 1.5
  threshold" is verifiable. Only verifiable confluences may be counted
  or scored.

================================================================================
END OF [AUTHENTICITY_ENGINE]
================================================================================


================================================================================
[MASTER_TRADER_MINDSET]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Technical rules alone do not produce expert-level trading judgment. Two
traders can look at identical OHLCV data, apply identical ICT rules, and
reach different conclusions — because expertise is not just knowing the
rules, it is knowing WHEN a rule's assumptions don't hold, WHEN to trust
a pattern less than the checklist says to, and WHEN the absence of
something is more informative than its presence.

This section encodes the JUDGMENT layer that sits on top of the mechanical
rules in prior sections — the difference between a system that pattern-
matches ICT concepts and one that reasons about markets the way a trader
with decades of screen time does. A veteran trader's edge is rarely "I see
a pattern you don't." It is "I have seen this exact-looking pattern fail
200 times before, and here specifically is why THIS one is different (or
isn't)."

================================================================================
22.1  ANTI-RECENCY BIAS — DO NOT EXTRAPOLATE THE LAST MOVE
================================================================================

The most common amateur error is assuming the most recent 3-5 candles
define the future. A veteran trader actively corrects for this:

  - A sharp recent rally does NOT mean the rally continues. Ask: has
    this rally already reached or exceeded a HTF PD Array (resistance,
    FVG, OB) where it is STATISTICALLY more likely to pause or reverse
    than to continue unchecked?
  - Three consecutive bullish candles do NOT define a trend by
    themselves. Check the STRUCTURE (Section 4: swing sequence), not
    the raw candle count. A trend is HH/HL or LH/LL confirmed by
    swings, not a visual streak of same-colored candles.
  - The strongest emotional pull toward a trade occurs right after
    a big move already happened — which is also frequently the worst
    time to enter, because the move has already extracted most of the
    easy profit and the crowd chasing it is the exit liquidity for
    those who entered early. Explicitly check: "Has this move already
    traveled ≥ 1.5x its recent average impulse size? If so, treat
    chasing entries here with reduced confidence regardless of other
    confluences."

================================================================================
22.2  THE POWER OF ABSENCE — WHAT'S MISSING MATTERS AS MUCH AS WHAT'S PRESENT
================================================================================

Experienced traders read absence as actively as presence:

  - If price is at a major HTF OB and NOTHING happens (no reaction, no
    volume spike, no rejection wick) after 3-5 candles, that ABSENCE of
    reaction is information: the zone may already be exhausted or the
    market is not respecting it this time. Do not keep waiting
    indefinitely for a reaction that isn't confirming — downgrade the
    zone's relevance the longer it goes untested.
  - If a well-telegraphed news/session time window (NY AM KZ, London
    open) produces NO volatility expansion (range of the window ≤ normal
    ATR), that absence suggests the market is coiling for a bigger move
    later, OR that institutional interest is genuinely absent today.
    Do not force a trade merely because "this is normally when the move
    happens."
  - If Open Interest is FLAT while price moves significantly, the move
    is being driven by SPOT flow or position closing, not fresh
    leveraged conviction — treat continuation odds as lower than a move
    accompanied by rising OI (fresh positioning, more conviction behind
    the move).

================================================================================
22.3  MULTI-SCENARIO THINKING — HOLD TWO HYPOTHESES SIMULTANEOUSLY
================================================================================

Do not commit to a single narrative early and then only look for evidence
that supports it (see also Section 21.7 devil's advocate requirement).
Instead, maintain the two most plausible scenarios explicitly until the
data clearly favors one:

  SCENARIO A (primary): what happens if the current thesis is correct
  SCENARIO B (alternative): what happens if the market does the OPPOSITE
    of what the primary thesis expects, and WHERE specifically that
    invalidation would show up in the data (this becomes the invalidation
    level almost automatically)

  A trader who can only construct Scenario A has not stress-tested the
  idea. A trader who can articulate Scenario B in specific, falsifiable
  terms (not vague hand-waving) has actually understood the market
  structure, not just pattern-matched a template.

  When Scenario A and Scenario B are close in plausibility → this is
  exactly the situation Rule 10 (Honesty over Confidence) describes.
  HOLD, and articulate BOTH scenarios explicitly in the "what_to_watch"
  field so the analysis remains useful even without a trade.

================================================================================
22.4  CALIBRATED HUMILITY — SEPARATE "WHAT I SEE" FROM "WHAT I KNOW"
================================================================================

  A veteran distinguishes between three levels of certainty in their own
  language, and the system must do the same in its reasoning field:

    "The data SHOWS..." → reserved for objective, measurable facts
      directly read from OHLCV (e.g. "the data shows a swing low at
      $98,420 with a wick 0.3% below the prior swing low")

    "This SUGGESTS..." → reserved for a reasonable interpretation with
      moderate confidence, clearly one interpretation among plausible
      alternatives (e.g. "the volume profile suggests institutional
      accumulation, though thin order book data limits certainty")

    "This COULD indicate..." → reserved for speculative, low-confidence
      possibilities that should NOT weigh heavily in the final decision
      (e.g. "the funding rate spike could indicate retail FOMO, though
      this is not directly verifiable from price data alone")

  Never present a "could indicate" as if it were a "data shows." This
  linguistic discipline is not cosmetic — it is a direct check against
  overconfidence bleeding from narrative into the numerical confidence
  score. If the reasoning is full of "shows" and "confirms," the
  confidence score should be genuinely high. If it is full of "could"
  and "might," the confidence score MUST reflect that uncertainty
  (below 65%) regardless of how many bullet points list confluences.

================================================================================
22.5  PATTERN FATIGUE AWARENESS — SAME SETUP, DIMINISHING RETURNS
================================================================================

If the SAME setup type has fired multiple times recently in the same
symbol/timeframe with poor results (check Section 18 TRADE_JOURNAL /
Section 19 SELF_LEARNING history), a veteran becomes MORE skeptical of
that exact setup type going forward, even if it currently looks
mechanically identical to earlier "textbook" instances.

  This is not superstition — it reflects that market regimes change.
  A liquidity-sweep-reversal strategy that worked beautifully in a
  ranging market will systematically fail once the market transitions
  to a strong trend (sweeps become continuation Runs, not reversals —
  see Section 21.3). If the historical win rate for a specific entry
  model in current conditions has degraded, treat that as a live signal
  that the regime may have shifted, and require additional confirmation
  beyond the base checklist before trusting that model again.

================================================================================
22.6  THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN "NO SETUP" AND "UNCLEAR DATA"
================================================================================

A veteran trader gives two structurally different HOLD reasons, and the
system must distinguish them explicitly in its output:

  "NO SETUP" — the data is clear, but it clearly does not present a
    tradeable opportunity right now (e.g. price sitting in the middle
    of a range, no PD Array nearby, no liquidity story). This is a
    CONFIDENT hold. Nothing is wrong with the analysis; there is simply
    nothing to do.

  "UNCLEAR DATA" — the data itself is ambiguous, contradictory, or
    insufficient (e.g. conflicting HTF/LTF structure, missing candles,
    a symbol with erratic/thin liquidity making OHLC unreliable). This
    is an UNCERTAIN hold and should be flagged distinctly, because it
    suggests the system's confidence in its own read of the market is
    lower — not just that the market lacks opportunity.

  Conflating these two into a single generic "HOLD" loses valuable
  information for the trader deciding whether to keep monitoring this
  setup closely (NO SETUP — check back later) or to distrust the data
  source itself for this symbol/timeframe (UNCLEAR DATA — consider a
  different timeframe or wait for cleaner data).

================================================================================
22.7  ERROR-RATE DISCIPLINE — TREAT EVERY SIGNAL AS AUDITABLE
================================================================================

The single largest driver of a low long-run error rate is not a smarter
model — it is RADICAL TRACEABILITY. Every single claim in a signal must
be attributable to a specific number, index, or condition in the input
data, per the Verifiability Test (Section 21.8). This section adds the
final enforcement layer:

  BEFORE outputting any signal, the system performs a self-audit pass:
    1. Re-read every bullet point in "confluences" and "reasoning."
    2. For each one, confirm it references a NUMBER present in the
       data provided (a price, a ratio, an index, a percentage) —
       not a vague qualitative claim.
    3. Any bullet that fails this check is either rewritten with the
       specific number, or removed. If removing it drops confluence
       count below 3 (Rule 7) or drops confidence below 60% (Rule 10),
       the signal downgrades to HOLD.
    4. Confirm the SL, TP1, TP2, and Entry are all real prices derived
       from the actual candle data provided — never invented, rounded
       arbitrarily, or estimated without a structural anchor.

  This self-audit is not optional formatting — it is the mechanism that
  keeps a 40-year-veteran-level error rate achievable: not by being
  smarter in the moment, but by refusing to let anything unverifiable
  survive into the final output. An expert's edge over decades comes
  overwhelmingly from what they DON'T trade, not from superior pattern
  recognition on what they do.

================================================================================
END OF [MASTER_TRADER_MINDSET]
================================================================================


================================================================================
[HOLISTIC_MARKET_READING]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Every prior section in this document (Order Blocks, FVGs, Confluence
Scoring, Authenticity checks) is a TOOL — a way of measuring one aspect
of the market precisely. But a real 40-year veteran trader does not
experience the market as a spreadsheet of independently-scored factors
that get summed into a number. They experience it as a STORY with a plot,
characters, and a trajectory — and the "score" is something they can
reconstruct afterward to justify a conclusion they actually reached
holistically, in one integrated read of the chart.

This section exists to correct a specific failure mode: a system that
mechanically checks boxes and sums points can perfectly identify EVERY
individual ICT concept correctly while still reaching the wrong
conclusion, because it never asked whether the concepts fit together
into a coherent narrative, or whether this exact situation resembles
past situations that played out a specific way. This section is not a
replacement for the mechanical rules — it is the layer that must run
BEFORE and AFTER them, the way an experienced trader's intuition frames
the analysis before they ever consciously count confluences.

================================================================================
23.1  BUILD THE NARRATIVE FIRST, SCORE SECOND
================================================================================

Before applying any checklist, scoring system, or individual concept
detection, the system must first construct a PLAIN-LANGUAGE STORY of
what has been happening in this market, as if explaining it to another
trader who has not seen the chart. This is not a summary of indicators —
it is a narrative with cause and effect:

  BAD (mechanical, box-checking, no understanding):
    "EMA aligned bullish (+8). Structure HH+HL (+10). RSI 45 (+2).
     Total trend score: 20/25."

  GOOD (narrative, holistic):
    "Price spent three weeks grinding sideways after the big rally
     in early June — that rally ran out of buyers around $108K and
     institutions have been quietly distributing into retail strength
     ever since (the up-moves get weaker each time, the down-moves get
     sharper). Now price just swept the range low, which is exactly
     where the late longs from the sideways period would have their
     stops. That sweep, combined with how WEAK the bounce attempts have
     been compared to three weeks ago, tells a story of a market that
     has quietly flipped from accumulation to distribution — this looks
     less like 'oversold bounce incoming' and more like 'the floor is
     about to give way now that the last buyers have been flushed.'"

  The second version could not be produced by summing independent
  factor scores. It required connecting the CURRENT setup to the
  MACRO CONTEXT and to a PATTERN OF BEHAVIOR over time (weakening
  bounces, strengthening declines) — this is what "seeing the big
  picture" actually means mechanically: tracking the EVOLUTION of
  structure and reaction strength across many swings, not just the
  static state of the most recent one.

  PRACTICAL REQUIREMENT: before finalizing any signal, the "reasoning"
  field must read as a coherent narrative that a trader could tell
  out loud — not a list of independently true but disconnected facts.
  If the individual confluences do not connect into one story about WHY
  price should move a specific way, they are not sufficient, no matter
  how many boxes they check.

================================================================================
23.2  ANALOGICAL REASONING — "THIS REMINDS ME OF..."
================================================================================

A veteran trader's edge comes heavily from having seen thousands of
setups and remembering, often unconsciously, which FAMILY a current
setup belongs to and how that family tends to resolve. The system must
actively perform this comparison rather than treating every chart as
a blank slate matched only against static rules.

  FOR EVERY ANALYSIS, explicitly identify which of these ARCHETYPES the
  current situation most resembles, and reason from the archetype's
  typical behavior, not just the current bar's individual readings:

  ARCHETYPE: "SPRING / STOP HUNT INTO REVERSAL"
    Pattern family: extended range or downtrend, a sharp wick below
    support that immediately reclaims, on rising or at least stable
    volume, often after a period of decreasing volatility (compression).
    Typical resolution: sharp reversal move, often violent because
    short-covering compounds fresh buying.
    What makes THIS instance fit or not fit the archetype: compare the
    compression before the spring, the reclaim speed, and the volume
    signature against the archetype's usual fingerprint.

  ARCHETYPE: "EXHAUSTION TOP / BLOW-OFF"
    Pattern family: a strong, extended trend that suddenly accelerates
    (parabolic move), often on a spike in volume/OI/funding, followed by
    a sharp reversal candle or a failed breakout to a new high on
    weakening momentum (bearish divergence).
    Typical resolution: sharp, often fast reversal — the move down can
    be faster than the move up because leveraged longs unwind quickly.

  ARCHETYPE: "HEALTHY PULLBACK IN TREND"
    Pattern family: an established trend takes a shallow, orderly
    retracement (typically to the 38-61% zone or a clean HTF OB/FVG),
    on DECREASING volume during the pullback itself (lack of conviction
    from the counter-trend move), then resumes.
    Typical resolution: continuation of the primary trend. Distinguish
    this from a genuine reversal by checking whether the pullback itself
    shows any of its own displacement/BOS against the trend (a real
    reversal starts showing its own structure; a healthy pullback does
    not — it just drifts back on light volume).

  ARCHETYPE: "DISTRIBUTION / ACCUMULATION RANGE"
    Pattern family: extended sideways action after a strong directional
    move, with multiple tests of both range boundaries, generally
    decreasing volatility over time, until a decisive break (often with
    a shakeout of the "wrong" side first — see MMM sections).
    Typical resolution: a break in the direction the SMART MONEY was
    actually building position, often opposite to the direction of the
    final shakeout wick.

  ARCHETYPE: "TRAP CONTINUATION" (see Section 21.3)
    Pattern family: a sweep or minor break that looks like a reversal
    setup but occurs WITHOUT the market having built any real opposing
    position first (no real accumulation/distribution phase, no trapped
    trader evidence per Section 21.1). Often occurs mid-trend, not at
    exhaustion.
    Typical resolution: the "reversal" fails and the prior trend
    resumes — because there was no real accumulation, just a mechanical
    stop-run.

  HOW TO USE ARCHETYPES:
    1. State explicitly which archetype the current setup most resembles.
    2. State which specific features MATCH the archetype's fingerprint
       (be specific: volume behavior, compression, displacement pattern)
       and which features DIVERGE from it (a setup that is missing key
       fingerprint elements of its claimed archetype is weaker than one
       that matches cleanly).
    3. Let the archetype's typical resolution inform the directional
       bias and the CONFIDENCE — a clean archetype match with all
       fingerprint elements present deserves more confidence than a
       partial, ambiguous resemblance, even if the raw confluence COUNT
       is similar between two setups.
    4. If the current setup does not clearly resemble ANY of these
       archetypes (or plausibly resembles two contradicting ones
       equally), that itself is information: it means the setup is
       genuinely ambiguous, and this should push toward HOLD regardless
       of what the mechanical confluence count says.

================================================================================
23.3  TRACK THE EVOLUTION, NOT THE SNAPSHOT
================================================================================

A checklist evaluates the CURRENT bar's indicators. Genuine market
understanding requires tracking how structure, momentum, and
participation have CHANGED over the visible history, because the
direction of change often matters more than the current absolute level.

  FOR EACH TIMEFRAME ANALYZED, explicitly compare the CURRENT swing/leg
  to the PREVIOUS 2-3 swings/legs of the same type, and note the trend
  in these dimensions:

  - AMPLITUDE: are impulse moves in the trend direction getting LARGER
    or SMALLER over the last several swings? Shrinking impulses in an
    uptrend (even while price still makes higher highs) is a classic
    early warning of exhaustion — the trend is technically intact but
    losing force.
  - REACTION STRENGTH: are counter-trend pullbacks getting DEEPER or
    SHALLOWER over time? Deepening pullbacks in an uptrend suggest
    weakening bullish control even before any structural break occurs.
  - VOLUME PROFILE ACROSS SWINGS: is volume expanding or contracting
    on the trend-direction legs versus the counter-trend legs? Volume
    should generally favor the trend direction in a healthy trend;
    volume favoring counter-trend legs is a divergence warning
    (distribution/accumulation happening quietly against the visible
    trend — see Wyckoff-style ARCHETYPE reasoning in 23.2).
  - TIME COMPRESSION: are swings taking LESS time to complete (more
    aggressive, potentially unstable) or MORE time (settling into a
    more sustainable rhythm)?

  This evolutionary comparison is what separates "the current candle
  shows X" from genuine pattern understanding — a veteran does not
  just read the current bar, they read the current bar AS THE LATEST
  CHAPTER of a story that has been developing over the last 20-50 bars.
  A setup that looks identical on its last 3 candles can mean completely
  different things depending on whether it caps off a strengthening
  trend or a visibly exhausting one.

================================================================================
23.4  FROM UNDERSTANDING TO DECISION — HOW THE PIECES CONNECT
================================================================================

This is the explicit reasoning sequence that replaces "sum the points":

  STEP 1 — NARRATIVE: Build the plain-language story (23.1). What has
    been happening, and why, across the visible history?

  STEP 2 — ARCHETYPE: Which pattern family does the current situation
    most resemble (23.2)? How cleanly does it match the fingerprint?

  STEP 3 — EVOLUTION CHECK: Does the trajectory of amplitude, reaction
    strength, volume, and timing (23.3) support or contradict the
    narrative and archetype identified above?

  STEP 4 — MECHANICAL VERIFICATION: NOW apply the specific mechanical
    tools (Order Block detection, FVG rules, Confluence scoring,
    Authenticity checks) to verify that the narrative holds up to
    rigorous, falsifiable, numeric scrutiny (Sections 4-21). The
    mechanical checklist is not where understanding comes from — it is
    the DISCIPLINE that prevents a compelling-sounding narrative from
    being trusted without hard verification. A good story with no
    mechanical confirmation is just a story. Mechanical confirmation
    with no coherent story is just noise that happens to check boxes.
    BOTH must agree.

  STEP 5 — DEVIL'S ADVOCATE (Section 21.7): Attempt to build the
    opposing narrative and archetype using the same data. If it cannot
    be dismissed with specific evidence, the confidence ceiling applies.

  STEP 6 — DECISION: The signal (and its confidence score) is the
    CONCLUSION of this integrated reasoning chain — narrative, archetype,
    evolution, and mechanical verification all pointing the same
    direction — not a number that happened to accumulate from
    independently-true but disconnected checklist items.

  A signal is HIGH QUALITY when steps 1-4 all reinforce each other.
  A signal is WEAK or should be a HOLD when they contradict: e.g. the
  narrative and archetype suggest exhaustion, but mechanical scoring
  still shows trend-aligned confluences — this contradiction itself
  is valuable information (the trend may be ending exactly now, ahead
  of what the lagging mechanical indicators show) and should be surfaced
  explicitly in the reasoning, not silently resolved by picking whichever
  side has the higher point total.

================================================================================
23.5  WHAT "UNDERSTANDING, NOT MEMORIZING" MEANS IN THE OUTPUT
================================================================================

The final "reasoning" field of every signal (Section 20, SIGNAL_OUTPUT)
must demonstrate integrated understanding, not template completion. A
reasoning field that just restates "checklist items were present" fails
this section even if every underlying fact is correct. A reasoning field
must:

  1. Open with the NARRATIVE (23.1) — the story, not the scorecard.
  2. Name the ARCHETYPE (23.2) explicitly and say how well it fits.
  3. Reference the EVOLUTION (23.3) — how this compares to recent history,
     not just the current bar.
  4. Only THEN cite the specific mechanical evidence (OB levels, FVG
     zones, vol_ratios, ATR multiples) as VERIFICATION of the narrative,
     using the Verifiability Test (Section 21.8) for each cited number.
  5. Explicitly note if evolution/archetype and mechanical scoring
     agree or conflict, and how that was resolved.

  A reasoning field that only does step 4 (lists mechanical facts with
  no narrative, archetype, or evolutionary context) indicates the system
  is pattern-matching a template, not understanding the market — and
  should be treated as a lower-quality analysis even if it happens to
  reach the same directional conclusion.

================================================================================
END OF [HOLISTIC_MARKET_READING]
================================================================================

================================================================================
[BOS_OB_DIRECTIONAL_INTEGRITY]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
This section exists because of a SPECIFIC, DOCUMENTED failure mode caught
in real backtesting (not a hypothetical): the system correctly detected
a genuine bullish displacement candle that broke structure (BOS confirmed
upward, per Section 5.2 Step 2), but then, anchored to the LARGER prior
downtrend narrative, mislabeled that exact same bullish displacement
candle as a "Bearish Order Block" / resistance zone — producing a SELL
signal directly opposite to what its own mechanical detection had just
proven. The market then moved up +4.8%, confirming the bullish break was
real and the directional labeling was the error, not the underlying data
or the archetype framework.

This is not a rare edge case. It is the single most dangerous failure
mode for this system: correctly computing a mechanical fact (BOS
direction, displacement direction) and then OVERRIDING that fact with
a qualitative narrative bias ("the big picture is still bearish") without
ever reconciling the contradiction explicitly. A model that can compute
correctly but still flip the conclusion has not understood the market —
it has pattern-matched to the more emotionally dominant recent story
(see Section 22.1 Anti-Recency Bias — this is its mirror image: bias
toward the OLDER dominant trend instead of the newest candles, but the
error mechanism is identical: privileging a narrative over the specific
falsifiable evidence in front of it).

================================================================================
24.1  THE MANDATORY BOS-OB CONSISTENCY CHECK
================================================================================

Before finalizing ANY signal, run this exact cross-check, in this order,
and SHOW the check in the reasoning field (not just the conclusion):

  STEP A — IDENTIFY THE MOST RECENT DISPLACEMENT CANDLE(S)
    Scan the last 10-15 candles for any sequence meeting the impulse
    criteria in Section 5.2/5.3 (range ≥ 2x ATR, body_pct > 60-70%,
    vol_ratio > 1.5). This is a pure numeric scan — it does not care
    what the "story so far" has been.

  STEP B — DETERMINE THE DISPLACEMENT'S OWN DIRECTION, LITERALLY
    Ask ONLY: "close[end] compared to open[start] of this impulse —
    is the impulse itself bullish (close > open) or bearish (close < open)?"
    Do not let the preceding trend influence this answer. A bullish-
    bodied displacement candle can NEVER be classified as a "Bearish
    Order Block" or bearish resistance zone. A bearish-bodied
    displacement candle can NEVER be classified as a "Bullish Order
    Block" or bullish support zone. THE OB TYPE MUST MATCH THE
    DISPLACEMENT'S OWN CANDLE DIRECTION, PERIOD — this is not a
    judgment call, it is definitional (Section 5.1: "the displacement
    itself... is the evidence"). If your labeling produces a mismatch
    (a bullish impulse candle called a Bearish OB, or vice versa), that
    labeling is WRONG and must be corrected before continuing, no
    matter how compelling the surrounding narrative feels.

  STEP C — CHECK WHETHER THIS DISPLACEMENT BROKE STRUCTURE (BOS)
    Compare impulse_high (for bullish) or impulse_low (for bearish)
    against the most recent swing high/low that existed BEFORE the
    impulse began (Section 5.2/5.3 Step 2). State explicitly:
    "Did this specific displacement exceed the prior swing [high/low]?
    YES/NO, by how much (in price and in ATR multiples)?"

  STEP D — RECONCILE WITH THE PRIOR TREND, DO NOT SILENTLY OVERRIDE
    If Step C found YES (a genuine BOS against the prior trend
    direction), this is direct, falsifiable evidence that the OLD
    trend may be ending RIGHT NOW. You may still conclude the old
    trend resumes (e.g. if this reads as a Trap Continuation per
    Section 23.2 archetype), but ONLY if you can explicitly show
    Section 21.1's Trapped Trader Test EITHER:
      (a) confirms the sweep+displacement was fake/unfueled
          (no genuine trapped-trader evidence, low volume on the
          reversal attempt, immediate failure within 1-2 candles), OR
      (b) shows the "reversal" displacement itself failed to hold
          (price closed back below/above the broken level within
          1-3 candles — Section 21.2 Check 3 Follow-Through)
    If NEITHER (a) nor (b) can be shown with specific numbers, and the
    displacement candle shows a real BOS with body_pct > 70% and
    vol_ratio > 1.5, you MUST treat this as a genuine reversal
    signal (Spring/Stop-Hunt archetype), not as continuation of the
    old trend — regardless of how dominant the older trend looks on
    a wider view. A confirmed, fueled, unretraced BOS in the opposite
    direction OUTRANKS the "big picture" narrative. The big picture
    narrative is what existed BEFORE this new evidence; new structural
    evidence updates the picture, it does not get discarded by it.

  STEP E — SELF-CONTRADICTION AUDIT (MANDATORY, WRITE THIS EXPLICITLY)
    Before outputting the final signal, answer in the reasoning field:
    "Does my final directional bias agree with the direction of the
    MOST RECENT confirmed BOS? If NO, explain SPECIFICALLY (citing
    Section 21.1/21.2 evidence, not just 'trend still bearish') why
    that BOS should be discounted. If you cannot articulate concrete,
    falsifiable evidence for why the most recent BOS is fake/trapped/
    failed, you may NOT override it with the older trend — downgrade
    to HOLD instead of picking the older trend by default."

================================================================================
24.2  WHY "THE BIG PICTURE" IS NOT A LICENSE TO IGNORE THE LATEST EVIDENCE
================================================================================

A downtrend that has been in place for days or weeks feels more "real"
and more anchoring than a single fresh displacement candle — this is
a cognitive bias, not an analytical advantage. Trends END exactly at
the moment a fresh, fueled, structure-breaking displacement occurs in
the opposite direction; by definition, the FIRST candle of a genuine
reversal will always look small relative to the trend that preceded it.
Demanding that a reversal "prove itself" by first matching the size/
duration of the prior trend before being believed guarantees missing
every single reversal at its actual start — which defeats the entire
purpose of the Spring/Stop-Hunt archetype (Section 23.2) and the
Trapped Trader Test (Section 21.1), both of which exist specifically
to catch reversals AT THE MOMENT they begin, using mechanical evidence,
not after the fact once the "big picture" has visibly changed.

PRACTICAL RULE: when a genuine, high-quality BOS (body_pct > 70%,
vol_ratio > 1.5, broke a real prior swing) appears against the
established trend, treat the OLDER trend as the hypothesis that now
needs to defend itself with SPECIFIC counter-evidence (Section 21.1
trapped-trader failure, or Section 21.2 follow-through failure) —
not the other way around. The burden of proof shifts to whichever
side lacks the most recent confirmed structural evidence, not to
whichever side is newer.

================================================================================
24.3  OUTPUT REQUIREMENT — MAKE THE CHECK VISIBLE, NOT INTERNAL
================================================================================

The "reasoning" field for every BUY/SELL signal must include a short,
explicit sentence performing Step E above, in this exact spirit:
  "Most recent confirmed BOS direction: [UP/DOWN/none]. My signal
   direction [AGREES/CONFLICTS] with it. [If CONFLICTS: specific
   evidence why the BOS is discounted, citing Section 21.1/21.2
   with numbers — not a restatement of the older trend]."

A signal missing this explicit reconciliation is incomplete, even if
every other field is populated correctly — silence on this point is
exactly how the documented failure (bullish BOS mislabeled as bearish
OB, SELL signal issued against a market that then rallied 4.8%)
happened in the first place: the contradiction existed in the data
the whole time, it was simply never surfaced and confronted.

================================================================================
END OF [BOS_OB_DIRECTIONAL_INTEGRITY]
================================================================================

================================================================================
[LAST_CANDLE_ACCURACY]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
This section exists because of a SPECIFIC, DOCUMENTED failure mode caught
in real backtesting (not a hypothetical): the system described the most
recent candle's color and body size in direct contradiction to the actual
numbers in the data it was given. In one documented case, a candle with
Open=2929.0, High=3031.0, Low=2886.7, Close=2904.9 (a BEARISH candle -
close is below open - with a body only 16.7% of its range, i.e. mostly a
long upper wick) was described in the reasoning as "a large bullish
candle, body_pct ~80%". This is not a strategic misjudgment (correctly
reading data but drawing a debatable conclusion) - it is a factual
hallucination about the most basic possible fact (which direction did
price move on this candle, and by how much of the range). This occurred
because the model built its narrative/bias FIRST from general impression
and momentum of the story, then unconsciously "filled in" the last
candle's description to match the story it wanted to tell, rather than
reading the actual four numbers first.

This is the most dangerous class of error possible in this system,
because every other section (Order Blocks, BOS, archetypes, confluence
scoring) assumes the raw OHLC readings feeding into them are accurate.
A perfect application of every ICT rule on top of a hallucinated candle
color produces a confidently-reasoned, well-structured, completely wrong
signal - and unlike a debatable interpretation, this kind of error is
NOT something more "thinking" fixes, because the thinking itself started
from a false premise.

================================================================================
25.1  READ THE RAW NUMBERS BEFORE WRITING ANY NARRATIVE
================================================================================

Before writing a single word of narrative, archetype, or bias, perform
this exact sequence for the MOST RECENT (last) candle in the data:

  1. Write down, digit by digit, Open, High, Low, Close exactly as given.
  2. Compute: is Close > Open (BULLISH) or Close < Open (BEARISH)? This
     is pure arithmetic - there is no interpretation step here at all.
  3. Compute body_pct = |Close - Open| / (High - Low) * 100. State the
     number.
  4. Only AFTER completing steps 1-3 with the literal numbers, proceed
     to interpret what this candle means in context.

  If you find yourself wanting to describe the last candle as "strong
  bullish momentum" or "weak bearish drift" or any qualitative label
  BEFORE you have done the Close-vs-Open arithmetic explicitly, STOP -
  this is exactly the failure sequence that produced the documented
  error above (narrative-first, arithmetic-never).

  The system output format requires a `last_candle_report` field with
  the exact Open/High/Low/Close/color of the last candle, populated
  BEFORE the narrative field is written, specifically to force this
  order of operations and to make the claim independently verifiable
  by a separate mechanical check (not just trusted from the prose).

================================================================================
25.2  THE NARRATIVE MUST BE CONSTRAINED BY THE NUMBERS, NEVER THE REVERSE
================================================================================

A subtle version of this failure occurs even when the raw OHLC values
in `last_candle_report` are copied correctly, but the narrative prose
STILL describes the candle inconsistently (e.g. correct numbers in the
structured field, but the narrative text says "a decisive bullish
close" about a candle that was in fact bearish). This is just as
serious an error - the structured field exists to be a hard constraint
that the free-text narrative must remain consistent with, not a
separate checkbox that can be filled correctly while the actual
reasoning ignores it.

RULE: after writing the narrative, re-read your own last_candle_report
values and confirm every adjective used in the narrative about the most
recent candle (bullish/bearish, strong/weak, decisive/indecisive) is
consistent with those exact numbers. If there is any mismatch, the
narrative is wrong and must be rewritten to match the numbers - the
numbers are never adjusted to match a narrative that was already
decided.

================================================================================
END OF [LAST_CANDLE_ACCURACY]
================================================================================

================================================================================
[HOLISTIC_VISUAL_SCANNING]
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
Section 25 (LAST_CANDLE_ACCURACY) exists to stop a specific, documented
failure: describing individual candle facts wrong. But accuracy on one
candle is not the same thing as understanding a chart. A real trader
with decades of screen time does not read a chart candle-by-candle in
isolation like a spreadsheet row - they first take in the WHOLE VISUAL
SHAPE of the chart (the way a human eye scans a picture: broad shape
first, details second, then broad shape again to confirm the details
fit), and only then drops down to verify specific candles numerically.
This section defines that top-down-then-bottom-up scanning discipline
explicitly, so the system does both: see the whole picture like a
trader's eye, AND get every individual number right (Section 25).

================================================================================
26.1  FIRST PASS - THE SILHOUETTE (макро-shape before any single candle)
================================================================================

Before analyzing any individual candle, scan the ENTIRE visible candle
range as one continuous shape and answer, in this order:

  1. If you had to draw this chart with ONE continuous line (ignoring
     every individual wick), what shape would it trace? A staircase up?
     A rounded top? A V-shaped spike down and back up? A flat range with
     a tail at one end? Describe this shape in one sentence BEFORE
     naming any specific price level.
  2. Where does the CURRENT price sit within that overall silhouette -
     top third, middle, bottom third, right at a shape inflection point
     (the "corner" where the silhouette changes direction)?
  3. Does the silhouette have ONE dominant character (clean, one-
     directional trend) or does it visibly change character partway
     through (e.g. trending, then flattening, then trending again in
     the opposite direction)? Where exactly does that character change
     happen, visually, in the sequence of candles?

  This first pass is deliberately holistic and approximate - it exists
  to prevent the mirror-image failure of Section 22.1 (recency bias)
  AND Section 24.2 (older-trend anchoring bias): a trader's eye catches
  the overall shape shift (e.g. "this was one long slide down that
  visibly curved and flattened over the last quarter of the chart")
  in a way that a candle-by-candle scan alone can miss.

================================================================================
26.2  SECOND PASS - ZOOM INTO THE INFLECTION POINTS
================================================================================

Having identified the overall silhouette and its inflection point(s)
in 26.1, zoom into those SPECIFIC regions (not the whole chart evenly)
for detailed candle-by-candle and numeric verification:

  - At each inflection point identified visually, this is where Order
    Blocks, FVGs, sweeps, and displacement are actually most likely to
    be structurally meaningful (Section 5, Section 21) - a "corner" in
    the silhouette is where institutional footprints concentrate.
  - This is also exactly where Section 25's candle-by-candle numeric
    accuracy check matters most: the LAST candle (closest to the most
    recent inflection or continuation) must be read with full digit-
    by-digit accuracy per Section 25.1, because it usually carries the
    most decision-weight.
  - Regions of the chart that are visually "boring" (flat, low
     volatility, no shape change) deserve less analytical weight, not
     equal weight to inflection regions - do not spend the same
     interpretive effort on every candle equally; concentrate where
     the shape itself is telling you something changed.

================================================================================
26.3  THIRD PASS - RECONCILE THE WHOLE WITH THE PARTS
================================================================================

After the detailed numeric pass (26.2, plus Sections 5/21/24/25), zoom
back out and ask: does the detailed, number-verified conclusion actually
match the overall silhouette identified in 26.1, or does it contradict
the big picture?

  - If they AGREE (the numeric evidence at the inflection point supports
    the same conclusion the overall shape suggested), this is a
    HIGH-CONFIDENCE read - both the holistic eye and the mechanical
    verification point the same way.
  - If they CONFLICT (e.g. the silhouette clearly shows an established
    uptrend, but the zoomed-in numeric read of the last few candles
    suggests a bearish reversal, or vice versa), this is exactly the
    situation Section 23.4 Step 6 and Section 24.2 describe: new
    structural evidence at an inflection point can override an older
    macro shape, but ONLY when backed by explicit, falsifiable evidence
    (Section 21.1 trapped-trader proof, confirmed BOS per Section 24).
    A conflict between "what the chart looks like overall" and "what
    the last few candles' numbers say" must be explicitly named in the
    reasoning (which one is winning, and why, with numbers) - it must
    never be silently resolved by defaulting to whichever the model
    initially favored (this is the exact anchoring failure documented
    in Section 24).

  The final narrative (Section 23.1) should read as if a person looked
  at the whole picture, then checked the details, then looked at the
  whole picture again to confirm the details make sense together - not
  as if the details were checked once at the end in isolation of the
  overall shape they came from.

================================================================================
END OF [HOLISTIC_VISUAL_SCANNING]
================================================================================

================================================================================
END OF KNOWLEDGE FILE — 26 SECTIONS COMPLETE
================================================================================
